In the Peṭaka it is said: 'Therefore, one who desires to be extinguished should seek meanings through what is heard; for that search, this sequence occurs: the sixteen modes of conveying, the five methods, the eighteen root-terms,' and so on (Peṭaka 3). He says that there is no special kind of meaning-commentary that is free from the examination of the modes of conveying and methods: 'The meaning-commentary of the Sutta is dependent on the instruction of the Netti.' This meaning will become evident later in the miscellaneous discourse. Since this Netti has such a vast scope, from where does it originate? He says, 'It originates from the Suttas' (suttappabhavā), thereby showing that the Netti is authoritative. And this is for the purpose of illuminating the Netti's reliance on the Sutta, not for the purpose of refuting its origination from the Elder. For the Elder, having immersed himself in the five great Nikāyas, spoke this treatise, which is an exposition, relying on them. Therefore, this very principle of exposition is this: reliance on the Dhamma to be expounded.

Pakaraṇaparicchedatoti pakaraṇassa vibhāgato. Hāravicārādayo hi tayo nettippakaraṇassa vibhāgā, pakaraṇabhūtaparicchedato vā. Tīṇi hi etāni pakaraṇāni tayo adhikārā, yadidaṃ hāravicārādayo. Pāḷivavatthānatoti pāṭhasannivesato.

By 'by the division of the treatise' (pakaraṇaparicchedato) is meant by the division of the treatise. For the three, namely, the examination of the modes of conveying and so on, are divisions of the Nettippakaraṇa; or by the division that constitutes the treatise. For these three are treatises, three sections, namely, the examination of the modes of conveying and so on. By 'by the arrangement of the text' (pāḷivavatthānato) is meant by the arrangement of the passage.

“Sabbo [V 0.0017] hi pakaraṇattho”tiādinā saṅgahavārassa anvatthasaññataṃ dasseti. “Nanu cettha paṭṭhānaṃ asaṅgahita”nti codako byabhicāramāha. Itaro yadipi sarūpato asaṅgahitaṃ, atthato pana saṅgahitanti dassento “nayidameva”tiādinā pariharati. Puna “tathā hī”tiādinā tamevatthaṃ pāḷiyā pākaṭataraṃ karoti. Atthanayā nandiyāvaṭṭādayo. Saṅkhārattikā puññābhisaṅkhārādayo, kāyasaṅkhārādayo ca. Tesu atthanayānaṃ aññamaññasaṅgaho parato āvi bhavissati. Itare pana kāmāvacarā, rūpāvacarā ca kusalā cetanā puññābhisaṅkhāro, akusalā cetanā apuññābhisaṅkhāro, arūpāvacarā kusalā cetanā āneñjābhisaṅkhāro. Puññābhisaṅkhāro ca apuññābhisaṅkhāro ca kāyadvārappavatto kāyasaṅkhāro, so eva vacīdvārappavatto vacīsaṅkhāro, manodvārappavatto pana tividhopi cittasaṅkhāro. Iti jātivasena purimattike vuttā eva dhammā dvāravasena dutiyattike vuttā, te eva ca purimattiketi aññamaññasaṅgaho veditabbo.

By 'for all the meaning of the treatise' (sabbo hi pakaraṇattho) and so on, he shows that the designation of the summary section corresponds to its meaning. The objector states a contradiction: 'But is not the Paṭṭhāna unsummarized here?' The other, showing that even if it is unsummarized in its own nature, it is summarized in meaning, refutes with 'this is not so' (nayidameva) and so on. Again, by 'for thus' (tathā hi) and so on, he makes that very meaning clearer through the text. The meaning-methods (atthanayā) are Nandiyāvaṭṭa and so on. The triads of formations (saṅkhārattikā) are the meritorious formations and so on, and the bodily formations and so on. Among these, the mutual inclusion of the meaning-methods will become evident later on. But the others are: wholesome volitions pertaining to the sense-sphere and form-sphere are the meritorious formation; unwholesome volition is the demeritorious formation; wholesome volition pertaining to the formless-sphere is the imperturbable formation. The meritorious formation and the demeritorious formation, when occurring through the body-door, are the bodily formation; the same, when occurring through the speech-door, is the verbal formation; but when occurring through the mind-door, all three are the mental formation. Thus, the very same phenomena mentioned in the first triad according to their kind are mentioned in the second triad according to the door, and they are the very same as those in the first triad; thus their mutual inclusion should be understood.

Yatthāti yasmiṃ vāre. Peṭaketi peṭakopadese. Sampatamānāti saṃvaṇṇanāvasena sannipatantā. “Byañjanavidhiputhuttā”ti idaṃ ekasmiṃ sutte anekesaṃ hārānaṃ sannipatanassa kāraṇavacanaṃ. Tathā hi “anekasāmatthiyanicitā saddā”ti akkharacintakā vadanti.

By 'where' (yattha) is meant in which section. By 'in the Peṭaka' (peṭake) is meant in the Peṭakopadesa. By 'converging' (sampatamānā) is meant converging in the manner of exposition. 'Due to the diversity of the methods of expression' (byañjanavidhiputhuttā): this is a statement of the reason for the convergence of many modes of conveying in one Sutta. Thus, grammarians say, 'Words are imbued with manifold capacities.'

“Na sarūpato”ti iminā saṅgahavāre viya uddesaniddesavāresupi paṭṭhānassa atthato uddhaṭataṃ dasseti. Mūlapadaggahaṇeneva gahitattā uddesavāre tāva evaṃ hotu, niddesavāre pana kathanti? Tatthāpi nayaggahaṇeneva mūlapadānipi gahitānīti veditabbaṃ. Na hi mūlapadehi vinā kāci nayayojanā sambhavati. Apare pana “hāranayā viya paṭṭhānaṃ na [M 0.0019] suttassa saṃvaṇṇanāviseso, atha kho tasmiṃ tasmiṃ sutte saṃkilesabhāgiyatādilabbhamānavisesamattanti na tassa pakaraṇassa padatthasaṅgaho. Evañca katvā tettiṃsāya nettipadatthesu paṭṭhānaṃ asaṅgahitaṃ, uddesaniddesavāresu ca anuddhaṭamevā”ti vadanti.

By 'not in its own form' (na sarūpato), he shows that the Paṭṭhāna is extracted in meaning in the enumeration and explanation sections as well, just as in the summary section. Since it is grasped by the grasping of the root-terms, let it be so in the enumeration section; but how in the explanation section? There too, it should be understood that the root-terms are also grasped by the grasping of the methods. For without the root-terms, no application of a method is possible. Others, however, say: 'The Paṭṭhāna, like the modes of conveying and methods, is not a special kind of exposition of the Sutta, but rather it is merely a specific aspect obtainable in each Sutta, such as the partaking of defilement; therefore, there is no collection of the meanings of the terms for that treatise. And so, the Paṭṭhāna is not included in the thirty-three meanings of the Netti terms, and in the enumeration and explanation sections, it is not extracted at all'.

“Pāḷito eva viññāyatī”ti vuttamatthaṃ samatthento “tathā hi…pe… ābhata”nti āha, tena therena bhāsitabhāvo viya bhagavatā anumoditabhāvopi pāḷianugato evāti dasseti. Sāvakabhāsitattā nidānaṃ na vuttanti na sakkā vattunti codento “sāvaka…pe… bhāsita”nti āha. Nayidaṃ ekantikanti ca sāvakabhāsitabuddhabhāsitabhāvo nidānāvacanassa, nidānavacanassa ca akāraṇaṃ ubhayatthāpi ubhayassa dassanato. Tasmā nidānāvacanena nettiyā asāvakabhāsitatā na sijjhatīti dasseti. Tenāha “na ca tāvatā tāni appamāṇaṃ, evamidhāpi daṭṭhabba”nti.

Supporting the stated meaning, “It is understood from the Pāḷi itself,” he says, “for thus …pe… brought,” thereby showing that just as it was spoken by that elder, so too its approval by the Blessed One is in conformity with the Pāḷi. Raising the objection, “It cannot be said that the introduction is omitted because this is the speech of a disciple,” he says, “a disciple …pe… spoken.” And this is not a one-sided matter, for being the speech of a disciple or the speech of the Buddha is not a reason for the omission or inclusion of the introduction, since both are found in both cases. Therefore, he shows that the Netti’s not being the speech of a disciple cannot be established by the omission of the introduction. Hence he says: “And those are not unauthoritative on that account; so too it should be understood here.”

Yeneva [V 0.0018] kāraṇena nidānāvacanassa pamāṇabhāvasādhanatā, teneva kāraṇena imassa pakaraṇassa pamāṇabhāvasiddhīti dasseti “nidānañca nāmā”tiādinā. Idāni “atha vā”tiādinā nettiyā nidānāvacanena abyabhicārahetumāha. Ayañhettha payogo na nettiyā nidānaṃ vattabbaṃ pāḷiyā atthasaṃvaṇṇanābhāvato. Yā hi pāḷiyā atthasaṃvaṇṇanā na tassā nidānavacanaṃ diṭṭhaṃ yathā paṭisambhidāmaggassa, niddesādīnañcāti.

With the words, “An introduction, namely…” and so on, he shows that for the very reason that the omission of an introduction does not disprove its authoritativeness, for that very reason the authoritativeness of this treatise is established. Now, with “Or else…” and so on, he states the reason why the Netti is not an exception regarding the omission of the introduction. Herein this is the application: the introduction to the Netti should not be stated, because it is a commentary on the meaning of a Pāḷi text. For an introduction is not seen for a text that is a commentary on the meaning of a Pāḷi text, such as the Paṭisambhidāmagga, the Niddesa, and so on.

“Ayaṃ vibhāgo”tiādinā ekavidhato paṭṭhāya yāva caturāsītisahassappabhedā, tāva yathādassitassa pakaraṇavibhāgassa puna “ādinā nayena pakaraṇavibhāgo veditabbo”ti idaṃ nigamanaṃ. Tattha ādinā nayenāti ādisaddena abhiññeyyadhammaniddesato paññattipaññapetabbadhammavibhajanato tiyaddhapariyāpannadhammavicārato caturoghanittharaṇatthato pañcābhinandanādippahānato chataṇhākāyupasamanato saṅgahavārādisattavārasaṅgahato aṭṭhamicchattasamugghātadīpanatoti evamādīnaṃ saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo.

With “This is the classification…” and so on, this is the conclusion for the classification of the treatise as shown, starting from the onefold division up to the eighty-four thousand divisions: “The classification of the treatise should be understood again by the initial method.” Therein, by “by the initial method” is meant that by the word “initial” (ādi), the inclusion of the following should be understood: the exposition of phenomena to be directly known; the classification of designated and designatable phenomena; the examination of phenomena included in the one-and-a-half rounds; for the purpose of crossing the four floods; the abandonment of the five kinds of delight, etc.; the pacification of the six bodies of craving; the inclusion of the seven sections beginning with the section on inclusion; and the illumination of the uprooting of the eight wrongnesses. Thus the inclusion of these and other such points should be understood.

1. Saṅgahavāravaṇṇanā

1. The Explanation of the Section on Inclusion

Yanti aniyamattho sabbanāmasaddo kammasādhanavasena vutto. Atthāvabodhanattho saddappayogo atthaparādhīno kevalo atthapadatthako[M 0.0020], so padatthavipariyesakārinā iti-saddena parabhūtena saddapadatthako jāyatīti āha “yanti aniyamato upayoganiddeso”ti. Lokoti kattuniddesotiādīsupi eseva nayo.

The pronoun yaṃ has an indefinite meaning and is used in the sense of the object. The use of a word for the purpose of conveying a meaning is dependent on the meaning; its primary object is the meaning of the term. He says that this—whose primary object is the meaning of the term—becomes one whose primary object is the word itself by means of the particle iti, which reverses the object of the term and is governed by another word. Thus he says: “The word yaṃ is an indefinite designation of application.” This same method applies in such cases as “The world is a designation of the agent.”

Evaṃ “ya”ntiādīnaṃ gāthāpadānaṃ kammakattukiriyākattuvisesanādidassanavasena atthaṃ vatvā idāni avayavajotanavasena padatthaṃ dassetuṃ “lokiyanti etthā”tiādimāha. Lokasaddo idha sāmatthiyato sattalokavacano daṭṭhabbo. Tenāha “pūjanakiriyāyogyabhūtatāvasenā”ti. Sāsanantaradhānato paraṃ pūjanā aññabuddhuppādena veditabbā, yathetarahi vipassīādisammāsambuddhānaṃ. “Dīpaṅkaro”tiādinā yadipi buddhavaṃsadesanāyaṃ (bu. vaṃ. 2.75) bhagavatāva vuttaṃ, sumedhapaṇḍitattabhāvena pana pavattiṃ sandhāya vuttanti āha “yathāha bhagavā sumedhabhūto”ti.

Having thus explained the meaning of the words of the verse beginning with “yaṃ” by way of showing the object, agent, action, qualifier of the agent, etc., he now says, “lokiyanti etthā…” and so on in order to show the meaning of the words by way of illuminating their components. Here the word “loka” should be understood, by virtue of its capacity, as denoting the world of beings. Hence he says: “by way of its being a fitting object for the act of veneration.” After the disappearance of the Dispensation, veneration should be understood as occurring with the arising of another Buddha, just as it is now for the Perfectly Self-Enlightened Ones such as Vipassī. Although “Dīpaṅkara…” and so on was spoken by the Blessed One himself in the discourse on the Chronicle of the Buddhas (Bv 2.75), he says that it was stated with reference to his conduct when he was the wise Sumedha. Thus he says: “as the Blessed One said when he was Sumedha.”

Pariññākkamenāti ñātapariññādipaṭipāṭiyā. Lakkhaṇāvabodhappaṭipattiyāti vipassanāya. Tena [V 0.0019] vuttaṃ “suññatamukhādīhī”ti. Tathā ca vuttanti viññūhi vedanīyatāya eva sāsanavarassa vuttaṃ bhagavatā –

“By the order of full understanding” means: by the sequence of full understanding as the known, etc. “By the practice of awakening to the characteristics” means: by insight. Therefore it is said: “by the gate of emptiness, etc.” And it has been stated thus, for the best of teachings has been spoken of by the Blessed One as something to be experienced by the wise:

“Etu viññū puriso asaṭho amāyāvī ujujātiko, ahamanusāsāmi, ahaṃ dhammaṃ desemi, yathānusiṭṭhaṃ tathā paṭipajjamāno na cirasseva sāmaññeva ussati, sāmaṃ dakkhitī”tiādi (ma. ni. 2.281).

“Let an intelligent person come, one who is not fraudulent, not deceitful, of an upright nature. I will instruct him, I will teach him the Dhamma. Practicing in accordance with the instruction, in no long time he will know for himself and see for himself…” and so on (MN 77, II 281).

Yaṃ-saddo sāsanavisayo, lokapālasaddo satthuvisayopi lokaṃ pati guṇībhūtoti “tassā”ti paṭiniddesassa kathaṃ satthuvisayatāti codanaṃ manasikatvā āha “salokapāloti cetthā”tiādi, guṇībhūtopi lokapālasaddo padhānabhūto viya paṭiniddesaṃ arahati. Añño hi saddakkamo, añño atthakkamoti.

The word yaṃ has the Teaching as its domain. The word lokapāla (protector of the world) also has the Teacher as its domain, but it is subordinate with respect to loka (world). Bearing in mind the objection, “How can the referent tassā (of that) have the Teacher as its domain?” he says, “salokapālo ti cettha…” and so on. Even though the word lokapāla is subordinate, it deserves to be the referent as if it were primary. For the order of words is one thing, and the order of meaning is another.

Dhammagāravena bhagavā dhammaṃ pūjento veneyyabandhave acintetvā samāpattisamāpajjanadhammapaccavekkhaṇāhi sattasattāhaṃ vītināmesīti āha “bhagavato…pe… dīpetabbā”ti. Tattha ādisaddena sāvakehi dhammassavanassa, tesaṃ paccuggamanādīnañca saṅgaho veditabbo.

Out of reverence for the Dhamma, the Blessed One, honoring the Dhamma, spent seven weeks without giving thought to his kinsmen to be trained, by entering attainments and reviewing the Dhamma. He says: “of the Blessed One …pe… should be shown.” Therein, by the word “etc.” should be understood the inclusion of the disciples’ hearing of the Dhamma, their going out to meet him, and so on.

Iccassāti [M 0.0021] iti assa, evaṃ bhagavato aviparītaanantarāyikaniyyānikadhammadesanāya sabbaññutānāvaraṇabhāvadīpanenāti attho. Tenāti catuvesārajjayogena. Tadavinābhāvinā dasabala…pe… pakāsitā hoti. Āveṇikabuddhadhammādīti ettha ādisaddena tīsu kālesu appaṭihatañāṇāni, catusaccañāṇāni, catupaṭisambhidāñāṇāni, pañcagatiparicchedakañāṇāni, cha abhiññāñāṇāni, satta ariyadhanāni, satta bojjhaṅgā, aṭṭha vijjā, aṭṭhasu parisāsu akampanañāṇāni, aṭṭha vimokkhā, nava samādhicariyā, nava anupubbavihārā, dasa nāthakaraṇā dhammā, dasa ariyavāsā, dvādasa dhammacakkākārā, terasa dhutadhammā, cuddasa buddhañāṇāni, pannarasa caraṇadhammā, soḷasa ñāṇacariyā, soḷasa ānāpānassatī, ekūnavīsati paccavekkhaṇañāṇāni, catuvīsati paccayavibhāvanañāṇāni, catucattārīsa ñāṇavatthūni, sattasattati ñāṇavatthūni, catuvīsatikoṭisatasahassasamāpattisañcārimahāvajirañāṇaṃ, anantanayasamantapaṭṭhānapavicayadesanākārappavattañāṇāni cāti evamādīnaṃ bhagavato guṇavisesānaṃ saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo.

Iccassa means iti assa (thus of this); the meaning is: thus, by revealing the unobstructed nature of his omniscience through the Blessed One’s teaching of the Dhamma, which is unerring, immediately effective, and leading out. Tena means: by the possession of the four kinds of confidence. Inseparably connected with that, the ten powers …pe… are revealed. In “the unique qualities of a Buddha, etc.,” by the word “etc.” the inclusion of the following distinguished qualities of the Blessed One should be understood: the knowledges unobstructed in the three times; the knowledges of the four truths; the four analytical knowledges; the knowledge that defines the five destinies; the six higher knowledges; the seven treasures of the ariyas; the seven factors of enlightenment; the eight kinds of knowledge; the knowledges unshaken in the eight assemblies; the eight liberations; the nine courses of concentration; the nine successive abodes; the ten qualities that make a protector; the ten abodes of the ariyas; the twelve aspects of the Wheel of Dhamma; the thirteen ascetic practices; the fourteen Buddha-knowledges; the fifteen items of conduct; the sixteen courses of knowledge; the sixteen aspects of mindfulness of breathing; the nineteen reflective knowledges; the twenty-four knowledges that analyze conditions; the forty-four bases of knowledge; the seventy-seven bases of knowledge; the great diamond-like knowledge that ranges over twenty-four trillion attainments; and the knowledges that proceed in the manner of teaching by investigating the Paṭṭhāna with its infinite methods.

Aparo [V 0.0020] nayo – guṇavisiṭṭhataṃ dīpeti, sā ca guṇavisiṭṭhatā mahākaruṇāmahāpaññāhi veditabbā tāhi satthusampattisiddhito. Tattha mahākaruṇāya pavattibhedo “bahukehi ākārehi passantānaṃ buddhānaṃ bhagavantānaṃ sattesu mahākaruṇā okkamatī”tiādinā paṭisambhidāmagge (paṭi. ma. 1.117) vuttanayena veditabbo. Mahāpaññāya pana pavattibhedo vutto eva. Tattha karuṇāya bhagavato caraṇasampatti, paññāya vijjāsampatti. Karuṇāya sattādhipatitā, paññāya dhammādhipatitā. Karuṇāya lokanāthatā, paññāya attanāthatā. Karuṇāya pubbakāritā, paññāya kataññutā. Karuṇāya aparantapatā, paññāya anattantapatā. Karuṇāya buddhakaradhammasiddhi, paññāya buddhabhāvasiddhi. Karuṇāya paresaṃ tāraṇaṃ, paññāya sayaṃ tāraṇaṃ. Karuṇāya sabbasattesu anuggahacittatā, paññāya sabbadhammesu virattacittatā pakāsitā hotīti anavasesato parahitapaṭipattiyā, attahitasampattiyā ca pāripūrī veditabbā. Tīsupi avatthāsūti hetuphalasattūpakārāvatthāsu.

Another method: it reveals the distinction of qualities. That distinction of qualities should be understood through great compassion and great wisdom, since the Teacher’s accomplishment is achieved through them. Herein, the diversity in the operation of great compassion should be understood in the way stated in the Paṭisambhidāmagga: “When the Buddhas, the Blessed Ones, see beings in many ways, great compassion descends upon them…” and so on (Ps 1.117). The diversity in the operation of great wisdom, however, has already been stated. Herein, through compassion the Blessed One has the accomplishment of conduct; through wisdom, the accomplishment of knowledge. Through compassion, he has sovereignty over beings; through wisdom, sovereignty over the Dhamma. Through compassion, he is the protector of the world; through wisdom, his own protector. Through compassion, he is a benefactor to others; through wisdom, he is grateful. Through compassion, he does not torment others; through wisdom, he does not torment himself. Through compassion, the accomplishment of the qualities that make a Buddha; through wisdom, the accomplishment of the state of a Buddha. Through compassion, the ferrying across of others; through wisdom, his own ferrying across. Through compassion, a mind of helpfulness towards all beings is made manifest; through wisdom, a mind of detachment from all phenomena. Thus, complete fulfillment should be understood through the practice for the welfare of others without remainder and through the attainment of one’s own welfare. “In all three states” means: in the states of cause, effect, and benefiting beings.

Abhisamayo [M 0.0022] paṭivedhasāsanassa, manasikaraṇaṃ paṭipattisāsanassa, savanādīhi paricayakaraṇaṃ pariyattisāsanassāti tiṇṇampi vasena yojetabbo. Tenāha “yathāraha”nti. “Sakkaccaṃ dhammadesanenā”ti iminā idha “sāsana”nti vuttassa tividhassāpi saddhammassa avisesena desanāpūjaṃ vatvā thomanāpūjanassa vasena taṃ vibhajitvā dassento “ariyaṃ, vo bhikkhave”tiādimāha. Tattha “thomanenā”ti padenāpi “sakkacca”nti padaṃ yojetabbaṃ. Pūjanādvayassāpi vā vasena idhāpi padayojanā veditabbā. Ariyabhāvādayoti ariyaseṭṭhaaggabhāvādayo. Niyyānādayoti niyyānahetudassanādayo. Svākkhātatādayoti svākkhātasandiṭṭhikatādayo.

Breakthrough belongs to the teaching of penetration, attention to the teaching of practice, and familiarization through hearing, etc., to the teaching of study; thus it should be connected in all three ways. Hence he says, “as is fitting.” With the phrase “by teaching the Dhamma respectfully,” having stated the veneration by teaching the threefold Good Dhamma—spoken of here as “the teaching”—without distinction, he then shows it by dividing it by way of veneration by praise, saying, “Noble, O bhikkhus…” and so on. Therein, the word “respectfully” should also be connected with the word “by praise.” Or else, the connection of the words here should be understood by way of the twofold veneration. “Nobility, etc.” means: the states of being noble, best, and foremost. “Leading out, etc.” means: showing the cause of leading out, etc. “Being well-expounded, etc.” means: being well-expounded, visible here and now, etc.

Idāni ariyasaṅghaguṇānampi imāya gāthāya pakāsitabhāvaṃ dassetuṃ “yasmā panā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Bālyādisamatikkamanatoti bālaabyattabhāvādisamatikkamanato.

Now, to show that the qualities of the noble Saṅgha are also revealed by this verse, it is said, “But since…” and so on. “From overcoming childishness, etc.” means: from overcoming the state of being childish, inept, etc.

Ñāṇaviseso sutacintābhāvanāmayañāṇāni. Sotabbamanasikātabbapaṭivijjhitabbāvatthā avatthābhedo. Ubhayanti byañjanapadaṃ, atthapadañca. Ubhayathāti karaṇakammasādhanavasena paccekaṃ yojetabbaṃ. Paṭipajjitabbattāti ñātabbattā.

The distinction of knowledge is the knowledge consisting of learning, reflection, and cultivation. The states that are to be heard, attended to, and thoroughly understood, and the distinction of states. “Both” refers to the verbal expression and the meaning. “In both ways” should be individually connected according to the instrumental, objective, and means of accomplishment. “Because it must be undertaken” means “because it must be known.”

“Ayañca gāthā”tiādi kesañci vādo. Tathā hi apare “therenevāyaṃ gāthā bhāsitā”ti vadanti. Attūpanāyikāpi hi kadāci dhammadesanā hoti eva yathā “dasabalasamannāgato[V 0.0021], bhikkhave, tathāgato catuvesārajjavisārado”tiādi (saṃ. ni. 2.21-22). Evañca katvā “katame soḷasa hārā”tiādivacanaṃ samatthitaṃ hoti.

“This is also a verse,” etc., is the opinion of some. For others say, “This verse was spoken by the Elder himself.” Indeed, sometimes the teaching is presented by way of referring to oneself, as when it is said, “Monks, the Tathāgata is endowed with the ten powers, skilled in the four kinds of intrepidity,” etc. (SN 2.21-22). And having done so, the statement, “What are the sixteen necklaces?” etc., is established.

Yathāvuttaatthamukhenevāti mūlapadasaṅkhātaatthuddhāreneva. Parato āgamissatīti niddesavārassa pariyosāne āgamissati “tīṇi ca nayā anūnā”tiādinā (netti. 4 dvādasapada).

As it has been said, namely, by extracting the meaning designated by the root words themselves. It will come later, that is, it will come at the end of the section of explanation with the words “three methods without omission,” and so on (Netti. 4, twelvefold method).

Vuccatīti kattari kammaniddesoti āha “vadatī”ti. Atha vā vuccatīti kammakattuniddesoyaṃ. Ayañhettha attho – hārā, nayā cāti ubhayaṃ pariggahitaṃ saṃvaṇṇakena sabbathā gahitañce, vuccati suttaṃ, sayameva suttaṃ saṃvaṇṇetīti[M 0.0023], etena hāranayesu vasībhāvena suttasaṃvaṇṇanāya sukarataṃ dasseti.

The passive `vuccati` (it is said) is an expression for the agent, hence he says `vadati` (he says). Or alternatively, `vuccati` is a medio-passive expression. The meaning here is this: if both the hāras and nayas are completely grasped in every way by the commentator, the sutta `vuccati` (is spoken), meaning the sutta itself explains; by this he shows the ease of commenting on the sutta due to mastery over the hāras and nayas.

Pakārantarenāti pubbe “sāsana”nti vuttamatthaṃ “desanā, desita”nti tato aññena pakārena. Niyametvāti tassa ekantato viññeyyataṃ avadhāretvā. Viññeyyatā visiṭṭhesu desanādesitesu viññeyyapade labbhamānā vijānanakiriyā.

“By another way” means: by another method than that previously stated as “the teaching,” namely as “instruction” and “what is instructed.” “Having determined” means: having definitively ascertained its comprehensibility. Comprehensibility is the act of knowing, which is found in the term “comprehensible” with regard to the excellent instruction and what is instructed.

Desanādesitāni ca yāvadeva vijānanatthānīti vijānanaṃ padhānanti tameva niddhārento “tatrāti tasmiṃ vijānane”ti āha.

Instruction and what is instructed are only for the purpose of understanding; understanding is primary. Determining just that, he said, “Therein, means in that understanding.”

Etthāhāti navaṅgasāsananavavidhasuttantāti etasmiṃ atthavacane āha codako. Tassāyaṃ adhippāyo – navahi aṅgehi vavatthitehi aññamaññasaṅkararahitehi bhavitabbaṃ, tathā ca sati asuttasabhāvāneva geyyaṅgādīnīti navavidhasuttantavacanaṃ virujjheyya. Atha suttasabhāvāni geyyaṅgādīni, evaṃ sati “sutta”nti visuṃ suttaṅgaṃ na siyā, evaṃ sante aṭṭhaṅgasāsanaṃ āpajjatīti. Tenāha “kathaṃ panā”tiādi. Geyyaṅgādīsu katipayānampi suttabhāve yathāvuttadosānativatti, pageva sabbesanti dasseti “yañcā”tiādinā. Saṅgahesūti aṭṭhakathāsu. Porāṇaṭṭhakathānañhi saṅkhepabhūtā idāni aṭṭhakathā “saṅgahā”ti vuttā. Suttaṃ nāma sagāthakaṃ vā siyā, niggāthakaṃ vāti aṅgadvayeneva tadubhayaṅgaṃ katanti visuṃ suttaṅgassa asambhavo tadubhayavinimuttassa suttassa abhāvato. Tena vuttaṃ “suttaṅgameva na siyā”ti. Athāpi kathañci. Siyāti vakkhamānaṃ sāmaññavidhiṃ sandhāyāha. Evampi ayaṃ dosoti dassento “maṅgalasuttādīna”ntiādimāha.

Here, the questioner says, “The ninefold teaching is the nine kinds of discourses,” regarding this statement of meaning. His point is this—the nine divisions should be distinct and free from mutual confusion. If that were the case, and the geyya and other divisions were not inherently sutta-like, then the term “nine kinds of discourses” would be contradicted. But if the geyya and other divisions are indeed sutta-like in nature, then the separate category of “sutta” as a distinct division would not exist. In that case, the teaching would only have eight divisions. Therefore, he says, “How then?” and so forth. Even if some of the geyya and other divisions were sutta-like, the previously mentioned fault would still not be avoided—how much more so if all were so. This is shown by the phrase, “And what is...” and so on. “In the Saṅgahā” means in the commentaries. For the present commentaries, being summaries of the ancient commentaries, are called “Saṅgahā.” A sutta may be with verses or without verses. Thus, by these two divisions, both aspects are covered, making a separate “sutta” division impossible, since there is no sutta apart from these two. Hence, it is said, “There would be no sutta division at all.” Yet, in some way, it might still be possible. This is said with reference to the general rule that will be spoken of. Even so, this is a fault, as shown by the mention of “the Maṅgala Sutta and others” and so forth.

Tabbhāvanimittanti [V 0.0022] geyyaṅgabhāvanimittaṃ. Veyyākaraṇassa tabbhāvanimittanti sambandho. Codako “gāthāvirahe”ti vacanaṃ aggaṇhanto “pucchāvissajjanaṃ byākaraṇa”nti vacanamattameva gahetvā “evaṃ sante”tiādinā codeti. Itaro pana okāsavidhito anokāso vidhi balavāti ñāyaṃ gāthāvirahitaṃyeva veyyākaraṇanti, idhādhippetanti ca dassento “nāpajjatī”tiādinā pariharati. Tathā hīti teneva kāraṇena, satipi saññantaranimittayoge anokāsasaññānaṃ balavabhāvenevāti attho.

“The characteristic of its being so” means the characteristic of its being the geyya division. The connection is with “the characteristic of the exposition’s being so.” The questioner, not grasping the statement “in the absence of verses,” takes only the mere statement “question and answer is the exposition” and then objects with “if that is so,” etc. But the other, showing that the maxim “a rule that admits no exception is stronger than one that admits exceptions” applies, and that what is intended here is that an exposition is indeed without verses, defends with “it is not faulty,” etc. “For this reason” means: for that very reason; even when there is a connection with another characteristic, it is due to the strength of the non-exceptional characteristics—that is the meaning.

Saṅgahavāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Explanation of the Section on Compilation is concluded.

2. Uddesavāravaṇṇanā

2. The Explanation of the Section on the Summary

1. Vibhāgenāti [M 0.0024] sarūpavibhāgena. Adiṭṭhaṃ jotīyati etāyāti adiṭṭhajotanā. Diṭṭhaṃ saṃsandīyati etāyāti diṭṭhasaṃsandanā, saṃsandanaṃ cettha sākacchāvasena vinicchayakaraṇaṃ. Vimati chijjati etāyāti vimaticchedanā. Anumatiyā pucchā anumatipucchā. “Taṃ kiṃ maññathā”ti hi kā tumhākaṃ anumatīti anumati pucchitā. Kathetukamyatāti kathetukamyatāya.

1. “By division” means by division of its intrinsic nature. “Illuminating the unseen” is so called because by this it illuminates what is unseen. “Connecting the seen” is so called because by this it connects what is seen; here, connecting means deciding through discussion. “Cutting off doubt” is so called because by this it cuts off doubt. “Asking for consent” is a question for consent. For when it is asked, “What do you think?” your consent is asked for. “Desire to speak” means with the desire to speak.

“Harīyanti etehī”tiādinā karaṇādhikaraṇakattubhāvakammasādhanānaṃ vasena hāra-saddassa atthaṃ vatvā sadisakappanāvasena dassetuṃ “hārā viyā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Puna ganthakaraṇādiatthena ganthādisaddānaṃ viya hārakaraṇādiatthena hārasaddasiddhiṃ dassetuṃ “hārayantī”tiādimāha. “Haraṇato, ramaṇato cā”ti iminā manoharā manoramā cete saṃvaṇṇanāvisesāti dasseti.

By the words, 'They are carried by these,' and so forth, having explained the meaning of the word 'hāra' in terms of the instrumental, locative, agentive, passive, and objective senses, the words 'like necklaces,' and so forth, are stated to demonstrate it by way of comparison. Again, to show the formation of the word 'hāra' through its meaning as the action of carrying, and so forth, just as with the words 'gantha,' and so forth, through their meaning as the action of composing, and so forth, he states, 'they carry,' and so forth. By 'By carrying, and by delighting,' this shows that these are special descriptions, namely, captivating and delightful.

Upapattisādhanayuttīti lakkhaṇahetu. Vuttanayenāti “nanu ca aññepi hārā yuttisahitā evā”tiādinā desanāhāre vuttanayānusārena.

Logic that establishes the reason is the characteristic cause. 'By the stated method' means according to the method explained in the Desanāhāra, as in, 'But are not other hāras also possessed of suitability?' and so forth.

Catunnaṃ byūho etthāti bhinnādhikaraṇānampi padānaṃ aññapadatthasamāso labbhati “urasilomo”tiādīnaṃ (dī. ni. ṭī. 3.54, 303) viyāti vuttaṃ.

'The arrangement of the four is here'; it is said that even for words with different bases, a compound with the meaning of another word is obtained, like 'hairy-chested,' and so forth (Dī. Ni. Ṭī. 3.54, 303).

Sesanti “vivacanameva vevacana”nti evamādi.

The rest is as in, 'A different expression is indeed a synonym,' and so forth.

Anuppavesīyantīti [V 0.0023] avagāhīyanti. Samādhīyantīti pariharīyanti. Vinā vikappenāti jāti sāmaññaṃ, bhedo sāmaññaṃ, sambandho sāmaññantiādinā padatthantarabhāvavikappanamantarena.

'They are made to enter' means they are penetrated. 'They are composed' means they are resolved. 'Without discrimination' means without the discrimination of the state of being another meaning of a term, such as 'birth is generality, distinction is generality, connection is generality,' and so on.

Padaṭṭhānādimukhenāti padaṭṭhānavevacanabhāvanāpahānamukhena. Kecīti padaṭṭhānaparikkhāraāvaṭṭaparivattanapaññattiotaraṇe sandhāya vadati.

'Through the means of foundation, and so forth,' means through the means of foundation, synonym, development, and abandonment. 'Some' refers to those who say this with reference to foundation, requisite, turning, revolving, designation, and descent.

2. Sambandhoti hetuphalabhāvayogo. Tathābhūtānañhi dhammānaṃ ekasantānasiddhatā ekattanayo. Vibhāgo satipi nesaṃ hetuphalabhāve vibhattasabhāvatā. Añño eva hi hetu, aññaṃ phalanti. Byāpāraviraho nirīhatā. Na hi hetuphalānaṃ evaṃ hoti “ahaṃ imaṃ nibbattemi, imināhaṃ nibbatto”ti. Anurūpaphalatā paccayuppannānaṃ paccayānukūlatā. Samūhādiṃ [M 0.0025] upādāya lokasaṅketasiddhā vohāramattatā sammutisabhāvo. Pathavīphassādīnaṃ kakkhaḷaphusanādilakkhaṇaṃ paramatthasabhāvo. Ayañhettha saṅkhepo – yasmiṃ bhinne, itarāpohe vā cittena katena tathā buddhi, idaṃ sammutisaccaṃ yathā ghaṭe, sasambhārajale ca, tabbipariyāyena paramatthasaccanti. Paramatthasaccappaṭivedhāyāti nibbānādhigamāya.

2. 'Connection' is the relationship of the state of cause and effect. For such phenomena, the establishment of a single continuity is the method of unity. 'Distinction' is their distinct nature, even though they are in a state of cause and effect. For the cause is indeed one thing, the result another. 'Absence of effort' is inactivity. For it does not occur to cause and effect, 'I produce this; by this I am produced.' 'Suitability of the result' is the conformity of what has arisen from a condition to its condition. 'Conventional nature' is merely a designation established by worldly convention, based on aggregates and so forth. 'Ultimate nature' is the characteristic of hardness, tactile sensation, and so forth, of the earth element, contact, and so forth. Herein, this is the summary: that wherein, when it is broken, or when another is excluded, or when it is mentally constructed, the cognition is thus, this is conventional truth, as with a pot, or with water together with its components; that which is the opposite of this is ultimate truth. 'For the realization of ultimate truth' means for the attainment of Nibbāna.

Antoti abbhantaro. Padhānāvayavenāti mūlabhāvena. “Nandī dukkhassa mūla”ntiādīsu (ma. ni. 1.13) taṇhā “nandī”ti vuttā. “Saṅgāme ca nandiṃ caratī”tiādīsu pamodoti āha “taṇhāya, pamodassa vā”ti.

'Inside' means interior. 'By the principal part' means by being the root. In such texts as, 'Delight is the root of suffering' (MN 1), craving is called 'delight.' In such texts as, 'and he finds delight in battle,' it means joy; therefore, he says, 'of craving or of joy.'

3. Jātibhedatoti kusalā, akusalāti imasmā visesā. Yujjantīti ettha hetuattho antonīto veditabboti āha “yojīyantī”ti. Kehi yojīyanti? Saṃvaṇṇanakehīti adhippāyo. Yujjantīti vā yuttā honti, tehi samānayogakkhamā taggahaṇeneva gahitā hontīti attho tadekaṭṭhabhāvato. Imasmiṃ atthe “navahi padehī”ti sahayoge karaṇavacanaṃ, purimasmiṃ karaṇe. “Ete kho”ti ca pāṭho. Tattha kho-saddassa padapūraṇatā, avadhāraṇatthatā vā veditabbā. Ete evāti ete taṇhādayo eva, na ito aññeti attho. Aṭṭhāraseva na tato uddhaṃ, adho vāti. Purimasmiṃ pakkhe mūlapadantarābhāvo, dutiyasmiṃ tesaṃ anūnādhikatā dīpitā hoti.

3. 'By distinction of kind' means from this distinction: wholesome and unwholesome. Here, in 'they are connected' (yujjanti), the meaning of cause should be understood as implied; therefore he says, 'they are joined' (yojīyanti). By whom are they joined? The intention is: by the commentators. Alternatively, 'they are connected' (yujjanti) means they are suitable; being capable of equal application with those, they are grasped by the very grasping of them, this being the meaning because they have one and the same meaning. In this sense, 'with nine terms' is an instrumental of association; in the former sense, it is an instrumental of means. There is also the reading 'ete kho.' There, the particle 'kho' should be understood as a mere expletive or as having the sense of emphasis. 'These very' means these, craving and so on, and nothing other than these. Just eighteen, not more or less than that. In the first alternative, there is no other root term; in the second, their completeness, without deficiency or excess, is shown.

Uddesavāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the chapter of the summary is concluded.

3. Niddesavāravaṇṇanā

3. Commentary on the Section of Exposition

4. Niddesavāre [V 0.0024] sāmaññatoti sādhāraṇato. Visesenāti asādhāraṇato. Padatthoti saddattho. Lakkhaṇanti sabhāvo. Kamoti anupubbī. Ettāvatāti ettakappamāṇabhāvo. Hetvādīti hetuphalabhūmiupanisāsabhāgavisabhāgalakkhaṇanayā. Visesato pana lakkhaṇanti sambandho.

4. In the Section of Exposition, 'generally' (sāmaññato) means commonly (sādhāraṇato). 'Specifically' (visesena) means uncommonly (asādhāraṇato). 'Meaning of the term' (padattho) is the meaning of the word (saddattho). 'Characteristic' (lakkhaṇa) is intrinsic nature (sabhāvo). 'Order' (kamo) is sequence (anupubbī). 'To this extent' (ettāvatā) is the state of being of such a measure. 'Cause, and so forth' (hetvādi) refers to cause, result, ground, proximate cause, similar part, dissimilar part, characteristic, and method. But specifically, 'characteristic' means connection.

Hārasaṅkhepavaṇṇanā

Commentary on the Summary of the Methods

1. Yaṃ, bhikkhaveti ettha yanti paccattavacanaṃ, tañca sukhaṃ, somanassanti dvayena samānādhikaraṇanti katvā “assādīyatīti assādo, sukhaṃ, somanassañcā”ti [M 0.0026] vuttaṃ. Sukhādivedanā viya manāpiyarūpādipi avītarāgassa assādetabbanti āha “evaṃ iṭṭhārammaṇampī”ti. “Assādeti etāyāti vā assādo, taṇhā”ti etena “ya”nti hetuatthe nipātoti dasseti. Tatrāyamattho – yena hetunā pañcupādānakkhandhe paṭicca assādanīyabhāvena uppajjati sukhaṃ somanassaṃ, ayaṃ taṇhāsaṅkhāto assādo assādanakiriyāya kāraṇanti. Iti katvā ayamattho diṭṭhābhinandanādibhāvato vipallāsesupi sambhavatīti āha “evaṃ vipallāsāpī”ti. Aniṭṭhampīti pi-saddena iṭṭhampīti yojetabbaṃ, anavasesā sāsavā dhammā idha ārammaṇaggahaṇena gahitāti āha “sabbesaṃ tebhūmakasaṅkhārāna”nti.

1. Herein, in 'Whatever, monks,' 'yaṃ' is a relative pronoun, and since it is in apposition with the two terms 'pleasure' and 'joy,' it is said: 'Gratification is what is relished, namely, pleasure and joy.' Just as feelings of pleasure and so forth, so too agreeable forms and so forth are to be relished by one who is not free from lust; thus he says, 'thus also desirable objects.' With 'Or, gratification is that by which one relishes, that is, craving,' he shows that 'yaṃ' is a particle in the sense of cause. The meaning here is this: that cause by which pleasure and joy arise in dependence on the five aggregates subject to clinging as something to be relished—this, called craving, is the gratification that is the cause for the act of relishing. Therefore, since this meaning is also possible in the case of perversions such as delighting in views, he says, 'thus also perversions.' In 'also the undesirable,' by the particle 'pi', it should be connected with 'also the desirable.' Since all phenomena with taints without remainder are included here by the taking of the object, he says, 'of all formations of the three planes.'

Dukkhādukkhamasukhavedanānanti ettha dukkhasabhāvā eva adukkhamasukhā vedanā gahitā aniṭṭhārammaṇassa adhippetattā, na sukhasabhāvā. Yaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ “yāyaṃ, bhante, adukkhamasukhā vedanā, santasmiṃ esā paṇīte sukhe vuttā bhagavatā”ti (ma. ni. 2.88; saṃ. ni. 4.267). “Sukhapariyāyasabbhāvato”ti iminā iṭṭhatāmattatopi lesena sattānaṃ ārammaṇassa assādanīyatā sambhavatīti dasseti.

Herein, regarding 'neither-painful-nor-pleasant feelings,' a neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling that has the nature of suffering is taken, because an undesirable object is intended, not one that has the nature of pleasure. It is with reference to this that it was said: 'Venerable sir, this neither-painful-nor-pleasant feeling has been declared by the Blessed One to be happiness in that it is peaceful and sublime' (MN 59; SN 36.11). By 'because of its existence as a synonym for pleasure,' he shows that the relishability of an object for beings is possible even from a slight degree of desirability.

Ādīnavo dosanissandanatāya doso, svāyaṃ pīḷanavuttiyā veditabboti āha “ādīnavo dukkhā vedanā, tissopi vā dukkhatā”ti. Evaṃ dosatthataṃ ādīnavassa dassetvā idāni kapaṇatthataṃ dassetuṃ “atha vā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Yatoti yasmā dosakapaṇasabhāvattāti vuttaṃ hoti.

'Danger' (ādīnava) is a fault (dosa) because it is an outflow of fault. This should be understood as having the function of oppression; thus he says: 'Danger is painful feeling, or its painfulness.' Having thus shown the meaning of danger as 'fault,' now to show its meaning as 'wretchedness,' the words 'or else...' are stated. 'Whence' (yato) means 'because it has the nature of being faulty and wretched.'

Nissaratīti [V 0.0025] vivitti, sabbasaṅkhāravivekoti attho. Sāmaññaniddesenāti nissaraṇasaddavacanīyatāsāmaññena. Purimānanti assādādīnavatānaṃ. Upāyo cātiādīsu ca-saddo padapūraṇamattanti katvā āha “pacchimānañcā”ti, phalādīnanti attho. Tadantogadhabhedānanti ariyamaggapariyāpannavisesānaṃ.

'It escapes' (nissarati) means seclusion (vivitti); the meaning is seclusion from all formations. 'By a general exposition' means by the generality of what is expressible by the word 'escape.' 'Of the former' means of gratification and danger. In 'and the means,' and so forth, since the particle 'ca' is a mere expletive, he says 'and of the latter,' meaning of the fruits, and so forth. 'Of the distinctions included within that' means of the special qualities comprised within the noble path.

Kāmabhavādīnanti ādi-saddena na rūpārūpabhavā eva gahitā, atha kho te ca saññībhavādayo ca ekavokārabhavādayo ca gahitā. Tenāha “tiṇṇaṃ tiṇṇaṃ bhavāna”nti.

In 'of sensual existence, and so forth,' by the word 'and so forth,' not only form and formless existence are taken, but also those, as well as existence with perception and the rest, and one-constituent existence and the rest. Therefore he says, 'of the three and three kinds of existence.'

Yāvadeva [M 0.0027] anupādāparinibbānatthā bhagavato desanāti āha “nanu ca…pe… nipphādīyatī”ti. “Vuttamevā”ti iminā punaruttidosaṃ codeti. Itaro “saccameta”nti anujānitvā “tañca kho”tiādinā pariharati. “Paramparāyā”ti etena ajjhattaṃ yonisomanasikāro viya na paratoghoso āsannakāraṇaṃ dhammādhigamassa dhammassa paccattaṃ vedanīyattāti dasseti. Tathā hi “akkhātāro tathāgatā, paṭipannā pamokkhanti, jhāyino mārabandhanā”ti (dha. pa. 276) vuttaṃ. Tadadhigamakāraṇaṃ ariyamaggādhigamakāraṇaṃ siyā. Kiṃ pana tanti āha “sampattibhavahetū”ti, tena carimattabhāvahetubhūtaṃ puññasampattiṃ vadati.

Since the Blessed One's teaching is solely for the sake of final Nibbāna without clinging, he says: 'Is it not the case... is accomplished?' With 'It has already been stated,' he raises the objection of the fault of repetition. The other, having acknowledged, 'This is true,' refutes it with 'but that...' With 'by way of tradition,' he shows that, unlike internal wise attention, the voice of another is not the proximate cause for the attainment of the Dhamma, because the Dhamma is to be experienced for oneself. For it has been said: 'The Tathāgatas are only proclaimers; those who practice are released; the meditative are freed from Māra’s bonds' (Dhp 276). The cause for its attainment would be the cause for the attainment of the noble path. But what is that? He says, 'because it is the cause for a prosperous existence.' By this he speaks of the accomplishment of merit that has become the cause for the final state of existence.

“Attānudiṭṭhiṃ ūhacca, evaṃ maccutaro siyā”ti idaṃ ariyamaggassa pubbabhāgapaṭipadāya phalabhāvasādhanaṃ. Yena hi vidhinā attānudiṭṭhisamugghāto, maccutaraṇañca siyā, so “eva”nti iminā pakāsitoti. Attānudiṭṭhisamugghātamaccutaraṇānaṃ phalabhāve vattabbameva natthi.

'Having uprooted the view of self, thus one may cross beyond death': this demonstrates that it is the fruit of the preliminary practice for the noble path. For the method by which there can be the uprooting of the view of self and the crossing beyond death is shown by this word 'thus.' There is nothing at all to be said about the uprooting of the view of self and the crossing beyond death being a fruit.

“Dhammo have”ti pana gāthāyaṃ lokiyassa puññaphalassa vuttattā āha “idaṃ phala”nti. Yaṃ nibbattetabbaṃ, taṃ phalaṃ. Yaṃ nibbattakaṃ, so upāyo. Ayamettha vinicchayo. Tenāha “etena nayenā”tiādi. Upadhisampattīti attabhāvasobhā.

But in the verse 'The Dhamma indeed...,' since the fruit of worldly merit is stated, he says, 'this is the fruit.' What is to be brought into being is the fruit; what brings it into being is the means. This is the distinction here. Therefore he says, 'by this method,' etc. 'Accomplishment in the substrates of existence' means the splendor of one's individual existence.

Visuddhīti ñāṇadassanavisuddhi adhippetāti āha – “etthāpi…pe… viññātu”nti. “Yasmā panā”tiādināpi tamevatthaṃ vacanantare pākaṭataraṃ karoti.

'Purification': the purification of knowledge and vision is intended; thus he says: 'here too... to be understood.' Also with the words 'But since...,' etc., he makes that same meaning clearer in another passage.

Sarūpato āgatāni “yato kho, bhikkhave, bhikkhu pañcannaṃ upādānakkhandhānaṃ samudayañca atthaṅgamañca assādañca ādīnavañca nissaraṇañca yathābhūtaṃ pajānātī”tiādīsu (saṃ. ni. 3.26-28). Ekadesena [V 0.0026] āgatāni “saṃyojaniyesu, bhikkhave, dhammesu assādānupassino viharato (saṃ. ni. 2.53), bahudukkhā bahupāyāsā, ādīnavo ettha bhiyyo (ma. ni. 1.117), saṅkhārānametaṃ nissaraṇaṃ, yadidaṃ nibbāna”ntiādīsu (paṭi. ma. 1.24; 3.41). Na sarūpena āgatāni yathā sāmaññaphalasuttādīsu. Atthavasenāti assādetabbādiatthavasena. Na papañcitoti na vitthārito.

Those that have come in their own form are in such passages as: 'When, monks, a bhikkhu understands as they really are the origin, the passing away, the gratification, the danger, and the escape in the case of the five aggregates subject to clinging,' etc. (SN 22:26). Those that have come in part are in such passages as: 'For one who dwells contemplating gratification in things that are fetters...' (SN 12:53); '...much suffering, much distress, the danger in them is greater' (MN 22); 'This is the escape from formations, that is, Nibbāna,' etc. (Paṭis I 85). Those that have not come in their own form are as in the Sāmaññaphala Sutta, etc. 'By way of meaning' means by way of the meaning of what is to be relished, etc. 'Not elaborated' means not expounded in detail.

2. Eseva [M 0.0028] nayoti atidesena viciyamānavacanaseso atidiṭṭho. Bhāvatthe tohi āha “vissajjitanti vissajjanā”ti. Sutte āgataṃ na atthasaṃvaṇṇanāvasena aṭṭhakathāyaṃ āgatanti adhippāyo. Pucchānurūpatā idha pubbāparanti catubyūhapubbāparato imaṃ visesetvā dasseti. Pucchānusandhīti pucchāya vissajjanena anusandhānaṃ. Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana heṭṭhimadesanāya pucchānimittapavattauparidesanāya sambandho “pucchānusandhī”ti vuttaṃ. Pubbāpekkhanti pucchitavissajjitapadāpekkhaṃ. “Suttassā”ti vā iminā pucchāvissajjanāanugītiyo ṭhapetvā seso vicayahārapadattho saṅgahitoti padassāpi saṅgaho veditabbo. Imasmiṃ pakkhe gāthāyaṃ ca-saddo padapūraṇamatte daṭṭhabbo.

2. By 'This is the method,' the remainder of the statement to be investigated is indicated by transference. For since it is in the sense of state, he says, '`vissajjitam` means answering (`vissajjanā`).' The meaning is that what has come in the sutta has not come in the commentary by way of an explanation of meaning. He shows that 'suitability to the question' here is a sequence, distinguishing it from the sequence of the fourfold array. 'Connection with the question' is the connection of the question with the answer. In the commentary, however, the connection of the upper teaching, which proceeds on the occasion of a question, with the lower teaching is called 'connection with the question.' 'Having regard for the preceding' means having regard for the words that were asked and answered. Or, by 'of the sutta,' having set aside the recitations of questions and answers, the remaining meaning of the terms of the method of investigation is included; thus the inclusion of a term should also be understood. In this case, the particle `ca` in the verse should be seen as a mere expletive.

“Cakkhu anicca”nti puṭṭhe “āma, cakkhu aniccamevā”ti ekantato vissajjanaṃ ekaṃsabyākaraṇaṃ. “Aññindriyaṃ bhāvetabbaṃ, sacchikātabbañcā”ti puṭṭhe “maggapariyāpannaṃ bhāvetabbaṃ, phalapariyāpannaṃ sacchikātabba”nti vibhajitvā vissajjanaṃ vibhajjabyākaraṇaṃ. “Aññindriyaṃ kusala”nti puṭṭhe “kiṃ anavajjaṭṭho kusalaṭṭho, udāhu sukhavipākaṭṭho”ti paṭipucchitvā vissajjanaṃ paṭipucchābyākaraṇaṃ. “Sassato attā, asassato vā”ti vutte “abyākatameta”ntiādinā avissajjanaṃ ṭhapanaṃ. “Kiṃ panete kusalāti vā dhammāti vā ekatthā, udāhu nānatthā”ti idaṃ pucchanaṃ sāvasesaṃ. Vissajjanassa pana sāvasesatā veneyyajjhāsayavasena desanāyaṃ veditabbā. Appāṭihīrakaṃ sauttaraṃ. Sappāṭihīrakaṃ niruttaraṃ. Sesaṃ vicayahāraniddese suviññeyyameva.

When asked, 'Is the eye impermanent?' to answer categorically, 'Yes, the eye is indeed impermanent,' is a categorical answer. When asked, 'Should the faculty of final knowledge be developed and realized?' to answer analytically, 'What is comprised in the path should be developed; what is comprised in the fruit should be realized,' is an analytical answer. When asked, 'Is the faculty of final knowledge wholesome?' to answer after asking a counter-question, 'In what sense is it wholesome? In the sense of being blameless, or in the sense of having a pleasant result?' is a counter-question answer. When one says, 'Is the self eternal or non-eternal?' not to answer but to state, 'This is undeclared,' is a setting aside. To ask, 'Are these things called "wholesome" and "dhamma" the same in meaning, or different?' is a question with a remainder. The remainder in an answer, however, should be understood in the teaching according to the disposition of the person to be trained. What is not extraordinary is answerable; what is extraordinary is unanswerable. The rest is easily understood in the exposition of the method of investigation.

Ettha ca assādo assādahetu yāva āṇattihetūti evaṃ hetūnampi assādādayo veditabbā. Tattha saṅkhepato sukhasukhapaccayalakkhaṇo assādo, so visesato saggasampattiyā dīpetabbo. Sā hi tassa ukkaṃso, sesā panettha bhavasampatti tadanvāyikā veditabbā. Tassa hetu dānamayaṃ, sīlamayañca puññakiriyavatthu. Dukkhadukkhapaccayalakkhaṇo ādīnavo. Vipariṇāmasaṅkhāradukkhatānaṃ tadavarodhato vaṭṭadukkhassāpi ettha saṅgaho. Visesato pana kāmānaṃ okāroti [V 0.0027] daṭṭhabbo, svāyaṃ saṃkilesavatthunā, ittarapaccupaṭṭhānatādīhi ca vibhāvetabbo, tassa hetu dasa akusalakammapathā. Nekkhammaṃ nissaraṇaṃ, tassa hetu yathārahaṃ tadanucchavikā pubbabhāgappaṭipadā. Phalaṃ desanāphalameva, tassa [M 0.0029] hetu desanā. Upāyo yathāvuttaupāyova, tassa hetu cattāri cakkāni. Āṇatti upadeso, tassa rāgaggiādīhi lokassa ādittatā, satthu mahākaruṇāyogo ca hetu.

Here, gratification, the cause of gratification, up to the cause of the injunction—in this way, the causes too should be understood as gratification and so on. Briefly, gratification has the characteristic of being a condition for happiness and pleasure; it should be specifically illustrated by heavenly attainment. For that is its excellence; other attainments of existence should be understood as following from it. Its cause is the basis for meritorious action consisting of giving and virtue. Danger has the characteristic of being a condition for suffering and pain. The suffering of change and the suffering of formations are included here, and by their being encompassed, the suffering of the round of existence is also included. Specifically, it should be seen as the degradation of sensual pleasures; this should be explained by its being a basis for defilement, by its transient appearance, and so on. Its cause is the ten unwholesome courses of action. Renunciation is the escape; its cause is the appropriate preliminary practice befitting it. The fruit is the fruit of the teaching; its cause is the teaching. The means is the means as stated; its cause is the four wheels. The injunction is the instruction; its causes are that the world is ablaze with the fire of lust, etc., and the Teacher’s great compassion.

Tathā catūsu ariyasaccesu samudayena assādo, dukkhena ādīnavo, magganirodhehi nissaraṇaṃ, maggo vā upāyo, tadupadeso āṇatti, anupādisesā nibbānadhātu phalaṃ. Iti anupubbakathāya saddhiṃ buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikāya dhammadesanāya niddhāraṇabhāvena vicayo veditabbo. Padassa padatthasambandho hetu. So hi tassa pavattinimittaṃ, pañhassa ñātukāmatā, kathekukāmatā ca. Adiṭṭhajotanādīnañhi catunnaṃ ñātukāmatā, itarassa itarā. Vissajjanassa pañho hetu. Evaṃ sesānampi yathārahaṃ vattabbaṃ.

Similarly, in the Four Noble Truths, gratification corresponds to the origin, danger to suffering, and escape to the path and cessation; the path is the means, its instruction is the injunction, and the Nibbāna-element with no residue remaining is the fruit. Thus, this investigation should be understood as a determination in accordance with the Buddhas' gradual instruction along with their personally discovered Dhamma teaching. The connection between a word and its meaning is the cause, for it is the sign of its occurrence. For a question, the cause is the desire to know and the desire to speak. For the four types of questions, such as that which illuminates the unseen, the cause is the desire to know; for the other, it is the other. The question is the cause for the answer. Thus the rest should be stated as is appropriate.

3. Byañjanatthānaṃ yuttāyuttaparikkhāti byañjanaggahaṇena padaṃ gahitaṃ, atthaggahaṇena pañhādīhi saddhiṃ assādādayo gahitā. Vicayahārapadatthā eva hi yuttāyuttādivisesasahitā yuttihārādīnaṃ padatthā. Tathā hi padaṭṭhānapadaṭṭhānikabhāvavisiṭṭhā teyeva padaṭṭhānahārassa padatthā. Lakkhaṇalakkhitabbatāvisiṭṭhā, niddhāritā ca lakkhaṇahārassa, nibbacanādivibhāvanāvisiṭṭhā catubyūhahārassa, sabhāgadhammavasena, visabhāgadhammavasena ca āvaṭṭanavisiṭṭhā āvaṭṭahārassa, bhūmivibhāgādivisiṭṭhā vibhattihārassa, paṭipakkhato parivattanavisiṭṭhā parivattanahārassa, pariyāyavevacanavisiṭṭhā vevacanahārassa, pabhavādipaññāpanavisiṭṭhā paññattihārassa, khandhādimukhehi otaraṇavisiṭṭhā otaraṇahārassa, padapadatthapañhārambhasodhanavisiṭṭhā sodhanahārassa, sāmaññavisesaniddhāraṇavisiṭṭhā adhiṭṭhānahārassa, paccayadhammehi parikkharaṇavisiṭṭhā parikkhārahārassa, pahātabbabhāvetabbatāniddhāraṇavisiṭṭhā samāropanahārassa padatthā. “Byañjanassa sabhāvaniruttitā, atthassa suttādīhi avilomanaṃ yuttabhāvo”ti iminā asabhāvaniruttitā, suttādīhi vilomanañca ayuttabhāvoti dīpeti, tena yuttāyuttīnaṃ hetuṃ dasseti.

3. “The examination of the suitability and unsuitability of phrasing and meaning”: by “phrasing,” the word is taken; by “meaning,” gratification and so on are taken along with questions and so on. For the subject matter of the mode of Investigation, when accompanied by distinctions such as suitable and unsuitable, is itself the subject matter for the mode of Reasoning and the others. Thus, that same subject matter, when distinguished by the state of being a proximate cause and what is based on it, becomes the subject matter for the mode of Proximate Cause. When distinguished by being a characteristic and what is to be characterized, and being determined, it is for the mode of Characteristics. When distinguished by elucidation through etymology and so on, it is for the mode of Fourfold Array. When distinguished by revolving in terms of similar and dissimilar phenomena, it is for the mode of Conveying Round. When distinguished by divisions of plane and so on, it is for the mode of Analysis. When distinguished by reversal from the opposite, it is for the mode of Reversal. When distinguished by synonyms and equivalent terms, it is for the mode of Synonyms. When distinguished by designation through origin and so on, it is for the mode of Designation. When distinguished by entry through aspects such as the aggregates, it is for the mode of Entry. When distinguished by purification through the clarification of words, meanings, and the start of questions, it is for the mode of Cleansing. When distinguished by the determination of the general and the specific, it is for the mode of Establishment. When distinguished by examination through conditioning phenomena, it is for the mode of Requisites. When distinguished by the determination of what is to be abandoned and what is to be developed, it is for the mode of Superimposition. By the statement, “Suitability is the natural expression of the phrasing and the non-conflict of the meaning with the suttas, etc.,” it is shown that unsuitability is unnatural expression and conflict with the suttas, etc. Thus it shows the cause for suitability and unsuitability.

4. Yonisomanasikārādīti ādisaddena saddhammassavanasappurisūpanissayādisādhāraṇaṃ, asādhāraṇañca deyyapaṭiggāhakādiṃ saṅgaṇhāti. Sambhavatoti yathārahaṃ tassa dhammassa anurūpaṃ. Yāva sabbadhammāti [M 0.0030] ettha sabbaṃ nāma padesasabbaṃ, na sabbasabbanti. Ayañhi sabbasaddo yathā paṭhamavikappe sutte āgatadhammavasena padesavisayo, evaṃ dutiyavikappe padaṭṭhānapadaṭṭhānikaniddhāraṇena [V 0.0028] taṃtaṃpakaraṇaparicchinnadhammaggahaṇato padesavisayo eva, na anavasesadhammavisayoti. Suttāgatadhammānaṃ yāni padaṭṭhānāni, tesañca yānīti evaṃ kāraṇaparamparāniddhāraṇalakkhaṇo padaṭṭhānahāro, parikkhārahāro pana suttāgatadhammānaṃ taṃtaṃpaccayuppannānaṃ paṭihetupaccayatāvisesavibhāvanalakkhaṇoti satipi kāraṇavicāraṇabhāve ayaṃ padaṭṭhānahāraparikkhārahārānaṃ viseso.

4. “By wise attention, etc.”: by the word “etc.” are included common factors such as listening to the good Dhamma and associating with true persons, and uncommon factors such as the gift and the recipient. “As is possible” means in a way that is suitable to that particular teaching. “As far as all phenomena”: here “all” means a partial all, not a complete all. For this word “all” has a partial scope. Just as in the first alternative it has a partial scope in terms of the teachings that have come in the sutta, so in the second alternative, by the determination of the proximate cause and what is based on it, it has a partial scope because it takes the teachings as defined by each particular treatise, not a scope including all phenomena without remainder. The mode of Proximate Cause has the characteristic of determining the chain of causes, thus: “what are the proximate causes of the teachings that have come in the suttas, and what are the proximate causes of those?” The mode of Requisites, however, has the characteristic of explaining the specific conditionality of the teachings that have come in the suttas, which have arisen from their respective conditions. Although both involve the investigation of causes, this is the difference between the mode of Proximate Cause and the mode of Requisites.

5. Yathā “samānādhikaraṇasamānapade”tiādīsu ekasaddassa attho samānasaddo, evaṃ ekarasaṭṭhena bhāvanā “ekuppādā”tiādīsu (kathā. 473) viya ekalakkhaṇāti ettha ekasaddo samānatthoti āha “samānalakkhaṇā”ti. Saṃvaṇṇanāvasenāti ettha kammatthe ana-saddo, saṃvaṇṇetabbatāvasenāti attho. Lakkhaṇāti upalakkhaṇā. “Nānattakāyanānattasaññino (dī. ni. 3.341, 357, 359; a. ni. 9.24), nānattasaññānaṃ amanasikārā”tiādīsu sahacāritā daṭṭhabbā. Saññāsahagatā hi dhammā tattha saññāggahaṇena gahitā. “Dadaṃ mittāni ganthatī”tiādīsu (saṃ. ni. 1.246; su. ni. 189) samānakiccatā. Piyavacanatthacariyā samānattatāpi hi tattha mittaganthanakiccena samānakiccā gayhanti saṅgahavatthubhāvato. “Phassapaccayā vedanā”tiādīsu (ma. ni. 3.126; saṃ. ni. 2.1, 39; mahāva. 1; vibha. 225; udā. 1; netti. 24) samānahetutā. Yathā hi phasso vedanāya, evaṃ saññādīnampi sahajātādinā paccayo hoti evāti tepi samānahetutāya vuttā eva honti. Tathā hi vuttaṃ “tajjāmanoviññāṇadhātusamphassajā cetanā”ti (dha. sa. 5), “phuṭṭho sañjānāti, phuṭṭho cetetī”tiādi (saṃ. ni. 4.93). Evaṃ “taṇhāpaccayā upādāna”nti (ma. ni. 3.126; saṃ. ni. 2.1, 39; mahāva. 1; vibha. 225; udā. 1; netti. 24) evamādipi udāharitabbaṃ. “Avijjāpaccayā saṅkhārā”tiādīsu (ma. ni. 3.126; saṃ. ni. 2.1, 39; mahāva. 1; vibha. 225; udā. 1; netti. 24) samānaphalatā daṭṭhabbā. Yathā hi saṅkhārā avijjāya phalaṃ, evaṃ taṇhupādādīnampīti tepi tattha gahitāva honti. Tenāha “purimakammabhavasmiṃ moho avijjā āyūhanā saṅkhārā nikanti taṇhā upagamanaṃ upādāna”nti. “Rūpaṃ assādeti abhinandati[M 0.0031], taṃ ārabbha rāgo uppajjatī”ti (paṭṭhā. 1.1.424) vutte taṃsampayuttā vedanādayo vuttā eva honti samānārammaṇabhāvato. Na hi tehi vinā tassa uppatti atthi. Evamādīhīti ettha ādisaddena atthappakaraṇaliṅgasaddantarasannidhānasāmatthiyādīnampi saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo. Atthādivasenapi hi sutte avuttānampi [V 0.0029] vuttānaṃ viya niddhāraṇaṃ sambhavatīti. Vuttappakārenāti “vadhakaṭṭhena ekalakkhaṇānī”tiādinā pāḷiyaṃ, “sahacāritā”tiādinā aṭṭhakathāyañca vuttena pakārena.

5. Just as in such phrases as “in the same case, in the same word,” the word eka has the meaning of the word samāna (same), so too, just as in “of single arising,” etc., there is the sense of a single taste, so here in “of single characteristic,” the word eka means “same”; thus it says “of the same characteristic.” “By way of explanation” (saṃvaṇṇanāvasena): here the suffix -ana is in the objective sense, meaning “by way of what is to be explained” (saṃvaṇṇetabbatāvasena). “Characteristic” (lakkhaṇa) is a distinguishing mark (upalakkhaṇa). In such passages as “beings with diverse bodies and diverse perceptions” and “through non-attention to diverse perceptions,” concomitance (sahacāritā) should be seen. For there, phenomena concomitant with perception are included by the term “perception.” In such passages as “By giving one makes friends,” there is sameness of function (samānakiccatā). For there, kind speech, beneficial conduct, and impartiality, having the same function of making friends, are included because they are bases of sympathy. In “with contact as condition, feeling arises,” etc., there is sameness of cause (samānahetutā). For just as contact is a condition for feeling, so it is a condition for perception and so on by way of co-nascence, etc.; thus they are said to have the same cause. For it is said: “volition born of contact with the mind-consciousness element,” and “having been contacted, one perceives; having been contacted, one wills,” etc. Similarly, “with craving as condition, clinging arises,” etc., should also be cited as an example. In “with ignorance as condition, formations arise,” etc., sameness of result (samānaphalatā) should be seen. For just as formations are the result of ignorance, so too are craving, clinging, etc., so they are included there. Therefore it is said: “In a previous kamma-existence, delusion is ignorance, accumulation is formations, delight is craving, approaching is clinging.” When it is said, “One savors and delights in form, and depending on that, lust arises,” the associated feeling, etc., are also stated because of having the same object (samānārammaṇabhāva). For without them, its arising does not occur. “And so on”: by this word “etc.” should be understood the inclusion of meaning, context, grammatical sign, proximity of other words, capacity, etc. For by way of meaning, etc., it is possible to determine even what is not stated in a sutta as if it were stated. “By the manner stated” means by the manner stated in the Pāli text, as in “they have a single characteristic in the sense of being destructive,” and in the commentary, as in “concomitance,” etc.

6. “Phusanaṭṭhena phasso”tiādinā niddhāretvā vacanaṃ nibbacanaṃ, taṃ pana padasseva, na vākyassāti āha “padanibbacana”nti. Adhippāyanidānānipettha byañjanamukheneva niddhāretabbāni. Nibbacanapubbāparasandhīsu vattabbameva natthīti āha “visesato byañjanadvāreneva atthapariyesanā”ti. Pavattinimittaṃ ajjhāsayādi.

6. An etymological explanation (nibbacana) is a statement that defines, such as, “Contact (phasso) is so called in the sense of touching (phusanaṭṭhena).” But this pertains only to a word, not to a sentence; hence it is called “word-explanation” (padanibbacana). Here, the intention and the source are to be determined only by way of the phrasing. Since there is nothing to be said about the connection between preceding and subsequent etymologies, it is said: “The search for meaning is especially through the door of the phrasing.” The sign of its occurrence is disposition, etc.

7. “Padaṭṭhāne”ti idaṃ sutte āgatadhammānaṃ kāraṇabhūtepi dhamme niddhāretvā sabhāgato, visabhāgato ca āvaṭṭanaṃ kātabbanti dassanatthaṃ vuttaṃ, na tantivasena. Tasmā padaṭṭhānaniddhāraṇāya vināpi āvaṭṭanaṃ yuttamevāti siddhaṃ hoti. Padassa vā saddapavattiṭṭhānaṃ padaṭṭhānaṃ padattho. Etasmiṃ pakkhe “ārambhatha nikkamathāti (saṃ. ni. 1.185; netti. 29; peṭako. 38; mi. pa. 5.1.4) vīriyassa padaṭṭhāna”nti (netti. 29) ettha yvāyamārambhadhātuādiko attho vutto, taṃ vīriyasaddassa pavattiṭṭhānaṃ vīriyasaddābhidheyyo atthoti evamattho veditabbo. Sesesupi eseva nayo. Sesakaṃ nāmagahitato itaraṃ, taṃ pana tassa paṭipakkhabhūtaṃ vā siyā, aññaṃ vāti āha “visabhāgatāya aggahaṇena vā”ti. Saṃvaṇṇanāya yojentoti yathāvuttavisabhāgadhammaniddhāraṇabhūtena atthakathanena pāḷiyaṃ yojento. Tenāha “desana”nti. “Paṭipakkhe”ti idaṃ nidassanamattaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ sabhāgadhammavasenapi āvaṭṭanassa icchitattā.

7. “Regarding ‘Foundation’”—this is said to show that even when discerning the causes of phenomena mentioned in the sutta, one should revolve them in terms of similarity and dissimilarity, not merely based on the text. Thus, it is established that revolving is indeed proper even without the determination of the foundation. Alternatively, the foundation of a word is the basis for the occurrence of the sound, the meaning of the word. In this respect, where “Exert yourselves, set forth!” (Saṃ 1:185; Netti 29; Peṭako 38; Mil 5.1.4) is called the “foundation of effort” (Netti 29), the meaning stated—of the root ārambha, etc.—should be understood as the basis for the occurrence of the word “effort,” the meaning denoted by the word “effort.” The same method applies to the rest. The “remainder” is what is other than what is taken by name, which may be its opposite or something else—hence, he says: “or by the non-apprehension of the dissimilar.” “Applying it to the commentary” means applying it to the Pāli text through an explanation of meaning that is the determination of dissimilar phenomena as stated above. Therefore, he says “teaching.” This word “opposite” should be regarded as a mere illustration, since revolving is also desired with reference to similar phenomena.

8. Nāmavasenāti sādhāraṇanāmavasena. Pāḷiyaṃ pana “micchattaniyatānaṃ sattānaṃ, aniyatānañca sattānaṃ dassanapahātabbā kilesā sādhāraṇā”tiāgatattā (netti. 34) “dassanapahātabbādināmavasenā”ti vuttaṃ. Vatthuvasenāti [M 0.0032] sattasantānavasena. So hi dhammānaṃ pavattiṭṭhānatāya idha “vatthū”ti adhippeto. Tenāha – “puthujjanassa, sotāpannassa ca kāmarāgabyāpādā sādhāraṇā”tiādi (netti. 34). Vuttavipariyāyenāti nāmato, vatthuto ca āveṇikatāya. Taṃtaṃmaggaphalaṭṭhānañhi taṃtaṃmaggaphalaṭṭhatā, bhabbānaṃ bhabbatā, abhabbānaṃ abhabbatā asādhāraṇā.

8. “By name” means by way of a common name. However, in the Pāli text, since it is stated, “Defilements to be abandoned by vision are common to beings fixed in wrongness and to beings not fixed” (Netti 34), it is said, “By way of the name ‘to be abandoned by vision,’ etc.” “By basis” means by way of the continuity of a being. For that is intended here as “basis” because it is the place of occurrence for phenomena. Hence, it is said: “Sensual desire and ill will are common to the worldling and the stream-enterer,” etc. (Netti 34). “By the opposite of what is stated” means by way of being unique in name and basis. For the state of being at a particular path and fruit, the capability of the capable, and the incapability of the incapable are unique.

9. “Bhāvite”ti idaṃ bhāvanākiriyāya upalakkhaṇaṃ, na ettha kālavacanicchāti āha “bhāvetabbeti attho”ti. Bhāvanā cettha āsevanāti, kusalasaddopi anavajjaṭṭhoti veditabbo[V 0.0030]. Paṭipakkhatoti vipakkhato. Visadisūdāharaṇena byatirekato yathādhippetadhammappatiṭṭhānā hesā.

9. “Bhāvite” (when developed) is an indicator of the activity of development; there is no intention of a temporal meaning here. Thus he says, “It means ‘should be developed.’” And here development is cultivation; the word “kusala” (wholesome) should also be understood as meaning “blameless.” “From the opposite” means from the opposing side. For this is an establishment of the intended principle through exclusion by means of a dissimilar example.

10. Padatthassāti padābhidheyyassa atthassa, sabhāvadhammassa vā.

10. “Of the word’s meaning” means of the meaning denoted by the word, or of the intrinsic nature of a phenomenon.

11.

11.

Nikkhepo desanā. Pabhavo samudayo.

“Laying down” is teaching. “Source” is origination.

12. “Avuttānampi saṅgaho”ti iminā avuttasamuccayattho ca-saddoti dasseti.

12. By “a collection also of what is unsaid,” he shows that the particle “ca” has the meaning of a collection of what is unsaid.

13. “Gāthāruḷhe”ti iminā pāḷiāgatova pañho veditabbo, na itaroti dasseti. Tenāha “buddhādīhi byākate”ti. Tassa atthassāti āraddhassa atthassa, tena ārambhasodhanassa visayamāha. Ettha ca atthadvāreneva padapucchāsodhanampi karīyatīti puna “tassa atthassā”ti vuttaṃ. Atha vā vissajjitamhīti vissajjane. Vissajjanasodhanena hi pañhāsodhanaṃ. Pañheti pucchāyaṃ. Gāthāyanti upalakkhaṇaṃ, tena gāthāyaṃ, suttageyyādīsu cāti vuttaṃ hoti. Yamārabbhāti yaṃ sīlādimārabbha gāthādīsu desitaṃ, tasmiṃ ārambheti attho. Pucchitāti pucchākārinī, “kā ettha padasuddhi, kā pañhāsuddhi, kā ārambhasuddhī”ti evaṃ pucchākārinī pucchaṃ katvā pavattitā suddhāsuddhaparikkhāti yojanā.

13. By “mounted on a verse,” he shows that the question to be understood is one that has come in the Pāli, not another. Therefore, he says, “explained by the Buddha and others.” “Of that meaning” refers to the meaning that has been undertaken; thereby he states the scope of the purification of the undertaking. And here, the purification of the question regarding the words is done through the meaning itself, which is why it is again stated, “of that meaning.” Alternatively, “in answering” refers to the answer. For the purification of the question is through the purification of the answer. “Question” refers to the act of questioning. “In a verse” is an indication, meaning it applies to verses, suttas, mixed prose-and-verse discourses, and so on. “Regarding which” means regarding that virtue, etc., about which it is taught in verses and so on; this is the meaning of “in that undertaking.” “Questioning” means the one who questions, asking: “What is the purity of the word here? What is the purity of the question? What is the purity of the undertaking?” The examination of purity and impurity proceeds, having made such a question.

14. Na vikappayitabbāti yathā loke “jāti sāmaññaṃ, bhedo sāmaññaṃ, sambandho sāmañña”ntiādinā sāmaññaṃ jātiādiṃ, tabbidhurañca visesaṃ [M 0.0033] vikappenti parikappenti, evaṃ na vikappayitabbāti attho. Yadā yo kālaviseso “sve”ti laddhavohāro, tadā so taṃdivasātikkame “ajjā”ti, puna taṃdivasātikkame “hiyyo”ti voharīyatīti anavaṭṭhitasabhāvā ete kālavisesā. Disāyapi “ekaṃ avadhiṃ apekkhitvā puratthimā disā, tato aññaṃ apekkhitvā pacchimā nāma hotī”tiādinā anavaṭṭhitasabhāvatā veditabbā. Jātiādiapekkhāyāti jātiādidukkhavisesāpekkhāya. Saccāpekkhāyāti saccasāmaññāpekkhāya. “Taṇhā”ti vuccamānaṃ kāmataṇhādiapekkhāya sāmaññampi samānaṃ saccāpekkhāya viseso hotīti evamādiṃ sandhāyāha “esa nayo samudayādīsupī”ti.

14. “Should not be conceptually constructed” means that, just as in the world they conceptually construct generalities like “birth is a generality,” “difference is a generality,” “connection is a generality,” etc., assigning generality to birth and so on, and also conceptually construct specifics devoid of those generalities, so too they should not be conceptually constructed here. When a specific time that has acquired the designation “tomorrow” passes, it is then called “today,” and when that day passes, it is called “yesterday”—these temporal specifics are unstable in nature. Similarly, the unstable nature of directions should be understood from such statements as: “With reference to one boundary it is the eastern direction, but with reference to another it is called the western direction.” “With reference to birth, etc.” means with reference to the specific sufferings of birth, etc. “With reference to truth” means with reference to the generality of truth. He said, “This is the method also for origin, etc.,” having in mind such things as that what is called “craving,” though it is a generality with reference to sensual craving, etc., becomes a specific with reference to truth.

16. Etthāti etasmiṃ buddhavacane. Tenāha “sikkhattayasaṅkhātassā”tiādi. Yathārutaṃ yathākathitaṃ saddato adhigataṃ niddhāritaṃ, na atthappakaraṇaliṅgasaddantarasannidhānādippamāṇantarādhigataṃ[V 0.0031]. “Atthato dassitā”ti idaṃ yasmiṃ sutte bhāvanāva kathitā, na pahānaṃ, taṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ.

16. “Here” means in this word of the Buddha. Therefore, he says, “of what is designated as the threefold training,” etc. “As heard” means as spoken, understood and determined from the word, not understood from another standard such as meaning, context, implication, the proximity of another word, or other kinds of evidence. “Shown by way of meaning” is said with reference to a sutta in which development alone is spoken of, not abandonment.

Nayasaṅkhepavaṇṇanā

Explanation of the Summary of Methods

17. Taṇhāvijjāhi karaṇabhūtāhi. Saṃkileso pakkho etassāti saṃkilesapakkho, saṃkilesapakkhiko suttattho, tassa nayanalakkhaṇoti yojanā. Vodānapakkhassa suttatthassāti sambandho. Vuṭṭhānagāminiyā, balavavipassanāya ca dukkhādīsu pariññeyyatādīni maggānuguṇo gahaṇākāro anugāhaṇanayo. Yadi evaṃ kathaṃ nayoti āha “tassa panā”tiādi. Tattha “nayavohāro”ti iminā nayādhiṭṭhānaṃ nayoti vuttanti dasseti.

17. By means of craving and ignorance as instruments. That which has defilement as its side is the side of defilement; the meaning of the sutta belonging to the side of defilement—its characteristic is the guiding principle, thus is the construction. This is connected to the meaning of the sutta belonging to the side of purification. By powerful insight that leads to emergence, the mode of grasping in regard to suffering, etc., as things to be fully understood, etc., which is in conformity with the path, is the method of comprehension. If so, how is it a method? He says: “But of that,” etc. Therein, by the phrase “the designation of a method,” it is shown that the basis of a method is called a method.

18. Bādhakādibhāvatoti bādhakapabhavasantiniyyānabhāvato. Aññathābhāvābhāvenāti abādhakaappabhavaasanti aniyyānabhāvābhāvena. Saccasabhāvattāti amusāsabhāvattā. Avisaṃvādanatoti ariyasabhāvādibhāvassa na visaṃvādanato ekantikattāti attho.

18. “Because of the state of being obstructive, etc.” means because of the state of being obstructive, a source, pacifying, and leading out. “By the absence of a state of being otherwise” means by the absence of a state of being non-obstructive, not a source, non-pacifying, and not leading out. “Because of its nature as truth” means because of its nature of not being false. “Because of not being deceptive” means because it does not deceive in regard to the nature of the noble ones, etc., and is thus conclusive; this is the meaning.

19. Saṃkiliṭṭhadhammāti saṃkilesasamannāgatā dhammā saddhammanayakovidāti saccapaṭiccasamuppādādidhammanayakusalā, ekattādinayakusalā vā.

19. “Defiled states” means states accompanied by defilements. “Experts in the method of the good Dhamma” means skilled in the method of the Dhamma concerning the truths, dependent origination, etc., or skilled in the methods of unity, etc.

20. Atthavissajjanesūti [M 0.0034] “ime dhammā kusalā”tiādinā (dha. sa. 1) sutte katapañhavissajjanesu ceva aṭṭhakathāya kataatthasaṃvaṇṇanāsu ca. “Vodāniyā”ti iminā anavajjadhammā idha kusalāti adhippetā, na sukhavipākāti dasseti. Tassa tassa atthanayassa yojanatthaṃ manasā volokayateti yojanā.

20. “In the answers on the meaning” refers to the answers to questions made in the suttas, such as, “These states are wholesome,” etc. (Dhs 1), and also to the explanations of meaning made in the commentary. By the word “purifying,” it is shown that here blameless states are intended as “wholesome,” not states that are the result of pleasure. The construction is that one should mentally survey it in order to apply each and every method of meaning.

21. Yadi karaṇabhūtaṃ, kathaṃ tassa atthantarābhāvoti āha “yena hī”tiādi. Disābhūtadhammānaṃ volokayanasamānayanabhāvato vohārabhūto, kammabhūto ca nayo, na nandiyāvaṭṭādayo viya atthabhūtoti “vohāranayo, kammanayo”ti ca vuccati.

21. If it is instrumental, how can it be without a different meaning? He says: “For by which,” etc. Because states that are like directions have the nature of surveying and bringing together, the method is one of designation and one of action. It is not a substantial entity like the nandiyāvaṭṭa, etc., and so it is called “the method of designation” and “the method of action.”

Dvādasapadavaṇṇanā

Explanation of the Twelve Terms

23. Apariyosite padeti uccāraṇavelāyaṃ pade asamatte, vippakateti attho. Pariyosite hi “pada”ntve samaññā siyā, na “akkhara”nti adhippāyo. Padassa vevacanatāya [V 0.0032] atthavasena pariyāyaṃ kharantaṃ sañcarantaṃ viya hoti, na evaṃ vaṇṇo avevacanattāti āha pariyāyavasena akkharaṇato”ti. Na hi vaṇṇassa pariyāyo vijjatī”ti idaṃ akārādivaṇṇavisesaṃ sandhāya vadati, na vaṇṇasāmaññaṃ. Tassa hi vaṇṇo akkharanti pariyāyo vutto evāti.

23. “When a word is unfinished” means when the word is incomplete at the time of utterance; the meaning is “scattered.” For when it is finished, the designation would be “word,” not “syllable”; this is the intention. Because a word has synonyms, by way of meaning it is as if it moves through its alternatives; but it is not so for a letter, because it has no synonyms. He says: “because of not being expressed by way of synonyms.” The statement, “For a letter has no synonym,” is said with reference to specific letters such as “a,” not to “letter” in general. For in regard to that, “vaṇṇa” has indeed been stated as a synonym for “akkhara.”

Akkharasaddassa atthaṃ vatvā tappasaṅgena vaṇṇasaddassapi vattuṃ “kenaṭṭhena vaṇṇo”tiādimāha. Tattha nanu padena, vākyena vā attho saṃvaṇṇīyati, na akkharenāti codanaṃ manasi katvā āha “vaṇṇo eva hī”tiādi. Padādibhāvenāti padavākyabhāvena. Yathāsambandhanti yathāsaṅketaṃ. Ayaṃ-saddo imassatthassa vācako, ayaṃ attho imassa saddassa vacanīyoti yathāgahitasaṅketānurūpaṃ saddatthānaṃ vācakavacanīyabhāvo. Atha vā yvāyaṃ saddatthānaṃ aññamaññaṃ avinābhāvo, so sambandho. Tadanurūpaṃ ekakkharaṃ nāmapadaṃ “mā evaṃ maññasī”tiādīsu mā-kārādi. Kecīti abhayagirivāsino.
