Te hi abhidhammadesanaṃ “manasādesanā”ti vadanti, yato rāhulācariyo “visuddhakaruṇānaṃ manasādesanā vācāya akkharaṇato akkharasaññitā”ti āha.

Having stated the meaning of the word “akkhara,” in that connection, in order to state the meaning of the word “vaṇṇa” too, he begins, “By what meaning is it a letter?” Here, having kept in mind the objection, “Surely meaning is described by a word or a sentence, not by a syllable,” he says, “For a letter indeed…,” etc. “By the state of being a word, etc.” means by the state of being a word and a sentence. “According to the connection” means according to convention. The relationship of designator and designated between a word and its meaning is in accordance with the grasped convention that “this word is the designator of this meaning, and this meaning is the designated of this word.” Or else, that mutual inseparability of word and meaning, that is the connection. In accordance with that, a single-syllable nominal word is, for example, the syllable “mā” in such phrases as “Do not think thus.” “Some” means the residents of Abhayagiri. For they say that the Abhidhamma teaching is a “mental teaching,” since the teacher Rāhula said: “For those of pure compassion, the mental teaching, by its expression in speech, is designated ‘syllable.’”

Satvappadhānanti [M 0.0035] drabyappadhānaṃ. Nāmapade hi drabyamāvibhūtarūpaṃ, kiriyā anāvibhūtarūpā yathā “phasso”ti (ma. ni. 3.126; saṃ. ni. 2.1, 39; mahāva. 1; vibha. 225; udā. 1; netti. 24). Ākhyātapade pana kiriyā āvibhūtarūpā, drabyamanāvibhūtarūpaṃ yathā “phusatī”ti. Tena nesaṃ satvakiriyāppadhānatā vuttā. Kiriyāvisesaggahaṇanimittanti kiriyāvisesāvabodhahetu kiriyāvisesadīpanato, yathā “cirappavāsi”nti (dha. pa. 219) ettha pa-saddo vasanakiriyāya viyogavisiṭṭhataṃ dīpeti. “Evaṃ manasi karotha, mā evaṃ manasākatthā”tiādīsu kiriyāvisesassa jotako evaṃ-saddo. “Evaṃsīlā (dī. ni. 3.142) evaṃdhammā”tiādīsu (dī. ni. 2.13; ma. ni. 3. 198; saṃ. ni. 5.378) satvavisesassa. Evaṃ sesanipātapadānampīti adhippāyo. Tenāha “kiriyāya…pe… nipātapada”nti.

“Predominant in being” means predominant in substance. For in a nominal word, substance has a manifest form, while action has an unmanifest form, as in “phasso” (contact). But in a verbal word, action has a manifest form, while substance has an unmanifest form, as in “phusati” (touches). Therefore, their predominance in substance and action is stated. “A sign for grasping a specific action” means a cause for understanding a specific action, because it illuminates a specific action. For example, here in “cirappavāsi” (long-absent dweller), the prefix “pa-” illuminates the distinctiveness of the action of dwelling, namely, separation. In such phrases as “Think thus, do not think thus,” the word “evaṃ” is an illuminator of a specific action. In such phrases as “of such virtue, of such nature,” it is an illuminator of a specific substance. The intention is that it is the same for the remaining particle-words. Therefore he says: “of action... (etc.)... a particle-word.”

Saṅkhepato vuttaṃ, kiṃ pana tanti āha “padābhihita”nti. Atha vā saṅkhepato vuttaṃ, yo akkharehi saṅkāsitoti vuccati. Padābhihitaṃ padehi kathitaṃ, yo padehi pakāsitoti vuccati. Tadubhayaṃ, yadi padasamudāyo vākyaṃ, tassa ko paricchedo. Yāvatā adhippetatthapariyosānaṃ, tāvatā ekavākyantipi vadanti, bahūpettha pakāre vaṇṇenti. Kiṃ tehi, sākhyātaṃ [V 0.0033] sābyayaṃ sakārakaṃ savisesanaṃ “vākya”nti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Nanu ca padenapi attho byañjīyatīti codanaṃ manasi katvā āha “padamattasavanepi hī”tiādi. Ākāresu vākyavibhāgesu abhihitaṃ kathitaṃ nibbacanaṃ ākārābhihitaṃ nibbacanaṃ. “Abhihitanti ca pāḷiāgata”nti vadanti.

It was stated in brief. But what is that? He says: “expressed by words.” Or else, it was stated in brief what is called “that which is illuminated by syllables.” “Expressed by words” means spoken by words; it is called “that which is made clear by words.” Regarding both of these, if a collection of words is a sentence, what is its definition? They say that one sentence extends as far as the intended meaning reaches its conclusion, and they describe many types here. What of them? A “sentence” should be understood as that which has a verb, an indeclinable, a case-role, and a qualifier. Having kept in mind the objection, “But is not meaning revealed by a single word too?” he says: “For even when just a single word is heard…,” etc. The derivation expressed or stated in the modes, that is, the divisions of a sentence, is a derivation expressed by mode. And they say: “The word ‘abhihita’ is found in the Pāli canon.”

“Nibbānaṃ maggati, nibbānatthikehi vā maggīyati, kilese vā mārento gacchatīti maggo”tiādinā (dha. sa. aṭṭha. 16) nibbacanānaṃ vitthāro. Taṃniddesakathanattā niddesoti imamatthamāha “nibbacanavitthāro niravasesadesanattā niddeso”ti. Padehīti vākyāvayavabhūtehi, vākyato vibhajjamānehi vā ākhyātādipadehi. Tenāha “vākyassa vibhāgo”ti, tathā cāha “apariyosite”tiādi. Apare pana “pakatipaccayalopādesādivasena akkharavibhāgo ākāro, niruttinayena padavibhāgo nibbacanaṃ, vākyavibhāgo niddeso. Vaṇṇapadavākyāni hi avibhattāni, vibhattāni ca cha byañjanapadānī”ti vadanti. Chaṭṭhaṃ vacananti chaṭṭhaṃ padaṃ. Kātabbanti “akkharaṃ [M 0.0036] padaṃ byañjanaṃ ākāro tatheva nirutti niddeso chaṭṭhavacana”nti gāthāyaṃ evaṃ kattabbaṃ, saṃvaṇṇanāvasena vā ākārapadaṃ catutthaṃ kātabbanti attho. Sabbo saddavohāro vibhattehi, avibhattehi ca akkharapadavākyeheva, tadaññappakāro natthīti āha “yānimānī”tiādi.

The detailed explanation of the word-derivation is by way of such passages as: “It seeks out Nibbāna, or it is sought out by those who seek Nibbāna, or it proceeds while killing the defilements, thus it is the path.” Because it is a statement giving that explanation, it is an exposition. He states this meaning thus: “The detailed derivation is an exposition because it is a teaching without remainder.” “By words” means by words such as verbs, etc., which are the components of a sentence or which are being divided from a sentence. Therefore he says, “the division of the sentence,” and similarly he says, “when it is unfinished,” etc. But others say: “The division of syllables by way of root, suffix, elision, substitution, etc., is mode; the division of words by way of the etymological method is derivation; the division of sentences is exposition. For letters, words, and sentences, both undivided and divided, are the six terms of expression.” “The sixth utterance” means the sixth term. It should be done thus: in the verse, “syllable, word, expression, mode, likewise etymology, exposition, the sixth utterance,” it should be constructed in this way; or, the meaning is that by way of explanation, the word “mode” should be made the fourth. All verbal discourse consists only of divided and undivided syllables, words, and sentences; there is no other kind. Therefore he says, “Whatever these are…,” etc.

24. Kāsanāsaddo kammatthoti dassetuṃ “kāsīyatī”tiādi vuttaṃ. Padehi tāva atthassa saṅkāsanā, pakāsanā ca hotu, padāvadhikāpi saṃvaṇṇanā icchitāti akkharehi pana kathanti āha “akkharehi suyyamānehī”tiādi. Padatthasampaṭipattīti padābhidheyyaatthāvabodho. “Akkharehi saṅkāsetī”tiādinā akkharakaraṇaṃ saṅkāsanabhūtaṃ ugghaṭanakiriyaṃ vadantena yathāvutto attho sādhitoti dassetuṃ “tathā hī”tiādi vuttaṃ.

24. To show that the word “kāsanā” has a passive meaning, “kāsīyati,” etc., is said. First, let there be illumination and clarification of the meaning by words; since an explanation limited to words is also desired, he asks how it is with syllables and says: “when syllables are heard,” etc. “Attainment of the word’s meaning” means understanding of the meaning designated by the word. To show that the aforesaid meaning is established by the one who states, with such phrases as “he illuminates by syllables,” that the making of syllables is an act of opening up that serves as illumination, the phrase “For so…,” etc., is said.

Vibhajanuttānīkammapaññattīti ekattaniddeso samāhāroti ayaṃ dvandasamāso. Ubhayenāti “vivaraṇā, vibhajanā”ti iminā dvayena. Etehīti ettha eva-kāro luttaniddiṭṭhoti āha “etehi evā”ti. “Saṅkāsanā…pe… abhāvato”ti iminā yathādhippetaanūnāvadhāraṇaphalaṃ dasseti. Ugghaṭanādīti ādisaddena vipañcananayāni saṅgaṇhāti.

“The designation of analysis and clarification” is a collective exposition in the singular; this is a dvanda compound. “By both” means by this pair: “opening up” and “analysis.” “By these”: here the particle “eva” is indicated as elided, so he says, “by these alone.” By the phrase “Illumination... (etc.)... because of the absence,” he shows the result of the determination, in accordance with what was intended, that there is no deficiency. “Opening up, etc.”: by the word “etc.” he includes the methods of detailed exposition.

25. Sammā [V 0.0034] yuttoti sammā aviparītaṃ, anavasesato ca yutto sahito. Tathā hi vuttaṃ “anūnā”ti. Sabbo hi pāḷiattho atthapadaatthanayehi anavasesato saṅgahito. Tenāha “sabbassa hī”tiādi.

25. “Rightly connected” means rightly, without distortion, and connected, included, without remainder. For so it was said: “without deficiency.” For the entire meaning of the Pāli is included without remainder by means of the meaning, the words, and the methods of meaning. Therefore he says: “For of all…,” etc.

26. Kasmā panettha mūlapadapadaṭṭhānāni asaṅgahitānīti? Padatthantarābhāvato. Mūlapadāni hi nayānaṃ samuṭṭhānamattattā padaṭṭhānānīti dassitoyaṃ nayo. Tena vuttaṃ “ito vinimutto koci nettipadattho natthī”ti.

26. Why are the root-words and the word-bases not included here? Because there is no other word-meaning. For the root-words, being merely the origin of the methods, are the word-bases; this method has been shown. Therefore it is said: “Apart from this, there is no meaning of any Netti-word.”

Nettiyā kāraṇabhūtāya. Hārā saṃvaṇṇetabbāti suttassa atthasaṃvaṇṇanāvasena hārā vitthāretabbā. Svāyanti so ayaṃ saṃvaṇṇanākkamo. Yena anukkamena nettiyaṃ desitā, teneva sutte atthasaṃvaṇṇanāvasena yojetabbāti. Evaṃ siddheti desanākkameneva siddhe. Ayaṃ ārambhoti “soḷasa hārā paṭhama”nti evaṃ pavatto ārambho. Imamatthanti imaṃ vuccamānaniyamasaṅkhātaṃ atthaṃ.

Because the Netti serves as the reason. “The expositions should be explained” means the expositions are to be elaborated by way of explaining the meaning of the sutta. “This is the sequence” means this is the sequence of explanation. By whatever sequence they are taught in the Netti, by that same sequence they should be applied in the sutta by way of explaining the meaning. “Thus it is established” means it is established by the very sequence of the teaching. “This is the beginning” means the beginning that proceeds thus: “Sixteen expositions at first.” “This meaning” means this meaning designated as the stated rule.

Yadi [M 0.0037] desitakkameneva hāranayā sutte yojetabbā siyuṃ, kiṃ so kamo kāraṇanirapekkho, udāhu kāraṇasāpekkhoti? Kiñcettha – yadi tāva kāraṇanirapekkho hāranayānaṃ anukkamo, aneke atthā vuccamānā avassaṃ ekena kamena vuccantīti. Evaṃ sante yena kenaci kamena sutte yojetabbā siyuṃ, tathā sati niyamo niratthako siyā. Atha kāraṇasāpekkho, kiṃ taṃ kāraṇanti? Itaro kāraṇagavesanaṃ akatvā attho evettha gavesitabboti adhippāyena “nāyamanuyogo na katthaci anukkame nivisatī”ti vatvā “na pana mayaṃ devānaṃpiyassa manorathavighātāya cetemā”ti kamakāraṇaṃ vicārento “apicā”tiādinā desanāhārassa tāva ādito desanāya kāraṇaṃ patiṭṭhapeti. Tattha dhammadesanāya nissayo assādādīnavanissaraṇāni, sarīraṃ āṇatti. Pakatiyā sabhāvena. Niddhāraṇena vināpi patiṭṭhābhāvato nissayabhāvato.

If the guidelines of the expositions are to be applied to the suttas only by the sequence in which they are taught, is that sequence independent of a reason, or is it dependent on a reason? And here: if the sequence of the guidelines of the expositions were independent of a reason, then many meanings being stated would necessarily be stated in a single sequence. That being so, they could be applied to the sutta in any sequence whatsoever, and in that case the rule would be pointless. But if it is dependent on a reason, what is that reason? The other, without searching for a reason, with the intention that 'the meaning itself should be sought here,' having said, 'This inquiry does not settle on any sequence anywhere,' and, 'But we do not intend to obstruct the wish of the dear-to-the-gods,' investigates the reason for the sequence. Beginning with 'Moreover,' he first establishes the reason for teaching the Exposition of Teaching at the beginning. Therein, the support for the teaching of the Dhamma is gratification, danger, and escape; the body is the injunction. By nature, by its intrinsic nature. Because it is a foundation even without specification, and because it is a support.

“Tathā hi vakkhatī”tiādinā yathāvuttaṃ atthaṃ pākaṭataraṃ karoti. Esa nayo itaresupi.

By the passage beginning, 'For so he will say,' he makes the aforesaid meaning clearer. This same method applies to the others as well.

Vicayānantaranti [V 0.0035] vicayahārānantaraṃ. Sesesupi eseva nayo. Tathā hīti lakkhaṇahāravibhaṅge yuttāyuttānaṃ kāraṇaparamparāya pariggahitasabhāvānaṃ avuttānampi ekalakkhaṇatāya gahaṇaṃ vuttaṃ.

'Immediately after the investigation' means immediately after the Exposition of Investigation. This same method applies to the rest as well. 'For so it is' means that in the analysis of the Exposition of Characteristics, it is stated that even unstated things—whose nature is grasped through a series of causes of the suitable and unsuitable—are included because they have a single characteristic.

Atthato niddhāritānanti atthuddhārapubbāparānusandhiādiatthato suttantarato uddhaṭānaṃ saṃvaṇṇiyamānasutte ānītānaṃ pāḷidhammānaṃ. Saddato, pamāṇantarato ca laddhānaṃ idha vicāretabbattā āha “niravasesato”ti. Atthassāti abhidheyyatthassa. Dhammassāti sabhāvadhammassa. Tattha tattha taṃ abhiniropetīti tasmiṃ tasmiṃ atthe, dhamme ca taṃ nāmaṃ abhiniropeti, “ayamevaṃnāmo”ti voharati. “Atthassa, dhammassā”ti padadvayena sāmaññato attho, dhammo ca anavasesetvā gahitoti āha “anavasesapariyādāna”nti, yato vuttaṃ “tattha tatthā”ti. Tathāti yathā anavasesatthāvabodhadīpakaṃ anavasesapariyādānaṃ kataṃ catubyūhapāḷiyaṃ, evaṃ punappunaṃ gabbhamupetīti ettha asaddavatī atthā pavattivasena labbhamānā sammāpaṭipatti uddhaṭāti upasaṃhārattho tathā-saddo.

'Ascertained by meaning' means of the Dhamma texts in the Pāli that have been extracted from other suttas based on meaning—such as the extraction of meaning, the connection between prior and posterior, etc.—and brought into the sutta being commented upon. Because those obtained by word and by other authorities are to be investigated here, he says 'without remainder.' 'Of the meaning' means of the meaning to be conveyed. 'Of the Dhamma' means of its intrinsic nature. 'There, in each case, it applies that' means in each respective meaning and Dhamma it applies that name, using the expression, 'This has such-and-such a name.' By the pair of words 'of the meaning, of the Dhamma,' the meaning and the Dhamma in general are grasped without remainder; therefore he says 'complete inclusion,' since it is said 'there, in each case.' 'Thus' means: just as the complete inclusion that illuminates the understanding of the entire meaning is made in the Pāli of the Fourfold Array, so here, in the phrase 'he enters the womb again and again,' the right practice—which is obtained by way of its occurrence as a meaning not expressed in words—is extracted. Thus the word 'thus' has the sense of a conclusion.

Tenevāti [M 0.0038] suttantarasaṃsandanassa sabhāgavisabhāgadhammantarāvaṭṭanūpāyabhāvato eva. Yatoti sabhāgavisabhāgadhammāvaṭṭanassa sādhāraṇādidhammavibhajanūpāyattā. Paṭivibhattasabhāveti paṭibhāgabhāvena vibhattasabhāve.

'For that very reason' means because the comparison with other suttas is a means for revolving among other similar and dissimilar dhammas. 'Because' means because the revolving among similar and dissimilar dhammas is a means for analyzing common and other dhammas. 'In what has a distinct nature' means in what has a nature distinguished by way of its counterpart.

Te dhammāti paṭipakkhato parivattitadhammā. Na pariyāyavibhāvanā paññattivibhāgapariggāhikāti āha “pariyā…pe… subodhanañcā”ti.

'Those dhammas' means the dhammas transformed from their opposites. Because the clarification of a synonym does not grasp the division of concepts, he says: 'by way of... and easy to understand.'

Pucchāvisodhanaṃ vissajjanaṃ. Ārambhavisodhanaṃ desanāya atthakathanaṃ. Tadubhayavicāro dhātādīsu asammuyhantasseva sambhavatīti āha “dhātāyatanā…pe… sampādetu”nti. Suddho ārambhotiādipāḷinidassanenapi ayamevattho udāhaṭoti veditabbaṃ.

The clarification of the question is the answer. The clarification of the beginning is the explanation of the meaning of the teaching. Because the investigation of both of these is possible only for one who is not confused about the elements, etc., he says: 'the elements, the sense bases... to accomplish.' It should be understood that this same meaning is also illustrated by the citation from the Pāli beginning 'a pure beginning.'

“Kāraṇākāro”ti padaṭṭhānaṃ sandhāya vadati. Pabhedato desanākāroti vevacanaṃ. Niddhāretvā vuccamānānīti uddharitvā samāropiyamānānīti adhippāyo. Suttassa atthaṃ tathattāvabodhāyāti suttassa padatthāvagamamukhena catusaccābhisamayāya.

He says 'the mode of the cause' with reference to the basis of the word. 'Mode of teaching' is a synonym for it, in respect of its divisions. 'Being stated after having been specified' means being applied after having been extracted; this is the intention. 'For understanding the meaning of the sutta as it really is' means for the breakthrough to the Four Noble Truths by means of understanding the meaning of the words of the sutta.

Veneyyattayayutto [V 0.0036] atthanayattayūpadeso “veneyyattayappayojito”ti vutto. Veneyyattayañhi paccayasamavāye tadupadesaphalaṃ adhigacchantaṃ atthaṃ payojeti nāmāti. Tadanukkamenevāti tesaṃ ugghaṭitaññuādīnaṃ desanānukkameneva. Teti tayo atthanayā. Tesanti ugghaṭitaññuādīnaṃ. Yathā uddesādīnaṃ saṅkhepamajjhimavitthāravuttiyā tiṇṇaṃ puggalānaṃ upakāratā, evaṃ tesaṃ atthanayānaṃ. Tassāti atthanayatthassa. Tatthāti tassaṃ tassaṃ bhūmiyaṃ.

The instruction on the three methods of meaning, which is connected with the three kinds of persons to be trained, is called 'applied to the three kinds of persons to be trained.' For when there is a confluence of conditions, the three kinds of persons to be trained indeed apply the meaning, attaining the fruit of that instruction. 'By that same sequence' means by the same sequence of teaching for those of quick understanding and so on. 'Those three' means the three methods of meaning. 'Of those' means of those of quick understanding and so on. Just as the brief, intermediate, and extensive treatments of the summaries, etc., are helpful to the three kinds of persons, so too are these methods of meaning. 'Of that' means of the meaning of the method of meaning. 'Therein' means in each respective plane.

Samuṭṭhānaṃ nidānaṃ. Anekadhā saddanayato, niruttinayato cāti anekappakāraṃ. Padattho saddattho. Vidhi anuvādoti idamettha vidhivacanaṃ, ayamanuvādoti ayaṃ vibhāgo veditabbo. Samādhātabboti pariharitabbo. Anusandhīyā anurūpaṃ nigametabbanti yāya anusandhiyā sutte upari desanā pavattā, tadanurūpaṃ saṃvaṇṇanā nigametabbā. Payojananti phalaṃ. Piṇḍatthoti saṅkhepattho. Anusandhīti pucchānusandhiādianusandhi. Upogghāṭoti nidassanaṃ. Cālanāti codanā. Paccupaṭṭhānaṃ parihāro.

'Origination' is source. 'In many ways' means of many kinds, that is, from the method of grammar and the method of etymology. 'Meaning of the word' is the literal meaning. 'Injunction and supplementary statement' means this distinction should be understood: 'this here is an injunctive statement, this is a supplementary statement.' 'It should be resolved' means it should be refuted. 'Should be concluded in conformity with the connection' means the explanation should be concluded in conformity with the connection by which the teaching proceeds later in the sutta. 'Purpose' means fruit. 'Condensed meaning' means summary meaning. 'Connection' means the connection of the question and other such connections. 'Introduction' is illustration. 'Challenge' is accusation. 'Response' is refutation.

Pakatiādipadāvayavaṃ [M 0.0039] bhinditvā kathanaṃ bhedakathā yathā “dibbantīti devā”ti (ma. ni. aṭṭha. 1.153). Padassa atthakathanaṃ tatvakathā yathā “buddhoti yo so bhagavā sayambhū anācariyako”ti (mahāni. 192; cūḷani. pārāyanatthutigāthāniddesa 97; paṭi. ma. 1.161). Pariyāyavacanaṃ vevacanaggahaṇaṃ yathā “paññā pajānanā”ti (dha. sa. 16). Vicayayutticatubyūhaparivattanahārekadesasaṅgahitā, vevacanahārasaṅgahitā cāti āha “te idha katipayahārasaṅgahitā”ti.

A statement that analyzes a word into its components, such as its root, is a 'statement of analysis' (bhedakathā), as in 'They are "devas" because they sport (dibbanti).' An explanation of the meaning of a word is a 'statement of reality' (tatvakathā), as in 'The Buddha is the Blessed One who is self-become, without a teacher.' The adducing of a synonym is the 'grasping of a synonym' (vevacanaggahaṇa), as in 'wisdom (paññā) is understanding (pajānanā).' Because they are included in a part of the Exposition of Investigation, the Exposition of Reasoning, the Exposition of the Fourfold Array, and the Exposition of Conversion, and are also included in the Exposition of Synonyms, he says: 'These are here included in a few expositions.'

Attano phalaṃ dhāretīti dhammoti hetuno dhammabhāvo veditabbo. Ñāpakahetūpi ñāṇakaraṇaṭṭhena kārake pakkhipitvā āha “kārako sampāpakoti duvidho”ti. Puna cakkhubījādinibbattakameva kāraṇaṃ katvā dassento “puna…pe… tividho”tiādimāha. “Tayo kusalahetū”tiādinā (dha. sa. 1059-1060) āgatā alobhādayo, lobhādayo ca hetuhetu nāma. “Cattāro kho, bhikkhave, mahābhūtā hetu, cattāro mahābhūtā paccayo rūpakkhandhassa paññāpanāyā”tiādinā (ma. ni. 3.86) āgato paccayahetu nāma. Kusalākusalaṃ kammaṃ attano vipākaṃ pati uttamahetu nāma. Cakkhādibījādi cakkhuviññāṇaaṅkurādīnaṃ asādhāraṇahetu nāma. Kusalākusalānaṃ satipi paccayadhammabhāve iṭṭhāniṭṭhaphalavisesahetubhāvadassanatthaṃ visuṃ gahaṇaṃ, saddamaggānaṃ pana ñāpakasampāpakahetubhāvadassanatthanti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Aṅkurādikassa asādhāraṇahetu bījādisamānajātiyahetutāya sabhāgahetu[V 0.0037]. Sādhāraṇahetu bhusasalilādiasamānajātiyatāya asabhāgahetu. Indriyabaddhasantāniko ajjhattikahetu, itaro bāhirahetu. Keci pana “sasantāniko ajjhattikahetu, itaro bāhirahetū”ti vadanti. Pariggāhako upatthambhako. Paramparahetu upanissayapaccayo.

'It holds its own fruit, thus it is a dhamma'; in this way the nature of a cause as a dhamma should be understood. Including the indicator-cause in the agent-cause because of its function of producing knowledge, he says: 'The agent-cause is twofold: the accomplisher and the indicator.' Again, showing that the cause is simply the producer of the eye, seeds, etc., he says, 'Again... threefold,' etc. The non-greed, etc., and the greed, etc., that come in the passage beginning 'The three wholesome roots' are called the 'root-cause.' What comes in the passage beginning 'Bhikkhus, the four great elements are a cause, the four great elements are a condition for the designation of the form aggregate' is called the 'condition-cause.' Wholesome and unwholesome kamma in relation to its own fruit is called the 'supreme cause.' The eye, seeds, etc., are the 'particular cause' for eye-consciousness, sprouts, etc. It should be seen that wholesome and unwholesome states are taken up separately, despite their being conditional phenomena, in order to show their status as causes for specific desirable and undesirable fruits; but in the case of sound and the path, it is to show their status as indicator-causes and accomplisher-causes. The particular cause of a sprout, etc., is a 'homogeneous cause' because it is a cause of the same kind as the seed, etc. A common cause, such as soil, water, etc., is a 'heterogeneous cause' because it is of a different kind. The 'internal cause' is what is connected to the continuity of the faculties; the other is the 'external cause.' Some, however, say: 'The internal cause is what belongs to one's own continuity; the other is the external cause.' The 'embracing cause' is the supporting cause. The 'successive cause' is the decisive support condition.

Nibbānassa anibbattaniyepi samudayappahānasamudayanirodhānaṃ adhigamādhigantabbabhāvato nibbānaṃ pati maggassa hetubhāvo viya maggaṃ pati nibbānassa phalabhāvo upacārasiddhoti āha “phalapariyāyo labbhatī”ti.

Although Nibbāna is not to be produced, because the abandoning of the origin and the cessation of the origin have the nature of an attainment and something to be attained, Nibbāna's status as a fruit in relation to the path is established by figurative usage, just as the path has the status of a cause in relation to Nibbāna. Therefore he says: 'a figurative expression for fruit is obtained.'

Paṭipajjamānabhūmi maggadhammā. Paṭipannabhūmi phaladhammā.

The plane of one who is practicing comprises the dhammas of the path. The plane of one who has practiced comprises the dhammas of the fruit.

Kiccatoti [M 0.0040] sarasato. Lakkhaṇatoti upalakkhaṇato. Sāmaññatoti samānabhāvato. Tena samānahetutā, samānaphalatā, samānārammaṇatā ca gahitā hotīti. Tattha yaṃ vattabbaṃ, taṃ heṭṭhā lakkhaṇahāraniddesavaṇṇanāyaṃ vuttameva.

'From the function' means from its essential nature. 'From the characteristic' means from its mark. 'From the general aspect' means from its common nature. By that, common cause, common fruit, and common object are included. Therein, what should be said has already been stated below in the commentary on the exposition of the Conveyance of Characteristics.

Apicettha sampayogavippayogavirodhapakaraṇaliṅgasaddantarasannidhānasāmatthiyādīnampi vasena nayavibhāgo veditabbo. Tattha sampayogato tāvanayavibhāgo – “nāhaṃ, bhikkhave, aññaṃ ekadhammampi samanupassāmi, yaṃ evaṃ lahuparivattaṃ, yathayidaṃ citta”nti (a. ni. 1.48) cittassa lahuparivattitā gahitā, taṃsampayogato cetasikānampi gahitāva hoti aññattha nesaṃ cittena sampayogadīpanato. Atha vā “saññino”ti. Saññāsahitatāvacanena hi nesaṃ vedanācetanādivantatāpi sampayogato dīpitā hoti.

Furthermore, here the division of method should be understood by way of association, dissociation, opposition, the treatise, sign, proximity of another word, capacity, and so on. Therein, firstly, the division of method by way of association: 'Bhikkhus, I do not see even one other thing that changes so swiftly as this mind' (AN 1:48). The swift-changing nature of the mind is grasped, and through its association, that of the mental factors is also grasped, since their association with the mind is indicated elsewhere. Or, 'possessing perception.' For by the statement of its being accompanied by perception, their possession of feeling, volition, and so on is also indicated by way of association.

Vippayogato – “ahetukā”ti. Hetusampayuttā hi dhammā “sahetukā”ti vuttāti tabbidhurā dhammā vippayogato “ahetukā”ti vuttāti viññāyati. Atha vā “asaññino”ti. Saññāvippayuttā hi dhammapavatti idhādhippetā, na saññāya abhāvamattanti viññāyati.

By way of dissociation: 'Rootless.' For since phenomena associated with roots are called 'with roots,' it is understood that phenomena devoid of them are called 'rootless' by way of dissociation. Or, 'without perception.' For it is understood that here a process of phenomena dissociated from perception is intended, not the mere absence of perception.

Virodhato – “aṭṭhamako (yama. 3.indriyayamakapāḷi.439), saddhānusārī”ti (pu. pa. mātikā 7.36) ca vutte taṃ santatiyaṃ saṃyojanattayappahānaṃ viññāyati, tathā “sati vā upādisese anāgāmitā”ti (dī. ni. 2.404; ma. ni. 1.137) vutte pañcorambhāgiyasaṃyojanappahānaṃ[V 0.0038], “diṭṭheva dhamme aññā”ti (dī. ni. 2.404; ma. ni. 1.137) vutte anavasesasaṃyojanappahānaṃ viññāyati.

By way of opposition: When it is said, 'the eighth person' (Yam. 3. Indriyayamakapāḷi.439), and 'the faith-follower' (Pug. Pa. Mātikā 7.36), the abandonment of the three fetters is understood in that continuity. Similarly, when it is said, 'non-returning while there is a residue of substratum remaining' (Dī. Ni. 2.404; Ma. Ni. 1.137), the abandonment of the five lower fetters is understood. And when it is said, 'final knowledge in this very life' (Dī. Ni. 2.404; Ma. Ni. 1.137), the abandonment of the fetters without remainder is understood.

Pakaraṇato – “abyākatā dhammā”ti (dha. sa. mātikā). Adhikārato hi kusalākusalabhāvena na kathitāti ñāyati. “Upadhī hi narassa socanā”ti (saṃ. ni. 1.12, 144) ca. Bāhirā hi dhammā idha “upadhī”ti adhippetāti viññāyati.

By way of the treatise: 'Indeterminate phenomena' (Dhs. mātikā). For from the context it is known that they are not spoken of as wholesome or unwholesome. And 'for acquisitions are a man's sorrow' (Saṃ. Ni. 1.12, 144). Here, it is understood that external phenomena are meant by 'acquisitions'.

Liṅgato – “sītenapi ruppati, uṇhenapi ruppatī”tiādi (saṃ. ni. 3.79). Sītādiggahaṇena hi liṅgena bhūtupādāyappakārasseva dhammassa rūpabhāvo, na itarassa.

By way of the sign: 'It is afflicted by cold, it is afflicted by heat,' and so on (Saṃ. Ni. 3.79). For by this sign—the mentioning of cold and so on—it is the material nature of a phenomenon derived from the primary elements that is indicated, not of another.

Saddantarasannidhānato – “kāyapassaddhi, kāyāyatana”nti. “Yā vedanākkhandhassā”tiādivacanato hi purimo kāyasaddo samūhavācī, itaro āyatanasaddasannidhānato pasādavācī.

By way of the proximity of another word: 'Bodily tranquility, the body as a sense base.' From statements such as 'that which is of the feeling aggregate,' the first word 'body' denotes a collection, while the other, due to the proximity of the word 'sense base,' denotes the sensitive faculty.

Sāmatthiyato [M 0.0041] – “sabbaṃ, bhikkhave, ādittaṃ (saṃ. ni. 4.28; mahāva. 54), sabbe tasanti daṇḍassā”ti (dha. pa. 129) ca, tathā “sabbāvantaṃ lokaṃ mettāsahagatena cetasā…pe… pharitvā viharatī”tiādi (dī. ni. 1.556; 3.308; ma. ni. 1.77, 232, 459, 509; 2.309; 3.230; vibha. 642). Ettha hi satipi sabbasaddassa anavasesasattavācakatte ādittatā sāpekkhasseva atthassa vācakattā padesavācī sabbasaddo, lokasaddopi sattavācī. Sattārammaṇā hi appamaññāti. Tathā “mātaraṃ pitaraṃ hantvā”ti (dha. pa. 294-295) sabbena sabbaṃ hi sapaṭikkhepato, mātupitughātakammassa ca mahāsāvajjatāpavedanato, idha ca tadanuññāya katāya mātupituṭṭhāniyā tādisā keci pāpadhammā veneyyavasena gahitā viññāyati. Ke pana teti? Taṇhāmānā. Taṇhā hi jananī sattānaṃ. “Taṇhā janeti purisa”nti (saṃ. ni. 1.55-57) hi vuttaṃ. Pituṭṭhāniyo māno taṃ nissāya attasampaggaṇhato “ahaṃ asukassa ruñño, rājamahāmattassa vā putto”ti yathā. Sāmatthiyādīnanti ādisaddena desapakatiādayo saṅgayhanti.

By way of capacity: 'Bhikkhus, all is burning' (Saṃ. Ni. 4.28; Mahāva. 54), and 'All tremble at punishment' (Dhp. 129), as well as 'With a mind imbued with loving-kindness... pervading the entire world...' (Dī. Ni. 1.556; 3.308; Ma. Ni. 1.77, 232, 459, 509; 2.309; 3.230; Vibha. 642). Here, even though the word 'all' can denote all beings without exception, because 'burning' has a relative meaning, the word 'all' is used in a partial sense; the word 'world' also denotes beings, for the immeasurables have beings as their object. Similarly, regarding 'Having slain mother and father' (Dhp. 294-295): since the literal act is completely rejected and the great blameworthiness of killing one's parents is declared, here, by the allowance made for it, it is understood that certain evil qualities standing in the place of mother and father are intended for the sake of those to be trained. But what are they? Craving and conceit. For craving is the mother of beings. It is said, 'Craving begets a person' (Saṃ. Ni. 1.55-57). Conceit is like a father, as it arises from relying on self-importance, such as thinking, 'I am the son of such a king or royal minister.' The phrase 'by way of capacity, etc.' includes local customs and the like under the term 'etc.'

Labbhamānapadatthaniddhāraṇamukhenāti tasmiṃ tasmiṃ sutte labbhamānaassādādihārapadatthaniddhāraṇadvārena. Yathālakkhaṇanti yaṃ yaṃ lakkhaṇaṃ, lakkhaṇānurūpaṃ vā yathālakkhaṇaṃ. Hetuphalādīni upadhāretvā yojetabbāni tesaṃ vasenāti adhippāyo. Idāni hetuphalādayo ye yasmiṃ [V 0.0039] hāre savisesaṃ icchitabbā, te dassetuṃ “visesato panā”tiādimāha. Taṃ suviññeyyameva.

'Through the means of ascertaining the meaning of words that are found': by way of ascertaining the meaning of the words of the conveyances, such as gratification, etc., that are found in each respective sutta. 'According to its characteristic': whatever the characteristic is, or in conformity with the characteristic. The meaning is that causes, effects, and so on should be investigated and applied by means of them. Now, to show which causes, effects, and so on are especially required in which conveyance, he says, 'But especially...' and so on. This is easily understood.

Niddesavāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The commentary on the Exposition Section is concluded.

4. Paṭiniddesavāravaṇṇanā

4. Commentary on the Section of Detailed Exposition

1. Desanāhāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Analysis of the Guideline of the Teaching

5. Anvatthasaññatanti atthānugatasaññabhāvaṃ, “desanāhāro”ti ayaṃ saññā anvatthā atthānugatāti attho.

5. The state of being a term that conforms to its meaning (anvatthasaññatanti) means the state of being a designation that follows the meaning. The meaning is that this designation, "Guideline of the Teaching" (desanāhāro), is one that conforms to its meaning (anvatthā); it follows the meaning.

Avuttamevāti [M 0.0042] pubbe asaṃvaṇṇitapadameva. “Dhammaṃ vo”tiādi (ma. ni. 3.420) vacanassa sambandhaṃ dassetuṃ “kattha panā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Tepiṭakassa hi buddhavacanassa saṃvaṇṇanālakkhaṇaṃ nettippakaraṇaṃ, tañca pariyattidhammasaṅgāhake suttapade saṃvaṇṇetabbabhāvena gahite gahitameva hoti. Tenāha “desanāhārena…pe… dassetī”ti.

“Just what was not stated” (avuttameva): this means just a term not previously explained. To show the connection with the statement beginning, “I will teach you the Dhamma,” the passage beginning “But where...” was stated. For the Netti treatise has the characteristic of being an explanation of the Buddha’s word, the Tipiṭaka; and when a sutta passage, which is a compendium of the scriptural Dhamma, is taken up to be explained, that treatise is also included. Therefore he said: “By the Guideline of the Teaching ... he shows.”

Yesaṃ assādādīnaṃ vibhajanalakkhaṇo desanāhāro, te gāthāya, idhāpi ca āgate “assādaṃ ādīnava”ntiādinā udāharaṇavasena vibhajituṃ “tattha katamo assādo”tiādi āraddhaṃ. Tattha tatthāti tassaṃ “assādādīnavatā”ti gāthāyaṃ vutto katamo assādo. Atha vā “assādaṃ ādīnava”ntiādinā yo idha assādādīnaṃ uddeso, tattha katamo assādoti ceti attho. Esa nayo sesesupi. Kammakaraṇatthabhinnassa visayavisayitālakkhaṇassa assādadvayassa nidassanatthaṃ gāthādvayudāharaṇaṃ, tathā kāmavipariṇāmalakkhaṇassa, vaṭṭadukkhalakkhaṇassa cāti duvidhassāpi ādīnavassa nidassanatthaṃ “ariyamaggo nibbāna”nti duvidhassāpi nissaraṇassa nidassananidassanatthañca dve dve gāthā udāhaṭā.

The Guideline of the Teaching is characterized by analyzing gratification and so on. To analyze these by way of example—using the verse and also the passage found here beginning “gratification, danger”—the section starting “Therein, what is gratification?” is introduced. Here, “therein” asks: “In that verse on ‘the state of having gratification and danger,’ what is the gratification spoken of?” Or else the meaning is: “In the summary of gratification and so on stated here beginning with ‘gratification, danger,’ what is gratification?” This method applies to the rest as well. To illustrate the two kinds of gratification, characterized by the subject-object relation which is distinct in its meaning as agent and instrument of action, an example of two verses is given. Similarly, to illustrate the two kinds of danger—one characterized by the alteration of sensual pleasures, the other by the suffering of the round—and to illustrate the two kinds of escape—the noble path and Nibbāna—two verses each are cited as examples.

Dhammo have rakkhati dhammacārinti (jā. 1.10.102-103; netti. 5, 26, 31; peṭako. 22) ettha [V 0.0040] dhammacārino maggaphalanibbānehi sātisayārakkhā sambhavati, sampattibhavassāpi vipariṇāmasaṅkhāradukkhatāhi duggatibhāvo icchitovāti adhippāyenāha “nissaraṇaṃ anāmasitvā”ti. Tathā hi vakkhati “nibbānaṃ vā upanidhāya sabbā upapattiyo duggatī”ti.

“The Dhamma indeed protects one who lives by the Dhamma.” Here, for one who lives by the Dhamma, superior protection arises through the path, its fruition, and Nibbāna. And with the intention that even a prosperous existence is considered a woeful state on account of the suffering due to change and the suffering of formations, he said, “without touching upon escape.” For he will say: “In comparison with Nibbāna, all rebirths are woeful states.”

Avekkhassūti vidhānaṃ. Tassā pana avekkhāya pavattiākāro, visayo, kattā ca “suññato, lokaṃ, mogharājā”ti padattayena vuttāti āha – “suññato…pe… āṇattī”ti. Tattha saṅkhārānaṃ suññatā anattasabhāvatāya, attasuññatāya ca siyā. Yato te na vasavattino, attasāravirahitā ca, yato te anattā, rittā, tucchā ca attanā, tadubhayaṃ dasseti “avasavattitā”tiādinā. Evaṃ maccutaro siyāti evaṃ paṭipattiyā maccutaro bhaveyyāti attho. Parikappetvā vidhiyamānassa maccutaraṇassa pubbabhāgapaṭipadā desanāya paccakkhato sijjhamānaṃ sātisayaṃ phalanti āha “tassa yaṃ…pe… phala”nti.

“Look upon” (avekkhassu) is an injunction. He then says that the mode of occurrence, the object, and the agent of that looking upon are stated by the triad of terms “as empty,” “the world,” and “Mogharāja,” with the words: “as empty ... command.” Therein, the emptiness of formations may be due to their nature as non-self and their being empty of a self. Since they are not subject to control and are devoid of a self-essence, and since they are non-self, void, and empty of a self, he shows both these aspects with the words “not being subject to control,” and so on. “Thus one may cross beyond death” means: by practicing thus, one may cross beyond death. He says “for him, whatever ... fruit” because the preliminary practice for crossing beyond death, which is enjoined, is a teaching for the direct accomplishment of a superior fruit.

6. Udāharaṇavasenāti [M 0.0043] nidassanavasena. Tattha “puggalavibhāgenā”ti iminā ugghaṭitaññuādipuggalapayojito assādādīsu bhagavato desanāvisesoti dasseti.

6. “By way of example” means by way of illustration. Here, by the phrase “by the classification of individuals,” he shows that the Blessed One’s specific teaching on gratification and so on is intended for individuals such as those of quick understanding.

Ghaṭitamattanti sotadvārānusārena manodvārikaviññāṇasantānena ālambitamattaṃ. Sassatādiākārassāti sassatucchedākārassa. Idañhi dvayaṃ dhammadesanāya cāletabbaṃ, na anulomikakhanti, yathābhūtañāṇaṃ vā. Etasmiñhi catukke āsayasāmaññatā. Vuttañhetaṃ –

“Merely contacted” (ghaṭitamattaṃ) means merely taken as an object by the stream of mind-door consciousness in accordance with the ear-door. “Of the type of eternalism, etc.” means of the type of eternalism and annihilationism. For these two are to be shaken by the teaching of the Dhamma, not conformity-patience or knowledge of things as they really are. For in this tetrad, there is a commonality of disposition. For this has been said:

“Sassatucchedadiṭṭhī ca, khanti cevānulomikā;

Yathābhūtañca yaṃ ñāṇaṃ, etaṃ āsayasaññita”nti. (visuddhi. mahāṭī. 1.136; dī. ni. ṭī. 1.paṭhamamahāsaṅgītikathāvaṇṇanā; sārattha. ṭī. 1.paṭhamamahāsaṅgītikathāvaṇṇanā, verañjakaṇḍavaṇṇanā; vi. vi. ṭī. 1.verañjakaṇḍavaṇṇanā);

“The view of eternalism and annihilationism,

and conformity-patience,

and the knowledge of things as they are—

this is designated as ‘disposition.’”

Calanāyāti vikkhambhanāya. Parānuvattiyāti samucchedanāya. Ugghaṭite jānātīti ugghaṭitaññūti mūlavibhujādipakkhepena saddasiddhi veditabbā. Vipañcitanti “visamaṃ candimasūriyā parivattantī”tiādīsu (a. ni. 4.70) viya bhāvanapuṃsakaniddesoti āha [V 0.0041] “mandaṃ saṇika”nti. Nissaraṇaādīnavanissaraṇaassādādīnavanissaraṇānaṃ vibhāvanā veneyyattayavinayanasamatthā.

“For shaking” (calanāya) means for suppressing. “For following another” (parānuvattiyā) means for eradicating. The grammatical formation of ugghaṭitaññū (one of quick understanding) as “one who understands when it is stated concisely” should be understood by the addition of suffixes of the mūlavibhuja class. “Expansively” (vipañcitam) is a neuter participle indicating a process, as in “the moon and sun revolve unevenly,” etc.; therefore he said “slowly, gradually.” The explanation of (1) escape, (2) danger and escape, and (3) gratification, danger, and escape is capable of training the three types of persons to be trained.

Cattāroti assādo ca ādīnavo ca assādo ādīnavo ca assādo nissaraṇañcāti ete cattāro. Yadi nissaraṇavibhāvanā veneyyavinayanasamatthā, kasmā pañcamo na gahitoti āha “ādīnavāvacanato”ti. Yadi hi ugghaṭitaññuṃ sandhāya ayaṃ nayo vuccati, nissaraṇamattena siddhaṃ siyā. Atha vipañcitaññuṃ, neyyaṃ vā, ādīnavo ca nissaraṇañca assādo ca ādīnavo nissaraṇañca vattabbo siyā? Tathā appavattattā na gahito. Tenāha “ādīnavāvacanato”tiādi. Desananti sāmaññato gahitaṃ “suttekadesaṃ gāthaṃ vā”ti viseseti. Padaparamaaggahaṇañcettha saupāyassa nissaraṇassa anāmaṭṭhattā.

Four: gratification, danger, gratification and danger, and gratification and escape—these are the four. If the explanation of escape is capable of training the trainable, why is a fifth not included? He says: “Because of the statement of danger.” For if this method is spoken with reference to one of quick understanding, it would be accomplished by escape alone. But if it is for one of expansive understanding or one to be guided, shouldn’t a teaching on danger and escape, or on gratification, danger, and escape be stated? It is not included because it is not applied thus. Therefore he said “Because of the statement of danger,” and so on. He specifies “teaching,” which was taken generally, as “a portion of a sutta or a verse.” And the reason the padaparama person is not included here is that the escape together with its means has not been touched upon.

“Kalyāṇa”nti iminā iṭṭhavipāko, “pāpaka”nti aniṭṭhavipāko adhippetoti āha “ayaṃ assādo, ayaṃ ādīnavo”ti. Lābhādīnaṃ puññaphalattā tadanurodhaṃ vā sandhāya “ayaṃ assādo”ti vuttaṃ. Tabbipariyāyena alābhādīnaṃ ādīnavatā veditabbā.

By “good” (kalyāṇa) a desirable result is intended, and by “evil” (pāpaka) an undesirable result is intended; this is what is meant when he says, “This is the gratification, this is the danger.” With reference to gain and so on, it is said, “This is the gratification” because they are the fruit of merit, or in accordance with that. Conversely, the danger should be understood in terms of loss and so on.

Kāmāti [M 0.0044] kilesakāmasahitā vatthukāmā. Virūparūpenāti appatirūpākārena. Mathentīti maddanti. Pabbajitomhīti pabbajjaṃ upagato amhi. Apaṇṇakanti avirajjhanakaṃ. Sāmaññanti samaṇabhāvo. Samitapāpabhāvoyeva seyyo sundarataro.

“Sensual pleasures” (kāmā): this means objects of sensual pleasure together with the defilement of sensual desire. “With a misshapen form” (virūparūpena): this means in an unsuitable manner. “They churn” (mathenti): this means they crush. “I have gone forth” (pabbajitomhī): this means I have undertaken the going forth. “Certain” (apaṇṇakaṃ): this means unfailing. “The ascetic life” (sāmaññaṃ): this means the state of being an ascetic. The state of having evil quelled is indeed better, more beautiful.

Tattha “kāmā hi citrā madhurā manoramā”ti ayaṃ assādo, “virūparūpena mathenti citta”nti ayaṃ ādīnavo, “apaṇṇakaṃ sāmañña”nti idaṃ nissaraṇanti āha “ayaṃ…pe… nissaraṇañcā”ti.

Here, “For sensual pleasures are varied, sweet, and delightful”—this is the gratification; “They churn the mind with their misshapen form”—this is the danger; “the certain ascetic life”—this is the escape. Thus he says: “this ... and escape.”

Phalādīnaṃ ekakavasena ca tikavasena ca pāḷiyaṃ udāhaṭattā vuttaṃ “dukavasenapī”ti.

Because fruits and so on are cited in the Pāli text both singly and in triads, it is said: “also in dyads.”

Sukhā paṭipadā, dukkhā paṭipadāti yā dve paṭipadā, tāsu ekekā dandhakhippābhiññatāya dve dve hontīti āha “paṭipadābhiññākato vibhāgo paṭipadākato hotī”ti. Katapubbakiccassa pathavīkasiṇādīsu sabbapaṭhamaṃ “pathavī”tiādinā pavattamanasikāro paṭhamasamannāhāro. Upacāranti upacārajjhānaṃ. Paṭipajjitabbatāya jhānampi “paṭipadā”ti [V 0.0042] vuccati. Tadaññā heṭṭhimapaññato adhikā paññāti katvā “abhiññā”ti vuccati.

Pleasant practice and painful practice—of these two practices, each becomes twofold due to slow or quick direct knowledge. Thus he says: “The division made according to practice and direct knowledge becomes a division made according to practice.” For one who has done the preliminary duties, the very first application of mind that occurs towards the earth kasina and so on, beginning with “earth,” is the first advertence. “Upacāra” means access concentration. Because it is to be practiced, jhāna too is called “practice.” Wisdom other than that, being higher than the lower wisdom, is called “direct knowledge.”

Kileseti nīvaraṇappakāre, taṃsahagatakilese ca. Aṅgapātubhāvanti vitakkādijhānaṅgapaṭilābhaṃ.

“Defilements”: this means the kinds of hindrances and the defilements associated with them. “Manifestation of the factors”: this means the attainment of the jhāna factors such as initial application and so on.

Abhinivisantoti paṭṭhapento. Rūpārūpaṃ pariggaṇhantoti rūpārūpadhamme lakkhaṇādīhi paricchinditvā gaṇhanto. Pariggahitarūpārūpassa maggapātubhāvadandhatā ca nāmarūpavavatthānādīnaṃ kicchasiddhiyā siyāti na rūpārūpapariggahakicchatāya eva dukkhāpaṭipadatā vattabbāti ce? Na, nāmarūpavavatthāpanādīnaṃ paccanīkakilesamandatāya sukhasiddhiyampi tathāsiddhavipassanāsahagatānaṃ indriyānaṃ mandatāya maggapātubhāvato. Rūpārūpaṃ pariggahetvāti akicchenapi pariggahetvā, kicchena pariggahite vattabbameva natthi. Evaṃ sesesupi. Nāmarūpaṃ vavatthāpentoti “nāmarūpamattametaṃ, na añño koci sattādiko”ti vavatthāpanaṃ karonto. Kataro panettha vāro yuttarūpoti? Yo koci sakiṃ, dvikkhattuṃ, anekasatakkhattunti evamādīsu hi vikkhambhanavāresu sakiṃ, dvikkhattuñca vikkhambhanavāro sukhā paṭipadā eva, na tato uddhaṃ sukhā paṭipadā hoti, tasmā tikkhattuṃ vikkhambhanavārato [M 0.0045] paṭṭhāya dukkhā paṭipadā veditabbā. Apica kalāpasammasanāvasāne udayabbayānupassanāya uppannassa vipassanupakkilesassa tikkhattuṃ vikkhambhanena kicchatāvāro dukkhā paṭipadā veditabbā. Ettha dandhattā paṭipadāya etassa akicchattepi purimānaṃ kicchatte dukkhāpaṭipadatā vuttanayāva. Yassa pana sabbattha akicchatā, tassa paramukkaṃsagatā sukhā paṭipadā veditabbā.

‘Striving’ means setting oneself to it. ‘Grasping material and immaterial phenomena’ means grasping material and immaterial phenomena after having defined them by their characteristics and so on. If, for one who has grasped material and immaterial phenomena, the slowness in the manifestation of the path may be due to the difficulty in accomplishing the determination of name-and-form, etc., then should it be said that the painfulness of the practice is due only to the difficulty in grasping material and immaterial phenomena? No, because even when the determination of name-and-form and so on is accomplished with ease due to the weakness of the opposing defilements, the path manifests slowly on account of the dullness of the faculties associated with the insight thus accomplished. ‘Having grasped material and immaterial phenomena’ means having grasped them even without difficulty; when they are grasped with difficulty, there is nothing more to be said. The same applies to the rest. ‘Determining name-and-form’ means making the determination: ‘This is mere name-and-form; there is no other being, etc.’ Which case here is the appropriate one? Among the cases of suppression—once, twice, a hundred times, and so on—suppression once or twice is easy practice; beyond that is not easy practice. Therefore, from the case of suppressing three times onwards, it should be understood as painful practice. Furthermore, at the conclusion of the comprehension of groups, when a defilement of insight has arisen during the contemplation of rise and fall, the case of suppressing it three times with difficulty is to be understood as painful practice. Here, even if this stage is without difficulty, due to the slowness of the practice overall, its painfulness is to be understood in the same way as when the preceding stages were difficult. But for one for whom everything is without difficulty, the practice should be understood as the easy practice that has reached the supreme peak.

Yathā nāmarūpapariggahakicchatāya maggapātubhāvadandhatāya dukkhā paṭipadā dandhābhiññā vuttā, tathā tabbipariyāyena catutthī, tadubhayavomissatāvasena dutiyā, tatiyā ca ñātabbāti dassento āha “iminā…pe… veditabbā”ti. Vaṭṭadukkhato niyyānassa adhippetattā “vipassanāpakkhikā evā”ti vuttaṃ.

Just as the painful practice with slow direct knowledge is said to be due to the difficulty in grasping name-and-form and the slowness in the manifestation of the path, so the fourth should be understood in the opposite way, and the second and third by a mixture of both. To show this, it is said: ‘By this…pe… should be understood.’ Because escape from the suffering of the round is intended, it is said: ‘They belong only to the side of insight.’

Hetupāyaphalehīti ettha taṇhācaritatā, mandapaññatā ca paṭhamāya paṭipadāya hetu, taṇhācaritatā, udatthapaññatā ca dutiyāya, diṭṭhicaritatā, mandapaññatā ca tatiyāya, diṭṭhicaritatā, udatthapaññatā ca catutthiyā. Upāyo pana yathākkamaṃ satisamādhivīriyapaññindriyāni[V 0.0043], satipaṭṭhānajhānasammappadhānasaccāni ca upanissayabhūtāni. Phalaṃ vaṭṭadukkhato niyyānaṃ.

Here, by causes, means, and fruits: a nature disposed to craving and dull wisdom are the cause for the first practice; a nature disposed to craving and keen wisdom for the second; a nature disposed to views and dull wisdom for the third; a nature disposed to views and keen wisdom for the fourth. The means, in order, are the faculties of mindfulness, concentration, energy, and wisdom, and the foundations of mindfulness, meditative absorptions, right exertions, and the truths, which serve as supporting conditions. The fruit is the escape from the suffering of the round.

Samādhimukhenāti samādhimukhena bhāvanānuyogena. Tenevāha “samathapubbaṅgamāya vipassanāyā”ti. Idhāti imasmiṃ nettippakaraṇe. Vakkhati “rāgavirāgā cetovimutti sekkhaphala”nti, “rāgavirāgā cetovimuttikāmadhātusamatikkama”nti ca. Soti anāgāmī.

‘By way of concentration’ means through the practice of development with concentration as the approach. For that reason it is said: ‘by insight preceded by serenity.’ ‘Here’ means in this Netti-pakaraṇa. It will be said: ‘The liberation of mind through the fading away of lust is the fruit of a trainee,’ and ‘The liberation of mind through the fading away of lust is the transcendence of the sense-sphere realm.’ ‘He’ is a non-returner.

Tenāti paṭipakkhena. Tatoti paṭipakkhato. Samānādhikaraṇavasena ca cetovimuttisaddānaṃ samāsaṃ katvā bhinnādhikaraṇavasena vattuṃ “atha vā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Puna “cetaso vā”tiādinā aññapadatthavasena cetovimuttipadānaṃ samāsaṃ dasseti. Viññāṇapariyāyena ceto-saddena vuttayojanā na sambhavatīti āha “yathāsambhava”nti.

‘By that’ means by the opponent. ‘From that’ means from the opponent. Having formed a compound of the words ‘liberation of mind’ in the sense of having the same referent, then in order to speak of them as having different referents, ‘Or else…’ is stated. Further, with ‘or of the mind,’ it shows the compounding of the words ‘liberation of mind’ as an exocentric compound. Since the construction stated with the word ‘ceto’ as a synonym for consciousness is not possible, it is said, ‘as is possible.’

Hā-saddo gatiattho, gati cettha ñāṇagati adhippetāti āha “hātabbāti gametabbā”ti. Netabbāti ñāpetabbā.

The word hā has the meaning of ‘going’; and here the intended going is the going of knowledge. Thus it is said: ‘“To be gone to” means “to be understood.”’ ‘To be led’ means ‘to be made known.’

7. Tanti [M 0.0046] puggalavibhāgaṃ. Ñāṇavibhāgenāti sutamayādiñāṇappabhedena. Nibbattananti uppādanaṃ. Tatthāti tasmiṃ ugghaṭitaññutātiādipuggalavibhāgabhūte desanābhājane. Desanāyanti sutte. Taṃ dassetunti taṃ puggalavibhāgaṃ dassetuṃ. “Svāyaṃ hāro kathaṃ sambhavatī”ti keci paṭhanti.

7. ‘The text’ is the classification of persons. ‘By the classification of knowledge’ means by the division of knowledge, such as that based on learning, etc. ‘Generation’ means production. ‘There’ means in that vessel of the teaching which is the classification of persons, such as the one of ready understanding, etc. ‘In the teaching’ means in the sutta. ‘To show that’ means to show that classification of persons. Some read: ‘How is this guideline possible?’

Sāti vuttappakāradhammatthānaṃ vīmaṃsanapaññā. Adhikāratoti “satthā vā dhammaṃ desayatī”tiādiadhikārato. Sāmatthiyato ugghaṭitaññuādiveneyyavinayanasamatthabhāvato. Pariyattidhammassa upadhāraṇanti etthāpi “adhikārato sāmatthiyato vā”ti ānetvā yojetabbaṃ.

‘That’ is the wisdom that investigates the items of the Dhamma of the kind stated. ‘From the governing principle’ means from the governing principle such as ‘the Teacher teaches the Dhamma,’ etc. ‘From capability’ means from the state of being capable of training those to be trained, such as the one of ready understanding, etc. In ‘the examination of the scriptural Dhamma,’ here too ‘from the governing principle or from capability’ should be brought in and connected.

“Vīmaṃsādipariyāyavatī paṭhamavikappavasena, vīmaṃsādivibhāgavatī dutiyavikappavasena, cintāya hetubhūtāya nibbattā cintāmayī”ti evamādivuttanayānusārena sakkā yojetunti āha “sesaṃ vuttanayamevā”ti.

‘The first alternative possesses synonyms such as “investigation”; the second alternative possesses divisions such as “investigation”; the wisdom based on reflection is that produced by reflection which has become the cause.’ He says, ‘The rest is just as was stated,’ meaning that it can be connected in accordance with the method stated in this way.

Sutacintāmayañāṇesūti sutamayañāṇe ca cintāmayañāṇe ca sutacintāmayañāṇesu ca sutacintāmayañāṇesūti ekadesasarūpekaseso veditabbo. Cintāmayañāṇeyeva hi patiṭṭhitā mahābodhisattā [V 0.0044] carimabhave vipassanaṃ ārabhanti, itare sutacintāmayañāṇesūti. Tehīti tathā paṭhantehi. Vuttanayenāti “upādārūpaṃ pariggaṇhāti, arūpaṃ pariggaṇhātī”tiādinā paṭipadākathāyaṃ (netti. aṭṭha. 5) vuttanayena.

‘In the knowledges based on learning and reflection’: this should be understood as a case of partial ellipsis of a similar form, standing for: ‘in the knowledge based on learning and in the knowledge based on reflection.’ For it is established only in the knowledge based on reflection that great bodhisattas in their final existence undertake insight; the others are established in the knowledges based on learning and reflection. ‘By them’ means by those who read thus. ‘In the stated way’ means in the way stated in the talk on the path, beginning with ‘he comprehends derived materiality, he comprehends immateriality,’ etc. (Netti-aṭṭha. 5).

8. Parato ghoso paccayabhūto etissāti adhippāyo. “Paccattasamuṭṭhitena ca yonisomanasikārenā”ti idaṃ āvuttinayena dutiyaṃ āvaṭṭatīti veditabbaṃ. Tena sāvakānaṃ bhāvanāmayañāṇuppatti saṅgahitā hoti. Sāvakānameva vā ñāṇuppatti idhādhippetā ugghaṭitaññuādivibhāgakathanato. Etasmiṃ pakkhe pubbe vuttaekasesanayopi paṭikkhitto daṭṭhabbo. “Āsayapayogapabodhassa nipphāditattā”ti etena pacchimacakkadvayapariyāpannāni pubbahetusaṅgahāni sutacintāmayañāṇāni sandhāya “imā dve paññā atthī”ti vuttanti dasseti. Atthibhāvo cetāsaṃ paṭipakkhena anupaddutatā veditabbā. Aparikkhatattā anabhisaṅkhatattā. Sutamayañāṇassāpi purimasiddhassa.

8. The intention is that the voice of another is its supporting condition. The phrase ‘and by wise attention that has arisen internally’ should be understood as recurring a second time by the method of repetition. By this, the arising of knowledge based on development in disciples is included. Or else, only the arising of knowledge in disciples is intended here, because of the statement on the division of persons such as the one of ready understanding. In this alternative, the previously stated method of partial ellipsis should be seen as rejected. By the phrase ‘because of the accomplishment of disposition, application, and awakening,’ it shows that it is with reference to the knowledges based on learning and reflection—which are included in the last two wheels and which comprise the collection of prior causes—that it is said, ‘these two wisdoms exist.’ Their existence should be understood as their being unoppressed by opponents. Because of being uninvestigated, unconditioned. This also applies to the previously established knowledge based on learning.

9. Desanāpaṭipadāñāṇavibhāgehīti [M 0.0047] nissaraṇadesanādidesanāvibhāgehi, dukkhāpaṭipadādipaṭipadāvibhāgehi, sutamayañāṇādiñāṇavibhāgehi.

9. ‘By the divisions of teaching, practice, and knowledge’ means: by the divisions of teaching, such as the teaching on escape; by the divisions of practice, such as painful practice; and by the divisions of knowledge, such as knowledge based on learning.

Avasiṭṭhapārisajjenāti khattiyagahapatiparisapariyāpannena. Aṭṭhannanti khattiyaparisā brāhmaṇagahapatisamaṇacātumahārājikatāvatiṃsamārabrahmaparisāti imāsaṃ aṭṭhannaṃ.

‘With the remaining assembly’ means with that included in the assembly of nobles and the assembly of householders. ‘Of the eight’ refers to these eight assemblies: the assembly of nobles, of brahmins, of householders, of ascetics, of the Four Great Kings, of the Tāvatiṃsa gods, of Māra, and of Brahmā.

Samatthetīti samatthaṃ sambandhatthaṃ karoti.

‘It harmonizes’ means it makes a connected meaning.

Tameva dvādasapadabhāvaṃ dīpetvāti sambandho. Tadatthassāti chachakkapariyāyatthassa (ma. ni. 3.420 ādayo). Sabbapariyattidhammasaṅgāhakattā chachakkapariyāyassa, tadatthassa ca dhammacakkappavattena suttena (saṃ. ni. 5.1081; mahāva. 13; paṭi. ma. 2.30) saṅgahitattā vuttaṃ “sabbassāpi…pe… vibhāvento”ti. Visayibhāvena byañjanapadānaṃ, visayabhāvena atthapadānaṃ sambandhaṃ sandhāyāha “tesaṃ…pe… sambandhabhāva”nti.

The connection is: ‘having illuminated that very state of twelve terms.’ ‘Of its meaning’ means of the meaning of the Discourse on the Six Sixes (M I 420ff.). Because the Discourse on the Six Sixes includes the entire scriptural Dhamma, and because its meaning is included in the Discourse on the Turning of the Wheel of Dhamma (S V 420ff.; Vin I 10ff.; Paṭis I 30ff.), it is said: ‘of all…pe… analyzing.’ With reference to the connection of the verbal terms as subject and the meaning-terms as object, it says: ‘of them…pe… the state of connection.’

Padāvayavo akkharāni. Padatthoti padatthāvayavo. Padatthaggahaṇassāti padatthāvabodhassa. Visesādhānaṃ visesuppatti. Vākyabhedeti vākyavisese. Cittaparitosanaṃ cittārādhanaṃ. Buddhinisānaṃ paññāya tejanaṃ tikkhabhāvakaraṇaṃ. Nānāvākyavisayatāpi siddhā hoti padādīhipi saṅkāsanassa siddhattā[V 0.0045]. Ekavākyavisayatāya hi atthapadānaṃ saṅkāsanādayo yathākkamaṃ akkharādivisayā evāti niyamo siyā. Etenāti atthapadānaṃ nānāvākyavisayatthena.

The components of a term are the letters. ‘Meaning of a term’ is the components of the meaning of a term. ‘Of the grasping of the meaning of a term’ means of the understanding of the meaning of a term. ‘Imparting a distinction’ is the arising of a distinction. ‘In the division of a sentence’ means in a particular sentence. ‘Delighting the mind’ is pleasing the mind. ‘Sharpening the intellect’ is the whetting of wisdom, making it keen. The state of having various sentences as object is also established, because indication by terms, etc., is established. For if there were the state of having a single sentence as object, there would be a rule that the indication, etc., of the meaning-terms would have as their objects the letters, etc., in sequence. ‘By this’ means by the fact that the meaning-terms have various sentences as their object.

Ugghaṭanādiatthānīti ugghaṭanavipañcananayanappayojanāni.

‘The meanings such as unfolding’ means the purposes of unfolding, analyzing, and guiding.

10. Upatiṭṭhati etthāti upaṭṭhitanti upaṭṭhitasaddassa adhikaraṇatthataṃ dassetuṃ “upatiṭṭhanaṭṭhāna”nti vuttaṃ yathā “padakkanta”nti. Tenāha “idaṃ nesa”ntiādi. Paṭipattidesanāgamanehīti paṭipattigamanadesanāgamanehi. “Kicchaṃ vatāyaṃ loko āpanno jāyati ca…pe… jarāmaraṇassā”tiādinā jarāmaraṇato paṭṭhāya paṭiccasamuppādamukhena vipassanaṃ abhinivisitvā mahāgahanaṃ chindituṃ nisānasilāyaṃ pharasuṃ nisento viya kilesagahanaṃ chindituṃ lokanātho ñāṇapharasuṃ tejento buddhabhāvāya hetusampattiyā paripākagatattā sabbaññutaññāṇādhigamāya vipassanāgabbhaṃ [M 0.0048] gaṇhāpento antarantarā nānāsamāpattiyo samāpajjitvā anupadadhammavipassanāvasena anekākāravokārasaṅkhāre sammasanto chattiṃsakoṭisatasahassamukhena yaṃ ñāṇaṃ pavattesi, taṃ “mahāvajirañāṇa”nti vadanti. Aṭṭhakathāyaṃ pana “catuvīsatikoṭisatasahassasamāpattisañcārimahāvajirañāṇa”nti (dī. ni. aṭṭha. 3.141) āgataṃ, taṃ devasikaṃ vaḷañjanakasamāpattīnaṃ purecarānucarañāṇaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Yaṃ pana vakkhati “ñāṇavajiramohajālapadālana”nti, taṃ saha vipassanāya maggañāṇaṃ veditabbaṃ. Etaṃ brahmacariyanti sāsanabrahmacariyaṃ adhippetanti taṃ dassento “brahmuno”tiādimāha.

10. ‘It stands near here,’ thus ‘it is present.’ To show that the word ‘present’ has a locative sense, it is said, ‘the place of presence,’ as in ‘stepped forward.’ Therefore he said, ‘this is theirs,’ etc. ‘By practice, teaching, and arrival’ means by the arrival of practice and the arrival of teaching. ‘Alas, this world has fallen into difficulty; it is born… and so on up to… aging and death’—starting from aging and death, having entered upon insight through the gateway of dependent origination, like one sharpening an axe on a whetstone to cut through a great thicket, the Lord of the World, sharpening the axe of knowledge to cut through the thicket of defilements, since he had reached maturity in the causal basis for Buddhahood, causing the womb of insight to be conceived for the attainment of the knowledge of omniscience, having entered various meditative attainments from time to time, contemplating formations of diverse kinds and aspects by way of uninterrupted insight into phenomena, he set in motion the knowledge that proceeds through thirty-six hundred thousand million gateways—this they call ‘the great diamond knowledge.’ In the commentary, however, it is said, ‘the great diamond knowledge that ranges through twenty-four hundred thousand million meditative attainments.’ This is said in reference to the daily preliminary and subsequent knowledge of the customary meditative attainments. But what will be said, ‘the splitting of the net of delusion by the diamond of knowledge,’ should be understood as the path knowledge together with insight. ‘This is the holy life’—intending the holy life of the teaching, showing this he says, ‘of Brahma,’ etc.

Desanāyāti karaṇatthe idaṃ karaṇavacanaṃ. Niyuttoti ettha hetuattho antonītoti dassento “niddhāretvā yojito”ti āha.

‘Desanāya’ is in the instrumental case, signifying instrument. In the word ‘niyutto’ (appointed), showing that the sense of cause is included, he said: ‘having determined, he was appointed.’

Desanāhāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Explanation of the Analysis of the Method of Teaching is finished.

2. Vicayahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

2. The Explanation of the Analysis of the Method of Investigation

11. Jātiliṅgakālasādhanavibhattisaṅkhyāvisesādito saddato padavicayo kātabbo. Tattha kariyamāno ca yathāsabhāvaniruttiyā eva kato sukato hotīti dassento “idaṃ nāmapadaṃ [V 0.0046] …pe… ayaṃ saddato padavicayo”ti vatvā “so panāya”ntiādimāha. Vattabbaatthasaṃvaṇṇanāti taṃtaṃpadavacanīyassa atthassa bhedaṃ vatvā pariyāyehi vivaritvā kathanaṃ.

11. The investigation of a word should be done from the sound, based on distinctions such as class, gender, tense, mood, case, and number. Showing that when this is done, it is well done only when done in accordance with its natural expression, having said, ‘This is a noun… this is the investigation of a word from the sound,’ he then says, ‘But this…,’ etc. The explanation of the meaning to be expressed is the exposition that explains by means of synonyms after stating the distinctions of meaning to be expressed by each particular word.

Viciyamānassa suttapadassāti pucchāvasena pavattasuttapadassa. “Suttantarapadānipi pucchāvaseneva pavattānī”ti vadanti “na sabbampi suttapada”nti. Ekasseva padassa sambhavantānaṃ anekesaṃ atthānaṃ uddhāro atthuddhāro. Ekasseva pana atthassa sambhavantānaṃ anekesaṃ padānaṃ uddhāro paduddhāro. Sabbe hi saṃvaṇṇiyamāne sutte labbhamāne sabbe padatthe. Nava suttanteti suttageyyādivasena navappakāre suttasmiṃ ānetvā vicinatīti [M 0.0049] yojanā. Atha vā “sabbe nava suttante”ti iminā pavicayalakkhaṇena hārena suttageyyādīni sabbānipi navappakārāni suttāni vicinatīti attho. Tenāha “suttageyyādike”tiādi.

‘Of a sutta-phrase that is being investigated’ means of a sutta-phrase that proceeds by way of a question. They say, ‘Suttāntara-phrases also proceed by way of a question,’ but ‘not every sutta-phrase does.’ The extraction of multiple meanings that can arise from a single word is the extraction of meaning (atthuddhāra). The extraction of multiple words that can arise from a single meaning is the extraction of words (paduddhāra). For all word-meanings are to be found in the sutta being explained. ‘The ninefold Suttanta’: the connection is that one investigates by bringing it into the nine-factored sutta, that is, by way of sutta, geyya, etc. Alternatively, ‘all nine suttantas’: the meaning is that by this method characterized by investigation, one investigates all nine kinds of suttas, such as sutta and geyya. Hence he says, ‘sutta, geyya, etc.’

“Kosalānaṃ purā rammā”tiādikā (su. ni. 982) chapaññāsa gāthā vatthugāthā. “Pārāyanamanugāyissa”nti (su. ni. 1137 ādayo) pana ādikā ekūnavīsati gāthā anugītigāthā. Idaṃ nāmaṃ katanti idaṃ “pārāyana”nti nāmaṃ kataṃ. Tenāha “pāraṃ gamanīyā ime dhammā, tasmā imassa dhammapariyāyassa ‘pārāyanantveva adhivacana’”nti (su. ni. pārāyanatthutigāthā; cūḷani. pārāyanatthutigāthā 149 ādayo). Buddhiyaṃ viparivattamānanti imassa vicayahāravibhaṅgassa desanākāle āyasmā mahākaccāno attano buddhiyaṃ vattamānaṃ katvā evamāhāti yojanā.

The fifty-six verses beginning ‘Of the Kosalans, in a delightful city’ (Snp 982) are the introductory verses. But the nineteen verses beginning ‘I will chant the Pārāyana’ (Snp 1137ff) are the concluding verses. ‘This name was made’ means the name ‘Pārāyana’ was made. Therefore it is said: ‘These dhammas lead to the far shore; therefore, “Pārāyana” is the designation for this Dhamma discourse.’ ‘Revolving in the wisdom’: the connection is that at the time of teaching this Analysis of the Method of Investigation, the Venerable Mahākaccāna spoke thus, having made it present in his own wisdom.

Ekaṃsabyākaraṇassa ayanti ekaṃsabyākaraṇīyā, ekaṃsena vā byākātabbattā ekaṃsabyākaraṇīyā, ekaṃsabyākaraṇayoggāti attho. Sesapadadvayepi eseva nayo. Ṭhapanīyāti ṭhapetabbattā abyākaraṇīyāti attho. Samayantaraparicayena nivāraṇadhammaṃ pati saṃsayapakkhando pucchatīti adhippāyenāha “vimaticchedana”nti. Pakatiyā pana nivāraṇadhammaṃ ajānanto ñātukāmatāya pucchatīti adiṭṭhajotanāya pucchāpi siyā. Tathā hi vakkhati “lokassa nivāraṇādīni ajānantenā”ti.

‘Of that which is to be answered categorically’: these are things to be answered categorically, or because they should be answered in one way, they are things to be answered categorically; the meaning is that they are suitable for a categorical answer. This same method applies to the other two terms. ‘To be set aside’: the meaning is that because it should be set aside, it is not to be answered. With the intention that one asks about the hindering state with an inclination to doubt due to familiarity with another teaching, he says the question is ‘for the cutting off of perplexity.’ But ordinarily, one who does not know the hindering state asks out of a desire to know, so it could also be a question for the illumination of what has not been seen. For it will be said: ‘by one who does not know the hindrances of the world, etc.’

Ekavatthupariggahāti ekassa abhidheyyatthassa gahaṇato.

‘Because of grasping a single subject matter’ means from grasping a single expressible meaning.

Vimuttiparipācakaindriyāni vivaṭṭapakkhe ṭhitassa saddhādayo dhammā, kiṃ panettha ariyānampi indriyalokena saṅgaho hotīti āha “pariyāpannadhammavasenā”tiādi.

The faculties that ripen liberation are the states such as faith belonging to one who stands on the side of emergence from the round. Herein, are even the noble ones included in the world of the faculties? He says: ‘By way of the included states,’ etc.

Kāḷapakkhacātuddasīghanavanasaṇḍameghapaṭalacchādanaaḍḍharattīnaṃ [V 0.0047] vasena caturaṅgasamannāgatena. Vivicchāti vicikicchāya. Tenāha “vicikicchāhetū”ti. Dukkhamassa mahabbhayanti ettha vuttaṃ “assā”ti padaṃ “jappābhilepanaṃ assa brūmī”ti ānetvā sambandhitabbanti dassento “jappā taṇhā assa lokassā”ti āha. “Sabbasattāna”ntiādinā, “sabbasovā”tiādinā ca anvayato, byatirekato ca sātisayaṃ avijjāya nīvaraṇabhāvaṃ [M 0.0050] dasseti. “Dūre santo pakāsanti (dha. pa. 304; netti. 11), ratto atthaṃ na jānātī”ti (netti. 11, 27) gāthādvayenāpi anugītivicayaṃ dassetīti yojetabbaṃ.

By that which is endowed with four factors, namely, the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, a dense forest thicket, a covering of cloud banks, and midnight. ‘Vivicchā’ is for ‘vicikicchā’ (doubt). Therefore he says, ‘because of doubt.’ In the phrase ‘suffering is its great fear,’ showing that the word ‘assa’ (its) should be connected by bringing in the line ‘craving, I say, is its smearing,’ he says, ‘craving is the smearing of this world.’ By means of ‘of all beings,’ etc., and ‘of all streams,’ etc., he shows both positively and negatively the pre-eminent nature of ignorance as a hindrance. It should be connected that by the two verses, ‘The good shine from afar’ (Dhp 304) and ‘One who is impassioned does not know the goal,’ he also shows the investigation of the concluding verses.

Rūpāvacarāti rūpāvacarasattā. Vipariṇāmadukkhatāya muccanassa kāraṇavacananti sambandho. Yato vaṭṭadukkhato muccanaṃ. Taṃ vaṭṭadukkhaṃ anavasesapariyādānavasena saṅkhāradukkhatāgahaṇena.

‘Beings of the form realm’ means beings of the form realm. The connection is that this is a statement of the cause for liberation from the suffering due to change. Whence there is liberation from the suffering of the round. That suffering of the round is overcome by grasping the suffering inherent in formations by way of complete and exhaustive comprehension.

Ekādhāranti ekavatthu adhiṭṭhānaṃ. Nivāraṇaṃ vikkhambhanaṃ pidhānaṃ samucchedoti atthadvayassa pucchitattā “anekādhāraṃ dassetu”nti vuttaṃ. Tenāha “nivāraṇasaṅkhātaṃ saṃvaraṃ…pe… pidhiyyanti pacchijjantī”ti (su. ni. aṭṭha. 2.1041; cūḷani. aṭṭha. 3). Tassattho “nivāraṇasaṅkhātaṃ vikkhambhanaṃ, saṃvaraṃ, pidhānañca kathehī”ti.

‘Having a single basis’ means having a single foundation. Because two meanings of hindrance are asked about—namely suppression, covering, and eradication—it is said, ‘to show the multiple bases.’ Therefore he says: ‘the restraint called hindrance… they are covered, they are cut off.’ Its meaning is: ‘Speak of the suppression, restraint, and covering called hindrance.’

“Vodāna”nti iminā sotānaṃ vikkhambhanavisuddhi, “vuṭṭhāna”nti iminā samucchedavisuddhi adhippetāti āha “pucchāya duvidhatthavisayataṃ vivarituṃ ‘eva’ntiādi vutta”nti. Tathā cāha “vodāyati…pe… ariyamaggo”ti.

By ‘cleansing’ is intended the purification of the streams by suppression; by ‘emerging’ is intended purification by eradication. He says: ‘To explain that the question has a twofold subject matter, “thus,” etc., was said.’ And so he says: ‘is cleansed… the noble path.’

Diṭṭhimānāvijjāsotāpi taṇhāsotānugāti āha “yebhuyyena anurodhavasenā”ti. Upacāravasenāti nissitupacāravasena. Sabbasmāti cakkhuto yāva manatoti sabbasmā dvārato. Sabbappakārenātti taṇhāyanamicchābhinivesanaunnamanādippakārena.

He says that the streams of views, conceit, and ignorance also follow the stream of craving, stating: ‘for the most part, by way of conformity.’ ‘By way of figurative usage’ means by way of dependent figurative usage. ‘From all’ means from all doors, from the eye up to the mind. ‘In every way’ means in ways such as craving, wrong adherence, and exaltation.

Tameva satinti yāyaṃ sati pubbabhāge sotānaṃ vikkhambhanavasena vuttā, tameva satiṃ. Maggakkhaṇe sotānaṃ saṃvaraṃ pidhānaṃ brūmi. Yasmā pana pidhāyikāpi sati maggakkhaṇe paññānugā, paññākiccamevettha adhikaṃ, tasmā vuttaṃ “paññāyete pidhīyare”ti.

‘That very mindfulness’: that mindfulness which was spoken of in the preliminary section by way of suppressing the streams, that very mindfulness. At the moment of the path, I call it the restraint and covering of the streams. However, since at the moment of the path even the mindfulness that covers is an accompaniment of wisdom, and the function of wisdom is predominant here, it is therefore said, ‘By wisdom they are covered.’

Saṃvarapidhānānanti ettha saṃvarasaddena nivāraṇaṃ vuttaṃ.

In the phrase ‘of restraint and covering,’ by the word ‘restraint’ is meant hindrance.

Yasmiṃ [V 0.0048] yasmiṃ ariyamagge anadhigate yaṃ yaṃ abhisaṅkhāraviññāṇaṃ uppajjanārahaṃ, tasmiṃ tasmiṃ adhigate taṃ taṃ viññāṇaṃ anuppādanirodhena nirujjhati saddhiṃ attanā sampayuttanāmarūpenāti āha “tassa tassa viññāṇassa nirodhena sahevā”ti. Anuppādanirodho hi ettha “nirodho”ti adhippeto anupādisesanibbānassa adhippetattāti. Anusandhīyati etenāti anusandhi, idha pucchiyamāno attho.

When a particular noble path has not been attained, whatever formational consciousness is fit to arise, when that particular path has been attained, that consciousness ceases through the cessation of non-arising, together with the name-and-form associated with it. Hence it is said: ‘with the very cessation of that consciousness.’ For here ‘cessation’ is intended as cessation through non-arising, because Nibbāna without remainder is intended. An anusandhi is that by which a connection is made; here, it is the meaning being questioned.

Saha [M 0.0051] visayena dassetunti ettha saccāni eva visayo. Pahātabbasabhāvaṃ samudayasaccaṃ, tassa visayo dukkhasaccaṃ. “Saṃyojaniyesu, bhikkhave, dhammesu assādānupassino viharato taṇhā pavaḍḍhatī”ti (saṃ. ni. 2.53, 57) hi vuttaṃ. Pahāyakasabhāvaṃ maggasaccaṃ, tassa visayo nirodhasaccanti āha “saha visayena…pe… saccesū”ti. Kāmañcettha “samudayo dvīsu bhūmīsu pahīyatī”ti āraddhaṃ, “dassanena tīṇi saṃyojanāni pahīyanti, bhāvanāya satta saṃyojanāni pahīyantī”ti pana vibhāgavacanameva vattanti āha “pahāyakavibhāgamukhena pahātabbavibhāgaṃ dassetu”nti.

Here, in the words ‘to show it together with its object,’ the truths themselves are the object. The truth of the origin has the nature of what is to be abandoned; its object is the truth of suffering. For it is said: ‘Bhikkhus, for one who dwells contemplating gratification in things that can fetter, craving increases’ (SN 12.53, 57). The truth of the path has the nature of that which abandons; its object is the truth of cessation. Thus he said: ‘together with the object…pe… among the truths.’ Although here it is begun with, ‘the origin is abandoned in two planes,’ the statement, ‘three fetters are abandoned by seeing, seven fetters are abandoned by cultivation,’ is just a statement of division. Thus he said: ‘to show the division of what is to be abandoned by way of the division of that which abandons.’

Niravasesakāmarāgabyāpādā tatiyamaggena pahīyanti, itare catutthamaggenāti vuttaṃ “itarehi pana niravasesa”nti. Tatthāti kammavipākavaṭṭappabhedena tedhātuke bhavattaye. Saṃyojanavasenāti sabbadā yojanavasena bandhanavasena.

Sensual desire and ill will are abandoned without remainder by the third path, the others by the fourth path. Thus it is said: ‘but the others without remainder.’ ‘Therein’ means: in the threefold existence in the three realms, in terms of the division into the round of kamma and the round of kamma-results. ‘By way of fetters’ means: always by way of yoking, by way of bondage.

12. Aggaphalañāṇatāya ekampi samānaṃ tannimittassa khayānuppādārammaṇassa paccavekkhaṇañāṇassa vasena phalavohārena dve nāmāni labhati.

12. Though it is single, because it is the knowledge of the highest fruit, it obtains two names by the designation ‘fruit’ on account of the reviewing knowledge, which has as its object the destruction and non-arising of that sign.

Somanassanāmalābho iminā ārammaṇasaṅketenāti tadatthaṃ vivaranto “khaye…pe… samaññāyā”ti āha.

The attainment of the name ‘joy’ is due to this object-sign. Explaining its meaning, he said: ‘in destruction…pe… by designation.’

13. Taggahaṇenevāti phassapañcamakapañcarūpindriyaggahaṇeneva. Sahacaraṇādināti sahajātādianantarādipaccayabhāvena ceva nissayārammaṇādinā ca. “Sampayutta”nti iminā sahitatā avisiṭṭhatā idhādhippetāti āha “avibhāgena gahaṇīyabhāvaṃ sandhāyā”ti.

13. ‘By grasping that’ means by grasping the five sense faculties which have contact as the fifth. ‘By association, etc.’ means by way of being a condition such as co-nascence, immediacy, and so on, and by way of dependence, object, and so on. By the term ‘associated,’ togetherness and non-distinction are intended here; thus he said: ‘referring to the state of being graspable without separation.’

Kathaṃ samādhindriyaṃ uppādetīti āha “satiggahaṇena cettha pariyuṭṭhānappahānaṃ idhādhippeta”nti. Na hi samādhinā pariyuṭṭhānappahānaṃ sambhavati.

How does one generate the faculty of concentration? He said: ‘Here, by the grasping of mindfulness, the abandoning of obsessions is intended.’ For the abandoning of obsessions by concentration alone is not possible.

Padahati [V 0.0049] etenāti padhānaṃ, vīriyaṃ. Teti vīriyasaṅkhārā. Ekarasenāti yathā indriyāni ekarasāni honti, evaṃ ekarasabhāvena saraṇato pavattanato. Tathā pavattiyā eva suṭṭhu vata vīriyaṃ vāhesīti yoginā saṅkappetabbato tadupagavīriyavāhanaṭṭhena “sampahaṃsanā”ti vuttaṃ. Tenāha “evaṃ me…pe… hetubhāvato”ti.

‘One strives with this,’ thus it is striving (padhāna); that is, energy. Those are the energy-formations. ‘Of one essence’ means: just as the faculties are of one essence, so it is by proceeding, by occurring, with the nature of one essence. Because the yogi should resolve, ‘By that very proceeding, I have indeed well exerted my energy,’ it is called ‘gladdening’ (sampahaṃsanā) in the sense of conveying the energy that has been undertaken. Hence he said: ‘Thus by me…pe… because of being the cause.’

Iddhisaddassa [M 0.0052] paṭhamo kattuattho, dutiyo karaṇattho vutto, pādasaddassa eko karaṇattho eva. Pajjitabbā ca iddhī vuttā, na ca ijjhanti. Pajjitabbā ca iddhī pajjanakaraṇena pādena samānādhikaraṇā na hontīti “paṭhamena atthena iddhi eva pādo”ti kathaṃ sakkā vattuṃ, tathā iddhikiriyākaraṇena sādhetabbā buddhisaṅkhātā iddhi pajjanakiriyākaraṇena pajjitabbāti dvinnaṃ karaṇānaṃ na samānādhikaraṇatā sambhavatīti “dutiyena atthena iddhiyā pādo”ti kathaṃ sakkā vattunti ce? Sakkā, pādassa ijjhamānakoṭṭhāsa ijjhanakaraṇūpāyabhāvato. Atha vā “paṭhamena atthena iddhiyā pādo, dutiyena atthena iddhi eva pādo iddhipādo”ti evaṃ yojanato. Kathaṃ? Anantarattho paccāsattiñāyena idha paṭhamoti adhippeto, tato purimo dutiyoti.
