The meaning of the word ‘by its opposite’ is explained as ‘by the practice of diligence,’ thereby indicating its nature as calm. ‘Dispelling’ means leading to destruction. ‘By means of qualities dissimilar to the side of purification’ means by means of that which is the side of purification and also a quality dissimilar to heedlessness—that is, purification… Dhamma, calm.

Samathe satīti adhiṭṭhānabhūte jhāne sati, taṃ pādakaṃ katvāti attho. Yā paññāti nāmarūpaparicchedādivasena pavattapaññā. Tenāha “ayaṃ vipassanā”ti. Pahīnesūti pahīyamānesu.

‘When calm is present’ means when jhāna, which serves as the foundation, is present, having made that the basis. ‘The wisdom’ refers to the wisdom that proceeds by way of discerning name-and-form and so on. Hence it is said, ‘This is insight.’ ‘When abandoned’ means when they are being abandoned.

Vodānapakkhanti ārambhadhātuādivodānapakkhaṃ nikkhipitvā. Visabhāgadhammavasenāti pamādavaseneva. Sabhāgadhammavasenāti pubbe nikkhittassa ārambhadhātuādivodānadhammassa samathādisabhāgadhammavasena.

‘The side of purification’ means having set aside the side of purification, beginning with the element of initiative. ‘By way of dissimilar qualities’ means simply by way of heedlessness. ‘By way of similar qualities’ means by way of similar qualities such as calm, etc., of the qualities of purification, beginning with the element of initiative, that were previously set aside.

Puna apariyodāpaniyaṃ sikhāppattapariyodāpanaṃ idhādhippetanti āha “taṃ pana arahattena hotī”ti.

Again, the purification that has reached its peak, which is not to be purified further, is intended here; thus it is said, ‘But that is by arahantship.’

Mohasamuṭṭhānatā vuttā “moho eva samuṭṭhāna”nti katvā. Aññathā pisuṇāvācāya dosasamuṭṭhānatā musāvādassa viya mohasamuṭṭhānabhāvā vattabbā siyā.

The arising from delusion is stated, taking it that ‘delusion alone is the origin.’ Otherwise, it would have to be said that malicious speech arises from hatred, just as false speech arises from delusion.

Kammapathabhāvaṃ pattānaṃ, appattānañca akusaladhammānaṃ “sabbapāpa”nti padena pariggahitattā vuttaṃ “kammapathakammavibhāgenā”ti.

Because unwholesome states, both those that have reached the level of courses of action and those that have not, are included by the word ‘all evil,’ it is stated, ‘by way of the distinction between courses of action and kamma.’

31. Sesapadānanti “kusalassa upasampadā”tiādīnaṃ (dī. ni. 2.90; dha. pa. 183; netti. 30, 50, 116, 124; peṭako. 29) gāthāya avasiṭṭhapadānaṃ. Yathādhigatanti attanā adhigatappakāraṃ, pacchā bhūmidisā.

31. ‘The remaining phrases’ refers to the remaining words in the verse, such as ‘the acquisition of the wholesome’ and so on (Dī. Ni. 2.90; Dha. Pa. 183; Netti. 30, 50, 116, 124; Peṭako. 29). ‘As attained’ means in the manner attained by oneself; later, ‘direction of the earth.’

Upari [M 0.0070] yāpentīti manussalokato upariṭṭhimaṃ devalokaṃ gamenti.

‘They cause to go upwards’ means they cause beings to go from the human world to the upper celestial world.

32. Yathāvuttassa dhammassāti sīlassa ca maggassa ca. Taṇhāvijjādīnanti ādisaddena tadekaṭṭhakilesā gayhanti, tesaṃ padaṭṭhānadhammā ca. Samathavipassanādīnanti ādisaddena sāmaññaphalānaṃ saṅgaho. Yadaggena cettha “nirodho rakkhatī”ti vutto, tadaggena maggo rakkhaṇakiriyāya [V 0.0066] karaṇaṃ vuttaṃ “yena rakkhatī”ti. Visabhāgadhammavasena purimāni sabhāgadhammāvaṭṭanavasena pacchimāni saccāni niddhāritānīti yojetabbaṃ.

32. ‘Of the Dhamma as stated’ refers to both virtue and the path. ‘Craving, ignorance, etc.’—by the word ‘etc.,’ the defilements with the same meaning are included, as well as their proximate causes. ‘Calm, insight, etc.’—by the word ‘etc.,’ the fruits of recluseship are encompassed. Where it is said ‘cessation protects,’ by that the path is stated as the instrument in the act of protection—‘by which it protects.’ It should be construed that the earlier truths are determined by way of dissimilar qualities, while the later ones are determined by way of the turning of similar qualities.

Āvaṭṭahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of the Method of Turning is concluded.

8. Vibhattihāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

8. The Commentary on the Analysis of the Method of Distinctions

33. Dhammesūti puññādidānādibhedabhinnesu sabhāvadhammesu. Tattha labbhamānoti tesu yathāvuttesu dhammesu labbhamāno. Bhūmivibhāgoti kāmāvacarādidassanādibhūmippabhedo. Padaṭṭhānavibhāgoti te puññādidhammā yesaṃ padaṭṭhānaṃ, tesaṃ vā ye dhammā padaṭṭhānaṃ, tabbibhāgo. Yesaṃ suttānanti mūlapadaṭṭhānabhūtānaṃ saṃkilesabhāgiyādīnaṃ catunnaṃ suttānaṃ vasena. Asaṅkaravavatthānena hi etesu suttesu sātisayaṃ dhammā vibhattā nāma honti. Tenāha “visesato”ti. Yadi evaṃ kasmā vāsanābhāgiyanibbedhabhāgiyasuttāni evettha gahitānīti? Nayidamevaṃ nikkhamanapariyosānabhāvena itaresampi gahitattā. Yato hi nissaṭā vāsanābhāgiyā dhammā, te saṃkilesabhāgiyā. Yaṃpariyosānā nibbedhabhāgiyā dhammā, te asekkhabhāgiyāti dvayaggahaṇeneva itarampi dvayaṃ gahitameva hoti. Tenāha “imesaṃ catunnaṃ suttānaṃ desanāyā”ti. Imāni cattāri suttānīti pāḷiyā, vakkhamānāya desanāya vā itaradvayasaṅgaho daṭṭhabbo, na paṭikkhepo.

33. ‘In the dhammas’ means in the intrinsic phenomena distinguished by divisions such as merit, giving, and so forth. ‘What is obtainable therein’ means what is obtainable in those dhammas as stated. ‘Division of planes’ means the classification of planes beginning with the sense-sphere and vision. ‘Division of proximate causes’ means the division of those meritorious dhammas which are proximate causes, or of those dhammas which are their proximate causes. ‘By which suttas’ means by means of the four suttas, such as the factors leading to defilement, which are the fundamental proximate causes. For in these suttas, the dhammas are indeed classified in a special way by means of non-confusion and determination. Therefore it is said, ‘especially.’ If so, why are only the suttas on the factors pertaining to latent tendencies and the factors leading to penetration included here? It is not so, for the others are also included by their nature of issuing forth and culminating. For those dhammas from which the factors pertaining to latent tendencies have issued forth are the factors leading to defilement. And those dhammas in which the factors leading to penetration culminate are the factors of the non-learner; thus, by grasping these two, the other two are also included. Therefore it is said, ‘by the teaching of these four suttas.’ In the Pāli, ‘These four suttas’—or in the teaching to be explained—the inclusion of the other two should be understood, not their rejection.

Tenevāti niyamassa akatattā, tato ca tena tannissitena ca brahmacārī bhavatīti siddhaṃ hoti. Eva-saddo vā samuccayattho daṭṭhabbo. Siyā tassa paṭikkhepoti tassa aṭṭhasamāpattibrahmacariyassa siyā paṭikkhepo. Evaṃ sati sāvasesā desanā siyā.

‘Therefore’ means: since no rule has been made, and because of that, and by that, and dependent on that, one becomes a practitioner of the holy life—this is established. Or the word ‘eva’ should be understood as having a conjunctive meaning. ‘There might be a rejection of that’ means there might be a rejection of that holy life of the eight attainments. In such a case, the teaching would be incomplete.

Tadaṅgādippahānadvayaṃ [M 0.0071] padaṭṭhānabhūtaṃ idha gaṇanūpagaṃ na hotīti “samucchedapaṭippassaddhippahānānaṃ vasenā”ti vuttaṃ. Tathā hettha keci “tenevā”ti paṭhanti. “Teneva brahmacariyenāti paṭhantī”ti idaṃ “saṃvarasīle ṭhito”ti (netti. 33) ettha vuttaṃ pāḷivikappaṃ sandhāya vadati. “Yasmā…pe… vakkhatī”ti idaṃ pacchimapāṭhasseva yuttatāya kāraṇavacanaṃ.

The two kinds of abandonment, beginning with abandonment by substitution, being proximate causes, are not included in the enumeration here; hence it is said, ‘by way of abandonment by eradication and abandonment by tranquilization.’ Similarly, some here read ‘teneva.’ ‘They read it as “by that very holy life”’—this is stated with reference to the Pāli variant mentioned in ‘established in the restraint of virtue’ (Netti. 33). ‘Because… he will explain’—this is a statement of reason for the suitability of the latter reading.

Kathaṃ [V 0.0067] mantāti? Anibbedhasabhāvattā mahaggatapuññānaṃ na nibbedhabhāgiyasuttena saṅgaho, vāsanābhāgiyattā pana vāsanābhāgiyasutteneva saṅgahoti. Tadupasaṅgā hi pacchimo eva pāṭho yuttataro. Itarathā sāvasesā desanā bhaveyya. Tenāha “na hi…pe… desetī”ti.

How is this to be understood? Because of the nature of the great meritorious deeds not leading to penetration, they are not included by the sutta on factors leading to penetration; however, because they pertain to latent tendencies, they are included only by the sutta on factors pertaining to latent tendencies. For this reason, the latter reading is indeed more suitable. Otherwise, the teaching would be incomplete. Therefore it is said, ‘For indeed… he does not teach.’

Saṃkilesabhāgiyaasekkhabhāgiyānaṃ parato vakkhamānattā vuttaṃ “vakkhamānānaṃ…pe… vasenā”ti. “Sabbato”ti idaṃ pubbaparāpekkhaṃ. Tassa parāpekkhatāya “sabbatobhāgena ekādasasu ṭhānesu pakkhipitvā”ti aṭṭhakathāyaṃ yojitaṃ. Tattha padādike vicayahārapadatthe sandhāya “ekādasasu ṭhānesū”ti vuttaṃ. Pubbapekkhatāya pana “sabbatobhāgena desanāya phalenā”tiādinā yojetabbaṃ.

Since the factors leading to defilement and the factors of the non-learner will be explained later, it is said, ‘by means of those to be explained…’ The word ‘sabbato’ (in all respects) refers to both what precedes and what follows. Due to its reference to what follows, it is construed in the commentary as ‘having included in eleven places in all aspects.’ Here, with reference to the meanings of terms in the analysis of investigation, such as ‘word,’ it is said, ‘in eleven places.’ However, due to its reference to what precedes, it should be construed as ‘in all respects, by the teaching, by the fruit,’ and so on.

Saṃkilesabhāgiyānaṃ taṇhāsaṃkilesādinā desanānayo veditabbo. Phalaṃ apāyadukkhena manussesu dobhaggiyena. Asekkhabhāgiyānaṃ asekkhehi sīlakkhandhādīhi desanānayo. Phalaṃ aggaphalena ca anupādisesāya ca nibbānadhātuyā veditabbaṃ. Itaresaṃ pāḷiyaṃ vuttameva. Kāmarāgabyāpādauddhambhāgiyasaṃyojanaggahaṇena saṃkilesabhāgiyānaṃ, virāgaggahaṇena asekkhaggahaṇeneva ca asekkhabhāgiyānaṃ vakkhamānattā vuttaṃ “vakkhamānānaṃ…pe… vasenā”ti. Padapadatthavicārayuttiniddhāraṇamukhena dhammavibhattiādivicāro kātabboti dassetuṃ pāḷiyaṃ “vicayena…pe… tabbānī”ti vuttanti aṭṭhakathāyaṃ “vicayena…pe… dassetī”ti vuttaṃ.

The method of teaching for the factors leading to defilement should be understood by means of craving, defilements, and so forth. The result is suffering in the lower realms and misfortune among humans. The method of teaching for the factors leading to the non-learner's stage should be understood by means of the non-learner's aggregate of virtue, and so forth. The result should be understood as the supreme fruit and the Nibbāna-element without residue. For the others, it is as stated in the Pāli. Since the factors leading to defilement will be explained by the inclusion of the fetters of sensual desire, ill-will, and those belonging to the higher part, and the factors leading to the non-learner's stage will be explained by the inclusion of dispassion and the non-learner's stage itself, it is said, "by means of those to be explained..." To show that the analysis of Dhamma classification, and so forth, should be done by means of examining word meanings and determining reasoned judgment, it is said in the Pāli, "by examination... should be understood"; and in the commentary, it is said, "by examination... shows."

34. Evanti iti. Dhammeti vuttasabhāgadhamme. Sādhāraṇāsādhāraṇabhāvehīti sāmaññavisesena visiṭṭhehi. Dve dhammā sādhāraṇāti dve ime dhammā yehi sabhāgadhammā sādhāraṇā nāma honti. Katame dve? Nāmaṃ, vatthu ca. Tattha nāmaṃ nāmapaññatti, taṃmukheneva saddato tadatthāvagamo. Saddena ca sāmaññarūpeneva tathārūpassa atthassa [M 0.0072] gahaṇaṃ, na visesarūpena, tasmā saddavacanīyā atthā sādhāraṇarūpanāmāyattagahaṇīyatāya nāmasādhāraṇā vuttā. Vatthūti pavattiṭṭhānaṃ. Yattha hi ye dhammā pavattanti, tesaṃ sabbesaṃ te dhammā sādhāraṇāti pavattiṭṭhānasaṅkhātānaṃ vatthūnaṃ sādhāraṇā. Yasmā panidaṃ dvayaṃ tesaṃ dhammānaṃ sādhāraṇabhāve pakatibhūtaṃ sabhāvabhūtaṃ, tasmā vuttaṃ “dve dhammāti duve pakatiyo”ti. Ekasantatipatitatāyāti samānasantatipavattiyā. Tenāha “samānavatthukā”ti. Dassanapahātabbānañhi yathā micchattaniyatasattā pavattiṭṭhānaṃ, evaṃ aniyatāpīti ubhaye hi te samānavatthukā. Esa nayo itaresupi. Sakkāyadiṭṭhivicikicchāsīlabbataparāmāsā hi bhinnasabhāvāpete dhammā dassanena pahātabbataṃ [V 0.0068] nātivattantīti te nāmasāmaññataṃ pattā, rūparāgādayo ca bhāvanāya pahātabbatanti āha “pahānekaṭṭhā nāmasādhāraṇā”ti. Yathā pana “vatthūnaṃ sādhāraṇā vatthusādhāraṇā”ti ayamattho labbhati, evaṃ “vatthunā sādhāraṇā vatthusādhāraṇā”ti ayampi attho labbhatīti dassento “sahajekaṭṭhā vatthusādhāraṇā”ti āha. Te hi aññamaññaṃ phusanādisabhāvato bhinnāpi yasmiṃ pavattanti, tena vatthunā sādhāraṇā nāma honti. Ettha ca labbhamānampi kusalādināmasādhāraṇaṃ anāmasitvā vatthusādhāraṇā tāva yojitāti veditabbā. Paṭipakkhādīhīti ādisaddena samānaphalatāsahabyatādike saṅgaṇhāti. Sesapadesūti “puthujjanassā”tiādivākyesu. Kathaṃ? Tattha hi puthujjanassa, sotāpannassa ca sambhavato anāgāmino, arahato ca asambhavatotiādinā yojetabbaṃ.

34. "Evanti"—that is, "thus." "Dhammeti"—that is, the phenomena of similar nature as stated. "By common and uncommon aspects"—that is, distinguished by generality and particularity. "Two phenomena are common"—that is, these two phenomena by which phenomena of similar nature are called common. Which two? Name and basis. Herein, "name" is name-concept; through it alone is the meaning understood from the sound. And by sound, the meaning of such a form is grasped only in its general aspect, not in its specific aspect; therefore, meanings expressible by sound are called "common by name" because their graspability depends on a common form-name. "Basis"—that is, the place of arising. For wherever phenomena arise, those phenomena are common to all of them; thus, they are common to the bases designated as places of arising. But since these two are naturally inherent and intrinsic to the commonality of those phenomena, it is said, "Two phenomena"—that is, two natural principles. "By falling into one continuity"—that is, by occurring in the same continuity. Therefore it is said, "having a common basis." For just as the beings fixed on wrong views are a place of arising for what is to be abandoned by seeing, so too are the unfixed; thus, both indeed have a common basis. This method applies to others as well. For wrong view of individuality, doubt, and adherence to rites and rituals—these phenomena, though differing in nature, do not go beyond being abandoned by seeing; thus, they attain commonality by name. And lust for form, etc., are to be abandoned by cultivation; therefore it is said, "common by name due to being abandoned in one way." Just as the meaning "common to bases" is understood as "common by basis," so too this meaning "common through the basis" is understood; thus, showing this, it is said, "common by basis due to arising together." For though they differ in nature due to contact with each other, etc., they are called "common by basis" by means of that basis in which they arise. And here, even though commonality by name regarding wholesome, etc., is obtainable, it should be understood that commonality by basis is explained first, without mentioning the former. "By opposites, etc."—by the word "etc." it includes similarity in result, companionship, and so forth. "In the remaining passages"—that is, in phrases such as "for an ordinary person," and so forth. How? For there, it should be interpreted thus: it is possible for an ordinary person and a stream-enterer, but not possible for a non-returner and an arahant, and so on.

Kathaṃ te odhiso gahitāti kenākārena te “sādhāraṇā”ti vuttadhammā bhāgaso gahitā. “Amukassa, amukassa cā”ti ayañhettha attho. Sāmaññabhūtā dhammā sādhāraṇā nāma, evaṃ sante kathaṃ tesaṃ micchattaniyatāniyatādivasena vibhāgena pavattiṭṭhānatā vuccati, na vattabbanti adhippāyo. Atha vibhāgena taṃ vattabbaṃ, nanu te sādhāraṇāti na vattabbamevāti? Evaṃ sādhāraṇāti micchattaniyatānaṃ, aniyatānanti imesaṃ ubhayesaṃyeva te dhammā sādhāraṇā. Tenāha – “na sabbasattānaṃ sādhāraṇatāya sādhāraṇā”ti. “Yasmā”tiādinā tattha kāraṇamāha, tenetaṃ dasseti “keci dhammā kesañcideva dhammānaṃ sādhāraṇā [M 0.0073] honti, aññesaṃ asādhāraṇā”ti. Tenāha “paṭiniyatañhi tesaṃ pavattiṭṭhāna”nti.

"How were they grasped with specification?"—that is, in what manner were those Dhammas called "common" grasped in parts? The meaning here is: "belonging to such and such, and such and such." Phenomena that are of a common nature are called common; if that is so, how can their locus of occurrence be spoken of as divided in terms of what is fixedly destined for wrongness, what is unfixed, and so forth? The intention is that it should not be spoken of. But if it is to be spoken of in a divided way, then surely they should not be called common at all, should they? Thus, "common" means that those Dhammas are common to both those fixedly destined for wrongness and those unfixed. Therefore it is said, "They are not common by being common to all beings." With "because," etc., the reason is stated there; thereby it shows: "Some Dhammas are common to certain Dhammas alone, but not common to others." Therefore it is said, "For their locus of occurrence is specifically determined."

Itarathāti aniyatapavattiṭṭhānatāya sabbesaṃ sādhāraṇā, asādhāraṇā vā siyuṃ, tathā sati. Tathā vohāroti “sādhāraṇā, asādhāraṇā”ti ca ayaṃ vohāro sāmaññā eva na bhaveyya. Ete eva dhammāti “sādhāraṇā”ti vuttadhammā eva. Evanti “micchattaniyatāna”ntiādinā vuttappakārena. Niyatavisayā paricchinnappavattiṭṭhānā. “Yopī”tiādi puggalādhiṭṭhānena vuttassevatthassa pākaṭakaraṇaṃ. “Na hī”tiādinā anvayato, byatirekato ca tamevatthaṃ vibhāveti. Sesepīti “bhāvanāpahātabbā”ti evamādimhipi.

"Otherwise"—that is, if it were so, due to the nature of having an indeterminate range of occurrence, they might be common to all or not common to all. "Such an expression"—that is, this expression "common" or "uncommon" would not be generally valid. "These very phenomena"—that is, precisely those phenomena called "common." "Thus"—that is, in the manner stated by terms like "those fixed on wrongness," and so forth. Their scope is fixed, with a defined range of occurrence. The phrase "whoever," and so on, is a clarification of the meaning already stated with reference to individuals. With "for indeed," and so on, the same point is explained both affirmatively and negatively. "In the rest as well"—that is, in passages beginning with "to be abandoned by cultivation," and so forth.

Paccattaniyatoti pāṭipuggaliko. Itarassāti apaccattaniyatassa. Tathāti asādhāraṇabhāvena. Koci dhammo kañci dhammaṃ upādāya sādhāraṇopi samāno tadaññaṃ upādāya asādhāraṇopi hotīti āha “sādhāraṇāvidhuratāyā”ti. Tenāha “taṃ taṃ upādāyā”tiādi. Tathā hi “dhammatā”ti vuttapaṭhamamaggaṭṭhatā dīpitā, tādisānaṃ eva anekesaṃ ariyānaṃ vasena sādhāraṇāti[V 0.0069]. Paṭhamassāti aṭṭhamakassa. Dutiyassāti sotāpannassa. Puna aṭṭhamakassāti “aṭṭhamakassa, anāgāmissa cā”ti ettha vuttaaṭṭhamakassa. Tenāha “anāgāmimaggaṭṭhassā”ti. Aggaphalaṭṭhato paṭṭhāya paṭilomato gaṇiyamāno paṭhamamaggaṭṭho aṭṭhamako, maggaṭṭhatāya, pahīyamānakilesatāya ca sabbepi maggaṭṭhā aṭṭhamakā viyāti aṭṭhamakā, “ekacittakkhaṇato uddhaṃ na tiṭṭhatīti aṭṭhamako”ti apare niruttinayena. “Sekkhā”ti nāmaṃ sādhāraṇanti sambandho. Itaresūti “bhabbābhabbā”ti vuttesu anariyesu. Tenāha pāḷiyaṃ “hīnukkaṭṭhamajjhimaṃ upādāyā”ti.

"Paccattaniyatoti" means individual. "Itarassāti" refers to what is not individually determined. "Tathāti" means in an unshared way. It is said, "due to the lack of a common attribute," that a certain dhamma, although common with respect to one thing, may become uncommon with respect to another. Therefore, it is stated, "depending on this and that," etc. For instance, the state of the one established in the first path is indicated by the word "dhammatā," since it is common to many noble ones of that sort. "Paṭhamassāti" refers to the eighth. "Dutiyassāti" refers to the stream-enterer. Again, "aṭṭhamakassāti" refers to the eighth mentioned in "for the eighth and the non-returner." Hence, it is said, "for one established in the path of non-returning." Counting backward from the state of the highest fruition, the one established in the first path is the eighth. Because of their path-attainment and the abandonment of defilements, all those established in the paths are like eighths, or are called "eighths." Others explain according to the etymological method that the eighth is so called because "he does not remain for longer than a single moment of consciousness." The connection is that "Sekkhā" is a shared designation. "Itaresūti" refers to the non-noble ones mentioned in "capable and incapable." Therefore, the Pāli text says, "depending on the inferior, superior, and middling."

Niyāmāvakkantiyāti avakkantaniyāmatāya. Ñāṇuttharassāti ñāṇādhikassa. Tathāvidhapaccayasamāyogeti ñāṇavisesapaccayasamavāye. Yathā hi ñāṇabalena dandhābhiññatā na hoti, evaṃ paṭipadāpaṭipannopi sukhena visosīyatīti. Sā hi sukhāpaṭipadā khippābhiññā taṃsamaṅgino ñāṇuttarattā vipassanāya padaṭṭhānanti vuttā.

"Entering into certainty" means "by the certainty that has been entered." "Of surpassing knowledge" means "of superior knowledge." "Such a conjunction of conditions" refers to "the coincidence of conditions for distinction in knowledge." For just as slow understanding does not occur through the power of knowledge, so too, one who has entered upon the path is easily purified. That easy practice and quick understanding, because of being accompanied by superior knowledge, is said to be the foundation of insight.

Dhammato [M 0.0074] anapetā cintā dhammacintā, yonisomanasikārena pavattitattā dhammesu cintā, dhammo vā ñāṇaṃ, tasmā dhammāvahā cintā dhammacintā, cintāmayañāṇassa hetubhūtā cintāti attho.

Thought not deviating from the Dhamma is Dhamma-thought. Because it proceeds through wise attention, it is thought about the Dhamma. Or, Dhamma is knowledge; therefore, thought that conveys Dhamma is Dhamma-thought. This thought is the cause of knowledge born of thought—this is the meaning.

Pāḷiyaṃ sutamayapaññāggahaṇena “ye te dhammā ādikalyāṇā…pe… tathārūpāssa dhammā bahussutā hontī”tiādi (a. ni. 8.2) suttapadasaṅgaho attho pariggahito, tathā yonisomanasikāraggahaṇena “so ‘anicca’nti yoniso manasi karotī”tiādinā vutto upāyamanasikāro pariggahito. Sammādiṭṭhiggahaṇena “sammādiṭṭhiṃ bhāveti vivekanissita”ntiādinā vuttā sammādiṭṭhi pariggahitāti dassento āha “atha kho…pe… dassetu”nti. Sesanti “dhammasvākkhātatā”ti evamādi.

In the Pāli, by the grasping of wisdom based on learning, the meaning, which is a summary of the sutta passages, is grasped, as in: "Those teachings that are good in the beginning... such teachings are well-learned by him" (AN 8.2), and so on. Similarly, by the grasping of appropriate attention, the method of attention spoken of in: "He attends appropriately, thinking, 'impermanent,'" and so on, is grasped. To show that by the grasping of right view, the right view spoken of in: "He develops right view dependent on seclusion," and so on, is grasped, he said: "Then... to show." The remainder is: "The Dhamma is well-proclaimed," and so forth.

Yassa ca pubbe attho na saṃvaṇṇito, tattha kalyāṇamittatāya āyatanagato pasādo, cittavūpasamo ca phalanti dassento “sappurisa…pe… padaṭṭhāna”nti āha. Attasammāpaṇihitattā pāpajegucchinibbidādibahulova hotīti dassento āha “attha…pe… padaṭṭhāna”nti. Dhammo svākkhāto ādito paṭṭhāya yāva pariyosānā sabbasampattipāripūrihetūti dassento “dhammasvākkhātatā…pe… padaṭṭhāna”nti āha. Kusalamūlaropanā hi samāpattipariyosānāti[V 0.0070]. Saṅghasuṭṭhutāya saṅghassa suṭṭhubhāvāya saṅghassa sappatissatāya “suṭṭhu, bhante”ti vacanasampaṭicchanabhāvāya. Itaraṃ suviññeyyameva.

And for one for whom the meaning was not previously explained, to show that confidence based on the sphere of good friendship and the calming of the mind bear fruit, he said: "A virtuous person... the foundation." To show that because his self is rightly directed, he becomes one who is often disgusted with evil, disenchanted, and so forth, he said: "Meaning... the foundation." To show that the Dhamma is well-proclaimed from the beginning up to the end, being the cause for the complete fulfillment of all attainments, he said: "The well-proclaimed Dhamma... the foundation." For the planting of wholesome roots culminates in attainment. Through the excellence of the Sangha, for the Sangha's well-being, for the Sangha's respectfulness, for the Sangha's state of accepting the words, "Well said, venerable sir." The rest is easily understood.

Vibhattihāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Distribution of Cases is finished.

9. Parivattanahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

9. The Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Transformation

35. Sammādiṭṭhissa…pe… nijjiṇṇā bhavatīti ettha yathā maggasammādiṭṭhivasenattho vutto, evaṃ kammassakatākammapathasammādiṭṭhīnampi vasena attho labbhateva. Kammapathakathā hesā. Yathāvuttenākārenāti “avimuttāva samānā”ti, “avimuttiya”nti ca vuttappakārena. Micchābhinivesavasenāti asammāsambuddhaṃ eva sammāsambuddhoti, aniyyānikaṃ eva niyyānikoti, asantaṃ eva pana santanti, anariyaṃ eva ariyoti viparītābhinivesavasena[M 0.0075]. Micchādhimokkhoti ayāthāvapasādo, ayāthāvasanniṭṭhānaṃ vā. Uppannamoho micchāvimuttiñāṇadassananti sambandho.

35. Here, in 'For one with right view... is exhausted,' just as the meaning is explained in terms of the right view of the path, so too the meaning is indeed obtained in terms of the right views of ownership of kamma and the paths of kamma. For this is a discussion on the paths of kamma. 'In the manner stated' means in the way it is said, 'not liberated while being so' and 'because of non-liberation.' 'Through the power of wrong adherence' means through the power of perverse adherence: regarding one who is not a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One as a Perfectly Self-Enlightened One, what is not the path of release as the path of release, what is not tranquil as tranquil, and what is not noble as noble. 'Wrong conviction' means inappropriate confidence or inappropriate determination. The connection is: arisen delusion, wrong liberation, and wrong knowledge and vision.

36. Vādānaṃ vā anuvādā vādānuvādā, tesaṃ vādānaṃ upādāti attho. Vādānupavattiyoti vādānaṃ dosānaṃ anupavattiyo.

36. Disputes or counter-disputes are 'disputes and counter-disputes'; the meaning is the taking up of those disputes. 'Non-following of disputes' means the non-following of the faults of disputes.

Antadvayaparivattananti kāmasukhaattakilamathānuyogasaṅkhātassa antadvayassa paṭipakkhavasena parivattanaṃ.

'The turning away from the two extremes' means the turning away, by way of opposition, from the two extremes designated as indulgence in sensual pleasures and the practice of self-mortification.

Etesupi vāresūti “niyyāniko dhammo tesaṃ adhammo, sukho tesaṃ adhammo”ti ca imesu vāresu. Vuttanayenāti yadi attaparitāpanaṃ attano dukkhāpanaṃ dhammo, dhammassa paṭiviruddho adhammo siyā, dukkhassa ca sukhapaṭiviruddhanti jhānamaggaphalasukhassa, anavajjapaccayaparibhogasukhassa ca tesaṃ adhammabhāvo āpajjatīti evaṃ vattabbā. “Yaṃ yaṃ vā panātiādinā”ti idaṃ avasesapāṭhāmasanaṃ. Ettha yaṃ yaṃ vā pana dhammanti yaṃ vā taṃ vā dhammaṃ, kusalaṃ vā akusalaṃ vā iṭṭhaṃ vā aniṭṭhaṃ vāti vuttaṃ hoti. Rocayati vā upagacchati vāti cittena rocati, diṭṭhiyā upagacchatīti. Tassa tassa dhammassa yo paṭipakkhoti tassa tassa rucitassa, upagatassa vā dhammassa yo paṭipakkho nāma. Svassa aniṭṭhato ajjhāpanno bhavatīti [V 0.0071] yo dhammo assa rucitassa, upagatassa vā dhammassa aniṭṭhato paccanīkato abbhupagato hoti, tena paṭipakkhena desanāya parivattanaṃ parivattano hāroti attho. Tenāha “paṭipakkhassa lakkhaṇaṃ vibhāvetī”ti.

'In these alternatives too' means in these alternatives: "The Dhamma that leads out is non-Dhamma for them, and happiness is non-Dhamma for them." 'By the stated method' means: if self-torment, the affliction of oneself, is Dhamma, then what is contrary to Dhamma would be non-Dhamma; and since suffering is contrary to happiness, it would follow that the happiness of jhāna, path, and fruition, and the happiness of the blameless enjoyment of requisites would become non-Dhamma for them. Thus it should be stated. "Whatever..." and so on: this is an examination of the remaining text. Here, "whatever Dhamma" means whatever Dhamma it may be, whether wholesome or unwholesome, desirable or undesirable—this is what is said. "Approves or accepts" means: one approves with the mind, one accepts with one's view. "The opposite of that Dhamma" means whatever is the opposite of that Dhamma that one approves of or accepts. The meaning is: "That becomes established for him as undesirable" means that whatever Dhamma is accepted by him as undesirable and contrary to the Dhamma he approves of or accepts, the transformation of the teaching through that opposition is the mode of transformation. Therefore, he said: "It reveals the characteristic of the opposite."

Parivattanahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Transformation is finished.

10. Vevacanahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

10. The Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Synonyms

37. Aññamaññehīti aññehi aññehi. Āyatinti paccavekkhaṇakāle. Kathañcīti yena kenaci pakārena, paṭhamaṃ vuttena pariyāyena appaṭivijjhanto aparena pariyāyena paṭivijjheyyāti adhippāyo. Pariyāyavacanaṃ niddisatīti sambandho. Evaṃ sabbattha. Tasmiṃ khaṇeti pariyāyavacanassa vuttakkhaṇe. Vikkhittacittānanti ārammaṇantarehi vividhakhittacittānaṃ[M 0.0076]. Aññavihitānanti aññaṃ cintentānaṃ. Kasmā pana aññena pariyāyena tadatthāvabodhanaṃ, nanu tena vutte daḷhīkaraṇaṃ hotīti codanaṃ sandhāyāha “tenevā”tiādi. Tattha tadaññesanti tehi vikkhittacittādīhi aññesaṃ, yehi paṭhamaṃ vacanaṃ sammadeva gahitaṃ. Tatthāti vuttavacaneneva punappunaṃ vacane. Adhigataanvatthatāya punarutti parivajjanatthaṃ visesanabhāvena tāhi tāhi saññāhipi ayampi saddo imassatthassa vācako, ayampi saddo imassatthassa vācakoti paññāpanehi. Desetabbassa tassa tassa atthassa attano citte upanibandhanaṃ ṭhapanaṃ. Tatthāti dhammaniruttipaṭisambhidāyaṃ. Bījāvāpanaṃ hetusampādanaṃ.

37. 'By one another' means by various others. 'In the future' means at the time of reflection. 'Somehow' means in whatever way; the intention is that if one does not penetrate by the first mode of expression stated, one might penetrate by another mode of expression. The connection is: 'it indicates the alternative expression.' Thus it is in all cases. 'At that moment' means at the moment the alternative expression is spoken. 'Of those with distracted minds' means of those whose minds are variously scattered by other objects. 'Of those otherwise disposed' means of those thinking of something else. Referring to the objection, 'But why is there understanding of that meaning through another mode of expression? Surely, when it is stated by that, it becomes firmly established?' he said, 'For that very reason...' and so on. Therein, 'other than them' means those other than those with distracted minds and so on, by whom the first statement was correctly grasped. 'Therein' means in the repeated statement of that very word which was spoken. For the sake of avoiding repetition when the meaning has already been understood, by making known through various designations as a way of specifying, 'This word too is expressive of this meaning, and this word too is expressive of this meaning.' The binding and establishing in one’s own mind of each meaning to be taught. 'Therein' means in the analytical knowledge of the language of the Dhamma. 'The sowing of seeds' is the accomplishment of a cause.

Evaṃ bhagavato pariyāyadesanāyaṃ anekāni payojanāni vatvā idāni attano sammāsambuddhatāya evaṃ tathāgatā buddhalīlāya anekehi pariyāyehi dhammaṃ desentīti dassento “kiṃ bahunā”tiādimāha.

Thus, having stated the many benefits of the Blessed One's teaching by alternative expressions, now, to show that the Tathāgatas, by virtue of their own perfect self-enlightenment, teach the Dhamma with the grace of a Buddha in many alternative ways, he said, "What more is there to say?" and so on.

Pāḷiyaṃ “pihā nāma yā vattamānassa atthassa patthanā”ti paccuppannavisayataṃ dassetvā puna anāgatavisayataṃ dassetuṃ “seyyataraṃ vā”tiādi vuttanti “anāgatapaccuppannatthavisayā taṇhā pihā”ti āha.

In the Pāli, having shown its domain to be the present with "Longing (pihā) is the aspiration for a present object," it is then said, "or for something better," and so on, to show its domain to be the future. Thus, he said, "Craving (taṇhā) and longing (pihā) have the future and present as their domain."

Atthanipphattipaṭipālanāti [V 0.0072] imasmiṃ vā pade pihāya evatthavasena anāgatapaccuppannatthavisayabhāvadīpanato.

'The maintenance of the accomplishment of the meaning' is so called because in this very term, the state of longing (pihā) as having the future and present as its domain is revealed by way of its meaning.

Dhammārammaṇeneva saṅgahitā “dhammārammaṇa”ntveva gahaṇaṃ gatā. Catuvīsati padānīti ettha gehasitadomanassūpavicārādīnaṃ catunnaṃ chakkānaṃ vasena catuvīsa koṭṭhāsā.

They are included by the mental object itself and have thus come to be designated as 'mental object.' Here, 'twenty-four terms' means twenty-four sections, by way of four sets of six, such as household-based dejection, investigation, and so on.

38. Sāyeva patthanākārena dhammanandītiādimāhāti ettha ayamattho – sā eva patthanākārena pavattiyā āsādipariyāyena vuttā taṇhā rūpādidhammesu nandanaṭṭhena dhammanandī. Tesaṃ eva piyāyanaṭṭhena dhammapemaṃ. Gilitvā pariniṭṭhapetvā ṭhānato dhammajjhosānanti.

38. Here, this is the meaning of 'That very thing, in the form of aspiration, is delight in phenomena, etc.': That very craving, which occurs in the form of aspiration and is spoken of by the synonym 'longing,' is 'delight in phenomena' (dhammanandī) in the sense of delighting in phenomena such as forms. In the sense of holding them dear, it is 'affection for phenomena' (dhammapemaṃ). Because it stands having swallowed and consumed them, it is 'absorption in phenomena' (dhammajjhosāna).

Imināpīti na kevalaṃ “paññā pajānanā”tiādiāveṇikapariyāyeneva vevacanaṃ vattabbaṃ, atha kho iminā ādhipateyyādisādhāraṇapariyāyenapi vevacanaṃ vattabbanti attho. Imināva nayenāti etena pariyāyavacanena[M 0.0077]. Na hi desanatthasādhanaṃ “itipi so bhagavā”tiādipāḷinayadassananti dasseti. Balanipphattigatotiādīsu dasasu tathāgatabalesu nipphattiṃ pāripūriṃ gato. Sambodhipahānantarāyadesanā visesacodanāsu visāradabhāvasaṅkhātāni cattāri ñāṇāni patto adhigatoti vesārajjappatto.

This means that the synonym should be stated not only by the unique mode of expression such as 'wisdom is discernment,' etc., but also by this common mode of expression such as sovereignty, etc. 'By this very method' means by this alternative expression. It shows that the accomplishment of the meaning of the teaching is not merely the showing of the way of the Pāli text, such as 'Indeed, the Blessed One,' etc. Regarding 'one who has attained the accomplishment of powers,' etc., it means he has attained accomplishment and fulfillment in the ten Tathāgata powers. He has attained, has reached, the four knowledges reckoned as the state of fearlessness concerning enlightenment, abandonment, the teaching of obstacles, and special challenges; thus, he has attained fearlessness.

Lobhajjhāsayādiajjhāsayaṃ visesena ativattoti ajjhāsayavītivatto. Atītahetusaṅkhepādisaṅkhepavirahitatāya asaṅkhepasaṅkhātaṃ nibbānaṃ, akuppadhammatāya guṇehi vā asaṅkhepaṃ asaṅkhyeyyaṃ gato upagatoti asaṅkhepagato. Sesaṃ suviññeyyameva. Uddheyyanti uddharitabbaṃ.

He who has especially transcended the inclination for greed, etc., is 'one who has transcended inclinations' (ajjhāsayavītivatto). Nibbāna is called 'unabbreviated' (asaṅkhepa) because it is devoid of brevity, such as the brevity of past causes, etc. And 'one who has gone to the unabbreviated' (asaṅkhepagata) means one who has gone to or approached the unabbreviated, the immeasurable (asaṅkhyeyya), by virtue of its unshakeable nature or its qualities. The rest is easily understood. 'Uddheyya' means 'what should be uplifted.'

Dhammānussatiyaṃ evaṃ attho daṭṭhabboti sambandho. Saṃsāradukkhato pāti, santena sukhena rameti cāti vā pāraṃ.

In the recollection of the Dhamma, the connection is that the meaning should be seen thus. Or, it is the 'far shore' (pāraṃ) because it protects (pāti) from the suffering of saṃsāra and delights (rameti) with peaceful happiness.

Abhūtapubbattāti anuppannapubbattā, tenassa niccataṃva vibhāveti kenaci devatopasaggādinā anupasajjanīyattā anupasaṭṭhattā.

'Because of never having been before' means because of never having arisen before; by that, it makes clear its permanence, as it were, because it is unassailable and unafflicted by any divine affliction, etc.

“Duppassa”ntipi pāḷi, duradhigamanti attho. Guṇasobhāsurabhibhāvenāti guṇehi sobhāya, sugandhibhāvena ca.

'Duppassa' is also a Pāli reading, meaning 'difficult to realize.' By 'the state of splendor of virtues and fragrance' (Guṇasobhāsurabhibhāvena) is meant by the splendor of virtues and by the quality of being fragrant.

Yathā [V 0.0073] akkhaṇavedhī puggalo sippanipphattiyā rattandhakāratimisāya acirakkhaṇālokena atisukhumampi dūragataṃ lakkhaṃ vijjhati, evaṃ ariyasāvako sīlasampattiyā atisukhumaṃ nibbānaṃ catusaccadhammaṃ ekapaṭivedheneva paṭivijjhatīti āha “sippañca sīlaṃ akkhaṇavedhitāyā”ti. Lokikanti nidassanamattaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ lokuttaradhammaolokanassāpi adhiṭṭhānabhāvato.

Just as a skilled marksman, through mastery of his craft, can strike an extremely subtle target far away even in the thick darkness of night with a momentary flash of light, so too does a noble disciple, through the perfection of virtue, penetrate the exceedingly subtle Nibbāna, the Dhamma of the Four Noble Truths, with a single penetration. Hence it is said, 'Skill and virtue are for hitting the mark in an instant.' The worldly aspect should be seen as an illustration, as it also serves as the foundation for contemplating supramundane truths.

Vevacanahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of the Application of Synonyms is concluded.

11. Paññattihāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

11. The Commentary on the Analysis of Designations

39. Bhagavato sābhāvikadhammakathāyāti attano bhāvo sabhāvo, sabhāvena nibbattā, tato vā āgatāti sābhāvikā, sā eva dhammakathāti sābhāvikadhammakathā, buddhānaṃ sāmukkaṃsikadhammakathāti attho[M 0.0078], tāya karaṇabhūtāya dhammadesanāya anaññattepi kathādesanānaṃ upacārasiddhena bhedenevaṃ vuttaṃ, avayavasamudāyavibhāgena vā. Tenāha “kā ca pakatikathāya desanā? Cattāri saccānī”ti. Idañhi atthassa desanāya abhedopacāraṃ katvā vuttaṃ. Tassā desanāya paññāpanā. Ayaṃ paññattihāroti saṅkhepeneva paññattihārassa sarūpamāha. Sāti yathāvuttadesanā. Tathā tathāti yathā yathā saccāni desetabbāni, tathā tathā. Kathañcetāni desetabbāni? Pariññeyyādippakārena. Yathādhippetanti adhippetānurūpaṃ, bodhaneyyabandhavānaṃ bodhanādhippāyānukūlanti attho. Atthanti desetabbatthaṃ, dukkhādiatthameva vā. Nikkhipatīti patiṭṭhāpeti. Yato “cattāro suttanikkhepā”tiādi (ma. ni. aṭṭha. 1.mūlapariyāyasuttavaṇṇanā) aṭṭhakathāsu vuccati.

39. The Blessed One’s natural discourse on the Dhamma: one’s own nature is intrinsic nature (sabhāva); what is produced from or comes from that intrinsic nature is natural (sābhāvikā); that itself is the discourse on the Dhamma, thus 'natural discourse on the Dhamma.' The meaning is the Buddhas' supreme discourse on the Dhamma. By that Dhamma-teaching, which is the instrument, although the talk and the teaching are not different, it is said thus by a distinction established through figurative usage, or by a division of part and whole. Therefore, it is said, 'And what is the natural teaching? The Four Truths.' This is stated by making a figurative identification of the teaching with its meaning. The exposition of that teaching. 'This is the application of designations'—thus, he briefly states the nature of the application of designations. 'That' refers to the teaching as described. 'In such and such a way' means in whatever way the truths are to be taught, in that way. And how are they to be taught? In the manner of what is to be fully understood, etc. 'As intended' means in conformity with what is intended; the meaning is that it is suitable for the intention of awakening those who are to be awakened. 'The meaning' means the meaning to be taught, or simply the meaning of suffering, etc. 'Establishes' (nikkhipati) means 'sets forth' (patiṭṭhāpeti). For it is said in the commentaries, 'There are four establishments of suttas,' etc.

Tatthāti nikkhepadesanāyanti attho. Maggapakkhiyāti dukkhasaccato bahikatāti adhippāyo.

'Therein' means in the teaching by way of establishment. 'Belonging to the path' means excluded from the truth of suffering.

Yasmiṃ ṭhāneti yasmiṃ bhavādisaṅkhāte ṭhāne. Yathāvuttā desanāti caturāhārapaṭibaddharāgādimukhena vaṭṭadīpanī vuttappakārā desanā.

'In whatever place' means in the place designated as existence, etc. 'The teaching as described' means the teaching that illuminates the round of existence, explained in the manner stated, by way of craving and so on, which are connected with the four nutriments.

41. Teparivaṭṭavasenāti etthāpi “saccesū”ti yojetabbaṃ. Pariññāpaññattīti āhāti [V 0.0074] sambandho. Ajjhattarato, samāhitoti padadvayena samādhānavisiṭṭhaṃ ajjhattaratatābhāvanaṃ dīpeti gocarajjhattatādīpanato. Kevalo hi ajjhattasaddo ajjhattajjhattagocarajjhattesupi vattati. Ajjhattaratatāvisiṭṭhañca samādhānaṃ sātisayaṃ cittaṭṭhitiṃ dīpetīti imamatthaṃ dasseti “samādhānavisiṭṭhassā”tiādinā.

41. 'By way of the three rounds'—here too, 'in the truths' should be connected. The connection is: he says, 'designation of full understanding.' By the two terms 'delighting inwardly' and 'concentrated,' he reveals the cultivation of delighting inwardly, distinguished by concentration, by indicating inward focus as its object, etc. Indeed, the word 'ajjhatta' alone applies to the internal, what pertains to oneself, and what has an internal object. And concentration distinguished by inward delight signifies a particularly excellent state of mind; he shows this meaning with 'of one distinguished by concentration,' etc.

Āsajjanaṭṭhenāti āsaṅganaṭṭhena. Tathā dassananti atathābhūtassāpi bhabbarūpassa viya attano vidaṃsanaṃ. Alakkhikoti vilakkhiko.

By the meaning 'assailing' is meant by the meaning 'clinging.' 'Displaying thus' is its own showing of itself as if it were of a suitable form, even though it is not so. 'Inauspicious' (alakkhiko) means 'unseemly' (vilakkhiko).

Kāmānanti kāmāvacaradhammānaṃ. Rūpānanti rūpāvacaradhammānaṃ. Nissaraṇanti kāmānaṃ rūpāvacaradhammā nissaraṇaṃ, tesaṃ arūpāvacaradhammā nissaraṇaṃ. Evaṃ taṃsabhāvānanti sauttarasabhāvānaṃ. Tathāti yathā saṅkhatadhammānaṃ nissaraṇabhāvato, kilesasamucchedakassa ariyamaggassa ārammaṇabhāvato ca [M 0.0079] attheva asaṅkhatā dhātu, tathā vuccamānenāpi kāraṇena attheva asaṅkhatā dhātūti dasseti. Katthaci visayeti asaṅkhatadhātuṃ sandhāya vadati. Aviparītatthoti bhūtattho. “Yato kho bho ayaṃ attā pañcahi kāmaguṇehi samappito samaṅgībhūto paricāreti, ettāvatā kho bho ayaṃ attā paramadiṭṭhadhammanibbānappatto hoti (dī. ni. 1.94), sapakaṭṭhanibbānabhāvino”ti ca evamādīsu upacāravuttisabbhāvato. Yathā taṃ sīhasaddoti yathā “sīho māṇavako”tiādinā māṇavakādīsu upacāravuttinā vattamāno migarāje bhūtatthavisaye diṭṭho, evaṃ nibbānasaddopi kāmaguṇarūpajjhānasamaṅgitāsu upacāravuttiyā vattamāno katthaci visaye aviparītattho. Yattha ca visaye aviparītattho, sā asaṅkhatā dhātu. Hatthatale saāmalakaṃ viya ñeyyaṃ paccakkhato passantassa ekappamāṇassa satthuvacanamevettha pamāṇanti dassento “kiṃ vā etāya yutticintāyā”tiādimāha. “Paṭiññātassa atthassa siddhiyā pakāsanāpaññattī”ti nigamaṃ sandhāyāhāti.

'Of sensual pleasures' means of sense-sphere phenomena. 'Of forms' means of form-sphere phenomena. 'Escape' means that for sense-sphere phenomena, form-sphere phenomena are the escape; for those, formless-sphere phenomena are the escape. Likewise, 'of those states' means of states that have a superior. 'Thus'—just as there is indeed an unconditioned element, because it is the escape for conditioned phenomena and the object for the noble path that eradicates defilements, so too, he shows that by the reason being stated, the unconditioned element indeed exists. 'In a certain sphere'—he speaks with reference to the unconditioned element. 'Non-perverted meaning' means the true meaning. Because of the existence of figurative usage in such statements as, 'When, friends, this self is endowed and furnished with the five strands of sensual pleasure and indulges in them, at that point, friends, this self has attained the highest Nibbāna visible in this life,' which refers to what has the nature of a supreme Nibbāna. Just as the word 'lion,' when used figuratively for a young man and so on, as in 'the young man is a lion,' is seen in its true meaning in the sphere of the king of beasts, so too the word 'Nibbāna,' when used figuratively for being endowed with sensual pleasures or form-sphere jhānas, has a non-perverted meaning in a certain sphere. And that sphere where it has a non-perverted meaning is the unconditioned element. Showing that here the word of the Teacher alone is the authority—who is the sole measure and sees the knowable directly like a myrobalan fruit on the palm—he says, 'Or what is the use of this logical thinking?' etc. He speaks with reference to the conclusion: 'A designation that elucidates, for the establishment of the asserted meaning.'

Paññattihāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of Designations is concluded.

12. Otaraṇahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

12. Commentary on the Analysis of Penetration

42. Indriyehīti [V 0.0075] karaṇe karaṇavacanaṃ “maggena gacchatī”tiādīsu viya, “pharasunā chindatī”ti evamādīsu viya ca. Otaraṇāti anuppavesanā.

42. 'By the faculties' (indriyehīti): the instrumental case is used in the sense of the instrument, as in 'one goes by the path,' etc., and as in 'one cuts with an axe,' etc. 'Penetration' (otaraṇa) means entering into (anuppavesanā).

Paññākkhandhe saṅgaṇhanavasena sammāsaṅkappo viyāti yojanā. Adhicittaanuyuttānaṃ saddahanussahanupaṭṭhānasamādahanehi saddhādīsu upakarontesu eva paññā dassanakiccaṃ sādhetīti dassento “saddhā..pe… vuttānī”ti āha. No ca bhavaṅgāti tesaṃ saṅkhārānaṃ pavattikāraṇatābhāvaṃ dasseti.

The connection is that right intention is, as it were, for the sake of inclusion in the aggregate of wisdom. Showing that for those devoted to the higher mind, wisdom accomplishes the task of seeing only when faith and the other faculties are assisting by way of belief, exertion, establishment, and concentration, he said, 'faith... etc., are stated.' 'And not the life-continuum' shows that it is not a cause for the arising of those formations.

43. Tathā vuttoti “nissayo”ti vutto. Cetanāsīsena taṇhaṃ eva vadati cetanāsahacaraṇato.

43. 'Stated thus' means it is called 'support.' With volition as its head, he speaks of craving itself, because it accompanies volition.

Rattassāti [M 0.0080] maggena asamucchinnarāgassa. Yena puggalo “ratto”ti vuccati, tassa rāgassa sambandhinī sukhā vedanā vuttā tattha tassa anusayanato. Tenāha “sukhāya…pe… vutta”nti. Esa nayo sesesupi. Tenāha “tathā”tiādi.

'Of one who is attached' means of one whose passion has not been cut off by the path. The pleasant feeling connected with that passion, by which a person is called 'attached,' is mentioned there because of its underlying tendency in him. Therefore, it is said, 'For the pleasant... and so on... is said.' This method applies to the rest as well. Therefore, it is said, 'similarly,' and so on.

Tāni eva indriyānīti sukhasomanassupekkhindriyāni. “Saṅkhārapariyāpannānī”ti vacanaṃ sandhāyāha “idha vedanāsīsena cetanā vuttā”ti. Na hi vedanā saṅkhārakkhandhapariyāpannā hoti. Taṇhāya, diṭṭhiyāti ca upayoge karaṇavacananti dassento “taṇhāyā”tiādimāha. Idāni upayogavaseneva “taṇhāyā”tiādīnaṃ atthaṃ dassetuṃ “yathā vā”tiādimāha. Tattha sesadhammānanti taṇhāvajjitaavisiṭṭhadhammānaṃ. Taṇhāya nissayabhāveti yadā taṇhā tesaṃ nissayo hoti.

'Those very faculties' means the faculties of pleasure, joy, and equanimity. With reference to the statement 'included in formations,' he says, 'Here, volition is stated under the heading of feeling,' for feeling is not included in the aggregate of formations. To show that 'by craving' and 'by view' are instrumental terms in usage, he says, 'by craving,' and so on. Now, to show the meaning of 'by craving,' and so on, simply by way of usage, he says, 'or as,' and so on. There, 'of remaining phenomena' means of the undifferentiated phenomena apart from craving. 'Craving being the support' means when craving is their support.

Taṇhāya sesadhammānaṃ paccayabhāveti yadā sesadhammā taṇhāpaccayā honti. “Karajakāyasannissitā”ti iminā vedanādikkhandhattayanissitāpi gahitā kāyappassaddhibhāvato. Kāraṇabhāvanti paramparahetubhāvaṃ. Taṇhādiṭṭhiupayenāti diṭṭhiupayena ca diṭṭhisahagatataṇhāupayena ca.

'Craving being the condition for the remaining phenomena' means when the remaining phenomena are conditioned by craving. By 'dependent on the physical body,' those phenomena dependent on the three aggregates beginning with feeling are also included, because of the tranquility of the body. 'The state of being a cause' means the state of being a successive cause. 'By means of craving and view' means by the means of view and by the means of craving associated with view.

“Āgatīti [V 0.0076] idhāgati, gatīti peccabhavo”ti padadvayena vuttamevatthaṃ pākaṭataraṃ kātuṃ pāḷiyaṃ “āgatigatīpi na bhavantī”ti vuttaṃ. Idha huranti dvārārammaṇadhammā dassitā āsannadūrabhāvehi dvārārammaṇehi vinivattetvā gahitattā. Idha dvārappavattadhammā “ubhayamantarenā”ti padassa atthabhāvena vuttā. Catubyūhahāre pana anindriyabaddharūpadhammā tathā vuttā. Kāraṇabhūtena anantarapaccayabhūtena, upanissayapaccayabhūtena ca. Ye dhammā upādāya “attā”ti samaññā, tesaṃ viññāṇādidhammānaṃ abhāvena anuppādadhammataṃ āpāditattāti attho. “Anissitassa calitaṃ natthī”tiādinā paṭilomato paccayabhāvo dassitoti dassento pāḷiyaṃ “esevanto dukkhassāti paṭiccasamuppādo”ti vatvā nanu “avijjāpaccayā saṅkhārā”tiādiko paṭiccasamuppādoti codanaṃ sandhāya yathāvuttassa paṭiccasamuppādabhāvaṃ dassetuṃ “so duvidho”tiādinā lokiyalokuttaravasena paṭiccasamuppādo vibhatto. Tadatthatāyāti vītarāgavimuttiatthatāya. Tabbhāvanti lokuttarapaṭiccasamuppādabhāvaṃ.

'Coming means arriving here, going means the next existence'—to make the meaning stated by these two terms clearer, it is said in the Pāli, 'There is neither coming nor going.' Here, `huranti` refers to phenomena of the sense-doors and their objects, which are shown as having been grasped by turning away from sense-doors and objects due to their proximity or distance. Here, phenomena occurring at the sense-doors are stated as the meaning of the term 'between both.' In the Fourfold Exposition, however, form-phenomena not bound to the faculties are similarly described. By that which is a cause, an immediate condition, and a decisive support condition. The meaning is that because of the absence of those phenomena such as consciousness and so on, on account of which there is the designation 'self' through clinging, their state of non-arising is brought about. By stating, 'For the unattached, there is no wavering,' etc., conditionality in reverse order is shown. To clarify this, after stating in the Pāli, 'This is the end of suffering—dependent origination,' then, in response to the objection as to whether dependent origination is that which begins with 'with ignorance as condition, formations arise,' etc., to show that what has been stated is indeed dependent origination, it is divided into two: mundane and supramundane. 'For the sake of that purpose' means for the purpose of liberation through dispassion. 'That state' refers to the state of supramundane dependent origination.

Otaraṇahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Penetration is concluded.

13. Sodhanahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

13. Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Purification

45. Sodheti [M 0.0081] nāmāti pucchitamatthaṃ hatthatale ṭhapitaāmalakaṃ viya paccakkhato dassento niggumbaṃ nijjaṭaṃ katvā vivaranto tabbisayaaññāṇasaṃsayādimalāpanayanena sodheti, evaṃ sodhento ca padassa atthena abhedopacāraṃ katvā “padaṃ sodheti”cceva vuccati. Tenāha “padaṃ sodheti nāmā”ti. Pucchāya vissajjanamevettha sodhananti āha “tadatthassa vissajjanato”ti. Ārabhīyatīti ārambho, desanāya pakāsiyamāno attho. Tenāha “na tāva…pe… pabodhitattā”ti. Idha sodhanaṃ nāma paṭicchannarūpassa atthassa desanānubhāvena vivaṭabhāvakaraṇanti tamatthaṃ upamāya vibhāvetuṃ “aññāṇapakkhandāna”ntiādi vuttaṃ.

45. 'What is called purifying?' It purifies by revealing the meaning that has been questioned, making it directly visible as if placing an āmalaka fruit in the palm of the hand, freeing it from obscurity and entanglement, and removing the defilements of ignorance, doubt, and so on, concerning that subject. And thus, when purifying, by making a figurative identification of the word with its meaning, it is said, 'one purifies the word.' Therefore, it is said, 'What is called purifying a word?' Here, purification is simply the answering of the question; hence, it is said, 'by answering its meaning.' `Ārambha` (commencement) means 'it is begun,' the meaning being clarified by the teaching. Therefore, it is said, 'not yet... and so on... because it has been awakened.' Here, purification means making the concealed nature of a meaning manifest through the power of the teaching. To illustrate that meaning by way of analogy, it is said, 'the assault of ignorance,' and so on.

Sodhanahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Purification is concluded.

14. Adhiṭṭhānahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

14. Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Determination

46. Dhārayitabbāti [V 0.0077] upadhāretabbā, upalakkhitabbāti attho. Vuttameva “sāmaññavisesakappanāya vohārabhāvena anavaṭṭhānato”tiādinā (netti. aṭṭha. 14).

46. 'To be borne' means 'to be kept in mind' or 'to be noted'—this is the meaning. As it was stated: 'Due to the nature of conventional usage for the purpose of positing common and distinctive characteristics, and because of the absence of fixed determination,' and so on (Netti. Aṭṭha. 14).

Taṃ taṃ phalanti nirayādiṃ taṃ taṃ phalaṃ. Añcitāti gatā. Yonīti ekajāti. Samānavasena missībhavati etāyāti hi yoni, upapatti. Peccāti maritvā. Ussannatāyāti vitakkabahulatāya. “Ussannattā”tipi vadanti. Sassatādīhi vā ussannattā. Asurajātiyā nibbattāpanako asurajātinibbattanako.

'That which fruits thus and thus' means the various fruits such as hell. 'Añcitā' means 'gone.' 'Yoni' means 'a single birth.' Indeed, because one becomes mixed together in it in a similar way, it is a `yoni` (womb), an `upapatti` (rebirth). 'Pecca' means 'having died.' 'Ussannatā' means 'due to an abundance of thought.' Some also say 'ussannattā.' Or, due to being excessive through eternalism, and so on. One who causes arising in the asura birth is an 'asura-birth-producer.'

Saṅkhāti paññā. Paññāpadhānā ca bhāvanāti āha “paṭisaṅkhāya paṭipakkhabhāvanāyā”ti.

'Saṅkhā' means wisdom. And since development has wisdom as its principal element, it is said, 'by developing the counterpart through reflection.'

47. Patthaṭabhāvena pathavī. Sabhāvadhāraṇaṭṭhena, nissattanijjīvaṭṭhena ca dhātu. Āpīyati, appāyatīti vā āpo. Tejanavasena tikkhatāvasena, dahanavasena vā tejo. Vāyanavasena vegagamanavasena, samudīraṇavasena [M 0.0082] vā vāyo. Vīsati ākārāti kesādayo vīsati koṭṭhāsā, pakārā vā. Kakkhaḷalakkhaṇādhikatāya kesādī kakkhaḷalakkhaṇā vuttā.

47. Earth (pathavī) is so called by reason of its expansive nature. By its inherent characteristic of sustaining, and by being inanimate and lifeless, it is an element (dhātu). Water (āpo) is so called because it permeates, or it coheres. Fire (tejo) is characterized by the property of heat, the property of sharpness, or the property of burning. Wind (vāyo) is characterized by the property of movement, the property of rapid motion, or the property of impelling. The 'twenty aspects' (vīsati ākārā) means the twenty parts beginning with hair, or the twenty categories. Hair and so on are said to have the characteristic of hardness due to the predominance of the characteristic of hardness.

Pāṭiyekko pathavīdhātukoṭṭhāsoti pathavīkoṭṭhāsamatto, attasuññadhammamattoti attho. Santappatīti ettha sarīrapakatimatikkamitvā uṇhabhāvo santāpo, sarīradahanavasena pavatto mahādāho, ayametesaṃ viseso. Yena ca jīrīyatīti ekāhikādijarārogena jarīyatīti ca attho yujjati. “Satavāraṃ tāpetvā tāpetvā udake pakkhipitvā uddhaṭasappi satadhotasappī”ti vadanti. Rasarudhiramaṃsamedanhāruaṭṭhiaṭṭhimiñjā rasādayo. Keci nhāruṃ apanetvā sukkaṃ sattamaṃ dhātuṃ vadanti. Vivekanti visuṃbhāvaṃ, visadisabhāvanti attho. Vatthusaṅkhāto hi āhāro pariṇāmaṃ gacchanto pāṇabhakkhagahaṇipadaniya muttakarīsabhāvehi viya attanāpi visadisarasasaṅkhātaṃ visuṃbhāvaṃ nibbattento tabbhāvaṃ gacchatīti vuccati, tathā rasādayopi rudhirādikoṭṭhāsaṃ. Tenāha “rasādibhāvena vivekaṃ gacchatī”ti.

'An individual part of the earth element' is merely a portion of the earth element, meaning it is merely a phenomenon void of self. `Santappati` (is heated) here refers to the heat exceeding the body's natural warmth, which is internal burning (`santāpa`); the great fever (`mahādāha`) is that which proceeds by way of burning the body; this is their distinction. And 'that by which one ages' means one ages due to the disease of aging, such as a one-day fever, and so on; this meaning fits. They say, 'Ghee that has been heated a hundred times, placed in water, and then removed is called hundred-washed ghee.' Nutriment, blood, flesh, fat, sinews, bones, and bone marrow are the 'nutriment, etc.' Some exclude sinews and speak of semen as the seventh element. `Viveka` (separation) means distinctness, the state of being dissimilar—this is the meaning. For food, designated as the basis, undergoes transformation and, like substances such as excrement and urine, produces a distinct state designated as dissimilar nutriment—thus it is said to reach that state. Similarly, nutriment, etc., become portions of blood, etc. Hence it is said, 'It attains separation through the state of nutriment, etc.'

Sabhāvalakkhaṇatoti asucibhāvena lakkhitabbato.

'From its own characteristic' means because it is to be characterized by its nature of impurity.

48. Yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇanti [V 0.0078] rasitabboti raso, paṭivijjhitabbo sabhāvo, attano raso saraso, yāthāvo saraso, yāthāvasaraso yāthāvasaraso eva lakkhitabbattā lakkhaṇanti yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇaṃ. Atha vā yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇanti aviparītaṃ attano pavattisaṅkhātaṃ kiccañceva pīḷanasaṅkhātaṃ lakkhaṇañca. “Idaṃ kiccaṃ, idaṃ lakkhaṇa”nti avijjāhetu ñātuṃ na sakkoti, tabbisayañāṇuppattiṃ nivārentī chādetvā pariyonandhitvā tiṭṭhatīti vuttā. Tena vuttaṃ “jānituṃ paṭivijjhituṃ na detī”ti. Tayidamassā kiccanti kiccato kathitā. Kathitāti ca vuttā, yato ca avijjā asampaṭivedharasāti vuccati. Jāyati etthāti jāti, uppattiṭṭhānaṃ. Yadipi nirodhamagge avijjā ārammaṇaṃ na karoti, te pana jānitukāmassa tappaṭicchādanavasena anirodhamaggesu nirodhamaggaggahaṇassa kāraṇabhāvena pavattamānā tattha uppajjatīti vuccati, tesampi avijjāya uppattiṭṭhānatā hoti, itaresaṃ ārammaṇabhāvena cāti.

48. Yāthāvasarasalakkhaṇa (the characteristic of true essence) means that rasa (essence) is 'to be savored,' its intrinsic nature is 'to be penetrated.' Its own essence is its essence; its true essence is its essence. Because it is to be characterized as truly its own essence, it is the characteristic of its true essence. Alternatively, the characteristic of true essence means its function, designated as its own activity, and its characteristic, designated as oppression, are not contrary. Due to ignorance, one cannot know, 'This is the function, this is the characteristic.' It stands obstructing the arising of knowledge concerning that object, concealing and enveloping it—thus it is said. Therefore, it is stated, 'It does not allow one to know or penetrate.' This is its function, spoken of in terms of its function. And it is said to be spoken of because ignorance is called 'that which has the essence of non-comprehension.' Jāti (birth) means 'it arises here,' the place of arising. Although ignorance does not take the path to cessation as its object, for one who wishes to know it, it is said to arise there by way of concealing that and by being the cause for grasping the path to cessation in non-cessation paths. Thus, for them too, ignorance becomes the place of arising, and for others, by way of being an object.

Atthānatthanti [M 0.0083] hitāhitaṃ. Sammohavinodaniyaṃ pana “atthattha”nti (vibha. aṭṭha. 226) vuttaṃ, tattha attho eva atthatthoti atthassa aviparītatādassanatthaṃ dutiyena atthasaddena visesanaṃ. Na hi ñāṇaṃ anatthe “attho”ti gaṇhātīti. Kāraṇākāraṇanti etthāpi evaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ. Atthatthanti vā āmeḍitavacanaṃ sabbesaṃ atthānaṃ pākaṭakaraṇabhāvappakāsanatthaṃ, phalaṃ phalanti attho, hitapariyāyepi eseva nayo. Nti atthānatthādikaṃ. Ākāranti atthādikāraṇameva.

Atthānattha (benefit and harm) means what is beneficial and unbeneficial. In the Sammohavinodanī, however, it is said, 'atthattha' (Vibhanga Aṭṭhakathā 226). There, 'atthattha' means benefit itself, the second word 'attha' being a qualifier to show the non-contrary nature of the benefit. For knowledge does not grasp what is unbeneficial as 'benefit'. Similarly, in the case of cause and non-cause, it should also be understood thus. Alternatively, 'atthattha' is a reiterated expression for the purpose of revealing the nature of making all benefits manifest; 'phalaṃ phalaṃ' means result, and the same method applies in the synonym of 'beneficial'. 'Nti' refers to benefit and harm, etc. Ākāra (aspect) is simply the cause of benefit, etc.

Paṭividdhassa puna avekkhanā paccavekkhaṇā. Ducintitacintitādilakkhaṇassa bālassa bhāvo bālyaṃ. Sampajānātīti samaṃ pakārehi jānāti. Balavamoho pamoho. Samantato mohanaṃ sammoho. Duggatigāmikammassa visesapaccayattā avindiyaṃ. Vindatīti labhati. Anavajjadhammānaṃ vijjā viya visesapaccayo na hotīti vindiyaṃ na vindati. Ayaṃ avijjāya vemattatāti ayaṃ “dukkhe aññāṇa”ntiādinā kiccajātilakkhaṇehi vutto avijjāya aviseso. Vijjātiādīnaṃ vuttanayānasārena attho veditabbo.

For one who has penetrated, looking again is reflection (paccavekkhaṇā). The state of a fool (bāla), characterized by ill-conceived thoughts and the like, is folly (bālya). 'Sampajānāti' means 'he knows rightly in various ways'. Strong delusion is great bewilderment (pamoho). Delusion from all sides is utter confusion (sammoho). Because it is a special condition for kamma leading to a bad destination, it is called avindiyaṃ (not leading to what is to be obtained). Vindati means 'obtains'. It is not a special condition for blameless states, as knowledge is; thus, one does not obtain (na vindati) what is to be obtained (vindiyaṃ). This is the diversity of ignorance. This, 'ignorance of suffering,' etc., stated by way of function, birth, and characteristic, is the non-distinction of ignorance. The meaning of 'knowledge' and the like should be understood according to the essence of the method stated.

Pāsāṇasakkharavālikāvirahitā bhūmi saṇhāti “saṇhaṭṭhenā”ti vuttaṃ.

Ground devoid of rocks, gravel, and sand is smooth—thus it is said, 'in the sense of smoothness'.

Tattakameva [V 0.0079] kālanti pañcakappasatāni. Vibhūtaṃ samattikkantaṃ rūpasaññāsaṅkhātaṃ rūpaṃ etāyāti vibhūtarūpaṃ, samāpattinti padattho. Na hi kāci arūpasamāpatti rūpasaññāsahagatā pavattīti. Nirodhasamāpattiyaṃ vattabbameva natthi, tattha nevasaññānāsaññāyatanasamāpattiyā visuṃ gahitattā vuttaṃ “sesāruppasamāpattiyo”ti.

‘That much time’ means five hundred aeons. That by which form, designated as form-perception, has been surpassed and transcended, is vibhūtarūpa (having transcended form); this is the meaning of the word samāpatti (attainment). For no formless attainment occurs accompanied by the perception of form. Regarding the attainment of cessation, there is nothing to be said, because there the attainment of the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception is taken separately; hence, it is said, 'the remaining formless attainments'.

Damathaṃ anupagacchanto duṭṭhasso khaluṅkasso. Uttaridamathāyāti ariyamaggadamathāya.

Not undergoing taming is a vicious horse, an unruly horse. 'For the higher taming' means for the taming by the Noble Path.

Itaroti dukkhāpaṭipado khippābhiñño, sukhāpaṭipado ca khippābhiñño. Ubhayatobhāgehīti rūpakāyanāmakāyabhāgehi. Ubhayatoti vikkhambhanasamucchedavimuttivasena.

'The other' refers to one with painful practice and quick direct knowledge, and one with pleasant practice and quick direct knowledge. 'By both parts' (ubhayatobhāgehi) means by the parts of the form-body and the name-body. 'By both' (ubhayato) means by way of liberation through suppression and through eradication.

Anekopīti [M 0.0084] sabhāvena anekopi. Ekasaddābhidheyyatāyāti sāmaññasaddābhidheyyatāya.

'Though many' (anekopi) means 'many by nature'. 'By the designation of a single term' (ekasaddābhidheyyatāya) means 'by the designation of a common term'.

Adhiṭṭhānahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Determination is concluded.

15. Parikkhārahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

15. Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Requisites

49. “Hinotī”ti padassa atthaṃ dassento “kāraṇabhāvaṃ gacchatī”ti āha anekatthattā dhātūnaṃ. Etīti āgacchati, uppajjatīti attho.

49. Showing the meaning of the word 'hinoti', he says, 'it becomes a cause', because verbal roots have many meanings. 'Eti' means 'it comes,' which is to say, 'it arises.'

Avijjāyapi hetubhāveti ettha avijjā anantarāya avijjāya anantarasamanantarūpanissayanatthivigatāsevanapaccayehi, anantarāya pana sahajātāya sahajātaaññamaññanissayasampayuttaatthiavigatahetupaccayehi, asahajātāya upanissayakoṭiyā eva paccayo hotīti veditabbaṃ. Attano phalaṃ karotīti kāraṇanti āha “kāraṇabhāvo ca phalāpekkhāyā”ti.

Here, in 'ignorance also being a cause', it should be understood that ignorance is a condition for the immediately following ignorance by way of immediacy, contiguity, decisive support, absence, disappearance, and repetition conditions. But for the immediately following ignorance that is co-nascent, it is a condition by way of co-nascence, mutuality, dependence, association, presence, non-disappearance, and root conditions. For that which is not co-nascent, it is a condition only in the category of decisive support. Because it produces its own result, it is a cause; thus he said: 'The state of being a cause is also with respect to the result.'

Nibbattiattho phalattho phalasaṅkhāto attho.

The meaning of production is the meaning of result, the meaning designated as result.

Yo sabhāvoti puññādiabhisaṅkhārānaṃ yo abhisaṅkharaṇasabhāvo, so hetu. Sesapadesūti viññāṇādipadesu. Yathāvuttappabhedoti “asādhāraṇalakkhaṇo hetū”tiādinā vuttappabhedo. Yo koci [V 0.0080] paccayoti janakādibhedaṃ yaṃ kiñci kāraṇaṃ. Abhisaṅkharaṇatoti paccakkhato, paramparāya ca nibbattanato.

'What is the intrinsic nature' means that the volitionally forming nature of volitional formations such as meritorious ones, etc., is the cause. 'In the remaining terms' means in terms such as consciousness, etc. 'The classification as stated' means the classification stated beginning with 'the cause has an uncommon characteristic'. 'Whatever condition' means any cause whatsoever, with its divisions such as generative, etc. 'Because of volitionally forming' means because of producing both directly and indirectly.

Parikkhārahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Requisites is concluded.

16. Samāropanahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā

16. Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Superimposition

50. Suttena gahiteti sutte vutte. Padaṭṭhānaggahaṇaṃ adhiṭṭhānavisayadassanatthaṃ, vevacanaggahaṇaṃ adhivacanavibhāgadassanatthanti yojanā. Visayādhiṭṭhānabhāvatoti visayasaṅkhātapavattiṭṭhānabhāvato. Vanīyatīti bhajīyati. Vanatīti bhajati sevati. Vanuteti yācati, patthetīti attho[M 0.0085]. Pañca kāmaguṇā kāmataṇhāya kāraṇaṃ hoti ārammaṇapaccayatāya. Nimittaggāho anubyañjanaggāhassa kāraṇaṃ hoti upanissayatāyāti evaṃ sesesupi yathārahaṃ kāraṇatā vattabbā.

50. 'Taken by the Sutta' (suttena gahita) means 'stated in the Sutta.' The taking of the basis is for the purpose of showing the object of determination, and the taking of synonyms is for the purpose of showing the division of terms; this is the connection. 'Because of being the basis of the object' (visayādhiṭṭhānabhāvato) means because of being the basis for the occurrence designated as the object. 'Vanīyati' means 'it is partaken of.' 'Vanati' means 'one partakes of or resorts to it.' 'Vanuteti' means 'one asks for, or desires it;' that is the meaning. The five strands of sensual pleasure are a cause for sensual craving by way of being an object condition. The grasping of the sign is a cause for grasping the details by way of being a decisive support condition. Similarly, the causal relation should be stated as appropriate in the remaining cases.

51. “Kāye kāyānupassī viharāhī”tiādīsu yaṃ vattabbaṃ, taṃ heṭṭhā lakkhaṇahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanāyaṃ (netti. aṭṭha. 23) vuttanayeneva veditabbaṃ. Ayaṃ pana viseso – rūpadhammapariññāyāti rūpūpikaviññāṇaṭṭhitipariññāya.

51. In passages such as 'Dwell contemplating the body in the body,' what should be said is to be understood according to the method stated earlier in the commentary on the analysis of the characteristic mode (Netti. Aṭṭhakathā. 23). However, this is the distinction: 'for the full understanding of material phenomena' means for the full understanding of the station of consciousness dependent on form.

“Dukkha”nti passantī sā vedanānupassanāti yojetabbaṃ. Vedanāhetupariññāyāti phassapariññāya. “Vedanāvasenā”ti padena attanā uppāditadukkhavasena. Vedanāpariññāyāti vedanūpikaviññāṇaṭṭhitipariññāya. Niccābhinivesapaṭipakkhato aniccānupassanāyāti adhippāyo. Niccasaññānimittassāti niccasaññāhetukassa. Saññāpariññāyāti saññūpikaviññāṇaṭṭhitipariññāya. Paṭhamamaggavajjhattā agatigamanassa vuttaṃ “diṭṭhābhinivesassa…pe… agatigamanassa cā”ti.

She who sees 'suffering'—that is the contemplation of feeling; thus it should be connected. 'For the full understanding of the cause of feeling' means for the full understanding of contact. By the word 'by means of feeling' (vedanāvasena) is meant by way of the suffering produced by oneself. 'For the full understanding of feeling' means for the full understanding of the station of consciousness dependent on feeling. The intention is 'the contemplation of impermanence' because of its opposition to the adherence to permanence. 'Of the sign of the perception of permanence' means 'of that which is caused by the perception of permanence.' 'For the full understanding of perception' means for the full understanding of the station of consciousness dependent on perception. Because going the wrong way is to be avoided by the first path, it is said, 'of adherence to views... and of going the wrong way'.

Saṅkhārapariññāyāti saṅkhārūpikaviññāṇaṭṭhitipariññāya.

'For the full understanding of formations' means for the full understanding of the station of consciousness dependent on formations.

Samāropanahāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Commentary on the Analysis of the Mode of Superimposition is concluded.

Niṭṭhitā ca hāravibhaṅgavaṇṇanā.

And the Commentary on the Analysis of Modes is concluded.

1. Desanāhārasampātavaṇṇanā

1. Commentary on the Confluence of Teaching Modes

“Eva”ntiādi [V 0.0081] hārasampātadesanāya sambandhadassanaṃ. Tattha purimena upamādvayena suparikammakatamaṇikoṭṭimasadisī, suviracitajambunadābharaṇasadisī ca pāḷi. Tattha katanānāvaṇṇapupphūpahārasadisī, vividharaṃsijālāsamujjalabaddhanānāratanāvalisadisī ca hāravibhaṅgadesanāti dasseti. Pacchimena tassa paṇītamahārahe jaṭāhi saddhiṃ dukkaratarataṃ dīpeti. Yāyaṃ gāthā vuttāti yojanā.

'Evaṃ,' and so on, shows the connection to the teaching of the confluence of modes. Therein, by the first pair of similes, the Pāli is like a well-prepared gem-paved floor and a finely crafted golden ornament. Therein, it shows that the exposition of the analysis of modes is like an offering of various colored flowers and a radiant, interwoven garland of various jewels. The latter illustrates its greater difficulty, along with its preciousness and great value, together with its intricacy. This is the construing of the verse that was spoken.

52. Yasmāyaṃ hāravibhaṅgavāro nappayojeti yathāvuttena kāraṇena, tasmā sā hāravibhaṅgavārassa ādimhi na paccāmaṭṭhāti adhippāyo[M 0.0086]. Hārasampātavāro pana taṃ payojetīti yasmā pana hārasampātavāro taṃ gāthaṃ payojeti yathāvutteneva kāraṇena, tasmā “soḷasa…pe… āhā”ti āha. Yojanānayadassananti yojanāya nayadassanaṃ.

52. Because this section on the analysis of modes does not apply for the stated reason, therefore, that verse is not taken up at the beginning of the section on the analysis of modes—such is the intention. However, the section on the confluence of modes does apply it. Because the section on the confluence of modes applies that verse for the stated reason, therefore, it says: 'Sixteen... up to... he said.' Showing the method of connection.

Tenāti “taṃ maccuno pada”nti vacanena. Sabbaṃ vipallāsanti dvādasavidhampi vipallāsaṃ. Sāmaññassa…pe… voharīyati yattha patiṭṭhitaṃ sāmaññaṃ, so viseso. Atthato pana saññādayo eva rūpādivisayaṃ viparītākārena gaṇhante vipallāsoti dassento “saññāvipallāso”tiādimāha.

'By that' (tena) means by the saying 'that is the footprint of Death.' 'All distortions' means all twelve kinds of distortions. 'Of commonality... it is spoken of where commonality is established; that is the distinction.' In meaning, however, showing that 'distortion' is perception and so on grasping objects such as form in a distorted manner, he says, 'perceptual distortion,' and so on.

Indajālādivasena maṇiādiākārena upaṭṭhahante upādānakkhandhapañcake ahaṃmamādikāraṇatāya niruttinayena “attā”ti vuccamāno taṃbuddhivohārappavattinimittatāya attabhāvo sukhādīnaṃ vatthutāya “attabhāvavatthū”ti pavuccatīti āha “tehī”tiādi. Tesanti upādānakkhandhānaṃ. Vipallāsānaṃ pavattiākāro “asubhe subha”ntiādi. Visayo kāyavedanācittadhammā. Avijjā ca…pe… eva sammohapubbakattā sabbavipallāsānaṃ. Ca-saddo subhasukhasaññānanti etthāpi ānetvā yojetabbo.

The five aggregates of clinging, appearing in various forms like a net of Indra's jewels, are called 'self' by way of conventional expression due to being the cause of 'I' and 'mine'; and because this individual existence is the sign for the arising of that concept and designation and the basis for pleasure and so forth, it is called 'the basis of individual existence'—thus it is said, 'by these,' and so on. 'These' refers to the aggregates of clinging. The mode of occurrence of distortions is 'perceiving the unattractive as attractive,' and so on. The objects are body, feeling, mind, and mental phenomena. And ignorance... thus, because of being preceded by delusion, it is indeed for all distortions. The word 'and' should also be brought and connected here to 'perception of the beautiful and the pleasurable.'

Tatthāyaṃ yojanā – “avijjā ca subhasukhasaññānaṃ paccayo eva, na taṇhā eva, avijjā subhasukhasaññānañca paccayo, na niccaattasaññānaṃ evā”ti. Evaṃ santepi purimānaṃ dvinnaṃ viparītasaññānaṃ taṇhā, pacchimānaṃ avijjā visesapaccayoti dassento āha “tathāpī”tiādi. Avijjāsīsena cettha diṭṭhiyā gahaṇaṃ veditabbaṃ. Tenāha “diṭṭhinivutaṃ citta”nti[V 0.0082], “yo diṭṭhivipallāso”ti ca ādi, yathā ca avijjāsīsena diṭṭhiyā gahaṇaṃ, evaṃ diṭṭhisīsena avijjāyapi gahaṇaṃ siyāti āha “diṭṭhisīsena avijjā vuttā”ti. Taṇhāvijjāsu subhasukhasaññānaṃ yathā taṇhā visesapaccayo, na evaṃ avijjā. Niccaattasaññānaṃ pana yathā avijjā visesapaccayo, na tathā taṇhāti dassento “moho visesapaccayo”ti āha.

Here, this is the construing: 'Ignorance is indeed a condition for perceptions of beauty and pleasure, not craving alone; and it is a condition for perceptions of beauty and pleasure, not only for perceptions of permanence and self.' Even so, showing that craving is the special condition for the first two distorted perceptions, while ignorance is the special condition for the latter, he says, 'Even so,' etc. Here, the taking up of views should be understood as being headed by ignorance. Hence it is said, 'the mind covered by views,' 'the distortion of view,' and so on. Just as the taking up of views is headed by ignorance, so too ignorance may also be taken up as headed by views—thus it is said, 'ignorance is spoken of as headed by view.' Regarding craving and ignorance, just as craving is a special condition for perceptions of beauty and pleasure, ignorance is not so. But for perceptions of permanence and self, just as ignorance is a special condition, craving is not so—thus it is said, 'delusion is the special condition.'

Pacchimānaṃ dvinnaṃ…pe… hotīti atītaṃse taṇhābhinivesassa balavabhāvābhāvato. Teneva hi “so atītaṃ rūpaṃ attato samanupassati”cceva [M 0.0087] vuttaṃ, na “abhinandatī”ti. Taṇhāvipallāsoti taṇhaṃ upanissāya pavatto vipallāso, na hi taṇhā sayaṃ vipallāso. Tenāha “taṇhāmūlako vipallāso”ti. Diṭṭhābhinandanavasenāti taṇhupanissayadiṭṭhābhinandanavasena, yato so “taṇhāvipallāso”ti vutto. Etenāti “yo taṇhāvipallāso”tiādipāṭhena. Soḷasa saññīvādā, aṭṭha asaññīvādā, aṭṭha nevasaññīnāsaññīvādā, pañca paramadiṭṭhadhammanibbānavādā ca, yathā attano gatāya, nibbānappattiyā ca parikappavasena subhasukhākāraggāhino, na evaṃ satta ucchedavādāti āha “yebhuyyenā”ti. Paṭipakkhavasenapīti visuddhivasenapi. Yāva hi upakkilesā, tāva cittaṃ na visujjhateva. Yadā ca te pahīnā, tadā visuddhameva. Tenāha “na hī”tiādi. “Arakkhitena cittenā”ti pāḷiṃ nikkhipitvā vipallāsamukheneva desanāya niddhāriyamānattā vuttaṃ “yathānusandhināva gāthaṃ niṭṭhapetu”nti.

Of the latter two... it is so because of the absence of strong adherence of craving in the past. Therefore, it is said, 'He regards past form as self,' but not 'he rejoices.' 'The distortion of craving' means a distortion arisen dependent on craving, for craving itself is not a distortion. Hence it is said, 'Distortion rooted in craving.' 'By way of delighting in views' means by way of delighting in views dependent on craving, since it is called 'the distortion of craving.' 'By this' means by the passage beginning, 'Whatever is the distortion of craving...' Sixteen views of perception, eight views of non-perception, eight views of neither-perception-nor-non-perception, and five views of Nibbāna attainable here and now—who, according to their own path and by way of conceiving the attainment of Nibbāna, grasp at aspects of the beautiful and happiness; but not so the seven annihilationists—hence it is said, 'for the most part.' 'Even by way of opposition' means even by way of purity. For as long as defilements remain, the mind is simply not purified. But when they are abandoned, then it is purified. Therefore, it is said, 'For not...'
