'With an unguarded mind'—having set aside the Pāli text, it is said 'to complete the verse according to the connection itself,' because the teaching is being determined only through the aspect of distortion.

Mārassāti kilesamārassa. Tassa hi vase ṭhito sesamārānaṃ hatthagato evāti. Tenāha “kilesamāraggahaṇenevā”tiādi.

'Of Māra' means of the Māra of defilements. Indeed, one who stands under his control is in the power of the remaining Māras. Therefore, he said, 'By the seizing of the Māra of defilements,' and so on.

Mārabandhananti sattamārapakkhe mārassa bandhananti mārabandhanaṃ. So hi kilesabandhanabhūte attano samārakaparise maññati. Tena vuttaṃ “antali…pe… mokkhasī”ti (mahāva. 33). Itaramārapakkhe mārova bandhananti mārabandhanaṃ. Visaṅkhāro nibbānaṃ.

'The bondage of Māra' means, in the context of the seven Māras, the bondage of Māra. For he considers his own retinue, together with Māra, to be the bondage of defilements. Therefore, it is said: 'Within...you will be liberated' (Mahāva. 33). In the other sense, 'the bondage of Māra' means Māra himself is the bondage. The unconditioned is Nibbāna.

Mohasampayogato cittaṃ “mūḷha”nti vuttanti rattaduṭṭhānampi mūḷhatāya sabbhāve “mūḷha”nti visuṃ vacanaṃ āveṇikamohavasena vuttanti dassento “dvinnaṃ momūhacittuppādānaṃ vasenā”ti āha. Evanti evaṃ rāgādiakusalappattiyā kusalabhaṇḍacchedanato arakkhitaṃ cittaṃ hoti, sabbopi micchābhiniveso ettheva saṅgahaṃ samosaraṇaṃ gacchatīti āha “micchādiṭṭhi [V 0.0083] …pe… veditabba”nti. Sabbepīti “arakkhitaṃ, micchādiṭṭhihataṃ, thinamiddhābhibhūta”nti tīhipi padehi vuttadhammā.

The mind is called "deluded" due to its association with delusion. And even those with lust and hatred are called "deluded" because of the presence of delusion in them. Showing that the separate statement "deluded" is made by way of exclusive delusion, he said, "due to two kinds of utterly deluded states of consciousness." Thus, the mind, being unguarded due to the occurrence of unwholesome states like greed, cuts off the store of wholesome qualities. Showing that all wrong inclinations converge and are comprised herein, he said, "Wrong view... should be understood." All these are the qualities stated by the three phrases: "unguarded," "afflicted by wrong view," and "overwhelmed by sloth and torpor."

Cakkhunāti dvārena. Rūpanti visabhāgavatthusannissitaṃ rūpāyatanaṃ. Nimittaggāhīti “itthī”ti vā puriso”ti vā “subha”nti vā “asubha”nti vā parikappitanimittaṃ [M 0.0088] gaṇhāti, tassa vā gahaṇasīlo. Anubyañjanaggāhīti hatthapādahasitakathitādippabhede kilesānaṃ anu anu byañjanato anubyañjanasaññite ākāre gaṇhāti, tesaṃ vā gahaṇasīlo. Yatvādhikaraṇanti yaṃ nimittaṃ, nimittānubyañjanaggahaṇanimittanti attho. Evaṃ “cakkhundriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharanta”nti, yo “nimittaggāhī, anubyañjanaggāhī”ti ca vutto puggalo, tamenaṃ cakkhundriyaṃ cakkhudvāraṃ asaṃvutaṃ satikavāṭena apihitaṃ katvā vattantaṃ, tassa ca rūpassa iṭṭhākāraggahaṇe abhijjhā, aniṭṭhākāraggahaṇe domanassaṃ, asamapekkhane moho micchābhinivese micchādiṭṭhīti evaṃ abhijjhābyāpādā, aññe ca lāmakaṭṭhena pāpakā akosallasambhūtaṭṭhena akusalā dhammā anvāssaveyyuṃ anu anu pavatteyyuṃ.

'With the eye' (cakkhunā) means through the door. 'Form' (rūpaṃ) means the form-base dependent on dissimilar objects. 'One who grasps the sign' (nimittaggāhī) means one who grasps the sign conceived as 'woman' or 'man,' 'beautiful' or 'ugly,' or is by nature one who grasps it. 'One who grasps the details' (anubyañjanaggāhī) means one who grasps the features designated as 'details' because they repeatedly manifest the defilements, such as hands, feet, smiles, speech, etc., or is by nature one who grasps them. 'On account of which' (yatvādhikaraṇaṃ) means on account of that sign, the cause for grasping signs and details. Thus, "one who dwells unrestrained in the eye faculty" (cakkhundriyaṃ asaṃvutaṃ viharantaṃ) means that person, described as "one who grasps the sign, one who grasps the details," dwells having made that eye faculty, the eye door, unrestrained, not closed by the door-bolt of mindfulness. And regarding that form, upon grasping a pleasing appearance, covetousness arises; upon grasping an unpleasing appearance, displeasure; upon inattention, delusion; upon wrong adherence, wrong view. Thus, covetousness and ill will, and other evil states, unwholesome by reason of their arising from unskillfulness, would flow in and repeatedly arise.

Tassa saṃvarāya na paṭipajjatīti tassa cakkhudvārassa saṃvarāya satikavāṭena pidahanatthaṃ na paṭipajjati. Sā pana appaṭipatti cakkhundriyassa anārakkhāsaṃvarassa anuppādoti dassento “na rakkhati…pe… āpajjatī”ti āha. Javane uppajjamānopi hi asaṃvaro tena dvārena pavattanato “cakkhundriyāsaṃvaro”tveva vuccatīti. Sesadvāresupi vuttanayeneva attho veditabbo. “Pubbantakappanavasena cā”tiādinā saṅkhepato vuttamatthaṃ vitthārato dassento “saṅkhepato ca vitthāro añño”ti katvā taṃ samuccinanto “yā ca kho imā”tiādimāha.

He does not practice for its restraint: this means he does not practice for the purpose of closing that eye-door with the door-bolt of mindfulness. Showing that this non-practice is the unguardedness and the non-arising of restraint for the eye faculty, he said, "He does not guard... he commits." For even when unrestraint arises in the impulsion, it is called "unrestraint of the eye faculty" because it proceeds through that door. The meaning should be understood in the same way for the remaining doors. "And by way of reflecting on the past," etc.—showing in detail the meaning stated in brief, and having made the distinction, 'The brief statement and the detailed explanation are different,' he, summarizing it, said, "But these..." and so on.

Yathāvuttā akusalā dhammāti dvādasa akusalacittuppādadhammā, tesaṃ vatthūni vā. Te hi samudayavajjā pañcupādānakkhandhā. “Eva”nti iminā nettipāḷiyaṃ, aṭṭhakathāyañca niddhāritappakārena. Idhāti imissaṃ “arakkhitena cittenā”ti gāthāyaṃ.

"Unwholesome states as mentioned" refers to the twelve unwholesome states of consciousness, or their bases. For these are the five aggregates of clinging, which are subject to origination. "Thus" (evaṃ) means in the manner determined in the Netti text and the commentary. "Here" (idha) means in this verse, 'with an unguarded mind'.

Yadipi desanāhārasampātapāḷiyaṃ “tasmā rakkhitacittassā”ti gāthā sarūpato na gahitā, atthato pana “tesaṃ bhagavā pariññāyā”tiādinā gahitā evāti tassā gahitabhāvaṃ vibhāvetuṃ “kathaṃ desetī”ti pucchitvā “tasmā rakkhitacittassā”ti gāthaṃ uddhari.

Even though the verse "Therefore, for one with a guarded mind" (tasmā rakkhitacittassā) was not taken in its exact form in the Pāli text of the Desanāhārasampāta, its meaning is indeed taken by the passage beginning 'The Blessed One, for their full understanding,' etc. To clarify its being taken, having asked, 'How does he teach?' he cited the verse 'Therefore, for one with a guarded mind'.

Yonisomanasikārena kammaṃ karontoti “so ‘idaṃ dukkha’nti yoniso manasi karotī”tiādinā [V 0.0084] nayena vipassanāsaṅkhātena yonisomanasikārena bhāvanākammaṃ karonto, bhāventoti attho[M 0.0089]. Yathābhūtañāṇanti ñātapariññāya pubbabhāgavipassanāya “avijjāsamudayā rūpasamudayo, avijjānirodhā rūpanirodho”tiādinā (paṭi. ma. 1.50) samapaññāsāya ākārehi. Nirayagatiyaṃ dukkhadukkhatā, sugativisese brahmalokekadese saṅkhāradukkhatā, itarattha dve tissopīti dassento āha “yathāsambhavaṃ tividhadukkhatāyogenā”ti.

By wisely attending, one performs the action (yonisomanasikārena kammaṃ karonto): this means one performs the action of development through wise attention, which is called insight, in the manner of 'He wisely attends, "This is suffering,"' etc.; that is, one develops. Knowledge of things as they truly are (yathābhūtañāṇaṃ) means by the preliminary insight of full understanding through knowledge, in fifty aspects, such as 'With the arising of ignorance, there is the arising of form; with the cessation of ignorance, there is the cessation of form,' etc. (Paṭisambhidāmagga 1.50). Showing that in the realm of hell there is suffering-as-pain, in a special higher realm, in a part of the Brahma world, there is suffering-of-conditioned-states, and elsewhere there are two or even all three, he said, "according to their occurrence, by means of the threefold suffering."

Desanāhārasampātavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Exposition of the Confluence of the Teaching-Nutriment is completed.

2. Vicayahārasampātavaṇṇanā

2. The Exposition of the Confluence of the Investigation-Nutriment.

53. Kusaladhammārammaṇāti kusaladhamme uddissa pavattimattaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ, na tesaṃ ārammaṇapaccayataṃ idha “kusalā dhammā”ti lokuttaradhammānaṃ adhippetattā. Na hi kadāci anupādāniyā dhammā upādānārammaṇā honti. Phaladhamme uddissa pavattāya taṇhāya gahitattā “kusala…pe… daṭṭhabbo”ti vuttaṃ. Desanāhāreti desanāhārasampāte. Kathaṃ pana kusalabhāvoti “kusalā”ti vacanamattaṃ gahetvā codeti, tañca nidassanamattaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ, pahānahetubhāvopissā siyā codakena sampaṭicchitova. “Mānopi duvidho”tiādinā mānassa ca tassā taṇhāya ca sevitabbabhāvo akusalānaṃ pahānāya, kusalānaṃ uppattiyā ca paccayabhāvato.

53. Having wholesome states as object (kusaladhammārammaṇā) is stated with reference merely to the occurrence directed towards wholesome states, not to their being an object-condition here, since 'wholesome states' (kusalā dhammā) implies supramundane states. For states not liable to clinging never become objects of clinging. Because craving seizes upon the occurrence directed towards fruition states, it is said, 'wholesome... should be seen.' Teaching-nutriment (desanāhāra) means in the confluence of the teaching-nutriment. But how is there wholesomeness? — he objects by taking merely the word 'wholesome.' And that should be seen as merely an illustration; the state of being a reason for abandoning would also be accepted by the objector. By 'Pride is twofold,' etc., it is shown that both pride and that craving are to be cultivated, because they are conditions for the abandoning of the unwholesome and for the arising of the wholesome.

Nekkhammassitaṃ domanassaṃ nāma “ariyabhūmiṃ pāpuṇituṃ nāsakkhi”nti anusocato uppannaṃ domanassanti sambandho. Evanti iminā pāḷiyaṃ vuttappakārena, pihaṃ upaṭṭhapetvā chasu dvāresu iṭṭhārammaṇe āpāthagate aniccādivasena vipassanaṃ paṭṭhapetvāti yojanā. Iṭṭhārammaṇañcettha yathāvuttaanusocanadomanassuppattīnaṃ yathābhiniviṭṭhassa ārammaṇassa aniṭṭhatāyāti dassanatthaṃ. “Kathaṃ nekkhammavasenā”ti padassa atthaṃ vivarituṃ “vipassanāvasenā”tiādi vuttaṃ. Vipassanādivinimuttā vā paṭhamajjhānādivasena vuttā kusalā dhammā idha nekkhammaṃ. Anussatiggahaṇena upacārajjhānameva gahitanti “paṭhamajjhānādivasenā”ti vuttaṃ. Ādisaddena dutiyajjhānato paṭṭhāya yāva aggaphalā uparivisesā saṅgahitā. Yāya paññāvimuttiyā.

Grief rooted in renunciation (nekkhammassitaṃ domanassaṃ) is the grief that arises from lamenting, 'I was unable to reach the noble state.' This is the connection. Thus (evaṃ) means, in the manner stated in the Pāli, by arousing longing and establishing insight into impermanence, etc., when a desirable object appears at the six doors—this is the connection. And here, the desirable object is mentioned to show how, due to the undesirability of the object to which one is attached, the arising of grief from lamentation occurs as stated. To explain the meaning of the phrase 'how through renunciation,' it is said, 'through insight,' etc. Or, the wholesome states stated by way of the first jhāna, etc., liberated from insight, etc., are renunciation here. By the taking of 'recollection,' only access concentration is taken, hence it is said, 'by way of the first jhāna, etc.' By the word 'etc.,' the higher special attainments from the second jhāna up to the supreme fruition are included. By which wisdom-liberation (yāya paññāvimuttiyā).

Upekkhāsatipārisuddhibhāvenāti [M 0.0090] upekkhājanitasatipārisuddhisabbhāvena. Kammayogganti vipassanābhāvanādikammassa yoggaṃ anurūpaṃ anucchavikaṃ. Assaddhiyeti assaddhiyahetu, “assaddhiyenā”tipi [V 0.0085] paṭhanti, so evattho. Obhāsagatanti ñāṇobhāsagataṃ. Kāmaṃ pubbepi paññā vuttā, assaddhiyādīhi pana aññesaṃ kilesānaṃ vidhamanampi paññāya eva hoti, sā ca evaṃbhūtāti dassanatthaṃ “obhāsagataṃ kilesandhakāre na iñjatī”ti vuttaṃ.

By the state of purity of mindfulness due to equanimity (upekkhāsatipārisuddhibhāvena) means by the presence of the purity of mindfulness born of equanimity. Suitable for work (kammayoggaṃ) means appropriate, suitable, and fitting for the work of insight development and other practices. By lack of faith (assaddhiyeti) means due to lack of faith. Some also read 'by lack of faith' (assaddhiyenā); the meaning is the same. Illuminated (obhāsagataṃ) means associated with the illumination of knowledge. Although wisdom was mentioned earlier, the dispelling of other defilements such as lack of faith, etc., also occurs by wisdom itself. And to show that it is of such a nature, it is said, 'illuminated, it does not waver in the darkness of defilements.'

Kopo kodho. Appaccayo domanassaṃ. Iddhividhañāṇādikā cha abhiññā pākaṭā evāti “dve ca visese”ti vuttadhamme dassetuṃ “manomayiddhi, vipassanāñāṇañcā”ti āha. Aṅgaṇāni rāgādayo. Upakkilesā abhijjhāvisamalobhādayo. Anulomanaṃ tadekaṭṭhatā. Phandanā dubbalā vikkhepappavatti. Balavatī anavaṭṭhānaṃ. Sabbo micchābhiniveso ayonisomanasikārena hoti, micchāvitakkena ca. Tattha ayonisomanasikāro akusalacittuppādo tappariyāpanno micchāvitakko vikkhepasahito evāti vuttaṃ “micchābhinivesahetutāya diṭṭhipakkho”ti vuttañhetaṃ “vitakkopi diṭṭhiṭṭhānaṃ, ayoniso manasikāropi diṭṭhiṭṭhāna”nti (paṭi. ma. 1.124).

Wrath (kopo) is anger (kodho). Discontent (appaccayo) is displeasure (domanassaṃ). The six higher knowledges, such as the knowledge of psychic power, are evident. Thus, to show the qualities stated as 'two special qualities,' it is said, 'mind-made psychic power and knowledge of insight.' Stains (aṅgaṇāni) are lust, etc. Corruptions (upakkilesā) are covetousness, unrighteous greed, etc. Conformity (anulomanaṃ) is being of one accord with that. Agitation (phandanā) is weak activity of distraction. Strong agitation is instability. All wrong adherence arises through unwise attention and through wrong thought. Here, unwise attention is an unwholesome state of consciousness, and the wrong thought pertaining to it is accompanied by distraction. Thus it is said, 'Because it is the cause of wrong adherence, it is on the side of wrong view.' For it is said, 'Thought is a ground for views, and unwise attention is also a ground for views' (Paṭisambhidāmagga 1.124).

Atha vā iñjanāti phandanā, diṭṭhaparittāso. Yathāha “tadapi tesaṃ bhavataṃ samaṇabrāhmaṇānaṃ ajānataṃ apassataṃ vedayitaṃ taṇhāgatānaṃ paritassitavipphanditamevā”ti (dī. ni. 1.105 ādayo). Aṭṭhitīti anavaṭṭhānaṃ, diṭṭhivitakko. Tena hi puthujjano kāle sassataṃ, kāle ucchedanti taṃ taṃ diṭṭhiggahaṇaṃ pakkhandanto sattato paribbhaṭṭhaandho viya, samudde vissaṭṭhavāhanikā viya, yante yuttagoṇo viya ca tathā tathā paribbhamati. Tenāha “diṭṭhiyopi diṭṭhiṭṭhānaṃ, vitakkopi diṭṭhiṭṭhāna”nti (paṭi. ma. 1.124) ca. Etasmiñca pakkhe micchābhinivesatāya, micchābhinivesahetutāya ca tassā dve pakkhāti ekadesasarūpekaseso katoti veditabbaṃ.

Alternatively, wavering (iñjanā) is agitation (phandanā), the trembling of views. As it is said: 'Even that experience of those venerable ascetics and brahmins who do not know, do not see, who are given to craving, is merely trembling and quivering' (Dī. Ni. 1.105, etc.). Instability (aṭṭhiti) means unsteadiness, a thought connected with views. For thus the ordinary person, at times grasping eternalism, at times annihilationism, rushing into various view-grasps, wanders this way and that, like a blind man strayed from the inhabited region, like a boat cast adrift in the ocean, or like an ox yoked to a mill. Therefore, it is said, 'A view is also a ground for views, and a thought is also a ground for views' (Paṭisambhidāmagga 1.124). And in this case, it should be understood that its two sides, by reason of being wrong adherence and by reason of being a cause for wrong adherence, are treated as a partial remainder of similar form.

“Eva”ntiādinā “so uparima”ntiādipāḷiyaṃ sambandhaṃ dasseti. Paṭighasaññāti bhummatthe paccattavacananti dassento “paṭigha…pe… saññāsū”ti āha. Rūpāvacarasaññāti saññāsīsena rūpāvacarajjhānāni vadati. Nānattasaññāti [M 0.0091] nānāsabhāvā, nānāsabhāve vā ārammaṇe saññā. Ṭhapetvā paṭighasaññā avasiṭṭhakāmāvacarasaññā hetā. Tā samatikkamatīti tā rūpasaññānānattasaññāyo ārammaṇehi saddhiṃ sammadeva atikkamati.

“Eva” and so forth indicates the connection in the Pali text, such as “so uparima” and so forth. “Paṭighasaññā” means perception of resistance, showing that it is a singular expression with a locative sense ('in the basis'), as stated by “paṭigha…pe… saññāsū.” “Rūpāvacarasaññā” means, by the heading of perception, he speaks of the form-sphere jhānas. “Nānattasaññā” means perception of diverse natures, or perception in diverse objects. These are the remaining sense-sphere perceptions, excluding perception of resistance. “Tā samatikkamatīti” means he completely transcends those perceptions of form and perceptions of diversity, together with their objects.

Rūpāvacarajjhānobhāsopi kasiṇārammaṇā. Kasiṇanissando hi āruppajjhānuppatti. Dassananti kasiṇarūpānaṃ dassanaṃ. Abhijjhābyāpādappahānena saddhiṃ vīriyārambho upakārako samatho [V 0.0086] satipassaddhiyo parikkhāraṅgatā vuttā eva. Satirahitaṃ sammasanaṃ nāma natthīti “yā upaṭṭhitā sati asammuṭṭhā, ayaṃ vipassanā”ti vuttaṃ. Tena satisīsena vipassanā gahitāti dasseti. Sammosānaṃ pahānamāhāti sambandho.

The radiance of the form-sphere jhānas also has kasiṇa objects. The arising of the formless jhānas is indeed the outflow from the kasiṇa. 'Seeing' means the seeing of kasiṇa forms. The arousal of energy, together with the abandonment of covetousness and ill-will, is helpful; serenity, mindfulness, and tranquility are indeed stated as having become factors of equipment. There is no such thing as examination devoid of mindfulness, as it is stated: 'That which is established mindfulness, unconfused—this is insight.' Thus, it shows that insight is grasped with mindfulness as its head. He speaks of the abandonment of confusion; this is the connection.

54. Paccuppannasukhaāyatisukhavipākakiriyanirāmisaakāpurisasevitabhāvehi eva sesā pāḷiyaṃ etassa samādhissa santapaṇītatādivisesā vuttā, tepi idha saṅgahitāti tesaṃ padānaṃ atthaṃ dassento “aṅgasantatāyā”tiādimāha. Tattha aṅgasantatāyāti phalajhānaṅgānaṃ upasantatāya. Kilesadarathasantatāyāti kilesadarathapaṭippassaddhiyā. Paṇītoti uḷāro. Ekodibhāvenāti maggasamādhisaṅkhātena ekodibhāvena. Ekodibhāvanti samādhānaṃ. Lokiyasamādhissa paccanīkanīvaraṇapaṭhamajjhānanikantiādīni niggahetabbāni, aññe ca kilesā vāretabbā. Imassa pana arahattasamādhissa paṭippassaddhakilesattā na niggahetabbaṃ, vāretabbañca atthīti so maggānantaraṃ samāpattikkhaṇeva appayogeneva adhigatattā, ṭhitattā ca aparihānivasena vā adhigatattā nasasaṅkhāraniggayhavāritagato.

54. The remaining special qualities of this concentration, such as its peacefulness and sublimity, are stated in the Pāli text by means of its being present happiness, having future happiness as its result, being functional and unworldly, and being cultivated by noble persons; and these too are included here. Thus, showing the meaning of these terms, he says, 'by the peacefulness of the factors,' and so forth. In this context, 'by the peacefulness of the factors' means by the peacefulness of the factors of the fruition jhāna. 'By the peacefulness of the distress of defilements' means by the calming of the distress of defilements. 'Sublime' means excellent, lofty. 'By one-pointedness' refers to the one-pointedness designated as path-concentration. 'One-pointedness' means concentration, unification. For mundane concentration, the opposing hindrances, the craving for the first jhāna, and so forth, should be suppressed, and other defilements should be warded off. However, for this concentration of an arahant, since the defilements are already calmed, there is nothing to be suppressed or warded off. Because it is attained without effort at the very moment of attainment immediately after the path, and because it is established and attained without falling away, it is not subject to suppression and warding off that requires exertion.

“Sativepullappatto”ti etena appavattamānāyapi satiyā satibahulatāya sato eva nāmāti dasseti. “Yathāparicchinnakālavasenā”ti etena paricchinnasatiyā satoti dasseti.

By the phrase, 'one who has attained an abundance of mindfulness,' it is shown that even when mindfulness is not actively occurring, one is still called mindful due to the abundance of mindfulness. By the phrase, 'according to a defined period of time,' it shows that one is mindful by means of a defined mindfulness.

Vakkhamānenāti “pītipharaṇā”tiādinā anantaraṃ vakkhamānena. “Pītipharaṇatā”ti pana pāḷi āgatā. Taṃ “pañcaṅgiko sammāsamādhī”ti (dī. ni. 3.355) samādhiaṅgabhāvena paññā uddiṭṭhāti katvā vuttaṃ. Tato eva aṭṭhakathāyaṃ “pītipharaṇatā”tiādīnañca atthasaṃvaṇṇanā katā. Tattha “so imameva kāyaṃ [M 0.0092] vivekajena pītisukhena abhisandetī”tiādinā (dī. ni. 1.226) nayena pītiyā, sukhassa ca pharaṇaṃ veditabbaṃ. Sesaṃ suviññeyyameva.

'By what will be said' refers to what will be stated immediately following, beginning with 'pervading with joy' and so forth. However, the Pāli reading that has come down is 'the state of pervading with joy.' That is stated, taking it that wisdom is designated as a factor of concentration, as in 'the five-factored right concentration' (Dī. Ni. 3.355). For that very reason, the commentary provides an explanation of the meaning of 'the state of pervading with joy' and the like. There, the pervading by joy and happiness should be understood in the manner of 'He drenches, steeps, permeates, and suffuses this very body with the rapture and happiness born of seclusion' (Dī. Ni. 1.226). The rest is easily understood.

Samādhivasena samatho uddhaṭoti sabhāvavasena samatho uddhaṭo, na upakārakadhammavasenāti adhippāyo.

'Tranquility is extracted by way of concentration' means that tranquility is extracted by way of its own nature, not by way of supportive qualities—this is the intended meaning.

55. Rāgapaṭipakkhattā samādhissa “adhicittasikkhāya sikkhanto”ti vuttaṃ. Vuttanayānusārenāti “sukhapaṇidhiādisamugghāṭanena appaṇihito”tiādinā. Ettha ca saṅkhārānaṃ khaṇabhaṅgurataṃ sammadeva passantassa na rāgo patiṭṭhaṃ labhatīti aniccānupassanā rāgacaritassa sappāyā [V 0.0087] vuttā, tathā saṅkhārānaṃ sabhāvadukkhataṃ sammadeva passantassa pakatiyāpi dukkhitesu dukkhuppādanaṃ vaṇe khārodakasekasadisanti na doso patiṭṭhaṃ labhatīti dukkhānupassanā dosacaritassa sappāyā vuttā, tathā saṅkhāresu sammadeva ghanavinibbhoge kate attasuññatāya upaṭṭhahamānāya na moho patiṭṭhaṃ labhatīti anattānupassanā mohacaritassa sappāyā vuttāti veditabbaṃ. Rāgapaṭipakkhattā samādhissa “adhicittasikkhāya sikkhanto”ti vuttaṃ. Esa nayo itaresu. Sesamettha suviññeyyaṃ.

55. Because concentration opposes lust, it is said, 'training in the training of the higher mind.' 'According to the method stated' means by such phrases as, 'by the eradication of aspirations for happiness and so forth, one is without aspiration.' And here, it should be understood that for one who rightly sees the momentary breaking up of formations, lust finds no footing; thus, the contemplation of impermanence is said to be suitable for one of a lustful temperament. Similarly, for one who rightly sees the inherent suffering of formations, hatred finds no footing—just as producing suffering in those who are already suffering is like sprinkling caustic water on a wound; thus, the contemplation of suffering is said to be suitable for one of a hateful temperament. Likewise, when a complete analysis of solidity in formations is rightly made, with the emptiness of self becoming manifest, delusion finds no footing; thus, the contemplation of not-self is said to be suitable for one of a deluded temperament. Because concentration opposes lust, it is said, 'training in the training of the higher mind.' This method applies to the others. The rest here is easily understood.

Khantibahulo uppannaṃ aratiṃ anabhiratiṃ abhibhuyya viharanto sukhena samādhiṃ adhigacchatīti khantippadhānatāpi samathapakkhabhajanassa kāraṇaṃ vuttā. Uṭṭhānaṃ sampajjatīti sampannakāyikavīriyaṃ. Sammākammantavāyāmānaṃ yo kāyikādivikappo vutto pāḷiyaṃ, so nesaṃ kāyikassa payogassa samuṭṭhānavasena veditabbo.

One who is abundant in patience, dwelling having overcome arisen discontent and dissatisfaction, attains concentration with ease; thus, the predominance of patience is also stated as a cause for resorting to the side of serenity. 'Energy is accomplished' means possessing accomplished bodily energy. The distinction into bodily and other types of effort, which is mentioned in the Pāli regarding right action and right effort, should be understood as being due to the arising of their bodily exertion.

“Khippādhigamo”ti iminā maggāsevanabhāvaṃ dasseti. “Vipassanāya vimuttādhigamo”ti iminā vipassanānubhāvena samucchedavimutti vikkhambhanavimutti viya samathānubhāvenāti dasseti. Lokiyehīti nissakkavacanaṃ. Mahantānanti uḷārānaṃ, paṇītānanti attho.

By 'swift attainment,' it indicates the state of cultivating the path. By 'attainment of liberation through insight,' it shows that liberation by eradication is attained through the power of insight, just as liberation by suppression is attained through the power of serenity. 'From the worldly' is a word indicating departure (ablative). 'Of the great' means of the lofty; the meaning is 'of the sublime'.

56. Tanti vicayahāraṃ. Visaṃvādanahetūnaṃ lobhādīnaṃ pāpadhammānaṃ. Sodhentoti yathā saraṇādivisayā aññāṇādisaṃkilesā na pavattanti, evaṃ sodhento. Paripūrentāti yathā sīlaṃ akhaṇḍādibhāvena paripuṇṇaṃ hoti anūnaṃ, evaṃ paripūrentā.

56. `Tanti` means the method of analysis. Of the evil states, such as greed, which are causes of deception. 'Purifying' means purifying so that defilements such as ignorance do not arise regarding objects such as the refuges. 'Fulfilling' means fulfilling so that virtue is complete by being unbroken and so forth, without deficiency.

“Tathā [M 0.0093] paṭipajjanto”ti iminā satthu mahāpatikārabhāvo paripuṇṇo dassitoti paṭhamavāde “dassanābhūmiñca bhāvanābhūmiñcā”ti vuttaṃ.

By this, 'thus practicing,' it is shown that the Teacher’s great service is fulfilled; for this reason, it was stated in the first statement: 'the sphere of vision and the sphere of development.'

Yassa atthāyāti yassa yassa pahānatthāya. Tathā paṭipannassāti yathā asubhajjhānādiṃ pādakaṃ katvā anāgāmimaggādiadhigamo hoti, tathā paṭipannassa.

'For the sake of which' means for the sake of abandoning whatsoever. 'For one who has practiced thus' means for one who has practiced in such a way that, by making meditation on the foul and so forth the basis, there is the attainment of the path of non-returning and so forth.

Vadhitanti ghātitaṃ.

'Slain' means killed.

“Manussabhūto”ti idaṃ pubbāparāpekkhaṃ katvā “pitā manussabhūto khīṇāsavo”ti ca tathā [V 0.0088] “mātā manussabhūtā”ti yojetabbaṃ. Bhedānurūpassa sāvanaṃ anussāvanaṃ, bhedānurūpena vā vacanena viññāpanaṃ.

'Having become a human being'—this should be connected by taking into account what comes before and after, as in 'the father, having become a human being, was one whose taints are destroyed,' and similarly, 'the mother, having become a human being.' Causing to hear and reminding is in accordance with the distinction; or, it is notification by a statement suitable to the distinction.

57. Manussattanti manussajātitā. Liṅgasampattīti purisabhāvo. Hetūti manovacīpaṇidhānasiddhiyā saddhiṃ pubbahetusampadā. Satthāradassananti satthu sammukhībhāvo. Guṇasampattīti abhiññāsamāpattilābho. Adhikāroti attano sarīranirapekkhaṃ satthu upakārakaraṇaṃ. Chandatāti buddhabhāvāya daḷhacchandatā anivattidhammatā.

57. Human state—the state of being born a human. Perfection of gender—the state of being male. Causes—the perfection of previous causes together with the success of mental and verbal aspirations. Seeing the Teacher—being in the presence of the Teacher. Possession of qualities—attainment of higher knowledge and meditative attainments. Qualification—rendering service to the Teacher without regard for one’s own body. Strong desire—firm desire for Buddhahood, the state of not turning back.

Na uppajjantīti pana atthīti “na me ācariyo atthi, sadiso me na vijjatī”tiādi (ma. ni. 1.285; 2.341; kathā. 405; mahāva. 11; mi. pa. 4.5.11) imissā lokadhātuyā ṭhatvā vadantena bhagavatā “kiṃ panāvuso sāriputta, atthetarahi añño samaṇo vā brāhmaṇo vā bhagavato samasamo sambodhiyanti evaṃ puṭṭho ahaṃ, bhante, ‘no’ti vadeyya”nti (dī. ni. 3.161) vatvā tassa kāraṇaṃ dassetuṃ “aṭṭhānametaṃ anavakāso, yaṃ ekissā lokadhātuyā dve arahanto sammāsambuddhā”ti (dī. ni. 3.161; a. ni. 1.277) imaṃ suttaṃ dassentena dhammasenāpatināva buddhakhettabhūtaṃ imaṃ lokadhātuṃ ṭhapetvā aññattha anuppatti vuttā hotīti adhippāyo.

But does “they do not arise” mean they exist (elsewhere)? As in, when the Blessed One, standing in this world-sphere, says, “I have no teacher, none like me exists,” etc.; and when asked, “Friend Sāriputta, is there now another ascetic or brahmin equal to the Blessed One in supreme enlightenment?”, one should answer, “No, venerable sir.” To explain the reason, the Dhamma general himself cites the sutta: “It is impossible, it cannot happen, that two Arahants, fully enlightened Buddhas, arise in one world-sphere.” The implication is that non-arising is spoken of elsewhere, excluding this world-sphere which is a Buddha-field.

Khettapariggaho kato nāma hoti “idaṃ buddhakhettaṃ nāmā”ti.

A field is considered designated when it is declared, “This is called a Buddha-field.”

Evaṃ ṭhānāṭṭhānabhāvaṃ gatāti vuttappakārena ṭhānabhūtā, vuttanayena vā aññepi yathārahaṃ ṭhānāṭṭhānabhāvena pavattā. Sattapaññattiyā upādānabhūtāti indriyabaddhe khandhe sandhāya vadati.

Thus, having reached the state of possibility and impossibility, they are established as possible in the manner stated; or, by the method stated, others also proceed as is fitting according to the state of possibility and impossibility. 'Being the basis for the designation of a being,' it refers to the aggregates bound by the faculties.

58. Phalassa [M 0.0094] paccakkhakāritāti “imassa kammassa idaṃ phala”nti taṃtaṃkammaphalāvabodho. Appadānābhāvoti paccayasamavāye kammassa ekantato phaluppādanaṃ. Tenāha “katūpacitāna”nti.

58. The direct realization of the fruit means the understanding, 'This is the result of this action,' which is the discernment of the specific fruit of each action. The non-failure to give its result refers to the certain production of the fruit by the action when conditions come together. Hence it is said, 'of actions done and accumulated.'

59. Ajjhositavatthunāti taṇhābhinivesavasena abhiniviṭṭhavatthunā. Rūpabhavaarūpabhavādināti bhavataṇhā viya sayaṃ dasseti.

59. 'By an object clung to' means by an object adhered to through the force of craving and attachment. 'By form existence, formless existence, and so on' shows craving for existence itself, as it were.

Khandhattayavasenāti [V 0.0089] sīlādikkhandhattayavasena. Paṭipadāvibhāgenāti “sabbatthagāminī”ti ādipaṭipadāya bhedena.

'By way of the three aggregates' means by way of the three aggregates beginning with virtue. 'By the division of the path' means by the distinction of the path beginning with 'leading everywhere' and so on.

Tatthatatthagāminīti nirayādinibbānanti dvīsu gandhabbaṭṭhānesu tattha tattheva gamanasīlā. Sabbatthagāminīti yathāvuttesu sabbaṭṭhānesu ca gamanasīlā.

'Going to this or that place' means inclined to go to those very places within the two spheres of rebirth, from the hells and so on up to Nibbāna. 'Going to all places' means inclined to go to all the places as stated.

Sañjīvo kāḷasuttaṃ saṅghāto roruvo mahāroruvo tāpano mahātāpano avīcīti ete aṭṭha mahānirayā. Ekekassa cattāri cattāri dvārāni, ekekasmiṃ dvāre cattāro cattāro gūthanirayādayoti evaṃ soḷasa ussadaniraye vaṇṇenti.

Sañjīva, Kāḷasutta, Saṅghāta, Roruva, Mahāroruva, Tapana, Mahātāpana, and Avīci—these are the eight great hells. Each has four gates, and at each gate there are four subsidiary hells such as the hell of excrement and so on. Thus, they describe sixteen subsidiary hells.

Sakkasuyāmādiko jeṭṭhakadevarājā. Pajāpativaruṇaīsānādayo viya dutiyādiṭṭhānantarakārako paricārako.

Sakka, Suyāma, and so forth are the chief kings of the devas. Like Pajāpati, Varuṇa, Īsāna, and so forth, they are attendants who act in the second and subsequent positions.

Kilesakāmapakkheti “saṅkappo kāmo, rāgo kāmo, saṅkapparāgo kāmoti (mahāni. 1) ettha vuttasaṅkappavasena vuttaṃ. Sopi hi vibādhati, upatāpeti cāti kilesattasambhavato kilesakāmo vutto, na kilesavatthubhāvato. Kāmapaṭisaṃyuttoti kāmarāgasaṅkhātena kāmena sampayutto, kāmapaṭibaddho vā. Aññesu ca kāmapaṭisaṃyuttesu dhammesu vijjamānesu vitakke eva kāmasaddo dhātusaddo niruḷhoti veditabbo vitakkassa kāmasaṅkappavuttiyā sātisayattā. Esa nayo byāpādadhātuādīsu. Parassa, attano ca dukkhāpanaṃ vihiṃsā. Taṃ tu micchāhi vihiṃsā.

Regarding the defilement of sensual desire, it is said: ‘Intention is desire, lust is desire, intention and lust are desire’ (Mahāni. 1)—here, it is spoken of in terms of the intention mentioned. Indeed, it afflicts and torments; thus, it is called the defilement of sensual desire due to its arising from the nature of defilements, not from being an object of defilement. ‘Connected with sensual desire’ means associated with desire, which is designated as sensual lust, or bound to sensual desire. Even though other phenomena connected with sensual desire exist, the word ‘desire’ should be understood as a conventional term for the element that refers to thought, due to the pre-eminence of thought’s activity as sensual intention. This same principle applies to the element of ill-will and so forth. Causing suffering to others and to oneself is violence. That, however, is wrong violence.

Bījādidhātunānattavasena khandhādinānattaṃ veditabbaṃ. Khandhoti dvidhābhūtaggo.

The diversity of aggregates and so forth should be understood by way of the diversity of elements beginning with seeds. ‘Aggregate’ means a twofold heap.

60. Ajjhāsayadhātūti [M 0.0095] ajjhāsayasabhāvo. Yathā gūthādīnaṃ sabhāvo eso yaṃ gūthādīheva saṃsandati, evaṃ puggalānaṃ ajjhāsayassevesa sabhāvo, yaṃ dussīlādayo dussīlādikeheva saṃsandanti.

60. The element of inclination means the nature of inclination. Just as it is the nature of feces and so forth to mix only with feces and so forth, so too is it the nature of people’s inclination that the immoral associate only with the immoral, and so forth.

Saddhāmūlakattā kusalakiriyāya vuttaṃ “yaṃ saddhāvasenā”tiādi. Tathā hi vuttaṃ “saddhā bīja”nti (su. ni. 77). Yaṃ lobhavasena, saddhāvasena ca dosavasena, saddhāvasena ca mohavasena[V 0.0090], saddhāvasena cāti yojetabbaṃ. Vīriyavasenāti sammappadhānavīriyavasena. Paññāvasenāti maggasammādiṭṭhivasena.

Because faith is the root of wholesome action, it is said, ‘that which is by means of faith,’ and so on. For it is said, ‘Faith is the seed’ (Snp 77). This should be connected as: ‘that which is by means of greed and by means of faith; and by means of hatred and by means of faith; and by means of delusion and by means of faith.’ ‘By means of energy’ means by means of the energy of right effort. ‘By means of wisdom’ means by means of right view of the path.

Akusalassa kammassa katokāsatāya pāḷiyaṃ vuttattā “vipākāvaraṇena nivuta”nti vuttaṃ. Taṃ pana nidassanamattaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ kammāvaraṇādīhipi nivutatāya icchitattā. Tathā hi yathā devadattaṃ kokālikaṃ sunakkhattaṃ licchaviputtanti udāhaṭaṃ, yadipi bhagavā paṭivedhassa aṭṭhānataṃ disvā nibbedhabhāgiyadesanaṃ na deseti, vāsanābhāgiyaṃ pana tathārūpassa deseti evāti dassento “saccappaṭivedha”ntiādimāha. Ajātasattuādīnanti ādisaddena saccakādīnaṃ saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo. Tassāpi bhagavā anāgate vāsanatthāya dhammaṃ desesi. Satthā hi “anāgate tambapaṇṇidīpe sāsanaṃ patiṭṭhahissatī”ti tatthāyaṃ kulaghare nibbatto pabbajitvā kāḷabuddharakkhitatthero nāma pabhinnapaṭisambhido mahākhīṇāsavo bhavissatīti idaṃ disvā dhammaṃ desesi, so ca tathā ahosīti.

Because it is stated in the Pāli that unwholesome kamma has been given an opportunity, it is said, ‘obstructed by the hindrance of its result.’ However, this should be seen as an example, since obstruction by kammic hindrances and so forth is also intended. For example, just as Devadatta, Kokālika, Sunakkhatta, and the Licchavi prince were cited, to show that even if the Blessed One, seeing the impossibility of their realization, did not teach them a discourse pertaining to breakthrough, he would still teach such a person a discourse pertaining to the implanting of a seed for the future, he said, ‘realization of the truth,’ and so on. By ‘Ajātasattu and so on,’ the inclusion of Saccaka and others by the term ‘and so on’ should be understood. Even to him, the Blessed One taught the Dhamma for the sake of future potential. For the Teacher, foreseeing that ‘in the future the Dispensation will be established in the island of Tambapaṇṇi,’ and that there, born into a good family, this person would go forth and become the elder Kāḷabuddharakkhita, a great Arahant with analytical knowledge, he taught the Dhamma, and so it came to be.

Asampuṇṇeti ekantato vipākadānasamatthatāvasena pāripūriṃ anupagate. Diṭṭhupanissayadiṭṭhisahagatassa kammaṃ sandhāya “kamme asampuṇṇe”ti vuttaṃ. Tenāha “kilesantarāya missakaṃ kammantarāyaṃ dassetvā”ti.

‘Incomplete’ means not having reached fulfillment by way of the capacity to definitely give a result. ‘When the kamma is incomplete’ is said referring to kamma accompanied by wrong view and having wrong view as its decisive support. Therefore, he said, ‘having shown the obstacle of kamma mixed with the obstacle of defilements.’

61. Diṭṭhi panettha padhānabhāvena pāḷiyaṃ gahitā sīlabbataparāmāsassa adhippetattā. Tathā hi vuttaṃ “yathā puṇṇañca govatikaṃ, acelañca kukkuravatika”nti. Asampuṇṇattā eva hi tassa micchādiṭṭhikammasamādānassa tesaṃ bhagavā “cattārimāni, puṇṇa, kammānī”tiādinā (ma. ni. 2.81) dhammaṃ desesi. Tāya ca desanāya te taṃ diṭṭhiṃ paṭinissajjitvā sammatte patiṭṭhahiṃsu.

61. Here, view is taken primarily in the Pāli because clinging to rules and rituals is intended. For it is said: ‘Just as Puṇṇa, the bull-practitioner, and the naked ascetic, the dog-practitioner.’ Indeed, it was precisely because of the incompleteness of their undertaking of kamma associated with wrong view that the Blessed One taught them the Dhamma with the words, ‘These four kinds of actions, Puṇṇa,’ and so forth (MN 57). Through that teaching, they abandoned that view and became established in rightness.

62. Paguṇatāya [M 0.0096] vodānaṃ paguṇavodānaṃ. Tadeva paṭhamajjhānādīhi vuṭṭhahitvā dutiyajjhānādiadhigamassa paccayattā vuṭṭhānaṃ nāma hotīti āha “vuṭṭhānaṃ paguṇavodāna”nti. Bhavaṅgavuṭṭhānaṃ bhavaṅguppatti. Bhavaṅgacitte hi uppanne taṃsamaṅgisamāpattito vuṭṭhito nāma hoti. Saññāvedayitaapagamo eva apagamavimokkho.

62. Purity through proficiency is proficient purity. That very purity, because it serves as a condition for attaining the second jhāna and so forth after emerging from the first jhāna and so on, is called ‘emergence’; thus, it is said, ‘emergence is proficient purity.’ Emergence from the life-continuum is the arising of the life-continuum. For when the life-continuum consciousness arises, one is said to have emerged from an attainment with which it is concurrent. The disappearance of perception and feeling is itself liberation through disappearance.

Idaṃ vuṭṭhānanti idaṃ yathāvuttaṃ kosallaṃ vuṭṭhānahetubhāvato vuṭṭhānaṃ. Tathā hi vuttaṃ “vodānampi tamhā tamhā samādhimhā vuṭṭhāna”nti (vibha. 828). Imāya pana vuṭṭhānapāḷiyā asaṅgahitattā [V 0.0091] “nirodhasamāpattiyā vuṭṭhānaṃ pāḷimuttakavuṭṭhānaṃ nāmā”ti sammohavinodaniyaṃ (vibha. aṭṭha. 828) vuttaṃ. Ye pana “nirodhato phalasamāpattiyā vuṭṭhāna”nti pāḷiyaṃ natthīti vadeyyuṃ, te “nirodhā vuṭṭhahantassa nevasaññānāsaññāyatanaṃ phalasamāpattiyā anantarapaccayena paccayo”ti (paṭṭhā. 1.1.417) imāya pāḷiyā paṭisedhetabbā.

‘This is emergence’ means this skill, as described, is emergence because it is a cause of emergence. As it is said, ‘purity is also emergence from each of those concentrations’ (Vibh 828). However, because it is not included in this Pāli passage on emergence, it is said in the Sammohavinodanī, ‘emergence from the attainment of cessation is called emergence outside the Pāli’ (Vibh-a 828). But those who would say, ‘There is no passage in the Pāli about emergence from cessation to the attainment of fruition,’ should be refuted by this passage: ‘For one emerging from cessation, the base of neither-perception-nor-non-perception is a condition by way of immediate precedence for the attainment of fruition’ (Paṭṭh 1.1.417).

63. Ayaṃ cassa āsayoti ettha āsayajānanādinā yehi indriyehi yehi paroparehi sattā kalyāṇapāpāsayādikā honti, tesaṃ pajānanaṃ vibhāvetīti veditabbaṃ. Evañca katvā indriyaparopariyattaāsayānusayañāṇānaṃ visuṃ asādhāraṇatā, indriyaparopariyattanānādhimuttikatāñāṇānaṃ visuṃ balatā ca siddhā hoti.

63. Here, regarding 'And this is his disposition,' it should be understood that this clarifies the full understanding of those faculties and those superior and inferior qualities by which beings come to have good or bad dispositions and so on. And having done so, the distinct uniqueness of the knowledges of faculties, of their superiority and inferiority, of dispositions, and of latent tendencies is established, as well as the distinct strength of the knowledges of faculties, of their superiority and inferiority, and of diverse inclinations.

Thāmagatoti ettha thāmagamanaṃ nāma aññesaṃ asādhāraṇo kāmarāgādīnaṃ eva āveṇiko sabhāvo veditabbo, yato “thāmagato anusayaṃ pajahatī”ti (paṭi. ma. 3.21) vuttaṃ.

Regarding 'firmly established,' here firm establishment should be understood as the intrinsic and unique nature of sensual desire and so forth, not shared by others, since it is said, 'One firmly established abandons the underlying tendency' (Paṭis I 123).

Āvajjanamatteneva sarati ākaṅkhāyattavuttikattā. Vuttañhi “ākaṅkhapaṭibaddhaṃ buddhassa bhagavato ñāṇaṃ, manasikārapaṭibaddhaṃ buddhassa bhagavato ñāṇa”ntiādi (mahāni. 156; cūḷani. mogharājamāṇavapucchāniddesa 85; paṭi. ma. 3.5). Sabbaññutaññāṇaṃ viya hi sabbaṃ bhagavato ñāṇaṃ parikammanirapekkhanti.

He recollects merely by adverting, because its mode of operation is dependent on aspiration. For it is said: ‘The Blessed One’s knowledge is bound by aspiration, the Blessed One’s knowledge is bound by attention,’ and so forth. Indeed, like the knowledge of omniscience, all the Blessed One’s knowledge is independent of preliminary effort.

64. Upakkilesavimuttattāti ettha cittādi eva upakkilesā, nibbattakassa vā kammassa pāribandhakilesā. Kasiṇakammaparikammajhānanibbattanakasiṇabhāvo cuddasavidhena cittaparidamanaṃ abhiññābhinīhāroti sabbatthāpi [M 0.0097] vīriyabalassa bahūpakārattā vuttaṃ “vīriyabhāvanābalanibbatta”nti. Dibbasadisattāti dibbe bhavanti dibbaṃ, yathāvuttaṃ pasādacakkhu, dibbaṃ viyāti dibbaṃ, aggataṃ abhiññāṇaṃ. Dibbavihāro cattāri rūpāvacarajjhānāni. Tesaṃ vasena nibbattitvā paṭiladdhabbattā dibbaṃ, tena dibbahetukattā dibbanti vuttanti dasseti. Dibbavihārasannissitattāti rūpāvacaracatutthajjhānena nissayapaccayena nibbattattā, tena dibbanissitaṃ dibbanti dasseti. Divusaddaṃ akkharacintakā kīḷādīsu paṭhantīti vuttaṃ “taṃ sabbaṃ saddasatthānusārena veditabba”nti. Purimā hi tayo atthā kīḷatthassa vasena, itare jutigatiatthavaseneva dassitāti.

64. ‘Because it is free from impurities’—here, the mind and so forth are the impurities, or the defilements that obstruct the kamma that generates them. The kasiṇa object, the preparatory work, the jhāna, the arising of the kasiṇa state, the fourteen kinds of mental mastery, and the directing towards higher knowledge—because the power of energy is of great assistance in all these, it is said, ‘born of the power of the development of energy.’ ‘Because it is like the divine’—what exists in the divine realms is divine, like the aforementioned sensitive eye; ‘like the divine’ means divine, supreme higher knowledge. The divine abidings are the four rūpa-sphere jhānas. Because it arises dependent on these and must be attained, it is called divine. Thus, it is shown that it is called divine because it has a divine cause. ‘Because it depends on divine abidings’—since it arises supported by the fourth rūpa-sphere jhāna as a dependence condition, it is shown to be called divine because it relies on the divine. Those who analyze words read the word ‘divu’ in the sense of play and so forth; thus, it is said, ‘all that should be understood according to the science of words.’ The first three meanings are shown in relation to the meaning of play, while the others are shown in relation to the meaning of radiance and movement.

Manussūpacāranti manussagocaraṃ. Daṭṭhuṃ na sakkā ittarakhaṇattā khaṇapaccuppannassa. “Āsannacutikā”tiādinā [V 0.0092] santatipaccuppannavasena “cavamāne upapajjamāne”ti vuttanti dasseti. “Mohanissandayuttattā”tiādinā sattānaṃ hīnapaṇītattādibhāvassa mohādikammanidānahetukataṃ, nissandaphalatañca dasseti. Dibbacakkhussa pādakaṃ etesanti dibbacakkhupādakāni. Tena vuttaṃ “dibbacakkhunā saheva ijjhantī”ti. Tāni hissa paribhaṇḍañāṇāni.

‘The sphere of humans’ means the range of humans. It cannot be seen because of its momentary nature, being present for only a moment. The phrase ‘those near death’ and so forth shows that ‘passing away and being reborn’ is said in terms of the continuous present. The phrase ‘connected with the outflow of delusion’ and so forth shows the state of beings as inferior, superior, and so on, its cause being delusion and so forth as the origin of kamma, and also its nature as an outflow-result. These are the foundations for the divine eye; hence, they are called ‘the supports for the divine eye.’ Therefore, it is said, ‘They are accomplished together with the divine eye.’ For these are his accessory knowledges.

Samādīyantīti samādānāni, kammāni samādānāni etesanti kammasamādānā. Samādātabbanānāvidhakammāti attho purime atthe, dutiye pana kammāni samādāpentīti kammasamādānā, micchādiṭṭhiyā kammasamādānā micchādiṭṭhikammasamādānā, hetuatthe cetaṃ karaṇavacanaṃ.

‘They undertake,’ thus they are undertakings; ‘actions are undertakings for them,’ thus they are ‘those for whom actions are undertakings’ (kammasamādānā). The meaning in the first case is ‘various actions to be undertaken.’ In the second case, however, ‘they cause actions to be undertaken,’ thus they are ‘those who cause actions to be undertaken’ (kammasamādānā). ‘Undertakings of actions by wrong view’ becomes ‘undertakings of actions due to wrong view’ (micchādiṭṭhikammasamādānā). This instrumental expression is in the sense of cause.

Taṃ vācanti taṃ ariyānaṃ upavadanavācaṃ. Taṃ cittanti samuṭṭhāpakacittaṃ. Taṃ diṭṭhinti yena micchāgāhena ariye anuddhaṃseti, micchābhinivesaṃ. Ayampettha attho – yathā nāma hetusampannassa bhikkhuno visuddhaṃ sīlaṃ, samādhiñca sampādetvā ṭhitassa dandho satuppādo khippābhiññāya diṭṭheva dhamme aññā, sati vā upādisese anāgāmitā. Evamevaṃ yo ariyūpavādī yathāvuttacittadiṭṭhīhi apakkamitvā appatirūpaṃ sabhāvaṃ “mayā, bhante, tumhākaṃ upari vutta’nti accayadesanāya te na khamāpeti, so kāyassa bhedā niraye evāti. Tesu pasannacittassa khamāpanañhettha tesaṃ vācādīnaṃ pahānaṃ paṭinissaggova. Ito sāvajjataraṃ nāma aññaṃ natthi sabbānatthavidhānato, sabbahitasukhaparidhaṃsanato ca.

‘That speech’ means that speech of reviling the noble ones. ‘That mind’ means the mind that gives rise to it. ‘That view’ means the wrong adherence, the wrong grasping by which one disparages the noble ones. Here, the meaning is this: just as for a bhikkhu endowed with the causes, who stands having perfected pure virtue and concentration, though the arising of mindfulness be slow, there is swift direct knowledge, final knowledge in this very life, or, if there is a remainder of clinging, non-returning; just so, one who reviles the noble ones, not having departed from the aforementioned mind and view, and due to his unsuitable nature does not ask for their forgiveness by confessing his transgression, saying, “Venerable sirs, I have spoken against you,” he, upon the breakup of the body, is indeed in hell. For one with a confident mind, asking for forgiveness is here the very abandoning and relinquishing of those words and so on. There is nothing more blameworthy than this, because it brings about all harm and destroys all welfare and happiness.

Kāyassa [M 0.0098] bhedāti idha kāyasaddo attabhāvapariyāyoti āha “upādinnakkhandhapariccāgā”ti. Tadanantaranti tassa maraṇasaṅkhātassa khandhapariccāgassa anantaraṃ. Abhinibbattakkhandhattho parasaddo, anorimabhūtavatthuvisayo vā siyā, avadhivisesanamattaṃ vā. Tesu purimaṃ sandhāyāha “abhinibbattakkhandhaggahaṇe”ti, pacchimassa pana vasena “cutito uddha”nti.

‘Upon the breakup of the body’—here, the word ‘body’ is a synonym for individual existence, thus he says, ‘the abandoning of the clung-to aggregates.’ ‘Immediately after that’ means immediately after that abandonment of the aggregates, which is called death. The word ‘after’ (para) has the meaning of newly arisen aggregates, or it might be the sphere of a thing not on this shore, or it might be merely a specification of a limit. Referring to the former, he says, ‘in the grasping of the newly arisen aggregates,’ but with reference to the latter, ‘upward from cessation.’

Vuttavipariyāyenāti “suṭṭhu caritaṃ, sobhanaṃ vā carita”ntiādinā. Hanananti ghātanaṃ.

‘By the opposite of what was said’ means by way of ‘well-practiced conduct’ or ‘excellent conduct,’ and so on. ‘Striking’ means killing.

Kāraṇākāraṇanti ṭhānāṭṭhānaṃ. Cetanācetanāsampayuttadhamme nirayādinibbānagāmipaṭipadābhūte kammanti gahetvā āha “kammaparicchedamevā”ti. Kammavipākantaraṃ kammavipākaviseso kammavipākassa vibhāgo. Appetuṃ na sakkoti aṭṭhamanavamabalāni viya, taṃsadisaṃ iddhividhañāṇaṃ viya vikubbituṃ, etenassa balasadisatañca nivāreti. Jhānādiñāṇaṃ viya vā appetuṃ[V 0.0093], vikubbituñca. Yadipi hi jhānādipaccavekkhaṇāñāṇaṃ idha chaṭṭhaṃ balanti tassa savitakkasavicāratā vuttā, tathāpi jhānādīhi vinā paccavekkhaṇā natthīti jhānādisahagataṃ ñāṇaṃ tadantogadhaṃ katvā evaṃ vuttaṃ. Atha vā sabbaññutaññāṇaṃ jhānādikiccaṃ viya na sabbaṃ balakiccaṃ kātuṃ sakkotīti dassetuṃ “jhānaṃ hutvā appetuṃ, iddhi hutvā vikubbituñca na sakkotī”ti (vibha. mūlaṭī. 831) vuttaṃ, na pana kassaci balassa jhānaiddhibhāvoti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

‘Cause and non-cause’ means the possible and the impossible. Taking ‘kamma’ as volition and phenomena associated with volition, which have become the path leading to hell, etc., and to Nibbāna, he says, ‘this is simply the delimitation of kamma.’ A distinction in kamma-result is a specific kind of kamma-result, a division of kamma-result. One cannot apply it—like the eighth or ninth powers—nor transform it, like the knowledge of the kinds of psychic power; by this, its equality with those powers is prevented. Or, like the knowledge of jhāna and so on, one cannot apply or transform it. For even though the reviewing knowledge of jhāna and so on is here called the sixth power, and its being accompanied by thought and examination is stated, still, without jhāna and so on, there is no reviewing. Thus, the knowledge associated with jhāna and so on is included within it, and so it is said. Alternatively, to show that the knowledge of omniscience, like the function of jhāna and so on, cannot perform all the functions of the powers, it is said, ‘one cannot apply it by becoming jhāna, nor transform it by becoming psychic power.’ But it should be understood that no power has the nature of jhāna or psychic power.

Vicayahārasampātavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Explanation of the Confluence of the Method of Investigation is concluded.

3. Yuttihārasampātavaṇṇanā

3. The Explanation of the Confluence of the Method of Reasoning

65. Paṭipakkhapaṭibāḷhā kusalā dhammā uppajjitumeva na sakkonti, uppannāpi sammadeva attano kiccaṃ kātuṃ asamatthatāya anuppannasadisāti paṭipakkhanivāraṇena kusalānaṃ dhammānaṃ kiccakaraṇabhāvaṃ dassetuṃ “manacchaṭṭhāni…pe… bhavissatī”ti vuttaṃ. Viharantassāti viharaṇahetu. Viharantoti etthāpi eseva nayo, tena rakkhitacittatā vuttanayena ekantato sammāsaṅkappagocaratāya ca saṃvattatīti dasseti. Vuttanayenāti [M 0.0099] micchāsaṅkappānaṃ avasaraṃ adatvā visodhitanekkhammādivitakkatāya. Aviparītamevāti kāyādiasubhādito ādānaṃ. Vinipātabhayanti duggatibhayaṃ. Sabbopi cāyamatthoti “sammāsaṅkappagocaro sammādiṭṭhi bhavissatī”tiādīsu attho yuttiyā yutto eva anarūpakāraṇabhāvato.

65. Wholesome states, being oppressed by opposing factors, are simply unable to arise; and even if they have arisen, they are like unarisen states due to their inability to properly perform their own function. To show that wholesome states perform their function by preventing opposition, it is said, ‘with mind as the sixth… will be.’ ‘For one dwelling’ means the cause for dwelling. ‘Dwelling’ here also follows the same method; by this, it is shown that guardedness of mind, in the manner stated, entirely conduces to the sphere of right thought. ‘By the stated method’ means by the state of having purified thoughts of renunciation and so on, without giving opportunity to wrong thoughts. ‘Not inverted’ means the taking up of the body, etc., as impure, etc. ‘Fear of ruin’ means fear of a bad destination. And all this meaning, in statements like ‘one whose sphere is right thought will have right view,’ etc., is indeed connected by reason, because of its nature as a not-unsuitable cause.

Yuttihārasampātavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Explanation of the Confluence of the Method of Reasoning is concluded.

4. Padaṭṭhānahārasampātādivaṇṇanā

4. The Explanation of the Confluence of the Method of Proximate Cause, etc.

66. Yasmā vā saṃkilesato rakkhitacittassa tīṇi sucaritāni pāripūriṃ gacchanti, tasmā rakkhitacittassāti ettha yāyaṃ rakkhitacittatā, sā kāyasucaritādīnaṃ tiṇṇaṃ sucaritānaṃ padaṭṭhānanti evamettha attho veditabbo. Attādhīnanti attaparādhīnaṃ.

66. Because the three kinds of good conduct reach fulfillment for one whose mind is guarded from defilements, therefore, concerning ‘for one whose mind is guarded,’ here, that state of guarding the mind is the proximate cause for the three kinds of good conduct, such as bodily good conduct. This is how the meaning should be understood here. ‘Self-dependent’ means dependent on self and others.

Tato evāti kāraṇaggahaṇena phalassa gahitattā eva.

‘From that’ means that because the cause is grasped, the result is grasped.

68. Tattha [V 0.0094] itisaddoti “paripālīyatī”ti itisaddo.

68. Here, the word ‘iti’ is the word ‘iti’ in ‘paripālīyati’.

73-4. Pāḷiyaṃ pañcindriyāni tīhi khandhehi saṅgahitānīti ettha saddhāvīriyasatindriyehi pātimokkhādi tividhaṃ sīlaṃ gahitaṃ sodhetabbattā. Tesanti tehi sīlakkhandho saṅgahito. Samādhipaññindriyehi samādhipaññākkhandhā gahitāti pākaṭoyamattho, tathā sesampīti āha “ito paresu…pe… vuttanayamevā”ti.

73-4. In the Pāli, where it is said that the five faculties are included in three aggregates, here, by the faculties of faith, energy, and mindfulness, the threefold virtue of the Pātimokkha, etc., is included, because it is to be purified. By them, the aggregate of virtue is included. The aggregates of concentration and wisdom are included by the faculties of concentration and wisdom—this meaning is obvious. Similarly, for the remainder, he says: “In those that follow… as stated above.”

76. Hetuhetusamuppannapaccayapaccayuppannasaṅkhātassāti ettha hetupaccayavibhāgo heṭṭhā vuttoyeva.

76. Of that which is designated as “cause, arisen from a cause, condition, and arisen from a condition”—herein, the analysis of cause and condition has already been stated below.

Padaṭṭhānahārasampātādivaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The explanation of the confluence of strands concerning the proximate cause, etc., is concluded.

Missakahārasampātavaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Confluence of Mixed Strands.

Idāni yasmā suttesu hārānaṃ yojanānayadassanattā hārasampātadesanā hāravibhaṅgadesanā viya na hārasarūpamattadassanattā, tasmā peṭakopadese [M 0.0100] āgatanayānusārena aparehi vipariyāyehi hārasampātayojanāvidhiṃ dassento “apicā”tiādimāha. Tattha vijjāvijjāya kusalākusalacittappavattiyā alobhādosalobhadosāpi paramparabhāvena pavattanti nidānabhāvatoti dassento “cha dhammā…pe… mūlānī”ti āha. Yathā ca nidānabhāvena pubbaṅgamatā, evaṃ attano vasevattanenāpi pubbaṅgamatā labbhatevāti vuttaṃ “sādhipatikānaṃ adhipati, sabbacittuppādānaṃ indriyānī”ti. Alobhassāti alobhayuttassa cittuppādassa. Nekkhammacchandenāti kusalacchandena. Nekkhammasaddo pabbajjādīsu niruḷho. Vuttañhi –

Now, because in the suttas the method of applying the strands is seen through the teaching on the confluence of strands and the teaching on the analysis of strands, and not merely by showing the nature of the strands themselves, therefore, following the method that comes in the Peṭakopadesa, he shows the method of applying the confluence of strands with other variations, beginning with “furthermore.” Herein, showing that by means of knowledge and ignorance, and by means of the occurrence of wholesome and unwholesome consciousness, even non-greed, non-hatred, greed, and hatred proceed in succession due to being a cause, he says, “six states… roots.” And just as there is precedence due to being a cause, so too is precedence obtained by their own dominance—this is stated thus: “for those with a dominant factor, the dominant factor; for all arisings of consciousness, the faculties.” “Of non-greed” means of an arising of consciousness associated with non-greed. “With the desire for renunciation” means with wholesome desire. The term “renunciation” is established in contexts such as going forth. For it is said:

“Pabbajjā paṭhamaṃ jhānaṃ, nibbānañca vipassanā;

Sabbepi kusalā dhammā, ‘nekkhamma’nti pavuccare”ti. (itivu. aṭṭha. 109; dī. ni. ṭī. 2.359; a. ni. ṭī. 2.2.66) –

“Going forth, the first jhāna, Nibbāna, and insight; all wholesome states are called ‘renunciation.’”

Tesu [V 0.0095] idha kusalā dhammā adhippetā. Tena vuttaṃ “kusalacchandenā”ti. Nekkhammacchandena upanissayabhūtena, na adhipatibhūtena. Idaṃ vuttaṃ hoti – alobhappadhāno ce cittuppādo hoti, nekkhammacchandena upanissayabhūtena mano tassa pubbaṅgamo hoti. Sesapadadvayepi eseva nayo.

Among these, wholesome states are intended here. Therefore, it is said, “with wholesome desire.” With the desire for renunciation as a supporting condition, not as a dominant one. This is what is said: if an arising of consciousness predominated by non-greed occurs, the mind, with the desire for renunciation as its supporting condition, becomes its forerunner. The same principle applies to the remaining two pairs of terms as well.

Yadaggena tesaṃ dhammānaṃ mano pubbaṅgamaṃ, tadaggena tesaṃ jeṭṭhaṃ, padhānañcāti vuttaṃ “manoseṭṭhāti mano tesaṃ dhammāna”ntiādi. Manomayatā manena katādibhāvo, so ca manassa tesaṃ sahajātādinā paccayabhāvo evāti vuttaṃ “manomayāti…pe… paccayo”ti. Te panāti ettha pana-saddo visesatthadīpako, tenetaṃ dasseti – yadipi tesaṃ dhammānaṃ chandādayopi paccayā eva, indriyādipaccayena pana savisesaṃ paccayabhūtassa manasseva vaseneva vuttaṃ “manomayā”ti. Tattha chandasamudānītāti yathāvuttanekkhammādichandena sammā uddhamuddhaṃ nītā, tato samudāgatāti attho. Tato eva nekkhammavitakkādito samuppannattā anāvilasaṅkappasamuṭṭhānā. Tajjāmanoviññāṇadhātusamphassena sahādhiṭṭhānato phassasamodhānā. “Phuṭṭho, bhikkhave, vedeti, phuṭṭho sañjānāti, phuṭṭho cetetī”ti (saṃ. ni. 4.93) hi vuttaṃ. Idaṃ manokammanti kāyaṅgavācaṅgacopanaṃ akatvā saddhāsamannāgatena pasannena manasā pavattaṃ idaṃ kusalaṃ manokammaṃ. Taṃ pana [M 0.0101] anabhijjhāsahagataṃ, abyāpādasahagataṃ, sammādiṭṭhisahagatanti tividhaṃ hoti.

By whatever foremost factor the mind is the forerunner of those states, by that foremost factor it is said to be their chief and principal: “Mind is the chief of states,” and so on. “Mind-made” means the state of being created by the mind, and that is precisely the mind’s conditionality for those states through co-nascence, etc.; thus it is said: “Mind-made… condition.” Here, the word “pana” indicates a special meaning; thus it is shown that—although desire and so forth are also conditions for those states—it is said “mind-made” specifically on account of the mind, which has become a condition with distinction through the condition of the faculties, etc. Therein, “brought together by desire” means rightly led higher and higher by the aforesaid desire for renunciation, etc.; from that it has come together, this is the meaning. Because of that, arising from thoughts of renunciation and so forth, they are of unclouded intention and origin. There is a confluence of contact due to co-establishment with the contact of the mind-consciousness-element born of that. For it is said: “Touched, bhikkhus, one feels; touched, one perceives; touched, one intends.” This is mental action—this wholesome mental action that proceeds in a mind endowed with faith and serenity, without agitating the bodily or verbal members. It is, moreover, threefold: accompanied by non-covetousness, accompanied by non-ill will, and accompanied by right view.

Bhāsatīti avisaṃvādanādinā vācaṅgacopanāvasena pavattentiyā vacīviññattiyā sādhetabbaṃ sādhetīti attho, tena kāyadvārato pavattakusalavacīkammampi saṅgahitaṃ hoti. Tathā hi vakkhati “vacīviññattivipphārato, tathā sādiyanato ca bhāsatī”ti. Sabbampi vacīkammaṃ saccādivasena catubbidhaṃ. Karotīti attano, paresañca hitāhitāni kāraṇākāraṇehi kāyaṅgacopanāvasena pavattentiyā kāyaviññattiyā sādhetabbaṃ sādhetīti attho, tena vacīdvārato pavattakusalakāyakammampi saṅgahitaṃ hoti. Tathā ca vakkhati “kāyaviññattivipphārato, tathā sādiyanato ca karotī”ti (netti. aṭṭha. 76 missakahārasampātavaṇṇanā). Kammapathavasena gayhamāne pāṇātipātādivasena taṃ tividhaṃ hoti. Tenāha “iti dasakusalakammapathādassitā”ti. Dasapuññakiriyavatthuvasenāpi gāthāya attho yujjati. Tathā hi vakkhati “so pasannacitto”tiādi. Bhāsati vā karoti vā kevalaṃ manasā pavattatīti aniyamattho vā-saddo. Tathā ceva saṃvaṇṇitaṃ.

“He speaks” means that one accomplishes what is to be accomplished by means of verbal intimation, which proceeds by way of agitating the speech members through non-deception and so forth. By this, wholesome verbal action proceeding from the body door is also included. For it will be said: “He speaks through the expansion of verbal intimation and likewise through assent.” All verbal action is fourfold by way of truthfulness and so forth. “He acts” means that one accomplishes what is to be accomplished by means of bodily intimation, which proceeds by way of agitating the bodily members regarding the benefit and harm of oneself and others, through causes and non-causes. By this, wholesome bodily action proceeding from the speech door is also included. And so it will be said: “He acts through the expansion of bodily intimation and likewise through assent.” When taken in terms of the path of action, it is threefold, by way of abstaining from killing living beings, and so forth. Hence it is said: “Thus, the ten wholesome paths of action are shown.” The meaning of the verse also fits by way of the ten bases of meritorious action. For it will be said: “He with a serene mind,” and so on. The word “vā” has an unrestricted meaning: “he speaks, or he acts, or it proceeds solely by mind.” And it is explained in just that way.

Dasavidhassa kusalakammassāti dasavidhassa kusalakammapathakammassa, vakkhamānanayena vā dasapuññakiriyavatthusaṅkhātassa [V 0.0096] kusalakammassa. Nanu tattha dānādimayaṃ tividhameva puññakiriyavatthu vuttanti? Saccaṃ, taṃ pana itaresaṃ tadantogadhattā.

“Of the tenfold wholesome kamma” means of the kamma of the tenfold wholesome path of action or, according to the method that will be explained, of the wholesome kamma designated as the ten bases of meritorious action. But is it not said there that the bases of meritorious action are just threefold, consisting of generosity and so forth? True, but that is because the others are included within them.

“Sukhamanvetī”ti saṅkhepena vuttaṃ sukhānugamaṃ vitthārena dassento “idhassu puriso”tiādimāha. Tattha evaṃ santanti evaṃ bhūtaṃ, appahīnānusayo hutvā sukhavedanīyaphassasambhūtanti attho.

To show in detail the “following of happiness,” which was stated concisely as “happiness follows,” he said what begins with “here, a certain person.” Therein, “evaṃ santaṃ” means “being thus”; the meaning is that, having not abandoned the underlying tendencies, it is arisen from contact that is to be experienced as pleasant.

Tattha “yaṃ mano”tiādinā gāthātthavasena catusaccaṃ niddhāreti. Ādito vavatthāpitesu khandhādīsu khandhamukhena saccānaṃ kathitattā sattānaṃ bhinnarucibhāvato nānānayehi vipassanābhūmikosallatthaṃ, pubbāparasambandhadassanatthañca evaṃ vuttaṃ “evaṃ…pe… niddhāretabbānī”ti. Saccamukhena assādādike niddhāretvā desanāhārasampātaṃ yojetuṃ “tattha samudayenā”tiādimāha, taṃ vuttanayameva. Yañhettha aññampi atthato na vibhattaṃ, taṃ heṭṭhā vuttanayattā, uttānatthattā cāti veditabbaṃ.

Herein, he determines the four truths according to the meaning of the verse beginning with “yaṃ mano.” Since the aggregates and so forth were established at the beginning, and because the truths are spoken of with the aggregates as the principal point, and due to the diverse inclinations of beings, it is said, “thus… should be determined,” for the sake of skill in the grounds of insight through various methods and for the sake of showing the connection between what precedes and what follows. Having determined gratification and so forth by way of the truths, in order to apply the confluence of strands of the teaching, he says what begins with “therein, by the origin,” and so on. That is by the method already stated. And whatever else here is not analyzed in terms of its meaning, that should be understood as being according to the method stated previously and because its meaning is plain.

Mananalakkhaṇeti [M 0.0102] mananalakkhaṇahetu. “Mananalakkhaṇenā”ti vā pāṭho. Īhābhāvato byāpārābhāvato. Yena pasādena samannāgatattā mano “pasanno”ti vutto, tassa pasādassa kiccaṃ mane āropetvā āha “akālusiyato, ārammaṇassa okappanato ca pasannenā”ti. Tathā sādiyanatoti vācāya vattabbaṃ avatvāva phassasādiyanato anujānato. Dutiye tathā sādiyanatoti kāyena kātabbaṃ yathā kataṃ hoti, tathā vācāya saṃvidhānato. Tathā pasutattāti yathā sukhamanveti, tathā upacitattā evāti attho. Tatoti tato kāraṇā, manasā pasannena, bhāsanena, karaṇena ca hetunāti vuttaṃ hoti. Anaññatthāti etasmiṃ pana atthe. Tatoti tato eva. Yo hi pasannamano tena yaṃ bhāsanaṃ, karaṇañca, tato eva naṃ sukhamanvetīti vuttaṃ hoti. Sātabhāvatoti sātavedanābhāvato. Iṭṭhabhāvatoti manāpabhāvato. Kammato vipākuppattiphaladānasamatthabhāvena kammassa nibbattattā vipākasseva anibbattattāti āha “katū…pe… anvetīti vutta”nti kāraṇāyatta vuttitoti katabhāvahetukattā kammassāti adhippāyo. Asaṅkantitoti yasmiṃ santāne kammaṃ nibbattaṃ, tadaññasantānā saṅkamanato.

'Characterized by minding' means the cause is the characteristic of minding. Alternatively, the reading is 'by the characteristic of minding.' Due to the absence of exertion, the absence of activity. Because it is endowed with serenity, the mind is called 'serene.' Having attributed the function of that serenity to the mind, it is said, 'due to non-turbidity, and due to inclination towards the object.' Or, 'by approving thus' means by assenting through approving contact, without yet saying what should be said by speech. In the second case, 'by approving thus' means arranging by speech how something to be done by the body is done. 'Because it is thus engaged' means because it is thus accumulated, just as happiness follows. 'From that' means from that cause; it is said to be by the cause of a serene mind, of speaking, and of acting. 'Not elsewhere' means in this very sense. 'From that' means from that very thing. For one whose mind is serene, from whatever speech or action there is, from that very thing happiness follows him—this is what is said. 'Because it is pleasant' means because of the nature of pleasant feeling. 'Because it is agreeable' means because of the nature of being pleasing. 'From kamma'—because kamma is produced with the capacity to give the fruit of resultant-production, while the result itself is not yet produced, he says, 'done... follows.' This is said because it is dependent on a cause, the intention being that kamma is the cause of the state of having been done. 'Not transferred' means because the kamma that is produced in one continuum does not transfer to another.

Ādhipaccayogatoti sahajātādhipativasena ādhipaccayuttattā. Sahajātadhammānañhi taṃsampayuttassa manassa vasena pubbaṅgamatā idhādhippetā. Tato evāti ādhipaccayogato eva. Manassāti upayogatthe sāmivacanaṃ. Tesaṃ dhammānanti sambandho. Kusalabhāvo yujjati pasādassa yonisomanasikārahetukattā[V 0.0097]. Nanu vibhajjabyākaraṇesu tesaṃ sāvakānaṃ saddhā uppajjatīti? Nāyaṃ saddhā, tadākārā pana akusalā dhammā tathā vuccantīti veditabbaṃ. Tathā hi vakkhati “nāyaṃ pasādo”tiādi (netti. aṭṭha. 76 missakahārasampātavaṇṇanā). Sukhaṃ anvetīti yujjati kammassa phaladāne samatthabhāvato. Yathā hi kataṃ kammaṃ phaladānasamatthaṃ hoti, tathā kataṃ upacitanti vuccatīti.

'Due to connection with predominance' means because of being connected with predominance by way of co-nascent predominance. For here, the forerunner-state of the mind associated with those co-nascent states is intended. 'From that very reason' means from the very connection with predominance. 'Manassa' is the genitive case in the sense of relation. The connection is 'of those states.' The wholesome nature is appropriate because serenity has wise attention as its cause. But is it not said in the analytical expositions that faith arises in those disciples? This is not faith; rather, unwholesome states of a similar kind are to be understood as being spoken of thus. For it will be stated: 'This is not serenity,' and so on (Netti. Aṭṭha. 76, Missakahārasampātavaṇṇanā). 'Happiness follows it' is appropriate because of kamma's capacity to give fruit. For just as kamma, when done, is capable of giving fruit, so what is done is called 'accumulated.'

Manopavicārā idha nekkhammasitā somanassūpavicārā, upekkhūpavicārā ca veditabbā kusalādhikārattā. Te pana yasmā cittaṃ nissāyeva pavattanti, nānissāya, tasmā vuttaṃ “mano manopavicārānaṃ padaṭṭhāna”nti. Kusalapakkhassa padaṭṭhānanti ettha kusalo tāva phasso kusalassa vedanākkhandhassa [M 0.0103] saññākkhandhassa saṅkhārakkhandhassa sahajātādinā paccayo hoti. “Phuṭṭho, bhikkhave, vedeti, phuṭṭho sañjānāti, phuṭṭho cetetī”ti (saṃ. ni. 4.93) vuttaṃ. Evaṃ vedanādīnampi veditabbaṃ. Saddhādīnampi paccayabhāve vattabbameva natthi. Sabbassāti catubhūmakassa. Kāmāvacarā hi kusalā dhammā yathārahaṃ catubhūmakassāpi kusalassa paccayā honti, evaṃ itarabhūmakāpi.

Here, mental investigations should be understood as those based on renunciation accompanied by joy, and those accompanied by equanimity, because the context is the wholesome. But since they arise only in dependence on the mind, not independently, it is therefore said, 'Mind is the proximate cause for mental investigations.' Regarding 'the proximate cause of the wholesome side': here, wholesome contact is a condition, by way of co-nascence and so on, for the wholesome aggregate of feeling, the aggregate of perception, and the aggregate of formations. For it is said: 'Having been contacted, bhikkhus, one feels; having been contacted, one perceives; having been contacted, one intends' (Saṃ. Ni. 4.93). This should be understood in the same way for feeling and the others. There is no need to even mention that faith and the others are conditions. 'Of all' means of the four planes. For wholesome phenomena of the sense-sphere, as is appropriate, are conditions for wholesome phenomena even of the four planes; likewise for those of the other planes.

“Pasannena manasā bhāsatī”ti vuttattā visesato sammāvācāpaccayaṃ bhāsanaṃ idhādhippetanti vuttaṃ “bhāsatīti sammāvācā”ti. Tatthāyamadhippāyo “bhāsatīti yamidaṃ padaṃ, iminā sammāvācā gahitā hotī”ti. Karotīti sammākammantoti etthāpi eseva nayo. Suparisuddhe kāyavacīkamme ṭhitassa ājīvapārisuddhi, na itarassāti vuttaṃ “te sammāājīvassa padaṭṭhāna”nti. Tattha teti sammāvācākammantā. Yasmā pana ājīvaṭṭhamake sīle patiṭṭhitassa uppannānuppannānaṃ akusaladhammānaṃ pahānānuppādanāni, anuppannuppannānaṃ kusaladhammānaṃ uppādanapāripūriyā ca sambhavanti, tathā sammāvāyāme ṭhitasseva kāyādīsu subhasaññādividdhaṃsinī sammāsati sambhavati, tasmā vuttaṃ “sammāājīvo…pe…padaṭṭhāna”nti. Jeṭṭhakasīlaṃ pātimokkhasaṃvaro, saddhāsādhano ca soti āha “taṃ sīlassa padaṭṭhāna”nti.

Because it is said, 'One speaks with a serene mind,' the act of speaking, which is a condition for right speech, is especially intended here; thus it is stated, 'Speaking is right speech.' Herein, the intention is: 'By this word, "speaks," right speech is taken.' 'One acts' is right action; here too, the same method applies. For one established in supremely purified bodily and verbal action, there is purity of livelihood, not for another; thus it is said, 'they are the proximate cause of right livelihood.' There, 'they' refers to right speech and right action. Furthermore, because for one established in the virtue with livelihood as the eighth factor, there occurs the abandoning of arisen unwholesome states and the non-arising of unarisen ones, and the arising of unarisen wholesome states and the bringing to fulfillment of arisen ones; and likewise, for one established in right effort, right mindfulness arises, which destroys the perception of beauty and so on in regard to the body, etc.; therefore it is said, 'right livelihood... is the proximate cause.' The chief virtue is the Pātimokkha restraint, and it is accomplished by faith; thus he says, 'that is the proximate cause of virtue.'

Tesanti kāyavacīkammānaṃ. Kammapaccayatāyāti kusalakammahetukatāya.

'Of them' means of bodily and verbal actions. 'By being a kamma condition' means by having wholesome kamma as a cause.

Padattho ca vuttanayenāti “mananato ārammaṇavijānanato”tiādinā.

And the meaning of the word is in the manner already stated, beginning with 'from minding, from cognizing the object.'

Ayaṃ [V 0.0098] āvaṭṭoti ayaṃ sabhāgavisabhāgadhammāvaṭṭanavasena āvaṭṭo. Ettha hi kusalamūlasammattamaggādiniddhāraṇā sabhāgadhammāvaṭṭanā. Avijjābhavataṇhānaṃ niddhāraṇā visabhāgadhammāvaṭṭanā.

'This is the cycle' means this is a cycle by way of the revolving of similar and dissimilar phenomena. Here, the determination of wholesome roots, the right path, and so on, is the revolving of similar phenomena. The determination of ignorance, existence, and craving is the revolving of dissimilar phenomena.

Vibhattihāre padaṭṭhānabhūmivibhāgā vuttanayā, suviññeyyā cāti dhammavibhāgameva dassento “nayida”ntiādimāha. Tattha “nayidaṃ yathārutavasena gahetabba”nti suttassa neyyatthataṃ vatvā “yohī”tiādinā taṃ vivarati. “Dukkhameva anvetī”ti kasmā vuttaṃ, nanu yattha katthaci hitesitā kusalamevāti? Nayidamīdisaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ, adhammaṃ pana dhammoti, dhammañca pana [M 0.0104] adhammoti dīpanena lokassa sabbānatthabījabhūtesu sakalahitasukhupāyapaṭikkhepakesu titthakaresu asantaguṇasambhāvanavasena pavattamicchādhimokkhaṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ. Yo hi loke appamattakampi puññaṃ kātukāmaṃ pāpikaṃ diṭṭhiṃ nissāya paṭibāhati, sopi gārayho, kimaṅgaṃ pana ariyavinaye sammāpaṭipattiṃ paṭibāhantesūti dukkhaphalāva tattha sambhāvanāpasaṃsā payirupāsanā. Tathā hi vuttaṃ – “na kho ahaṃ, moghapurisa, arahattassa maccharāyāmi, apica tuyhevetaṃ pāpakaṃ diṭṭhigataṃ…pe… dīgharattaṃ ahitāya dukkhāya saṃvattatī”ti (dī. ni. 3.7), “yo nindiyaṃ pasaṃsati (su. ni. 663; saṃ. ni. 1.180, 181; a. ni. 4.3; netti. 92), sabbassāpi anatthassa mūlaṃ bālūpasevanā”ti ca.

In the Mode of Distribution, the divisions of the proximate cause and its ground are in the manner already stated and are easily understood. Thus, showing only the division of phenomena, he says, 'Not this,' and so on. There, having stated that the sutta's meaning is one that needs to be inferred by saying, 'This should not be taken according to the literal wording,' he then explains it with 'For whoever,' and so on. Why is it said, 'Suffering alone follows'? Is it not the case that wherever there is a desire for welfare, it is only wholesome? This was not said with reference to such a case. Rather, it was said with reference to the wrong resolve that proceeds by way of esteeming non-existent qualities in heretical teachers—who are the seed of all that is unbeneficial in the world and who reject all means to welfare and happiness by presenting non-Dhamma as Dhamma and Dhamma as non-Dhamma. For if someone in the world who wishes to do even a small amount of merit is obstructed by relying on a wrong view, he is blameworthy; how much more so those who obstruct right practice in the noble discipline? Therefore, in that case, esteeming, praising, and associating with them result only in suffering. For thus it is said: 'It is not, foolish man, that I am jealous of your arahantship, but it is this evil view of yours... that will lead to your harm and suffering for a long time' (Dī. Ni. 3.7). And: 'Whoever praises one who is blameworthy (Su. Ni. 663; Saṃ. Ni. 1.180, 181; A. Ni. 4.3; Netti. 92)... association with fools is the root of all that is unbeneficial.'

Idañhi suttanti “manopubbaṅgamā…pe…pada”nti (dha. pa. 1, 2) paṭhamaṃ gāthaṃ sandhāya vadati. Etassāti saṃvaṇṇiyamānasuttassa.

'This discourse'—he says this with reference to the first verse, 'Mind is the forerunner of phenomena... (Dhammapada 1, 2).' 'Of this' means of the discourse that is being explained.

Kiccapaññattīti adhipatipaccayasaṅkhātassa kiccassa paññāpanaṃ. Padhānapaññattīti padhānabhāvassa paññāpanā. Sahajātapaññattīti tesaṃ dhammānaṃ manasā sahabhāvapaññāpanā.

'Designation of function' is the designation of the function known as the dominance condition. 'Designation of the principal' is the designation of the state of being primary. 'Designation of co-nascence' is the designation of the state of co-existence of those phenomena with the mind.

Mahābhūtātīti itisaddo ādiattho, tena mahābhūtāvinābhāvī sabbo rūpadhammo saṅgayhati.

In 'mahābhūtāti,' the word 'iti' has the sense of 'and so on'; thereby, all material phenomena that are inseparable from the great elements are included.

“Manopubbaṅgamā”ti samāsapade “mano”ti padaṃ tadavayavamattanti āha “neva padasuddhī”ti. Tenevāha “manopubbaṅgamāti padasuddhī”ti. “Chāyāva anapāyinī”ti idaṃ sukhānugamassa udāharaṇamattaṃ, na yathādhippetatthaparisamāpanaṃ. “Sukhamanvetī”ti pana yathādhippetatthaparisamāpananti vuttaṃ “padasuddhi ceva ārambhasuddhi cā”ti.

Regarding the compound 'Manopubbaṅgamā', because the word 'mano' is merely a component thereof, he says, 'it is not merely purity of the word.' Therefore he says, 'the phrase “manopubbaṅgamā” is purity of the word.' 'Like a shadow that never departs' is merely an example of happiness following; it is not the completion of the intended meaning. However, 'happiness follows' is the completion of the intended meaning, as it is said, 'both purity of the word and purity of the commencement.'

Ekattatāti [V 0.0099] manopubbaṅgamādisāmaññaṃ sandhāya vadati. Evaṃ sesesupi. Vemattatā “manopubbaṅgamā”tiādinā sāmaññato vuttadhamme pasādo dhāraṇāya nivattetvā pasannasaṅkhāte visese avaṭṭhāpanato. Sesesupi eseva nayo.
