Pasādo sinehasabhāvo, assaddhiyaṃ viya lūkhasabhāvaṃ dosaṃ vinodetīti āha “byāpādavikkhambhanato”ti. Bahiddhāti saddheyyavatthuṃ sandhāyāha. Okappanatoti ārammaṇaṃ anupavisitvā anupakkhanditvā saddahanato.

'Unity' is said with reference to the generality of 'preceded by mind,' etc. The same applies to the rest. 'Diversity' is due to establishing serene faith in the specific quality called 'serene', having turned it away from the qualities spoken of in general terms, such as 'preceded by mind,' by means of its supportive function. The same method applies to the rest. Serene faith has the nature of affection; just as lack of faith has a harsh nature, it removes the fault of aversion. Thus he says, 'by the suppression of ill-will.' 'External' is said with reference to an object worthy of faith. 'Inclining' means believing by entering into and penetrating the object.

Deyyadhammādayoti [M 0.0105] ettha ādisaddena saṃvegahirottappakasiṇamaṇḍalādayo saṅgayhanti. Iṭṭhārammaṇādayoti ādisaddena iṭṭhamajjhattārammaṇā, dvāradhammā, manasikāroti evamādīnaṃ saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo. Tathā phassoti yathā vedanādīnaṃ iṭṭhārammaṇādayo paccayo, evaṃ phassopīti paccayatāsāmaññameva upasaṃharati tathā-saddo. Vedanādīnanti hi vedanādayo tayo khandhā gahitā. Viññāṇassa vedanādayoti nāmarūpaṃ sandhāya vadati.

'Regarding 'gifts, etc.' (deyyadhammādayo), here by the word 'etc.' are included things like objects that inspire spiritual urgency, moral shame, kasiṇa circles, and so on. 'Regarding 'pleasant objects, etc.' (iṭṭhārammaṇādayo), here the word 'etc.' should be understood to include pleasant and neutral objects, door-phenomena, attention, and so on. 'Similarly, contact': just as pleasant objects, etc., are a condition for feelings, etc., so too is contact. The word 'similarly' (tathā) simply summarizes the commonality of being a condition. By 'of feelings, etc.' (vedanādīnaṃ), the three aggregates beginning with feeling are taken. 'Feelings, etc., of consciousness' (viññāṇassa vedanādayo) is said with reference to name-and-form.

“Sīlamayassa adoso padaṭṭhāna”nti vuttaṃ khantipadhānattā sīlassa. Adhiṭṭhātīti anuyuñjati uppādeti. Soti evaṃ kusalacittaṃ bhāvento. “Anuppannāna”ntiādinā bhāvanāpahānasamāropanāni dassento nibbedhabhāgiyavasena gāthāya atthaṃ vicinitvā samāropeti, evampi sakkā yojetunti vāsanābhāgiyavasena padaṭṭhānaniddese udāharīyati.

'Non-aversion is the proximate cause for that which is comprised of virtue' is said because patience is the chief component of virtue. 'Adhiṭṭhāti' means one applies oneself, one generates. 'He' means one who thus develops a wholesome mind. By means of 'of the unarisen,' etc., showing the applications of development and abandonment, he investigates and applies the meaning of the verse in terms of the part pertaining to penetration. Or, 'it can also be connected thus' is exemplified in the explanation of proximate causes in terms of the part pertaining to habituation.

Evaṃ “manopubbaṅgamā dhammā”ti gāthāya vasena hārasampātayojanāvidhiṃ dassetvā idāni gāthāntarena dassetuṃ “tathā dadato puñña”ntiādimāha. Tattha bhāvanāmayanti paññābhāvanāmayaṃ.

Having thus shown the method of applying the exegetical modes by way of the verse 'Phenomena are preceded by mind,' now, to show it with another verse, he states, 'Thus, for one who gives, merit…,' etc. Therein, 'comprised of development' means comprised of the development of wisdom.

“Alobho kusalamūla”ntiādi dānādīnaṃ alobhādipadhānattā vuttaṃ, sabbattha ca “vutta”nti padaṃ ānetvā yojetabbaṃ. Tesanti rāgādīnaṃ. Nissaraṇanti ca parinibbānameva sandhāya vadati.

'Non-greed is a wholesome root,' etc., is said because non-greed and so on are the chief components of giving and so on. And everywhere, the word 'is said' should be supplied and connected. 'Of them' means of lust and the others. And 'escape' is said with reference to final Nibbāna itself.

Pariccāgasīlo alobhajjhāsayo kāmesu ādīnavadassāvī sammadeva sīlaṃ paripūretīti āha “dadato…pe… padaṭṭhāna”nti. Idha oḷārikā nāma kilesā vītikkamāvatthānaṃ, tappahānaṃ tadaṅgappahānena veditabbaṃ. Majjhimānanti pariyuṭṭhānāvatthānaṃ. Sukhumānanti anusayāvatthānaṃ. Katāvībhūmi nti khīṇāsavabhūmiṃ.

One who is virtuous in relinquishment, whose disposition is non-greed, and who sees the danger in sensual pleasures, rightly fulfills virtue. Thus he says, 'for the giver... is the proximate cause.' Here, defilements called 'coarse' are the state of transgression; their abandonment should be understood as abandonment by substitution of opposites. 'Intermediate' means the state of obsession. 'Subtle' means the state of latent tendencies. 'The stage of one who has done' means the stage of one whose taints are destroyed.

Dadatoti maggasahagatena alobhena sadevakassa lokassa abhayadānaṃ dadato. Puññanti lokuttarakusalaṃ. [V 0.0100] Saṃyamatoti maggapariyāpannehi sammāvācākammantājīvehi diṭṭhekaṭṭhādisaṃkilesato maggasaṃyamena saṃyamantassa. Veranti pāṇātipātādipāpaṃ. Kusaloti maggasammādiṭṭhiyā kusalo vicakkhaṇo. Jahāti pāpakanti tehi tehi maggehi taṃ taṃ pahātabbaṃ pāpadhammaṃ odhiso jahāti samucchindati. Tenāha “maggo vutto”ti.

'Giving' means giving the gift of fearlessness to the world with its devas by means of non-greed associated with the path. 'Merit' means supramundane wholesome action. 'By restraint' means of one restraining by the restraint of the path from defilements such as fixed wrong views, by means of right speech, right action, and right livelihood, which are included in the path. 'Enmity' means evil action such as the destruction of life, and so on. 'Skillful' means one who is skilled and discerning through the right view of the path. 'Abandons evil' means that by those respective paths, one abandons and eradicates, part by part, each evil quality that is to be abandoned. Therefore he says, 'the path is spoken of.'

“Dadato”tiādinā [M 0.0106] pubbe avibhāgena kusalamūlāni uddhaṭānīti idāni vibhāgena tāni uddharanto “lokiyakusalamūla”ntiādimāha.

By means of 'giving,' etc., the wholesome roots were previously set forth without distinction; thus, now, setting them forth with distinction, he says, 'mundane wholesome root,' etc.

Puthujjanabhūmi sekkhabhūmi dassitā pahānassa vippakatabhāvadīpanato. Asekkhabhūmi dassitā anupādāparinibbānadīpanato.

The stage of the worldling and the stage of the trainee are shown by indicating the incompleteness of abandonment. The stage of the non-trainee is shown by indicating the final Nibbāna without clinging.

Saggagāminī paṭipadā pubbabhāgappaṭipatti.

The practice leading to heaven is the preliminary practice.

Puññe kathite puññaphalampi kathitameva hotīti vuttaṃ “dadato…pe… desanamāhā”ti. Saccakammaṭṭhānena vinā saṃkilesappahānaṃ natthīti dassento āha “kusalo…pe… desanamāhā”ti.

Because when merit is spoken of, the fruit of merit is also spoken of, it is said: 'For the giver… this is the teaching.' Showing that without the meditation subject of the truths there is no abandonment of defilements, he said: 'The skillful one… this is the teaching.'

Verasaddo adinnādānādipāpadhammesupi niruḷhoti vuttaṃ “evaṃ sabbānipi sikkhāpadāni vitthāretabbānī”ti. Dvepi vimuttiyo sekkhāsekkhavimuttiyo, saupādisesaanupādisesavimuttiyo ca. Tathā hi vakkhati “nibbutoti dve nibbānadhātuyo”tiādi (netti. aṭṭha. 76).

The term 'enmity' (vera) is also established in relation to evil qualities such as taking what is not given; thus it is said: 'In this way all the training rules should be elaborated.' The two liberations are the liberation of the trainee and the non-trainee, and the liberation with residue and without residue. For so it will be said: ''Extinguished' means the two Nibbāna-elements,' etc. (Netti. Aṭṭha. 76).

Kāraṇūpacārena, kāraṇaggahaṇena vā phalaṃ gahitanti āha “dve sugatiyo”tiādi. Vaṭṭavivaṭṭasampattiyo imissā desanāya phalaṃ, tassa dānaṃ sīlaṃ bhāvanā upāyo, “sampattidvayaṃ icchantena dānādīsu appamattena bhavitabba”nti ayamettha bhagavato āṇattīti imamatthaṃ sandhāyāha “phalādīni yathārahaṃ veditabbānī”ti.

By way of causal attribution or by taking the cause, the fruit is taken; hence it is said, 'two happy destinies,' etc. The attainments of the round of existence and of deliverance are the fruit of this teaching; its means are giving, virtue, and development. 'One who desires the twofold attainment should be diligent in giving, etc.'—this is the Blessed One's instruction here. With this meaning in mind, he said, 'the fruits, etc., should be understood appropriately.'

Vicayoti vicayahārasampāto, so vuccatīti attho. Esa nayo ito paresupi. “Tividhampi dānamaya”ntiādinā padatthavicayaṃ dasseti, tena assādādayo, itare ca vicayahārapadatthā [V 0.0101] atthato vicitā eva hontīti. Rūpādiārammaṇassa pariccāgo vuttoti sambandho. Sabboti sakalo anavasesato kiccassa vuttattā.

'Investigation' means the application of the mode of investigation; that is the meaning. This is the method for the subsequent ones as well. By means of 'the threefold merit of giving,' etc., he shows the investigation of the meaning of the terms. Thus, gratification, etc., and the other meanings of the terms in the mode of investigation are indeed investigated as to their meaning. The relinquishment of form, etc., as objects has been stated; such is the connection. 'All' means the whole, without remainder, because the function has been stated.

Dānābhiratassa cāgādhiṭṭhānaṃ pāripūriṃ gacchatīti vuttaṃ “dadato…pe… padaṭṭhāna”nti. Viratisacce, vacīsacce ca tiṭṭhato saccādhiṭṭhānaṃ pāripūriṃ gacchatīti vuttaṃ “saṃyama…pe… padaṭṭhāna”nti. Kosallayogato ca pāpappahānato ca paññāpāripūriṃ gacchatīti vuttaṃ “kusalo…pe… padaṭṭhāna”nti. Anavasesarāgādīsu pahīnesu upasamo upaṭṭhito nāma hotīti vuttaṃ “rāga…pe… padaṭṭhāna”nti.

For one who delights in giving, the resolution on generosity reaches fulfillment, as it is said: 'For the giver... the proximate cause.' For one who abides in the truth of abstinence and in truthful speech, the resolution on truth reaches fulfillment, as it is said: 'For the restrained... the proximate cause.' Through application of skill and the abandoning of evil, the perfection of wisdom reaches fulfillment, as it is said: 'For the skillful... the proximate cause.' When lust and other defilements are abandoned without remainder, tranquility is said to be established, as it is said: 'For the one free from lust... the proximate cause.'

Kusaloti [M 0.0107] puggalādhiṭṭhānena kosallasammādiṭṭhi vuttāti āha “kusalo…pe… maggaṅgādibhāvena ekalakkhaṇattā”ti. Ādisaddena bodhipakkhiyabhāvādiṃ saṅgaṇhāti. Khepetabbabhāvenāti pahātabbabhāvena.

'Skillful' means that right view as skillfulness is spoken of by way of personal reference, as stated: 'the skillful one... because of having a single characteristic in being a path factor, etc.' By the word 'etc.' he includes being a factor of enlightenment, and so on. 'By the nature of what is to be destroyed' means by the nature of what is to be abandoned.

Averatanti asapattataṃ. Kusaladhammehīti anavajjadhammehi, phalanibbānehīti adhippāyo. Dānassa mahapphalatā, sīlādiguṇehi satthu anuttaradakkhiṇeyyabhāvo, anupādāparinibbānanti imesaṃ paccavekkhaṇā imassa dānassa nidānanti ayamattho pāḷiyaṃ niruḷhova. Nibbacananidānasandhayo suviññeyyāvāti āha “nibbacananidānasandhayo vattabbā”ti.

'Non-enmity' means the absence of hostility. 'Through wholesome qualities' means through blameless qualities; the intention is 'by the fruits and Nibbāna.' The great fruitfulness of generosity, the Teacher's unsurpassed worthiness of offerings due to virtues such as morality, and the attainment of final Nibbāna without clinging—reflection on these is the source of this gift. This meaning is firmly established in the Pāli text. Because the derivation, source, and connection are easily understood, he says, 'The derivation, source, and connection should be stated.'

Paṭipakkhaniddesena samudayoti desanatthaṃ paṭipakkhaniddesanena niddhārito ayaṃ macchariyādisaṃkilesapakkhiko samudayo. Alobhena…pe… dānādīhīti yehi alobhādīhi dānādayo dhammā sambhavanti, tāni dānādiggahaṇeneva gahitānīti kusalamūlāni niddhāreti “imāni tīṇi kusalānī”ti. Tesanti kusalamūlānaṃ.

By explaining the opposite, the meaning of 'arising' is taught. Through the explanation of the opposite, this arising, which belongs to the side of defilements such as stinginess, is determined. By 'non-greed... and so on... through generosity and so forth'—since wholesome states such as generosity arise from non-greed and the like, they are included by the very mention of 'generosity and so on'. Thus, he determines the wholesome roots: 'These three are wholesome.' 'Of these' means of the wholesome roots.

Bhayahetu deti paṇṇākārādivasena. Rāgahetu deti sabhāgavatthussa. Āmisakiñcikkhahetu deti lañjādivasena. Anukampanto vā karuṇākhette. Apacayamāno guṇakhette, upakārakhette vā. Bhayūparatoti bhayena orato. Tena tathārūpena saṃyamena veraṃ na ciyateva. Evaṃ sabbassa akusalassa pāpako vipākoti yojanā.

One gives due to fear, by way of tribute and the like. One gives due to lust, of a thing of a similar kind. One gives due to hope for some small material gain, by way of a bribe and the like. Or, being compassionate, one gives in a field of compassion. Showing respect, one gives in a field of virtue, or to a benefactor. 'Ceased from fear' (bhayūparato) means removed from fear. Through such restraint, enmity is indeed not accumulated. Thus, the connection should be made: the result of all that is unwholesome is evil.

“Dadato”tiādinā yathā dānapaṭikkhepena parivattanaṃ dassitaṃ, evaṃ pahānapaṭikkhepenapi parivattanaṃ dassetabbanti vuttaṃ “akusalo pana na jahātī”ti.

Just as by 'for the giver' and so on, the turning away through the rejection of giving is shown, so too the turning away through the rejection of abandoning should be shown; thus it is said: 'But the unwholesome person does not abandon.'

Kammaphalaṃ [V 0.0102] saddahanto dānakiriyāyaṃ padahanto yena vidhinā dānaṃ dātabbaṃ, tattha satiṃ upaṭṭhapento cittaṃ samādahanto sammādiṭṭhiṃ purakkharonto dāne sammāpaṭipanno hotīti āha “dānaṃ nāma…pe… hotī”ti.

Believing in the fruit of kamma, striving in the act of giving, establishing mindfulness on the way in which a gift should be given, concentrating the mind, and placing right view at the forefront, one is rightly engaged in giving. Thus it is said: 'Giving is... indeed...'

Bhāvanāpahānasamāropanāni pāḷiyaṃ sarūpato viññāyantīti padaṭṭhānavevacanasamāropanāni dassetuṃ “taṃ sīlassa padaṭṭhāna”ntiādi vuttaṃ, taṃ suviññeyyaṃ. Aññañca yadettha atthato na vibhattaṃ, taṃ vuttanayattā, uttānatthattā cāti veditabbaṃ.

Since the applications of development and abandoning are understood in their own nature in the Pāḷi text, in order to show the applications of proximate cause and synonyms, it is said, 'That is the proximate cause of virtue,' and so on, which is easily understood. Furthermore, whatever here is not analyzed in terms of meaning should be understood on account of the method stated and because its meaning is plain.

Hārasampātavāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The explanation of the section on the Combination of Methods is concluded.

Nayasamuṭṭhānavāravaṇṇanā

The Explanation of the Section on the Arising of Methods

79. “Visayabhedato”ti [M 0.0108] saṅkhepena vuttamatthaṃ vitthārato vivarituṃ “yathā hī”tiādimāha. Tattha nayatoti nayaggāhato. Na hi paṭivedhañāṇaṃ viya vipassanāñāṇaṃ paccakkhato pavattatīti. Anubujjhiyamānoti abhisamayañāṇassa anurūpaṃ bujjhiyamāno. Yathā ekapaṭivedheneva maggañāṇaṃ pavattati, evaṃ tadanucchavikaṃ vipassanāñāṇena gayhamānoti attho. Evañca katvā nandiyāvaṭṭādīnaṃ tiṇṇaṃ atthanayabhāvo samatthito hotīti. Tathā hi atthavisesasarūpatāya tayo nayā “suttattho”ti vuttā, padatthavicārabhāvepi pana hārā “byañjanavicayo”ti. Yadi evaṃ kathaṃ tayoti codanaṃ sandhāyāha “paṭivijjhantānaṃ panā”tiādi. Tattha ekameko saṃkilesavodānānaṃ vibhāgato dvisaṅgahoti yojanā. Catuchaaṭṭhadiso cāti na paccekaṃ te nandiyāvaṭṭādayo catuchaaṭṭhadisā, atha kho yathākkamanti. “Eva”ntiādi yathāvuttassa atthassa nigamanaṃ.

79. To explain in detail the meaning briefly stated as 'Because of the diversity of objects,' he begins with 'Just as...' Herein, 'by the method' means by grasping the method. For insight knowledge does not proceed by direct perception like the knowledge of penetration. 'Being awakened to' means being awakened in a manner corresponding to the knowledge of realization. Just as path knowledge proceeds with a single penetration, so too is it grasped by the corresponding insight knowledge—this is the meaning. And thus, the status of Nandiyāvaṭṭa and the others as three meaning-methods is established. Indeed, because they are the embodiment of distinct meanings, the three methods are called 'the meaning of the sutta'; but the hārās, being an investigation of the meaning of words, are called 'the analysis of the letter.' Anticipating the objection, 'If so, how are there three?', he says, 'But for those who penetrate...' and so on. Therein, the connection is: each one, by the division of defilement and purification, is a twofold collection. And 'four, six, and eight directions'—it is not that Nandiyāvaṭṭa and so on are each four-, six-, and eight-directional, but rather they are so in sequence. 'Thus,' and so on, is the conclusion for the meaning as stated.

Tathā cāti yathāvuttassa atthassa upacayena samatthanā. Pubbā koṭi na paññāyatīti ettha yaṃ vattabbaṃ, taṃ parato paṭṭhānakathāyaṃ āvi bhavissati. “Andhaṃ tamaṃ tadā hoti, yaṃ lobho sahate nara”ntiādi (cūḷani. khaggavisāṇasuttaniddesa 128) vacanato kāmataṇhāpi paṭicchādanasabhāvā, yato kāmacchandaṃ “nīvaraṇa”nti vuttaṃ. Avijjāya pana bhavesu ādīnavappaṭicchādanaṃ [V 0.0103] sātisayanti. Tathā avijjāpi saṃyojanasabhāvā, yato sā bahiddhā saṃyojanabhāvena vuttā. Evaṃ santepi taṇhāya bandhanaṭṭho sātisayo apekkhitabhāvatoti imamatthaṃ dassento “tathāpi…pe… vutta”nti āha.

'And so' signifies an establishment of the stated meaning by way of elaboration. Whatever is to be said here regarding 'The prior limit is not discerned' will become clear later in the discourse on Causal Relations. Because of the statement, 'Then there is blinding darkness when greed overcomes a person,' etc. (Cūḷaniddesa, Khaggavisāṇasutta Niddesa 128), craving for sensual pleasures also has a concealing nature, for which reason sensual desire is called a 'hindrance.' But the concealing of the danger in existences by ignorance is pre-eminent. Likewise, ignorance also has the nature of a fetter, since it is spoken of as an external fetter. Even so, the binding quality of craving is pre-eminent because of its nature of longing. To show this meaning, he said, 'Nevertheless... it is said...'

“Saṃyuttā”ti padassa sampayuttāti atthoti āha “missitā”ti. “Avijjābhibhūtā…pe… abhinivisantā”ti etena avijjāya ayāthāvagahaṇahetutaṃ dasseti, tato so avindiyaṃ vindatīti avijjāti vuccati. Kilissanaṃ upatāpananti āha “kilissanappayogaṃ attaparitāpanapaṭipatti”nti. Allīyanaṃ sevanaṃ.

The meaning of the word 'saṃyuttā' is 'associated'; thus he says 'mixed.' By 'Overcome by ignorance... they adhere,' he shows that ignorance is the cause of not grasping in accordance with reality. Therefore, because one 'finds' what is 'not to be found,' it is called ignorance (avijjā). 'Defilement is torment'; thus he says 'the application of defilement, the practice of tormenting oneself.' Adhering (allīyana) is cultivation (sevana).

Dukkhanti [M 0.0109] …pe… jānantīti attanā anubhūyamānaṃ tathā tathā upaṭṭhitaṃ kāyikacetasikadukkhaṃ, itarampi vā ekadesaṃ jānanti. Taṇhāyapi eseva nayo. Sabhāgavisabhāgapaṭipajjitabbākārato tattha tesaṃ ñāṇaṃ natthevātidassento “idaṃ dukkha”ntiādimāha. Pavattipavattihetumattampīti “pavatti pavattihetū”ti ettakampi. Kā pana kathāti pacurajanasādhāraṇe lokiyepi nāma atthe yesaṃ ñāṇassa paṭighāto, paramagambhīre ariyānaṃ eva visayabhūte lokuttare nivattinivattihetusaṅkhāte atthe kā nāma kathā, chinnā kathāti attho. Aṭṭhasamāpattipabhedassa kevalassa samathassa tādise kāle bāhirakānañca ijjhanato “vipassanādhiṭṭhāna”nti visesitaṃ. Vūpasamo samucchedo, paṭippassaddhi ca.

'Suffering... they know' means they know the bodily and mental suffering experienced by themselves that has arisen in various ways, or they know some other part of it. For craving, too, this is the method. Showing that they have no knowledge of it at all from the perspective of the similar and dissimilar modes that should be practiced, he says, 'This is suffering,' and so on. 'Even just the occurrence and its cause' means 'just this much: occurrence and the cause of occurrence.' But what is to be said? If there is an obstruction to their knowledge even in worldly matters common to many people, what can be said about the supremely profound, supramundane matter—the domain of the noble ones alone, designated as cessation and the cause of cessation? The discussion is cut off—this is the meaning. Because at such a time mere serenity, of the kind comprising the eight attainments, is successfully achieved even by outsiders, it is specified as 'the basis of insight.' Vūpasama is eradication and also tranquilization.

“Saṃsārassa anupacchedanato”ti idaṃ diṭṭhigatānaṃ diṭṭhigatikamatadassanaṃ. So hi puttamukhadassane asati saṃsāro ucchijjeyyāti bhāyati. Yato vuttaṃ –

'Because of the non-severance of saṃsāra'—this is the perspective, the inclination, the view of those holding to views. For such a person fears that if he does not see his son's face, saṃsāra might be cut off. As it has been said:

“Gaṇḍuppādo kikī ceva, kuntī brāhmaṇadhammiko;

Ete abhayaṃ bhāyanti, sammūḷhā caturo janā”ti. (su. ni. aṭṭha. 2.293; a. ni. ṭī. 3.5.192);

Gaṇḍuppāda, Kikī, Kuntī, and the Brahmin practicing his dharma—these four bewildered people fear what is not fearful.

Tadabhiññāti taṃ yathāvuttaantadvayaṃ abhijānanti guṇaṃ āropetvā jānantīti tadabhiññā. Atthabhañjanato, rogagaṇḍasallasadisatāya attabhāvasaṃkilesānañca rogagaṇḍasallatā.

Tadabhiññā means: they directly know that pair of ends as stated; they know it by attributing a quality to it, thus tadabhiññā. From the breaking down of the meaning, and because of the similarity to disease, boil, and dart, the defilements of personal existence are also a disease, boil, and dart.

Sakkāyadassaneti ettha diṭṭhidassanaṃ, sakkāyova dassanaṃ sakkāyadassananti attho veditabbo. Tesanti diṭṭhicaritānaṃ. Attābhiniveso balavā. Tasmā yathāupaṭṭhitaṃ rūpaṃ “attā”icceva [V 0.0104] gaṇhantīti adhippāyo. Tathā vedanādiṃ. Taṇhācarito pana yathāupaṭṭhitaṃ rūpaṃ taṇhāvatthuṃ katvā attaniyābhinivesena abhinivisantā tadaññameva attato samanupassanti. Evaṃ vedanādīsu. Tenāha “taṇhācaritā”tiādi. Vijjamāneti paramatthato upalabbhamāne. Kāyeti samūhe. Diṭṭhiyā parikappito attādi eva paramatthato nupalabbhati, diṭṭhi pana labbhatevāti āha “satī vā vijjamānā”ti.

Regarding 'the seeing of the existing body' (sakkāyadassane): here, diṭṭhi is seeing; the existing body itself is the seeing, thus the meaning should be understood as 'the seeing that is the existing body.' 'Of them' means of those with a speculative temperament. Adherence to self is strong. Therefore, the intention is that they grasp whatever form presents itself as 'self.' Similarly with feeling and so on. But one of a craving temperament, having made whatever form presents itself an object of craving and adhering through adherence to what is 'mine,' sees something other than that as self. Thus with feelings and so on. Therefore he said, 'those of a craving temperament,' and so on. 'Existing' means being found in an ultimate sense. 'Body' means in the sense of a collection. The self and so on, conceived by the view, is not found in an ultimate sense, but the view itself is indeed found; thus he says, 'or being existent.'

Sakkāyadassanamukhenāti sakkāyadiṭṭhimukhena.

'By way of the seeing of the existing body' means by way of the view of the existing body.

Ucchedasassatanti [M 0.0110] taṃsahacaraṇato ucchedasassatadiṭṭhi vuttā. “Ucchedasassatavādā”tipi pāṭho.

'Annihilation and eternalism' refers to the views of annihilation and eternalism, so called because of their association with that personality-view. There is also the reading 'proponents of annihilation and eternalism'.

Kasiṇāyatanānīti kasiṇajjhānāni.

'The kasiṇa spheres' means the kasiṇa absorptions.

Tejetvāti nisānetvā.

Having sharpened (tejetvā) means having whetted (nisānetvā).

81. Ettāvatā nandiyāvaṭṭassa bhūmiracanavasena saṃkilesapakkho dassitoti āha “tattha diṭṭhicaritotiādinā vodānapakkhaṃ dassetī”ti. “Yasmā sallekhe tibbagāravo”ti iminā tattha tibbagāravattā saṃlekhānusantatavuttinā bhavatīti dasseti. Sesesupi eseva nayo. Micchādhimokkho saddhāpatirūpako avatthusmiṃ pasādo.

81. Thus, by way of the arrangement of the ground of the Nandiyāvaṭṭa, the aspect of defilement is shown; therefore, he says: “Here, by the conduct characterized by wrong view, etc., the aspect of cleansing is shown." “Because he has intense respect for expunging”—by this, he shows that because of his intense respect there, he lives in a continuous course of expunging. The same method applies to the rest. Wrong conviction is a semblance of faith—confidence in an unreal object.

Puggalādhiṭṭhānena dhammameva vibhajatīti āha “sattāpi…pe… dassetī”ti.

By focusing on individuals, he is actually classifying the Dhamma; thus it is said: "Beings... indeed... he demonstrates."

Ye hi kecīti ettha hi-saddo nipātamattaṃ. “Imāhi eva catūhi paṭipadāhī”tipi pāḷi. Dukkhāpaṭipadādivibhāgena maggo eva idha vuttoti āha “paṭipadā hi maggo”ti catuddisāsaṅkhātaṃ magganti catuddisāsaṅkhātaṃ pavattanupāyaṃ. Dve disā etissāti dvidisā. Nandiyāvaṭṭassāti nandiyāvaṭṭanayassa.

Here, the word "hi" is merely a particle. "Indeed, by these four practices"—this is also a reading. The path here is explained by way of the painful practice, etc., hence it is said, "Practice is indeed the path." The path is designated as fourfold—the means of progress is designated as fourfold. Having two aspects—hence twofold. Nandiyāvaṭṭassa means of the Nandiyāvaṭṭa method.

82. Vivattati vaṭṭaṃ etthāti vivattaṃ, vivattaṃ eva vivaṭṭaṃ, asaṅkhatadhātu, nibbuti eva vā. Tena vuttaṃ “nibbāna”nti.

82. "The cycle ceases to revolve here, thus it is vivattaṃ; vivattaṃ is indeed vivaṭṭaṃ, the unconditioned element, or simply quenching. Therefore, it is called 'nibbāna'."

“Kattha [V 0.0105] daṭṭhabba”nti vā pāḷi. Upacayeti upacayāvatthāyanti attho. Dasannanti lobhādikilesavatthūnaṃ. Vipallāsahetubhāvatoti subhasaññādivipallāsahetukabhāvato. Vipariyesaggāhavasena hi ādīnavesu eva saṃyojaniyesu dhammesu assādānupassitā. Na hi yathābhūtañāṇe sati tathā sambhavo. Tena vuttaṃ “dasannaṃ…pe… bhāvato”ti. Dasavidhakāraṇeti dasavidhe kāraṇe, dasavidhassa vā kāraṇe. Ayonisomanasikāraparikkhatā dhammā subhārammaṇādayo.

'Where should it be seen?' is also a reading in the Pāḷi. 'In accumulation' means in the state of accumulation. 'Of the ten' means of the ten bases of defilements, such as greed. 'Because of being the cause of distortion' means because of being the cause of distortions of perception, such as the perception of beauty. For, through perverted grasping, one dwells contemplating gratification in things that are in fact dangers and fetters. Indeed, when there is knowledge of things as they truly are, such a thing is not possible. Therefore, it is said, 'of the ten... because of its nature.' 'In the tenfold cause' means in a tenfold cause, or of a tenfold cause. Things such as beautiful objects are encompassed by unwise attention.

Tabbisayā kilesāti āhārapariññāparibandhabhūtā kilesā. Viññāṇaṭṭhitīsupi eseva nayo. Kāye pavattamāno paṭhamo vipallāso kāyasamudāye, kāyekadese ca kabaḷīkāre āhāre pavatto eva hotīti vuttaṃ “paṭhame āhāre visayabhūte paṭhamo vipallāso [M 0.0111] pavattatī”ti. Tathā vedanāyaṃ pavattamāno dutiyavipallāso tappaccaye phassāhāre, citte pavattamāno tatiyavipallāso tappaccaye manosañcetanāhāre, dhammesu pavattamāno catutthavipallāso tappaccaye viññāṇāhāre pavatto eva hotīti vuttaṃ “catutthe āhāre catuttho vipallāso”ti. Tenāha “sesāhāresupi eseva nayo”ti. Āhārasīsena vā āhārapaṭibaddho chandarāgo gahito. Viññāṇaṭṭhitīsupi eseva nayo. Tenevāha “āhārasīsena tabbisayā kilesā adhippetā”ti. Paṭhame āhāre visayabhūteti ca paṭhame āhāre chandarāgassa visayabhāvaṃ patte, tabbhāvaṃ anatikkanteti attho. Appahīnacchandarāgassa hi tattha vipallāsā sambhavanti, na itarassa. Tathā dutiyavipallāsādīsu appahīnesu. Itare upādānāni pavattanteva appahīnattāti āha “sesapadesupi eseva nayo”ti. Yasmā ca upādānādīsu appahīnesupi yogādayo pavattanteva yathārahaṃ taṃsabhāvattā, tadekaṭṭhasabhāvato ca, tasmā vuttaṃ pāḷiyaṃ “paṭhame upādāne paṭhamo yogo”tiādi. Tenāha “sesapadesupi eseva nayo”ti.

The defilements that pertain to that object are the defilements that obstruct the full understanding of nutriment. The same applies to the stations of consciousness. The first distortion occurring in the body—whether in the body as a whole or in a part of the body, and occurring in edible food—is said to arise in relation to the first nutriment: "The first distortion arises concerning the first nutriment as its object." Similarly, the second distortion occurring in feeling arises dependent on its condition, the nutriment of contact. The third distortion occurring in the mind arises dependent on its condition, the nutriment of mental volition. The fourth distortion occurring in phenomena arises dependent on its condition, the nutriment of consciousness. Thus it is said: "The fourth distortion arises concerning the fourth nutriment." Therefore, it is stated: "The same applies to the remaining nutriments." Alternatively, desire and lust connected to nutriment are grasped under the heading of nutriment. The same applies to the stations of consciousness. Hence, it is said: "The defilements pertaining to that object are intended under the heading of nutriment." Regarding "the first nutriment as its object," it means that the first nutriment becomes the object of desire and lust, without transcending that state. For distortions arise there only for one who has not abandoned desire and lust, not for others. The same applies when the second distortion and so on are unabandoned. Because the other clingings continue to arise due to not being abandoned, it is said: "The same applies to the remaining cases." Moreover, since the yokes and so on continue to arise appropriately in relation to the unabandoned clingings—due to their being of that nature and of the same essential nature—it is therefore stated in the Pāḷi: "The first yoke arises concerning the first clinging," and so on. Therefore, it is said: "The same applies to the remaining cases."

83. Aparijānantassāti ñātapariññāya, tīraṇapariññāya, pahānapariññāyāti tīhi pariññāhi paricchinditvā ajānantassa, tesaṃ samudayañca atthaṅgamañca assādañca ādīnavañca nissaraṇañca yathābhūtaṃ anavabujjhantassāti attho. Tibbo bahalo chandarāgo hoti taṇhācaritabhāvatoti adhippāyo. Iti upakkilesassa diṭṭhābhinivesassa hetubhāvatoti imamatthaṃ sandhāyāha “vuttanayenevā”ti. Subhasukhasaññākāmupādānakāmabhavayogaabhijjhākāyaganthakāmabhavāsava- kāmabhavogharāgasallachandāgatigamanāni taṇhāpakkhikatāya, taṇhāsabhāvatāya ca taṇhāpadhānāni. Sīlabbatupādānabyāpādakāyaganthadosasalladosāgatigamanāni pana taṇhābhāve bhāvato[V 0.0106], viññāṇaṭṭhitiyo taṇhāvisayato, sabbesaṃ vā taṇhāvisayato taṇhāpadhānatā labbhateva. Pacchimakānaṃ diṭṭhipadhānatā vuttanayānusārena veditabbā.

83. 'Not fully understanding' means not understanding after distinguishing with the three kinds of full understanding—the full understanding of knowing, the full understanding of scrutinizing, and the full understanding of abandoning; that is, not comprehending as they truly are the origin, cessation, gratification, danger, and escape of those things. The intention is that intense, strong desire and lust arise because of having a craving-natured character. Thus, referring to this point—that it is the cause of the defilement of adherence to views—it is said, 'in the very way stated.' The perceptions of beauty and pleasure, clinging to sensual pleasures, the yoke of sensual existence, covetousness as a bodily knot, the taints of sensual desire and existence, the floods of sensual desire and existence, the dart of lust, and going to a wrong course through desire are dominated by craving because they are on the side of craving and have the nature of craving. As for clinging to rites and rituals, ill will, the bodily knot of aversion, the dart of aversion, and going to a wrong course through aversion, their dominance by craving is obtained because they exist when craving exists; and for the stations of consciousness, because they are the domain of craving; or for all of them, because they are the domain of craving. The dominance of views for the latter should be understood according to the method stated.

84. Kabaḷīkāre āhāreti kabaḷīkārāhāravisaye chandarāge. “Appahīne”tiādikaṃ pariyāyakathaṃ muñcitvā nippariyāyameva dassento kabaḷīkārāhārassa [M 0.0112] “asubhasabhāvattā, asubhasamuṭṭhānattā cā”ti vuttaṃ. Labbhamāne hi ujuke atthe kiṃ pariyāyakathāyāti. Chandarāgo vā tattha atthasiddhoti evampettha attho vutto. Na hi tattha asati chandarāge vipallāso sambhavati. Dukkhasabhāvattāti saṅkhāradukkhatāya dukkhasabhāvattā. Dukkhapaccayattāti tividhadukkhatālakkhaṇassa dukkhassa kāraṇato. Viññāṇe niccasaññino. Tathā hi sāti nāma bhikkhu kevaṭṭaputto “taṃyeva viññāṇaṃ sandhāvati saṃsaratī”ti tattha niccābhinivesaṃ saṃvedesi. Yebhuyyena saṅkhāresu attasaññitā diṭṭhigatikānaṃ “cetanā attā”tiādidiṭṭhiparidīpanesu veditabbā. “Bhavavisuddhī”ti padassa atthavacanaṃ “nibbutisukha”nti. “Sīlabbatehi…pe… sukhanti daḷhaṃ gaṇhātī”ti iminā sīlabbatupādānaṃ idha bhavupādānanti dasseti. Tathā hi vakkhati “sīlabbatupādānasaṅkhātena bhavupādānenā”ti.

84. 'In physical nutriment' means in desire and lust concerning the sphere of physical nutriment. Setting aside the roundabout explanation 'unabandoned,' etc., and showing just the direct meaning, it is said of physical nutriment that it is 'because of its impure nature and because of its impure origin.' For when a straightforward meaning is available, what is the use of a roundabout explanation? Or, 'desire and lust are accomplished in purpose there'—thus the meaning is stated here. Indeed, without desire and lust there, distortion is not possible. 'Because of its nature of suffering' means because of its nature of suffering due to the suffering of formations. 'Because it is a condition for suffering' means because it is a cause of suffering characterized by the three kinds of suffering. Of one who perceives permanence in consciousness: for instance, the monk Sāti, son of a fisherman, experienced an adherence to permanence there, declaring, 'It is this very consciousness that runs on and transmigrates.' For the most part, for those of a speculative bent, the perception of self in formations should be understood in the expositions of views such as 'volition is self.' The explanation of the meaning of the term 'purification of existence' is 'the bliss of Nibbāna.' By this quote, 'Through rites and vows… he firmly grasps as happiness,' he shows that clinging to rites and vows is here clinging to existence. For so he will say, 'by the clinging to existence designated as clinging to rites and vows.'

Paccayā honti upanissayapaccayādinā. Paṭhame yoge ṭhitoti paṭhame yoge patiṭṭhito. Appahīnā hi kilesā kammavaṭṭādīnaṃ kāraṇabhūtā taṃsamaṅgino sattassa patiṭṭhāti vuccanti. Parassa abhijjhāyanaṃ parābhijjhāyanaṃ. Bhavapatthanāya bhavadiṭṭhibhavarāgavasena piyāyitassa vatthuno vipariṇāmaññathābhāve domanassuppattiṃ sandhāya vuttaṃ “bhavarāga…pe… padūsentī”ti.

They are conditions by way of decisive support condition and so on. 'Standing in the first yoke' means established in the first yoke. For unabandoned defilements, being the cause of the round of kamma and so on, are said to be the foundation for a being endowed with them. Coveting what is another's is covetousness. Regarding the longing for existence, 'Lust for existence... defiles...' is said with reference to the arising of displeasure due to the alteration and change of a cherished object—cherished through view of existence and lust for existence.

Ganthitvāti ganthiṃ katvā. Dvidhābhūtaṃ rajjuādike viya ganthikaraṇañhi ganthanaṃ. Cittaṃ pariyādāya tiṭṭhantā āsavānaṃ uppattihetu hontīti sambandho. Pariyuṭṭhānappattā ekacce kilesā visesato āsavuppattihetu hontīti dassanatthaṃ aṭṭhakathāyaṃ uppaṭipāṭivacanaṃ. Tappaṭipakkhe visaye patthetīti yojanā. Tabbisayabahule bhave patthetīti yathā mānusakehi kāmehi nibbinnarūpā devūpapatti. Taṃsabhāvattāti diṭṭhisabhāvattā. Aparāparanti aññamaññaṃ. Ekaccā hi diṭṭhi ekaccassa diṭṭhābhinivesassa kāraṇaṃ hoti, yathā sakkāyadiṭṭhi itarāsaṃ. Abhinivisantassāti abhinivesanahetu. “Ayonisomanasikārato…pe… avijjāsavo uppajjatī”ti idaṃ saccābhinivesassa phalabhūtaṃ avijjāsavaṃ dasseti ekantavassimeghavuṭṭhānena viya mahoghappavatti. Avijjāsavo siddho hoti vuṭṭhihetukamahoghasiddhiyā uparimeghavuṭṭhānaṃ viya.

‘Having tied’ means having made a knot. For tying is the making of a knot, as when a rope or the like is made twofold. The connection is that by persisting and taking hold of the mind, they become a cause for the arising of the taints. The commentary’s statement in reverse order is to show that certain defilements, having reached the stage of obsession, are a specific cause for the arising of the taints. The construction is that one longs for an object that is its opposite. This means one longs for an existence abundant in that object, as when those disgusted with human sensual pleasures aspire to rebirth among the devas. ‘Because of its nature’ means because of the nature of views. ‘One after another’ means mutually. For one view becomes the cause for adherence to another view, as identity view does for the others. ‘For one who adheres’ means because of adherence. ‘From unwise attention… the taint of ignorance arises’—this shows the taint of ignorance, which is the result of adherence to a view as truth, arising like the occurrence of a great flood from the rising of a continuously raining cloud. The taint of ignorance is established, just as a great flood caused by rain is established by the rising of a cloud above.

“Nandīrāgasahagatā”tiādīsu [V 0.0107][M 0.0113] (mahāva. 14) viya tabbhāvattho sahagatasaddoti āha “anusaya…pe… bhūtā vā”ti. Cittassa abbhantarasaṅkhātaṃ hadayanti vipākacittappavattiṃ sandhāya vadati. Vipākavaṭṭepi kilesavāsanāhitā atthi kāci visesamattā.

As in such phrases as 'accompanied by delight and lust' (Mahāvagga 14), the word 'accompanied' has the meaning of that very state; thus he says, 'being a latent tendency... or...'. He speaks of the heart, which is designated as internal to the mind, with reference to the occurrence of resultant consciousness. Even within the round of resultants, there exists a certain particular measure deposited by the latent impressions of the defilements.

Lobhasahagatassa viññāṇassa. Itarassa dosasahagatādikassa. Byañjanena viya bhojanassa ārammaṇassa abhisaṅkharaṇaṃ visesāpādanaṃ upasecanaṃ, nandī sappītikataṇhā upasecanaṃ etassāti nandūpasecanaṃ upasecanabhūtāyapi nandiyā rāgasallaupanisato. Upasitte pana vattabbameva natthīti dassetuṃ pāḷiyā “rāgasallena nandūpasecanena viññāṇenā”ti vuttanti tamatthaṃ pākaṭaṃ kātuṃ “kena pana taṃ nandūpasecana”nti pucchati.

Of consciousness accompanied by greed. Of the other, such as that accompanied by aversion. The preparing of an object, the producing of a special quality, is a supplement, like the seasoning of food with a condiment. Delight, which is craving accompanied by joy, is a supplement; because this has delight as its supplement, it is called 'delight-supplemented', and this is because delight, which has become a supplement, has the dart of lust as its proximate cause. To show that when it is thus supplemented, there is nothing more to be said, the Pāli says, 'by the dart of lust, by the delight-supplement, by consciousness.' To make that meaning clear, it asks, 'But by what is that delight-supplement?'

Rāgasallenāti hetumhi karaṇavacananti dassento “rāgasallena hetubhūtenā”ti āha. Upanissayapaccayattho cettha hetvattho. Upagantabbato viññāṇenāti vibhattiṃ pariṇāmetvā yojetabbaṃ. “Patiṭṭhābhāvato”ti iminā viññāṇassa nissayādipaccayataṃ vadati. Tenāha “rūpakkhandhaṃ nissāya tiṭṭhatī”ti. Evaṃ dutiyādiviññāṇaṭṭhitīsupi nissayādipaccayatā vattabbā patiṭṭhāvacanato.

'By the dart of lust': showing that the instrumental case is used here in the sense of cause, he says, 'by the dart of lust, which is the cause.' Here, the meaning of 'cause' is that of a proximate condition. Regarding 'by consciousness', the case ending should be changed and it should be construed. By the phrase 'because of being a foundation'—a foundation being that which is to be approached—he states that consciousness has the nature of a support condition and so on. Therefore he says, 'it stands depending on the aggregate of form.' In this way, for the second and other stations of consciousness too, their nature as a support condition and so on should be stated, because the word 'foundation' is used.

85. Yadipi akusalamūlādike tipukkhalassa, taṇhādike nandiyāvaṭṭassa disābhāvena vakkhati, tathāpi aññamaññānuppavesato ekasmiṃ naye siddhe itarepi siddhā eva hontīti imassa visesassa dassanatthaṃ “āhārādayo…pe… vavatthapetu”nti vuttaṃ. Vakkhamāne vā akusalamūlataṇhādike ādisaddena saṅgahetvā “āhārādayo”ti vadanto “nayāna”nti bahuvacanamāha. Ekassa atthassāti rāgacaritassa upakkilesatāsaṅkhātassa ekassa payojanassa. Byañjanatthopi gahito, na byañjanameva gahitanti suttapadāni aññamaññapariyāyavacanāni yathārahaṃ taṇhāvatthūnaṃ tattha kathitattā vuttaṃ “savatthukā taṇhā vuttā”ti. Dosavatthūnaṃ, diṭṭhivatthūnañca tattha kathitattā “savatthuko doso, savatthukā diṭṭhi ca vuttā”ti imamatthaṃ sandhāyāha “vuttanayānusārenā”ti.

85. Although the unwholesome roots and so on will be spoken of as an aspect of the three persons, and craving and so on as an aspect of one who delights in the round, nevertheless, because of their mutual interpenetration, when one method is established, the others are also established. To show this distinction, it is said, 'nutriments and so on... to define.' Or, having included the unwholesome roots, craving, and so on, which are about to be mentioned, with the word 'and so on', when he says 'nutriments and so on', he uses the plural 'methods'. 'For one meaning' means for one purpose, designated as the defilement of one with a lustful temperament. The meaning of the expression is also taken, not just the expression itself. Because the words of the sutta are mutually synonymous expressions, and because the objects of craving are spoken of there appropriately, it is said, 'craving with its objects has been stated.' And because the objects of aversion and the objects of views are also spoken of there, referring to this meaning—'aversion with its objects and views with their objects have been stated'—he says, 'in accordance with the method stated.'

Dukkhākārena [M 0.0114] saha dukkhākāraṃ gahetvāti attho. Evañcetanti yadi taṃtaṃanupassanābahulassa vasena purimāhāradvayādīsu vimokkhamukhavisesaniddhāraṇaṃ kataṃ, etaṃ evameva veditabbaṃ, na aññathā. Tattha kāraṇaṃ vadanto “na hī”tiādimāha. Tassattho – yathā [V 0.0108] ariyamaggānaṃ odhiso kilesappajahanato pahātabbesu dhammesu niyamo atthi, na evaṃ vipassanāya pariññāpahānānaṃ aniccantikattāti.

'Taking the aspect of suffering along with the aspect of suffering'—this is the meaning. And if, by being one who frequently practices such-and-such a contemplation, the determination of the distinction of the doors to liberation in the first two nutriments, etc., has been made, this should be understood in just this way, not otherwise. There, stating the reason, he says, 'For indeed...' The meaning of this is: just as for the noble paths there is a fixed rule regarding the states to be abandoned, owing to the specific abandoning of defilements, so it is not for the abandonments through the full understanding of insight, because they are not final.

Apare panāhu – purime āhāradvaye parikilesabhāvena, dukkhapaccayattā ca dukkhalakkhaṇaṃ supākaṭaṃ. Tattha purime viññāṇaṭṭhitidvayaviññāṇāhāre tatiyaviññāṇaṭṭhitiyaṃ aniccalakkhaṇaṃ, manosañcetanāhāre catutthaviññāṇaṭṭhitiyaṃ anattalakkhaṇaṃ supākaṭanti tissannaṃ anupassanānaṃ pavattimukhatāya tehi appaṇihitādivimokkhamukhehi pariññaṃ gacchantīti. Tathā vipallāsādīsu purimadvayaṃ dukkhānupassanāya ujuvipaccanīkaṃ, itaradvayaṃ aniccānattānupassanānaṃ. Iti pavattimukhatāya ca ujuvipaccanīkatāya ca ime dhammā yathārahaṃ appaṇihitādivimokkhamukhehi pariññeyyā, pahātabbā ca vuttā. Tattha subhasukhasaññākāmupādānasīlabbatupādānakāmayogabhavayogaabhijjhākāya ganthakāmāsavakāmogha bhavogha rāgasallachandaagatigamanāni sukhassādavasena pavattanato dukkhānupassanāya paṭipakkhabhāvato byāpādakāyaganthadosasalladosaagatigamanāni pavattimukhatāya appaṇihitavimokkhamukhena pahātabbāni. Tatiyasaññādayo niccābhinivesatannimittāhi aniccānupassanāya paṭipakkhabhāvato animittavimokkhamukhena pahātabbā. Catutthasaññādayo attābhinivesatannittāhi anattānupassanāya paṭipakkhabhāvato suññatavimokkhamukhena pahātabbā. Tattha mānasallabhayaagatigamanānaṃ niccābhinivesanimittatā veditabbā. Na hi aniccato passato mānajappanaṃ, bhayaṃ vā sambhavati. Avijjāyogādīnaṃ attābhinivesanimittatā pākaṭā evāti.

Others, however, say: In the first two kinds of nutriment, the characteristic of suffering is very evident due to their being a basis for defilement and a condition for suffering. Therein, in the first two stations of consciousness, in consciousness-nutriment, and in the third station of consciousness, the characteristic of impermanence is very evident; in the nutriment of mental volition and in the fourth station of consciousness, the characteristic of non-self is very evident. Thus, because they are the portals for the arising of the three contemplations, they are fully comprehended through the doors to liberation, the desireless and so on. Similarly, among the perversions and so on, the first two are directly contrary to the contemplation of suffering, and the other two to the contemplations of impermanence and non-self. Thus, because they are portals for arising and are directly contrary, these states are said to be, respectively, fully comprehended and abandoned through the doors to liberation, the desireless and so on. Herein, the perceptions of beauty and pleasure, the clinging to sensual pleasures, the clinging to rules and observances, the bond of sensuality, the bond of existence, covetousness, the bodily tie, the taint of sensuality, the flood of sensuality, the flood of existence, the dart of lust, and going the wrong way through desire—because they proceed by way of enjoying pleasure and are thus opposed to the contemplation of suffering—and the bodily tie of ill will, the dart of hatred, and going the wrong way through hatred—because they are portals for arising—are to be abandoned through the door to desireless liberation. The third perception and so on, being signs of adherence to permanence, are opposed to the contemplation of impermanence and are to be abandoned through the door to signless liberation. The fourth perception and so on, being signs of adherence to self, are opposed to the contemplation of non-self and are to be abandoned through the door to emptiness liberation. Herein, the dart of conceit, fear, and going the wrong way through fear should be understood as being signs of adherence to permanence. For one who sees impermanence, the muttering of conceit or fear is not possible. That the bond of ignorance and so on are signs of adherence to self is self-evident.

86. Appamaññāvajjā rūpāvacarasamāpattiyo dibbavihārā “devūpapattisaṃvattanikakusalasamāpattiyo cā”ti katvā, satipi tabbhāve parahitapaṭipattito, niddosatāya ca seṭṭhā vihārāti catasso appamaññā brahmavihārā, catasso phalasamāpattiyo ariyavihārā “ārakā kilesehi ariyānaṃ vihārā”ti. Catasso āruppasamāpattiyo āneñjavihārā[M 0.0115], satipi devūpapattisaṃvattanikakusalasamāpattibhāve āneñjasantatāhi lokiyesu sikhāppattito.

86. The form-sphere attainments, excluding the immeasurables, are divine abodes, and they are also wholesome attainments conducive to rebirth among the devas. Although the four immeasurables are also of that nature, they are called Brahmavihāras because they are superior abodes due to their practice for the welfare of others and their faultlessness. The four fruition attainments are noble abodes, as they are 'the abodes of the noble ones, far from defilements.' The four formless attainments are imperturbable abodes, for although they too are wholesome attainments conducive to rebirth among the devas, they reach the summit among worldly states due to their imperturbable continuity.

Adhikaraṇabhedenāti vatthubhedena.

By distinction of legal issues: that is, by distinction of subject matter.

Yaṃ abhiṇhaṃ na pavattati, taṃ acchariyanti dassetuṃ “andhassa…pe… uppajjanaka”nti vuttaṃ. Adhitiṭṭhati sīlādi etena saccena, ettha vā sacce nimittabhūte, adhiṭṭhānamattameva vā taṃ saccanti evaṃ karaṇādhikaraṇabhāvatthā paccayavasena veditabbā samānādhikaraṇasamāsapakkhe. Tathā [V 0.0109] aññapadatthasamāsapakkhe. Itarasmiṃ pana samāse karaṇādhikaraṇatthā eva, te ca kho sīlādivasena ca veditabbā. Sukhanti jhānavipassanāmaggaphalanibbānasukhaṃ. Lokiyavipākasukhampi labbhateva. “Nāññatra bojjhā…pe… pāṇina”nti (saṃ. ni. 1. 98) hi imāya gāthāya saṅgahitā anattaparihāramukhena sattānaṃ abhayā nibbānasampattisukhāvahā cattāro dhammā idha “sukhabhāgiyā”ti vuttāti. Anavasesapariyādānatoti pharaṇavasena anavasesaggahaṇato.

To show that what does not occur frequently is marvelous, it is said, 'For the blind... up to arising.' Virtue, etc., is established by this truth, or here, in the truth as a sign, or that truth is merely a determination. Thus, in the case of a compound with the same referent, the meanings of the instrumental and locative cases should be understood by way of a condition. Similarly, in the case of a compound with a different referent. But in the other compound, the meanings are purely instrumental and locative, and those should be understood in terms of virtue and so on. 'Happiness' refers to the happiness of jhāna, insight, path, fruition, and Nibbāna. Even mundane resultant happiness is obtainable. Indeed, by this verse, 'Not elsewhere than from the factors of enlightenment... in the hand' (Saṃyutta Nikāya 1.98), the four qualities which, by way of avoiding the non-self, bring to beings the happiness of attaining Nibbāna, the state of fearlessness, are included here and are called 'conducive to happiness.' 'Without remainder and completely' means grasping entirely by way of pervasion.

Paṭhamassa satipaṭṭhānassa paṭhamapaṭipadāvasena pavattassāti adhippāyo. Evaṃ sesesupi. “Yathā hī”tiādinā yathāvuttapaṭipadāsatipaṭṭhānānaṃ nānantariyakataṃ upamāya vibhāveti. Satipi ca sabbāhi paṭipadāhi sabbesaṃ satipaṭṭhānānaṃ niyamābhāve nānantarikabhāvena desanākkamenevettha nesaṃ ayamanukkamo katoti veditabbo. Atha vā kāyavedanāsu subhasukhasaññānaṃ dubbiniveṭhiyatāya asubhadukkhānupassanānaṃ kiccasiddhito purimena paṭipadādvayena purimaṃ satipaṭṭhānadvayaṃ yojitaṃ tadabhāvato. Itarena itaraṃ. Tāni hi purimesu satipaṭṭhānesu katakammassa icchitabbāni. Atha vā yathā taṇhācaritadiṭṭhicaritānaṃ mandatikkhapaññānaṃ vasena catasso paṭipadā yojitā, evaṃ cattāri satipaṭṭhānāni sambhavantīti dassetuṃ paṭipadāsatipaṭṭhānānaṃ ayamanukkamo kato.

The intention is that the first foundation of mindfulness proceeds by way of the first practice. Similarly for the rest. By 'Just as,' and so on, it clarifies with a simile the non-immediacy of the foundations of mindfulness and their respective practices as previously described. Moreover, even though there is no fixed rule for all foundations of mindfulness with all practices, this sequence of them here should be understood as having been made in accordance with the order of teaching due to their non-immediacy. Alternatively, because the perceptions of beauty and pleasure regarding the body and feelings are difficult to dislodge, and this task is accomplished by the contemplation of the unattractive and of suffering, the first two practices are connected to the first two foundations of mindfulness. Due to the absence of those perceptions in the latter cases, the other practices are connected to the other foundations. For these are what is desired by one who has accomplished the task in the earlier foundations of mindfulness. Or else, just as the four practices are connected based on those with craving-tendencies, view-tendencies, and dull or sharp wisdom, so too the four foundations of mindfulness are possible accordingly. Thus, this sequence of practices and foundations of mindfulness has been arranged to show this.

“Tathā”ti iminā yathā samānapaṭipakkhatāya paṭhamassa satipaṭṭhānassa bhāvanā paṭhamassa jhānassa visesāvahā, evaṃ pītisahagatādisamānatāya dutiyasatipaṭṭhānādibhāvanā dutiyajjhānādīnaṃ visesāvahāti imamatthaṃ upasaṃharati. Pītipaṭisaṃvedanādīti ādisaddena sukhapaṭisaṃvedanaṃ, cittasaṅkhārapaṭisaṃvedanaṃ, passambhanañca saṅgaṇhāti. Cittassa abhippamodanaggahaṇañcettha [M 0.0116] nidassanamattaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ paṭisaṃvedanasamādahanavimocanānampi vasena pavattiyā icchitabbattā. Aniccavirāgādīti ādisaddena nirodhapaṭinissaggā saṅgayhanti.

By 'Thus,' it summarizes the meaning that just as the cultivation of the first foundation of mindfulness brings distinction to the first jhāna due to being a similar counterpart, so too the cultivation of the second foundation of mindfulness and the like brings distinction to the second jhāna and the like due to their similarity, such as being accompanied by joy. By 'experiencing joy, etc.,' the term 'etc.' includes experiencing pleasure, experiencing mental formations, and tranquility. And here, the mention of gladdening the mind should be seen as a mere illustration, since its occurrence is desirable in terms of experiencing, concentrating, and liberating. By 'impermanence, dispassion, etc.,' the term 'etc.' includes cessation and relinquishment.

Rūpāvacarasamāpattīnanti ettha paṭiladdhamattaṃ paṭhamajjhānaṃ paṭhamajjhānasamāpattiyā paguṇavasībhāvāpādanassa paccayo hoti, na itarāsaṃ. Itarāsaṃ pana adhiṭṭhānabhāvena paramparāya paccayo hotīti veditabbaṃ. Byāpādavihiṃsāvitakkaaratirāgā byāpādavitakkādayo. Sukhenāti akicchena, akasirenāti attho.

Regarding the attainments of the form realm: here, the mere attainment of the first jhāna becomes a condition for producing proficiency and mastery over the first jhāna attainment, but not for the others. For the others, however, it should be understood that it serves as a condition successively by being a foundation. Thoughts of ill will, thoughts of harm, discontent, and lust are 'thoughts of ill will, etc.' 'With ease' means without difficulty, that is, without strain.

Dibbavihārādike cattāro vihāre padaṭṭhānaṃ katvā nānāsantānesu uppannāya vuṭṭhānagāminivipassanāya [V 0.0110] yathākkamaṃ anuppannākusalānuppādanādivasena pavattavisayaṃ sandhāya pāḷiyaṃ “paṭhamo vihāro bhāvito bahulīkato”tiādi vuttaṃ. Sammappadhānasadisañhettha sammappadhānaṃ vuttaṃ. Ariyavihāre ca heṭṭhime nissāya uparimaggādhigamāya vāyamantassa ayaṃ nayo labbhati. Maggapariyāpannasseva vā sammappadhānassa nānāsantānikassa yathāvuttavipassanāgamanena taṃtaṃkiccādikassa vasenetaṃ vuttaṃ. Sakkā hi vipassanāgamanena saddhindriyāditikkhatāviseso viya vīriyassa kiccavisesavisayo maggo viññātuṃ.

Having made the four abodes, beginning with the divine abode, a foundation, and with reference to the scope of operation of the insight leading to emergence—which arises in various continuities and proceeds sequentially by way of not giving rise to unarisen unwholesome states, etc.—it is said in the Pāli, 'The first abode is developed and cultivated,' and so on. Here, right effort is spoken of as analogous to the Right Efforts. And for one striving to attain the higher path by relying on the lower noble abodes, this method is obtained. Alternatively, this is said with respect to the respective functions, etc., of the Right Effort—which is included in the path and pertains to various continuities—through the aforementioned process of insight. For indeed, through the process of insight, the path can be understood as the sphere of the specific function of energy, just as the specific sharpness of the faculty of faith, etc., is understood.

Tathā sikhāppattaupekkhāsatipārisuddhidibbavihāraṃ nissāya uppannaṃ paṭhamaṃ sammappadhānaṃ mānappahānaṃ ukkaṃseti, brahmavihārasannissaye uppannaṃ dutiyaṃ sammappadhānaṃ kāmālayasamugghātaṃ, ariyavihārasannissayena uppannaṃ tatiyaṃ sammappadhānaṃ avijjāpahānaṃ, santavimokkhasannissayena uppannaṃ catutthaṃ sammappadhānaṃ bhavūpasamaṃ ukkaṃsetīti dassetuṃ “paṭhamaṃ sammappadhāna”ntiādi vuttaṃ.

Thus, to show that the first right effort, arisen dependent on the divine abode of equanimity and purity of mindfulness that has reached its peak, extols the abandonment of conceit; that the second right effort, arisen dependent on the Brahmavihāra, extols the uprooting of the lair of sensual desire; that the third right effort, arisen dependent on the noble abiding, extols the abandonment of ignorance; and that the fourth right effort, arisen dependent on peaceful liberation, extols the calming of becoming, it is said: 'the first right effort,' and so on.

Pahīnamāno na visaṃvādeyyāti mānappahānaṃ saccādhiṭṭhānaṃ vaḍḍheti visaṃvādananimittasseva abhāvato. Appahīnamāno hi mānanissayena kiñci visaṃvādeyya. Kāmālaye, diṭṭhālaye ca samugghāṭite cāgapaṭipakkhassa avasaro eva natthīti ālayasamugghāto cāgādhiṭṭhānaṃ vaḍḍheti. Avijjāya samucchinnāya paññābuddhiyā paribandhova natthi, bhavasaṅkhāresu ossaṭṭhesu abhavūpasamassa okāsova natthīti mānappahānādayo saccādhiṭṭhānādike saṃvaḍḍhentīti dassetuṃ “mānappahāna”ntiādi vuttaṃ.

One who has abandoned conceit would not deceive; thus the abandonment of conceit strengthens the determination for truth, as there is no cause for deceit. For one who has not abandoned conceit might deceive in some way, relying on conceit. When the lair of sensual desire and the lair of views are uprooted, there is no opportunity for what is opposed to relinquishment; thus the uprooting of the lairs strengthens the determination for relinquishment. When ignorance is cut off, there is no hindrance to wisdom and understanding. When the formations of existence are relinquished, there is no opportunity for existence not to be calmed. Thus, to show that the abandonment of conceit and so forth strengthen the determination for truth and so forth, it is said: 'the abandonment of conceit,' and so on.

Avisaṃvādanasīlo [M 0.0117] dhammacchandabahulo chandādhipateyyaṃ samādhiṃ nibbatteti. Cāgādhimutto nekkhammajjhāsayo akosajjabahulatāya vīriyādhipateyyaṃ, ñāṇuttaro cittaṃ attano vase vattento cittādhipateyyaṃ, vūpasantasabhāvo upasamahetubhūtāya vīmaṃsāya vīmaṃsayato vīmaṃsādhipateyyaṃ samādhiṃ nibbattetīti saccādhiṭṭhānādipārisuddhichandasamādhiādīnaṃ pāripūriyā saṃvattatīti dassetuṃ “saccādhiṭṭhānaṃ bhāvita”ntiādi vuttaṃ.

One whose nature is not to deceive, being abundant in zeal for the Dhamma, develops concentration governed by zeal. One who is resolved on relinquishment, with an inclination for renunciation, through an abundance of non-indolence, develops concentration governed by energy. One who is superior in wisdom, bringing the mind under one's own control, develops concentration governed by mind. One whose nature is tranquil, investigating with an investigation that is the cause of tranquility, develops concentration governed by investigation. Thus, to show that the purity of the determination for truth and so on conduces to the fulfillment of concentration born of zeal and so on, it is said, 'The determination for truth has been developed,' and so forth.

Dhammacchandabahulo chandasamādhimhi ṭhito iṭṭhāniṭṭhachaḷārammaṇāpāte anavajjasevī hoti. Āraddhavīriyo vīriyasamādhimhi ṭhito saṃkilesapakkhassa santapanavaseneva puññaṃ paripūreti. Cittaṃ attano vase vattento cittasamādhimhi ṭhito paññāya upakārānupakārake dhamme pariggaṇhanto [V 0.0111] buddhiṃ phātiṃ gamissati. Vīmaṃsāsamādhimhi ṭhito dhammavicayabahulo upadhipaṭinissaggāvahameva paṭipattiṃ brūhetīti imamatthaṃ dassetuṃ “chandasamādhi bhāvito”tiādi vuttaṃ.

One who is abundant in zeal for the Dhamma, established in concentration born of zeal, partakes of what is blameless when confronted with the six agreeable and disagreeable sense objects. One who has aroused energy, established in concentration born of energy, fulfills merit precisely by way of scorching the faction of defilements. One who keeps the mind under one's control, established in concentration born of mind, discerning with wisdom things helpful and unhelpful, will bring about the growth of understanding. One who is established in concentration born of investigation, being abundant in the investigation of phenomena, cultivates only the practice that leads to the relinquishment of the substrata of existence. To convey this meaning, it is said: “Concentration born of zeal is developed,” and so on.

Dūrādūrapaccatthikanivāraṇe bahūpakāro indriyasaṃvaro mettāya visesuppattihetuto mettaṃ vaḍḍheti. Tapena saṃkilesadhamme vikkhambhento vīriyādhiko paradukkhāpanayanakāmataṃ sāhatthikaṃ karotīti tapo karuṇaṃ saṃvaḍḍheti. Paññā pariyodāpitā sāvajjānavajjadhamme pariggaṇhantī pahāsanipātato muditaṃ rakkhantī paribrūheti. Upadhinissaggo pakkhando ninnapoṇapabbhārova sammadeva sattasaṅkhāresu udāsino hotīti so upekkhāvihāraṃ parivaḍḍhetīti imamatthaṃ dassetuṃ “indriyasaṃvaro bhāvito”tiādi vuttaṃ. Tenāha “yo yassa visesapaccayo, so taṃ paripūretīti vutto”ti.

Restraint of the faculties, being greatly helpful in warding off distant and near adversaries, cultivates loving-kindness because it is a special condition for the arising of loving-kindness. By austerity, suppressing defiling states and being abundant in energy, one personally undertakes the wish to remove the suffering of others; thus austerity cultivates compassion. Wisdom, when purified, discerning blameworthy and blameless qualities, protecting sympathetic joy from falling away, develops it. The relinquishment of substrata, inclining like a downward slope, becomes truly indifferent to beings and formations; thus it cultivates the abiding in equanimity. To convey this meaning, it is said: “Restraint of the faculties is developed,” and so on. Therefore, it is stated: “Whatever is the specific condition for something, that fulfills it.”

87. Disābhāvenāti nayānaṃ disābhāvenāti yojetabbaṃ. Atthopissa pubbe vuttanayeneva veditabbo. Yena catukkena yassa rāgacaritādipuggalassa vodānaṃ visuddhi. Yathā apariññātā, appahīnā ca paṭhamāhāravipallāsādayo rāgacaritādīnaṃ puggalānaṃ upakkilesā, evaṃ paṭhamapaṭipadādayo bhāvitā bahulīkatā nesaṃ visuddhiyo hontīti vuttanayānusārena sakkā viññātunti āha “heṭṭhā vuttanayamevā”ti.

87. The phrase Disābhāvena should be connected as nayānaṃ disābhāvena, 'by the absence of direction of the methods.' Its meaning should be understood in the same way as previously stated. By which fourfold group the purification and cleansing of a person of lustful or other temperament occurs: just as the perversions regarding the first nutriment, etc., when uncomprehended and unabandoned, are defilements for persons of lustful temperament, etc., so too the first practices, etc., when developed and frequently cultivated, become their purifications. Because this can be understood according to the method stated, he said, 'It is just the method stated before.'

Atha [M 0.0118] vā purimāhi dvīhi paṭipadāhi sijjhamānā vipassanā attano kiccavuttisaṅkhātaṃ pavattidukkhampi saṅgaṇhantī dukkhānupassanābāhullavisesato dukkhalakkhaṇaṃ paṭivijjhantī “appaṇihitaṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttā. Tatiyāya paṭipadāya sijjhamānā sukhappavattikatāya sammadeva santatighanaṃ bhinditvā aniccalakkhaṇaṃ vibhāventī “animittaṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttā. Catutthāya pana paṭipadāya sijjhamānā sukhappavattikatāya, visadañāṇatāya ca samūhakiccārammaṇaghanaṃ bhinditvā sammadeva anattalakkhaṇaṃ vibhāventī “suññataṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttā.

Alternatively, the insight developed through the first two ways of progress, encompassing even the suffering of continuity, which is called the mode of its own activity, penetrating the characteristic of suffering especially due to the abundance of contemplating suffering, is called “the liberation door of the desireless.” The insight developed through the third way of progress, due to its pleasant progression, thoroughly breaking the compactness of continuity and clearly revealing the characteristic of impermanence, is called “the liberation door of the signless.” The insight developed through the fourth way of progress, due to its pleasant progression and clarity of knowledge, breaking the compactness of the object as a collective function and clearly revealing the characteristic of non-self, is called “the liberation door of emptiness.”

Tathā kāyavedanānupassanā visesato dukkhalakkhaṇaṃ vibhāventī, cittānupassanā aniccalakkhaṇaṃ, dhammānupassanā anattalakkhaṇanti tā yathākkamaṃ “appaṇihitādivimokkhamukha”nti vuttā.

Similarly, contemplation of body and feeling, particularly revealing the characteristic of suffering; contemplation of mind, revealing the characteristic of impermanence; and contemplation of phenomena, revealing the characteristic of non-self—these are respectively called “the liberation doors beginning with the desireless.”

Sappītikatāya assādāni paṭhamadutiyajjhānāni virajjanavasena visesato dukkhato passantiyā [V 0.0112] vipassanāya vasena “appaṇihitaṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttāni. Tatiyaṃ santasukhatāya bāhirakānaṃ niccābhinivesavatthubhūtaṃ sabhāvato “anicca”nti passantiyā vipassanāya vasena “animittaṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttaṃ. Catutthaṃ upakkilesavigamādīhi parisuddhaṃ susamāhitaṃ yathā paresaṃ, evaṃ attano ca yathābhūtasabhāvāvabodhahetutāya sammadeva “suñña”nti passantiyā vipassanāya vasena “suññataṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttaṃ.

The first and second jhānas, being delightful with joy, are called “the liberation door of the desireless” by means of the insight that, through dispassion, especially sees them as suffering. The third jhāna, which due to its tranquil happiness is a basis for outsiders' clinging to permanence, is called “the liberation door of the signless” by means of the insight that sees it as “impermanent” by nature. The fourth jhāna, purified through the removal of defilements and well-concentrated, is called “the liberation door of emptiness” by means of the insight that correctly sees it as “empty,” being the cause for the comprehension of the true nature of both others and oneself.

Evaṃ vihārānaṃ vipassanāvaseneva vimokkhamukhatā, tattha “dibbabrahmavihārānaṃ santasukhatāya assādanīyatā”tiādinā appaṇihitavimokkhamukhatā yojetabbā. Ariyavihārassa paññādhikattā visesato anattānupassanāsannissayatāya suññatavimokkhatā. Āneñjavihārassa santavimokkhatāya aniccalakkhaṇappaṭivedhassa visesapaccayasabhāvato animittavimokkhamukhatā yojetabbā.

Thus, for the abodes, their quality as a door to liberation is by way of insight. Here, for the divine and brahma abodes, due to their tranquil happiness and enjoyableness, their quality as the liberation door of the desireless should be connected. For the noble abode, due to the predominance of wisdom and its special reliance on the contemplation of non-self, there is the quality of the liberation door of emptiness. For the imperturbable abode, due to its tranquil liberation and its nature as a special condition for penetrating the characteristic of impermanence, its quality as the liberation door of the signless should be connected.

Tathā purimānaṃ dvinnaṃ sammappadhānānaṃ saṃkilesavisayattā kilesadukkhavītikkamassa dukkhānupassanābāhullattā appaṇihitavimokkhamukhatā. Tatiyassa anuppannakusaluppādanena dhammānaṃ udayavayavantatāvibhāvanato aniccalakkhaṇaṃ pākaṭanti animittavimokkhamukhatā. Catutthassa uppannānaṃ ṭhitattaṃ byāpārāpajjanena dhammānaṃ avasavattitādīpanato anattalakkhaṇaṃ supākaṭanti suññatavimokkhamukhatā.

Similarly, for the first two right efforts, because their object is defilement and they are for the overcoming of the suffering of defilements, and due to the abundance of contemplation of suffering, they lead to the liberation door of the desireless. For the third, because by generating unarisen wholesome states it reveals the nature of phenomena as having arising and passing away, the characteristic of impermanence is made manifest, leading to the liberation door of the signless. For the fourth, because by undertaking activity for the stability of arisen states it illuminates the uncontrollability of phenomena, the characteristic of non-self is made very manifest, leading to the liberation door of emptiness.

Mānappahānālayasamugghātānaṃ [M 0.0119] sahāyataṇhāpahānatāya taṇhāpaṇidhivisodhanato appaṇihitavimokkhamukhatā. Avijjāpahānassa paññākiccādhikatāya suññatavimokkhamukhatā. Bhavūpasamassa saṅkhāranimittapaṭipakkhatāya animittavimokkhamukhatā.

For the abandoning of conceit and the uprooting of attachment, by abandoning their companion, craving, and by purifying the aspiration of craving, there is the liberation door of the desireless. For the abandoning of ignorance, due to the predominance of the function of wisdom, there is the liberation door of emptiness. For the cessation of becoming, due to its being the opposite of the sign of formations, there is the liberation door of the signless.

Pakatiyā dukkhasabhāve saṅkhāre ñāṇasaccena avisaṃvādento dukkhato eva passati, cāgādhivimuttatāya taṇhaṃ vidūrīkaronto rāgappaṇidhiṃ visosetīti purimaṃ adhiṭṭhānadvayaṃ “appaṇihitaṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttaṃ. Itarassa pana adhiṭṭhānadvayassa suññatānimittavimokkhamukhatā vuttanayā eva.

One who does not contradict the truth of knowledge sees formations, which are by nature suffering, only as suffering. By being intent on relinquishment, one distances craving and dries up the aspiration for lust. Thus, the first pair of determinations is called “the liberation door of the desireless.” As for the other pair of determinations, their quality as the liberation doors of emptiness and the signless is in the way already stated.

Chandādhipateyyā cittekaggatā visesato dhammacchandavato nekkhammavitakkabahulassa hoti, vīriyādhipateyyā pana kāmavitakkādike vinodentassāti tadubhayaṃ nissāya pavattā vipassanā visesato rāgādippaṇidhīnaṃ visosanato “appaṇihitaṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttā. Cittādhipateyyaṃ[V 0.0113], vīmaṃsādhipateyyañca nissāya pavattā yathākkamaṃ aniccānattānupassanābāhullato “animittaṃ vimokkhamukhaṃ, appaṇihitaṃ vimokkhamukha”nti ca vuttā.

One-pointedness of mind with zeal as its dominant principle arises especially in one who has zeal for the Dhamma and is abundant in thoughts of renunciation. One-pointedness of mind with energy as its dominant principle arises in one who dispels thoughts of sensuality and the like. Insight arisen depending on both of these, especially because it dries up the aspirations for lust, etc., is called “the liberation door of the desireless.” Insight arisen depending on mind as a dominant principle and investigation as a dominant principle, due to an abundance of contemplation of impermanence and non-self respectively, are called “the liberation door of the signless” and “the liberation door of the desireless” respectively.

Abhijjhāvinayano indriyasaṃvaro, kāmasaṅkappādivinodano tapo ca vuttanayeneva paṇidhipaṭipakkhato appaṇihitaṃ vimokkhamukhaṃ, buddhi anattānupassanānimittaṃ, upadhipaṭinissaggo nimittaggāhapaṭipakkhoti tadubhayasannissayā vipassanā yathākkamaṃ “suññataṃ, animittaṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttā.

The restraint of the faculties, which is the removal of covetousness, and austerity, which is the dispelling of sensual thoughts and the like, are, in the manner stated, the liberation door of the desireless because they are the opposite of aspiration. Wisdom is the basis for the contemplation of non-self, and the relinquishment of substrata is the opposite of grasping at signs; insight dependent on both of these is respectively called “the liberation door of emptiness” and “the liberation door of the signless.”

Āsannapaccatthikarāgaṃ paṭibāhantī mettā rāgapaṇidhiyā paṭipakkho, karuṇā paradukkhāpanayanākāravuttikā dukkhasahagatāya dukkhānupassanāya visesapaccayoti tadubhayasannissayā vipassanā “appaṇihitaṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttā. Muditā sattānaṃ modaggahaṇabahulā tadaniccatādassanato aniccānupassanāya visesapaccayoti tannissayā vipassanā animittaṃ vimokkhamukhaṃ. Upekkhā ñāṇakiccādhikatāya anattānupassanāya visesapaccayoti tannissayā vipassanā “suññataṃ vimokkhamukha”nti vuttāti evamettha pavattiākārato vipassato nissayato, kiccato ca bhinditvā vimokkhamukhāni yojitānīti.

Loving-kindness, by warding off lust as its proximate enemy, is the opposite of the aspiration for lust. Compassion, whose mode of operation is the removal of others’ suffering and which is accompanied by suffering, is a special condition for the contemplation of suffering; thus, insight dependent on both is called “the liberation door of the desireless.” Sympathetic joy, which is abundant in taking delight in the joy of beings, is, by seeing the impermanence of that joy, a special condition for the contemplation of impermanence; thus, insight dependent on it is called “the liberation door of the signless.” Equanimity, due to its predominance in the function of knowledge, is a special condition for the contemplation of non-self; thus, insight dependent on it is called “the liberation door of emptiness.” In this way, here, the doors of liberation are connected by distinguishing them according to their mode of occurrence, their support for insight, and their function.

Samatikkamanaṃ [M 0.0120] pariññāpahānañca. Saparasantāneti attano, paresañca santāne, tena kāyiko, vācasiko ca vihāro “vikkīḷita”nti vuttoti dasseti “vividho hāro”ti katvā. Tassa pana vibhāvanā idha adhippetā nayassa bhūmibhāvato. Yena paṭipakkhabhāvena. Tesaṃ paṭipakkhabhāvoti tesaṃ āhārādīnaṃ paṭipakkhabhāvo pahātabbabhāvo paṭipadādīnaṃ paṭipakkhabhāvo pahāyakabhāvoti yojetabbaṃ. Tattha paṭipadāggahaṇena vipassanā kathitā. Vipassanā ca cattāro āhāre parijānantī tappaṭibaddhachandarāgaṃ pajahatīti ujukameva tesaṃ paṭipakkhatā, evaṃ jhānādīnampi upādānādipaṭipakkhatā veditabbā tadupadesena vipassanāya kathitattā. Vipallāsasatipaṭṭhānānaṃ paṭipakkhabhāvo pākaṭo eva. Nti sīhavikkīḷitaṃ. Vīsatiyā catukkehi visabhāgato vitthārena vibhattanti tīhi padehi saṅgahetvā kathikattā vuttaṃ “saṅkhepena dassento”ti.

Overcoming and abandoning through full understanding. In one's own and others' continuities: by this, he shows that the physical and verbal behavior is called 'play' (`vikkīḷita`), by means of the phrase 'various modes'. However, its clarification here is intended from the standpoint of the method's ground. By way of being the opposite. 'Their opposite nature' means that the opposite nature of nutriments, etc., is the state to be abandoned, and the opposite nature of the path, etc., is the state that abandons; thus it should be construed. Therein, by the inclusion of 'path,' insight is spoken of. And insight, fully understanding the four nutriments, abandons the desire and lust connected with them; thus, their opposition is straightforward. Similarly, the opposition of jhānas, etc., to clinging, etc., should be understood, because insight is spoken of by that teaching. The opposition of distortions and foundations of mindfulness is evident. Thus, the lion's play. Because it is explained in detail by twenty tetrads in dissimilar ways, summarized by three phrases, it is therefore said, 'showing briefly'.

Indriyānanti saddhādiindriyānaṃ. Dasannaṃ catukkānaṃ niddhāraṇāti yojanā.

Of the faculties, meaning of the faculties headed by faith. The construction is 'the determination of the ten tetrads'.

88. Niggacchatīti [V 0.0114] nikkhamati. Tato niddhāretvā vuccamāno hi niggacchanto viya hotīti. Cattāro puggaleti “taṇhācarito mando”tiādinā (netti. 6) vutte cattāro puggale. Puggalādhiṭṭhānena cettha dhammo vuttoti āha “bhūmiṃ niddisitvā”ti. Tato evāti yathāvuttapuggalacatukkato eva. Itaratthāpīti “sukhāya…pe… puggalā”ti etthāpi. Sādhāraṇāyāti paṭhamacatutthāhipi vimissāya. Yathāvuttāsūti dutiyatatiyāsu.

88. 'Emerges' means 'comes forth'. For when one is spoken of after being determined, it is as if one is emerging. 'Four persons' refers to the four persons mentioned in the phrase beginning 'one of craving-nature, dull' (Netti. 6). Here, the Dhamma is spoken of with reference to persons, hence it is said, 'having pointed out the ground'. 'From that very' means from the aforementioned fourfold classification of persons. 'Elsewhere too' refers to the passage beginning 'for happiness... persons' (Netti. 6), which applies here too. 'Common' means mixed with the first and fourth. 'As stated' refers to the second and third.

Heṭṭhāti desanāhāravibhaṅgavicayahārasampātavaṇṇanāsu.

'Below' refers to the explanations of the Exposition Mode, the Analysis Mode, the Investigation Mode, and the Concurrence Mode.

Eseva nayoti kusalamūlādidvādasatikasaṅgaho anavajjapakkho. “Vodāyati sujjhati etenāti vodāna”nti (netti. aṭṭha. 11) vaṃ netabbataṃ sandhāyāha.

This is the method: the collection of the twelve triads beginning with wholesome roots is the faultless division. 'It cleanses and purifies by this, therefore it is purification' (Netti. Aṭṭha. 11) – this is said with reference to its conduciveness to being led.

Yathā hārauddeso kato, evaṃ nayānaṃ akaraṇe kāraṇaṃ, payojanañca vibhāvetukāmo “kasmā panā”tiādimāha. Nayehi nayantarehi. Sambhavadassanatthanti upapattidassanatthaṃ. Tattha sambhavo anuddesakkamena niddisane karaṇaṃ dassanaṃ payojanaṃ. Yadi hi ime nayā uppattiṭṭhānavasena asaṃkiṇṇā bhaveyyuṃ, hārā viya uddesānukkameneva niddisitabbā [M 0.0121] siyuṃ. Tathā hi vuttaṃ hārānaṃ uddesāvasāne “ete soḷasa hārā pakittitā atthato asaṃkiṇṇā”ti (netti. 1).

Just as the enumeration of modes was made, so too, desiring to explain the reason and purpose for not making an enumeration of the methods, he begins with 'But why...'. By methods, by other methods. 'For showing the possibility' means for showing the justification. There, possibility is the reason, and showing is the purpose of pointing them out not in the order of enumeration. For if these methods were unmixed according to their places of origin, they should be pointed out just in the order of enumeration, like the modes. Thus it is said at the end of the enumeration of modes: 'These sixteen modes are declared, unmixed in meaning' (Netti. 1).

Yasmā panete mūlapadehi mūlapadantaraniddhāraṇena aññamaññaṃ te niggacchanti, tasmā ekasmiṃ niddiṭṭhe itaropi atthato niddiṭṭhoyeva nāma hotīti imassa atthassa dassanatthaṃ “uddesānukkamena niddeso na kato”ti.

But because these, through the determination of other root terms from the root terms, issue forth from one another, therefore, when one is specified, the others too are, in meaning, specified as well. To show this meaning, it is said, 'The exposition is not made in the order of enumeration'.

Idāni tameva saṅkhepena vuttamatthaṃ vitthārena dassetuṃ “paṭhamanayato hī”tiādi vuttaṃ. Tattha taṇhādiṭṭhicaritavasena dvidhā puggale vibhajitvā tesaṃ vasena nandiyāvaṭṭanayaṃ nīharitvā puna te eva taṇhādiṭṭhicarite catuppaṭipadāvibhāgena vibhajitvā sīhavikkīḷitassa nayassa sambhavo dassito, te eva catuppaṭipadābhedabhinne puggale puna ugghaṭitaññuādivibhāgena tidhā vibhajitvā tipukkhalassa nayassa sambhavo dassito. Taṃ sandhāyāha “paṭhamanayato…pe… niddiṭṭho”ti.
