In the second, it is in the sense of location, for that location is Nibbāna. Or, it is an indication of the equal burden of giving requisites and of worthiness, for the purpose of showing the distinction of superior qualities in the duties of householders and renunciants. Or again, ‘by whatever object’ means by the object of craving, such as the cessation of form and formlessness. In the phrase ‘by the objects of the eel-wrigglers and so on,’ the term ‘and so on’ includes the object of wrong adherence to beauty, pleasure, and the like. Just as there is a division of suttas pertaining to defilement based on craving, wrong view, and misconduct, so too, to show the division of suttas pertaining to purification from craving and so on, through serenity, insight, and good conduct, it is said in the Pāli: ‘Craving… and so on… should be indicated.’

Idaṃ [V 0.0132][M 0.0139] evaṃ pavattanti yathā ducintitādivasena bālo hoti puggalo, evaṃ tassa ducintitacintitādibhāvanāvasena pavattaṃ idaṃ saṃkilesabhāgiyaṃ nāma suttanti pubbe saṃkilesadhammavibhāgena vuttaṃ idāni sāmaññato saṅgahetvā vadati. Idaṃ vāsanābhāgiyaṃ suttanti etthāpi iminā nayena attho veditabbo.

This proceeds thus: Just as a person is a fool by way of wrong thinking and so on, so this, which proceeds by way of cultivating wrong thinking and so on, is a discourse named ‘pertaining to defilement.’ Previously, this was explained by way of a classification of defiling states; now he speaks of it in a general way, having gathered it together. In the phrase, ‘This is a discourse pertaining to latent tendencies,’ here too the meaning should be understood in this way.

Kilesaṭṭhānehīti kilesānaṃ pavattiṭṭhānehi. Kilesāvatthāhīti kilesānaṃ pavattiākāravisesehi. Kāmarāgādīhi saṃyujjati kāmarāgādihetu kammavipākādinā. Satipi tesaṃ kālantaravuttiyaṃ saṃyutto nāma hoti, yato kāmarāgādayo “saṃyojana”nti vuccanti. Upādiyatīti daḷhaṃ gaṇhāti pavatteti. Sesaṃ vuttanayattā, uttānattā ca saṃvaṇṇitaṃ.

“By the grounds of defilements” means by the grounds where defilements proceed. “By the states of defilements” means by the specific modes in which defilements proceed. One is bound by sensual desire and so on, by the cause of sensual desire and so on, and by karmic results and so on. Even if they occur at another time, one is still called “bound,” since sensual desire and so on are called “fetters.” “One clings” means one grasps firmly and perpetuates. The rest has been explained because of the stated method and because it is clear.

112. Udāharaṇavasenāti nidassanavasena, ekadesadassanavasenāti attho. Sakalassa hi pariyattisāsanassa soḷasahi paṭṭhānabhāgehi gahitattā. Yathā tadekadesānaṃ soḷasannampi paṭṭhānabhāgānaṃ gahaṇaṃ udāharaṇamattaṃ, tesaṃ pana soḷasannaṃ ekadesaggahaṇaṃ udāharaṇanti kimettha vattabbaṃ. Tena vuttaṃ “ekadesadassanavasenāti attho”ti. Kasmā panettha pāḷiyaṃ paṭṭhānassa ekadesova udāhaṭo, na avasesoti? Nayanidassanatthaṃ. Iminā nayena avasesopi paṭṭhānabhāvo veditabboti.

112. “By way of example” means by way of illustration; the meaning is “by showing a part.” For the entire teaching of the texts is included by the sixteen divisions of the Paṭṭhāna. Just as taking a part of those sixteen Paṭṭhāna divisions is merely an example, what then needs to be said about taking a part of those sixteen being an example? Therefore, it is said: “The meaning is ‘by showing a part.’” But why is only a part of the Paṭṭhāna quoted here in the Pāli, and not the rest? For the purpose of showing the method. By this method, the rest of the Paṭṭhāna divisions should also be understood.

Tattha “appampi ce saṃhita bhāsamāno…pe… sa bhāgavā sāmaññassa hotī”ti (dha. pa. 20) idaṃ vāsanābhāgiyañca asekkhabhāgiyañca. Ettha hi “appampi ce saṃhita bhāsamāno”ti idaṃ vāsanābhāgiyaṃ, “sa bhāgavā sāmaññassa hotī”ti idaṃ asekkhabhāgiyaṃ.

Therein, “Even if one recites little of the sacred text… he is a partaker of the ascetic life” (Dhammapada 20)—this pertains to habit and to the state of one beyond training. Herein, “Even if one recites little of the sacred text” refers to what pertains to habit, and “he is a partaker of the ascetic life” refers to what pertains to the state of one beyond training.

Tathā maghadevasuttaṃ. Tattha hi “bhūtapubbaṃ, ānanda, imissāyeva mithilāyaṃ maghadevo nāma rājā ahosi dhammiko dhammarājā dhamme ṭhito mahādhammarājā, dhammaṃ carati brāhmaṇagahapatikesu negamesu ceva janapadesu ca, uposathañca upavasati cātuddasiṃ, pañcadasiṃ, aṭṭhamiñca pakkhassā”tiādi (ma. ni. 2.308), idaṃ vāsanābhāgiyaṃ. “Idaṃ kho panānanda, etarahi mayā kalyāṇaṃ vattaṃ nīharitaṃ ekantanibbidāya virāgāya nirodhāya upasamāya [M 0.0140] abhiññāya sambodhāya nibbānāya saṃvattatī”ti (ma. ni. 3.189) idaṃ asekkhabhāgiyaṃ. “Pamādaṃ appamādena, yadā nudati paṇḍito”ti (dha. pa. 28) idaṃ nibbedhabhāgiyaṃ. “Paññāpāsāda…pe… avekkhatī”ti (dha. pa. 28) idaṃ asekkhabhāgiyanti idaṃ nibbedhabhāgiyañca asekkhabhāgiyañca.

Similarly, the Maghadeva Sutta. Therein, 'Formerly, Ānanda, in this very Mithilā, there was a king named Maghadeva, a righteous king, a king of Dhamma, established in Dhamma, a great king of Dhamma. He practiced Dhamma among brahmins and householders, both townspeople and country people, and he observed the Uposatha on the fourteenth, fifteenth, and eighth days of the fortnight' (MN 2.308)—this is the portion pertaining to habit. 'But now, Ānanda, this noble practice has been brought forth by me, leading solely to disenchantment, dispassion, cessation, peace, direct knowledge, full enlightenment, and Nibbāna' (MN 3.189)—this is the portion pertaining to the adept. 'When the wise one drives away negligence by diligence' (Dhp 28)—this is the portion pertaining to penetration. 'From the palace of wisdom... he looks down' (Dhp 28)—this is the portion pertaining to the adept. Thus, this example pertains to both penetration and the adept.

Tathā [V 0.0133] “tīṇimāni, bhikkhave, indriyānī”ti (saṃ. ni. 5.493) suttaṃ. Tattha “tīṇimāni, bhikkhave, indriyāni. Katamāni tīṇīti? Anaññātaññassāmītindriyaṃ aññindriya”nti idaṃ nibbedhabhāgiyaṃ, “aññātāvindriya”nti (saṃ. ni. 5.493) idaṃ asekkhabhāgiyaṃ.

Similarly, the sutta beginning 'Monks, there are these three faculties' (SN 5.493). Therein, 'Monks, there are these three faculties. Which three? The faculty of “I shall know what is not yet known,” the faculty of final knowledge'—this is the portion pertaining to penetration. 'The faculty of one who knows' (SN 5.493)—this is the portion pertaining to the adept.

Raṭṭhapālasuttaṃ (ma. ni. 2.293 ādayo) saṃkilesabhāgiyañca vāsanābhāgiyañca asekkhabhāgiyañca. Tattha hi “ūno loko atitto taṇhādāso”tiādinā (ma. ni. 2.306) saṃkileso vibhatto, “eko vūpakaṭṭho”tiādinā (ma. ni. 2.299) asekkhadhammā, itarena vāsanādhammāti.

The Raṭṭhapāla Sutta (MN 2.293 ff.) pertains to defilement, to habit, and to the adept. Therein, defilement is analyzed by phrases such as 'the world is deficient, insatiable, a slave to craving' (MN 2.306); the states of the adept by phrases such as 'alone, secluded' (MN 2.299); and the remaining are the states pertaining to habit.

“Dhamme ca ye ariyapavedite ratā, anuttaro te vacasā manasā kammunā ca;

Te santisoraccasamādhisaṇṭhitā, sutassa paññāya ca sāramajjhagū”ti. (su. ni. 332);

'Those who delight in the Dhamma proclaimed by the Noble Ones, they are unsurpassed in speech, mind, and action; they are established in peace, gentleness, and concentration, having attained the essence of what is heard and of wisdom.' (Sn 332)

Idaṃ vāsanābhāgiyañca nibbedhabhāgiyañca asekkhabhāgiyañca. Ettha hi “dhamme ca ye ariyapavedite ratā”ti ayaṃ vāsanā, “anuttarā…pe… saṇṭhitā”ti ayaṃ nibbedho, “sutassa paññāya ca sāramajjhagū”ti asekkhadhammā.

This pertains to habit, to penetration, and to the adept. Herein, 'those who delight in the Dhamma proclaimed by the Noble Ones' is habit; 'unsurpassed... established' is penetration; and 'having attained the essence of what is heard and of wisdom' are the states of the adept.

Tathā “saddho sutavā niyāmadassī”ti gāthā (su. ni. 373). Tattha hi “saddho sutavā”ti vāsanā, “niyāmadassī vaggagatesu na vaggasāri dhīro, lobhaṃ dosaṃ vineyya paṭigha”nti nibbedho, “sammā so loke paribbajeyyā”ti asekkhadhammā.

Similarly, the verse 'Faithful, learned, seeing the path' (Sn 373). Therein, 'faithful, learned' is habit; 'seeing the path, not siding with factions, the wise one, having removed greed, hatred, and aversion' is penetration; 'rightly would he wander in the world' are the states of the adept.

Sabbāsavasaṃvaro parissayādīnaṃ vasena sabbabhāgiyaṃ veditabbaṃ. Tattha hi saṃkilesadhammā, lokiyasucaritadhammā, sekkhadhammā, asekkhadhammā ca vibhattā. Asabbabhāgiyaṃ pana “passaṃ passatī”tiādikaṃ (ma. ni. 1.203) udakādianuvādanavacanaṃ [M 0.0141] veditabbaṃ. Evametasmiṃ soḷasavidhe sāsanapaṭṭhāne ete taṇhādivasena tayo [V 0.0134] saṃkilesabhāgā, vodānādivasena tayo vāsanābhāgā, sekkhānaṃ sīlakkhandhādīnaṃ vasena tayo nibbedhabhāgā, asekkhānaṃ sīlakkhandhādīnaṃ eva vasena tayo asekkhabhāgā, tesaṃ vasena mūlapaṭṭhānāni eva dvādasa honti. Tāni pana vitthāranayena vibhajiyamānāni channavutādhikāni cattāri sahassāni honti. Yathādassanaṃ panetāni uddharitabbāni. Tāni pana yasmā saṅgahato kāmataṇhādivasena tayo taṇhāsaṃkilesabhāgā, sassatucchedavasena dve diṭṭhisaṃkilesabhāgā, kāyaduccaritādivasena tayo duccaritasaṃkilesabhāgāti aṭṭha saṃkilesabhāgā. Dhammāmisābhayadānavasena tividhaṃ dānamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu, kāyasucaritādivasena tividhaṃ sīlamayaṃ puññakiriyavatthu, samathavipassanāvasena duvidhaṃ bhāvanāmayaṃ puññakiriyavatthūti aṭṭheva vāsanābhāgā.

The restraint of all taints should be understood as all-inclusive by way of dangers and so on. For therein, defiling states, mundane wholesome states, trainee states, and adept states are distinguished. But the non-all-inclusive should be understood as statements that are mere repetitions, such as 'water' and so on, for example, 'seeing, he sees' (MN 1.203). Thus, in this sixteenfold basis of the Teaching, there are these three portions related to defilement by way of craving and so on; three portions related to habit by way of purification and so on; three portions related to penetration by way of the aggregates of virtue and so on of trainees; and three portions related to the adept by way of the very same aggregates of virtue and so on of adepts. Thus, by way of these, there are twelve root bases. But these, when analyzed in detail, amount to four thousand and ninety-six. These, however, should be extracted as they are seen. Moreover, because when grouped together, there are three defilement-related portions of craving by way of craving for sensual pleasures and so on, two defilement-related portions of views by way of eternalism and annihilationism, and three defilement-related portions of misconduct by way of bodily misconduct and so on—making eight defilement-related portions. By way of the gift of Dhamma, material gifts, and the gift of fearlessness, there are three bases of meritorious action consisting of giving; by way of bodily good conduct and so on, three bases of meritorious action consisting of virtue; and by way of serenity and insight, two bases of meritorious action consisting of development—making eight habit-related portions.

Saddhānusārī saddhāvimutto dhammānusārī diṭṭhippatto kāyasakkhīti (pu. pa. mātikā 7.32-36; pu. pa. 26-30) pañcannaṃ sekkhānaṃ paccekaṃ tayo sīlādikkhandhāti pannarasa nibbedhabhāgā, suññatānimittāpaṇihitabhedā paññāvimuttānaṃ tayo aggaphaladhammā, tesu paccekaṃ tayo tayo sīlādikkhandhā, tathā ubhatobhāgavimuttānanti aṭṭhārasa, sikkhitabbābhāvasāmaññena asaṅkhatadhātuṃ pakkhipitvā ekūnavīsati asekkhabhāgā, iti purimāni ekatiṃsa, imāni ekūnavīsatīti samapaññāsa saṃkilesabhāgiyādidhammā honti. Tasmā imesaṃ samapaññāsāya saṃkilesabhāgiyādidhammānaṃ vasena samapaññāsa suttāni honti.

The faith-follower, the one liberated by faith, the Dhamma-follower, the one attained to view, the body-witness (Pug. Pa. Mātikā 7.32-36; Pug. Pa. 26-30)—for these five trainees individually, there are three aggregates beginning with virtue, making fifteen portions pertaining to penetration. For those liberated by wisdom, the three highest fruit-states, divided into emptiness, signless, and desireless—in each of these, three aggregates beginning with virtue, and likewise for those liberated both ways—make eighteen. Including the unconditioned element, due to the commonality of no longer needing training, there are nineteen portions pertaining to the adept. Thus, the former thirty-one and these nineteen make fifty in total—these are the states pertaining to defilement and so forth. Therefore, based on these fifty states pertaining to defilement and so forth, there are fifty discourses.

Yasmā ca te paññāvimuttā ubhatobhāgavimuttavibhāgaṃ akatvā asaṅkhatāya dhātuyā aggahaṇena nippariyāyena asekkhabhāgābhāvato naveva asekkhabhāgāti samacattālīsa honti, tasmā peṭake “cattārīsāya ākārehi pariyesitabbaṃ, paññāsāya ākārehi sāsanapaṭṭhānaṃ niddiṭṭha”nti (peṭako. 21) ca vuttaṃ. Saṅgahato eva pana pubbe vuttavitthāranayena soḷasa honti, puna tividhasaṃkilesabhāgiyādivasena dvādasa honti, puna taṇhādiṭṭhiduccaritasaṃkilesataṇhādiṭṭhiduccaritavodānabhāvena cha honti, puna saṃkilesabhāgiyaṃ vāsanābhāgiyaṃ dassanabhāgiyaṃ [M 0.0142] vāsanābhāgiyaṃ asekkhabhāgiyanti pañca honti, puna mūlapaṭṭhānavasena cattāri honti, puthujjanabhāgiyasekkhabhāgiyaasekkhabhāgiyabhāvena tīṇi honti, puna saṃkilesabhāgiyavodānabhāgiyabhāvena dve eva honti. Paṭṭhānabhāvena pana ekavidhameva, iti paṭṭhānabhāvena ekavidhampi saṃkilesavodānabhāgiyabhāvena duvidhanti vibhāgato yāva channavutādhikaṃ catusahassappabhedaṃ hoti, tāva netabbaṃ. Evametaṃ paṭṭhānaṃ saṅgahato, vibhāgato ca veditabbaṃ.

Since those liberated by wisdom, by not making the distinction of being liberated in both ways and by not grasping the unconditioned element, unconditionally lack the adept's portion, there are thus only nine adept's portions, making forty in total. Therefore, in the Peṭaka, it is said: 'One should investigate through forty aspects, and the establishment of the teaching is explained through fifty aspects' (Peṭaka 21). However, from a collective standpoint, according to the detailed method previously stated, they are sixteen. Again, by way of the threefold division pertaining to defilement and so on, they are twelve. Further, by way of defilement by craving, views, and misconduct, and purification from craving, views, and misconduct, they are six. Again, as the portion pertaining to defilement, the portion pertaining to habit, the portion pertaining to vision, the portion pertaining to habit, and the portion pertaining to the adept, they are five. Again, by way of the root establishments, they are four. By way of the portion pertaining to ordinary persons, the portion pertaining to trainees, and the portion pertaining to adepts, they are three. Again, by way of the portion pertaining to defilement and the portion pertaining to purification, they are only two. But by way of establishment, it is of only one kind. Thus, although the establishment is of one kind, by way of the portions pertaining to defilement and purification it is of two kinds—in this way, by classification, it should be taken up to a division into four thousand and ninety-six kinds. In this way, this establishment should be understood both collectively and by classification.

Imassāpi paṭṭhānavibhāgassa, na purimassevāti adhippāyo. Lokikaṃ assatthīti, lokikasahacaraṇato vā lokiyaṃ, suttaṃ padesenāti ekadesena. Sabbapadesūti taṃtaṃtikānaṃ tatiyapadesu. Buddhādīnanti buddhapaccekabuddhabuddhasāvakānaṃ. Dhammo panettha buddhādiggahaṇena veditabbo, ādisaddena vā.

The intention is that this refers to the division of establishment, not just to the former. 'It has a worldly meaning' (lokikaṃ assatthi), or it is worldly (lokiyaṃ) due to association with the worldly. 'Partially by the sutta' (suttaṃ padesena) means by a single part. 'In all sections' (sabbapadesu) means in the third sections of the respective treatises. 'Of the Buddhas, etc.' (buddhādīnaṃ) means of Buddhas, Paccekabuddhas, and the Buddha's disciples. Herein, the Dhamma should be understood by the inclusion of 'Buddhas, etc.', or by the word 'etc.'

Pariṇamatīti [V 0.0135] paripaccati. Dharantīti pabandhavasena pavattanti. Nti pāpakammaṃ. Teti kusalābhinibbattakkhandhā. Rakkhanti vipākadānato vipaccituṃ okāsaṃ na dentīti attho. Ayañca attho upapajjavedanīyesu yujjati, itarasmimpi yathārahaṃ labbhateva. Tenāha “dutiye vā tatiye vā attabhāve”ti.

'It ripens' (pariṇamati) means it matures completely. 'They sustain' (dharanti) means they proceed by way of continuity. 'Nti' refers to evil kamma. 'Te' refers to the aggregates produced by wholesome actions. 'They protect' (rakkhanti) means they do not give an opportunity to mature and yield a result; this is the meaning. And this meaning is applicable to kamma to be experienced in the next life, but it is also obtained as appropriate in other cases. Therefore, he said, 'in the second or third existence.'

113. Attano anavajjasukhāvahaṃ paṭipattiṃ paṭipajjanto paramatthato attakāmo nāmāti āha “attano sukhakāmo”ti. Sukhānubandhañhi sukhaṃ kāmento sukhameva kāmetīti ca sukhakāmoti.

113. One who undertakes a practice that brings blameless happiness to oneself is, in the ultimate sense, called a lover of self; thus it is said, 'one who desires one's own happiness.' For, in desiring happiness that is connected with happiness, one desires happiness itself, and is thus called 'a desirer of happiness'.

Vitthataṭṭhenāti suvipphāradiṭṭhīnaṃ pavattanaṭṭhānatāsaṅkhātena vitthāraṭṭhena.

'By the meaning of being extensive' (vitthataṭṭhena) means by the meaning of expansion (vitthāraṭṭhena), which is designated as the state of being the sphere of operation for those with very widespread views.

114. Diṭṭhe dukkhādidhammeti bhāvenabhāvalakkhaṇe bhummaṃ, dukkhādidhamme diṭṭhe ñāteti attho.

114. In the phrase 'when the phenomena of suffering and so on are seen' (diṭṭhe dukkhādidhamme), the locative case is used to denote the characteristic of a state from a state. The meaning is: 'when the phenomena of suffering and so on are seen, they are known'.

“Uddha”ntiādi kāladesānaṃ anavasesapariyādānanti āha “uddhanti anāgataṃ, upari cā”tiādi. Gamanenāti cutūpapātagamanena.

That 'upwards' (uddhaṃ) and so on indicates the complete inclusion of time and place, he says, 'uddhaṃ means the future, and also above,' and so on. 'By going' (gamanena) means by the going of decease and rebirth.

115. Nagaradvārathirakaraṇatthanti nagarassa dvārabāhathirakaraṇatthaṃ. Gambhīranematāyāti “nemaṃ”vuccati nikhātathambhādīnaṃ pathaviṃ anupavisitvā ṭhitappadeso[M 0.0143], gambhīraṃ nemaṃ etassāti gambhīranemo, tassa bhāvo gambhīranematā, tāya. Kampanaṃ yathāṭhitassa ito cito ca sañcopanaṃ, cālanaṃ ṭhitaṭṭhānato cāvanaṃ. Ajjhogāhetvāti aviparītasabhāvābhisamayavasena anupavisitvā, anupaviṭṭho viya hutvāti attho.

115. 'For the purpose of making the city gate firm' (nagaradvārathirakaraṇatthaṃ) means for the purpose of making the city's gateposts firm. 'By deep foundations' (gambhīranematāya) means by the state of having deep foundations (gambhīranematā). 'Nema' is said to be the area where embedded pillars and so on stand, having entered the earth. One with a deep foundation is 'gambhīranemo'; the state of that is 'gambhīranematā'. 'Shaking' (kampanaṃ) is the moving of something stationary from side to side; 'stirring' (cālanaṃ) is the dislodging from its stationary position. 'Having plunged into' (ajjhogāhetvā) means having penetrated by way of directly understanding its true nature without distortion; the meaning is having become as if one has penetrated it.

Saṃyojanānaṃ pajahanavasenāti gāthāya vacanasesaṃ ānetvā dasseti. Atha vā pahātabbassa pahānena vinā na bhāvanāsiddhīti atthasiddhaṃ pahātabbapahānaṃ ajjhattaṃ, bahiddhāti padadvayena yojetvā dassetuṃ ajjhattaṃ bahiddhāti orambhāgiyauddhambhāgiyasaṃyojanānaṃ visaṃyogagahitoti imamatthaṃ pāḷiyā samatthetuṃ “tenāha sabbaloke”ti vuttaṃ.

By 'by way of abandoning the fetters,' he shows this by supplying the remainder of the verse. Alternatively, since there is no success in development without abandoning what should be abandoned, in order to show that the abandoning of what should be abandoned—which is established by the meaning—is connected with the two words 'internal' and 'external,' meaning that 'internal' and 'external' are taken as disjunction from the lower and higher fetters, it is said, 'Therefore he said, "in all the world,"' to support this meaning with the Pāli text.

Alobhasīsenāti alobhena pubbaṅgamena, yato yogāvacaro “nekkhammacchando”ti vuccati. Asubhasaññā rāgappaṭipakkhatāya “visesato alobhappadhānā”ti vuttā, dasāsubhavasena [V 0.0136] vā. Adhigatajjhānādīnīti ādisaddena vipassanādīni saṅgaṇhāti. Vihiṃsāratirāgānaṃ byāpādahetukato cattāropi brahmavihārā abyāpādapadhānāti āha “catu…pe… abyāpādo dhammapada”nti. Adhigatāni jhānādīnīti yojanā. Dasānussati…pe… adhigatāni sammāsati dhammapadaṃ satisīsena tesaṃ adhigantabbattāti adhippāyo. Ānāpānabhāvanāyaṃ samādhipi padhāno, na sati evāti dassanatthaṃ “dasakasiṇa…pe… sammāsamādhi dhammapada”nti vuttaṃ. Catudhātuvavatthānavasena adhigatānampi ettheva saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo.

'With non-greed as the chief' (alobhasīsena) means with non-greed as the forerunner, for which reason the practitioner is called 'one whose desire is for renunciation.' The perception of foulness, being the opponent of lust, is said to be 'especially prominent in non-greed,' or it is so by way of the ten kinds of foulness. By 'attained jhānas, etc.' (adhigatajjhānādīni), with the word 'etc.' he includes insight and so on. Because cruelty and lustful delight are caused by ill will, he says that all four divine abodes are prominent in non-ill-will: 'the four... non-ill-will is the path of Dhamma.' The connection is 'the jhānas, etc., have been attained.' The intention is that the ten recollections... are attained; right mindfulness is the path of Dhamma, because they are to be attained with mindfulness as the chief factor. To show that in the development of mindfulness of breathing, concentration is also prominent, not only mindfulness, it is said: 'the ten kasiṇas... right concentration is the path of Dhamma.' The inclusion here of what is attained by way of the analysis of the four elements should also be understood.

116. Upalakkhaṇakāraṇānīti sañjānananimittāni.

116. 'Causes for distinguishing' (upalakkhaṇakāraṇāni) means signs for perceiving (sañjānananimittāni).

Pāpameva pāpiyoti āha “pāpaṃ hotī”ti, “pāpiyo”ti ca liṅgavipallāsavasena vuttaṃ. Ekavacane bahuvacananti ekavacane vattabbe bahuvacanaṃ vuttaṃ.

He says 'evil is worse' (pāpameva pāpiyo). 'It is evil' (pāpaṃ hoti) and 'worse' (pāpiyo) are stated with a reversal of gender. 'Plural in the singular' (ekavacane bahuvacanaṃ) means the plural is stated where the singular should be stated.

117. Olīyanataṇhābhinivesavasenāti bhavataṇhābhavadiṭṭhivasena. Tā hi bhavesu satte allīyāpenti. Atidhāvanābhinivesavasenāti ucchedadiṭṭhivasena. Sā hi avaṭṭupacchedameva vaṭṭupacchedaṃ katvā abhinivisanato atidhāvanābhiniveso nāma. Olīyantīti sammāpaṭipattito saṅkocaṃ āpajjanti. Abhidhāvantīti sammāpaṭipattiṃ atikkamanti.

117. 'By way of adherence to the craving that lags behind' (olīyanataṇhābhinivesavasena) means by way of craving for becoming and the view of becoming. For these cause beings to cling to states of becoming. 'By way of the adherence of over-running' (atidhāvanābhinivesavasena) means by way of the view of annihilation. For that, because it adheres by taking the cutting off of what is not the cycle as the cutting off of the cycle, is called the adherence of over-running. 'They lag behind' (olīyanti) means they fall into contraction from the right practice. 'They rush ahead' (abhidhāvanti) means they transgress the right practice.

Tesañcāti [M 0.0144] tesaṃ ubhinnaṃ abhinivesānaṃ, tadaññesañca sabbamaññitānaṃ.

'And of these' (tesañca) means of these two adherences, and also of all other forms of conceit.

118. Idaṃ iṭṭhavipākaṃ aniṭṭhavipākanti idaṃ iṭṭhavipākasaṅkhātaṃ aniṭṭhavipākasaṅkhātaṃ phalaṃ.

118. 'This is a desirable result, an undesirable result' (idaṃ iṭṭhavipākaṃ aniṭṭhavipākaṃ) means this fruit is designated as a desirable result and designated as an undesirable result.

“Akaṅkhato na jāneyyu”nti etena “ākaṅkhato”ti iminā padena saddhiṃ sambandhadassanamukhena “na jaññā”ti padassa atthaṃ dasseti.

By the phrase 'not wishing, they would not know' (akaṅkhato na jāneyyu), he shows the meaning of the word 'would not know' (na jaññā) by way of showing its connection with the word 'wishing' (ākaṅkhato).

Na upalabbhatīti natthīti attho.

'It is not found' (na upalabbhati) means 'it does not exist'.

120. Tānīti kammakammanimittagatinimittāni. Pattharaṇākāroyeva hesa, yadidaṃ chāyānaṃ [V 0.0137] volambanaṃ. Evaṃ hotīti “akataṃ vata me kalyāṇa”ntiādippakārena vippaṭisāro hoti.

120. 'Those' (tāni) means kamma, the sign of kamma, and the sign of destination. This is just the manner of spreading out, that is, the hanging down of shadows. 'Thus it is' (evaṃ hoti) means regret arises in such ways as, 'Alas, good was not done by me,' and so on.

122. Esakehīti gavesakehi saparasantāne sampādakehi. Dukkhudrayanti dukkhaphalaṃ. Tīhi kāraṇehīti kāyavācācittehi. Tāni hi taṃtaṃsaṃvarānaṃ dvārabhāvena kāraṇānīti vuttāni. Tīhi ṭhānehīti vā tīhi uppattiṭṭhānehi. Pihitanti pidhāyakaṃ.

122. 'By some' (esakehi) means by seekers, by those who accomplish in their own and others' continuums. 'Having suffering as its outcome' (dukkhudrayaṃ) means having suffering as its fruit. 'By three causes' (tīhi kāraṇehi) means by body, speech, and mind. For these are said to be causes in the sense of being doors for the respective restraints. Or, 'by three bases' (tīhi ṭhānehi) means by the three places of origin. 'Closed' (pihitaṃ) means obstructive.

“Uṭṭhānaṭṭhānasaṅkhāta”nti idaṃ pāsāṇabhāvasāmaññaṃ gahetvā vuttaṃ.

The phrase 'designated as a place of exertion' (uṭṭhānaṭṭhānasaṅkhātaṃ) is said by taking the common characteristic of being a rock.

123. Rajamissakanti puppharajamissakaṃ. Tassāti tassa sekkhāsekkhamunino. Mahicchādīnaṃ viya gāme caraṇappaccayā gāmavāsīnaṃ saddhāhāni vā bhogahāni vā na hoti, atha kho uparūpari vuddhiyeva hotīti dassento “pākatikameva hotī”ti āha. Ajjhattikakammaṭṭhānanti catusaccakammaṭṭhānaṃ.

123. 'Mixed with dust' (rajamissakaṃ) means mixed with flower pollen. 'Of that' (tassa) means of that sage who is a trainee or an adept. Showing that, just as for those with great desire and so on, on account of his wandering in the village there is no loss of faith or wealth for the villagers, but on the contrary only ever-increasing growth, he says, 'it is simply natural' (pākatikameva hoti). 'Internal meditation subject' (ajjhattikakammaṭṭhānaṃ) means the meditation subject of the Four Noble Truths.

Tenāti kusalena kāyavacīkammena. Thirabhāvo thāmaṃ nāmāti tassa atthaṃ dassento “thāmavāti ṭhitimā”ti āha.

By 'that' (tena) is meant wholesome bodily and verbal action. Explaining the meaning of 'steadfastness is called strength' (thirabhāvo thāmaṃ nāma), he says, 'strong means stable' (thāmavāti ṭhitimā).

Attasaṃnissayaṃ pemaṃ attāti gahetvā “attasama”nti vuttanti āha “attapemena samaṃ pemaṃ natthī”ti. Bhagavato vipassanāñāṇobhāsappavattiṃ sandhāyāha “paññā pana…pe… sakkotī”ti. Sabbaññutaññāṇaṃ, pana abhiññāñāṇāni ca anantāparimāṇaṃ lokadhātuṃ obhāsenti.

He says that 'equal to self' (attasamaṃ) is said, taking 'love dependent on self' as 'self,' adding, 'there is no love equal to self-love' (attapemena samaṃ pemaṃ natthi). Referring to the arising of the Blessed One's insight-knowledge and its radiance, he says, 'but wisdom... is able...' The Omniscient Knowledge and the knowledges of direct knowing illuminate an immeasurable and infinite world-system.

124. Kissa bhītāti kena kāraṇena bhītā.

124. 'Afraid of what?' (kissa bhītā) means 'afraid for what reason?'

Ṭhapetvāti [M 0.0145] pavattetvā. Vacanīyo yācakānanti yojanā, yācitabbayuttoti attho. Yaññaupakkharoti yaññopakaraṇaṃ. “Etesu dhammesu ṭhito catūsū”ti vuttaṃ catukkaṃ vavatthapetuṃ “saddhoti ekaṃ aṅga”ntiādi vuttaṃ.

'Having set aside' (ṭhapetvā) means having established. The connection is 'to be addressed by supplicants' (vacanīyo yācakānaṃ), meaning 'fit to be asked.' 'Sacrificial implements' (yaññaupakkharo) means the equipment for sacrifice. To specify the tetrad stated as 'established in these four things' (etesu dhammesu ṭhito catūsu), it is said, 'faith is one factor,' and so on.

Gāthāyaṃ vuttadhamme dve dve ekaṃ katvā aṅgakaraṇaṃ dukanayo.

The twofold method is to make the qualities mentioned in the verse into factors by taking them two by two.

Jātidhammanti [V 0.0138] pavattidhammaṃ sandhāya vadati.

By 'subject to birth' (jātidhammaṃ), he speaks with reference to the nature of continuation.

125. Saccekadesato saccasamudāyo anavasesapariyādānato visiṭṭhoti dassento “paramatthasaccaṃ vā hotū”ti āha. Caturo padāti cattāri padāni, liṅgavipallāsena vuttaṃ, cattāro dhammakoṭṭhāsāti attho. Kevalaṃ sattavibhāgadassanatthameva catupadaggahaṇaṃ, na adhigatadhammānurūpatāya.

125. To show that the totality of truth is superior to the truth from a single aspect due to its complete and unreserved appropriation, he says, 'Or let it be the ultimate truth' (paramatthasaccaṃ vā hotu). 'Four phrases' (caturo padā) means four terms, stated with a reversal of gender, meaning the four divisions of the Dhamma. The taking up of the four terms is solely for the purpose of showing the classification of beings, not in conformity with the Dhamma that has been attained.

Nimmadāti na madā.

'Without intoxication' (nimmadā) means 'not pride'.

“Saccavādī jino romo”tipi pāṭho. Tattha romoti diṭṭhirāgarattānaṃ titthiyānaṃ, titthakarānañca adhammavādīnaṃ rāgaviparītadhammadesanato bhayajanako, adhammavādīnaṃ vā tattha ādīnavadassanena bhāyitabbo, appahīnāsaṃvarānaṃ vā durupasaṅkamanato durāsadoti attho.

There is also the reading, 'The truthful conqueror is formidable' (saccavādī jino romo). Here, 'formidable' (romo) means fear-inducing for the sectarians attached to views and lust, and for the heretical teachers who preach a false doctrine, because of his teaching a doctrine contrary to lust. Or, it means he is to be feared by the preachers of a false doctrine because of his showing the danger in that. Or, it means he is hard to approach and difficult to assail for those for whom restraint has not been abandoned.

Sacco ca so dhammo cāti saccadhammo. Tenāha “ekantanissaraṇabhāvenā”tiādi.

'Saccadhammo' is 'he is both truth and Dhamma' (sacco ca so dhammo ca). Therefore he said, 'in the sense of being the sole escape,' and so on.

Ekāyanabhāvanti ekamaggabhāvaṃ, aññamaggabhāvanti attho.

'The state of being the one way' (ekāyanabhāvaṃ) means the state of being a single path, that is, the absence of any other path.

Dassanabhāgiyaṃ bhāvanābhāgiyanti nibbedhabhāgiyameva dvidhā vibhajitvā vuttanti āha “saṃkilesabhāgiyādīhi catūhi padehī”ti. Sesattikānanti sattādhiṭṭhānattikādīnaṃ aṭṭhannaṃ tikānaṃ. Sesapadānañcāti saṃkilesabhāgiyañca vāsanābhāgiyañcātiādimissakapadānañca. Ca-saddena saṃkilesabhāgiyādipadāni ca saṅgaṇhāti. Lokiyattikasseva hi “sesapadānī”ti vuttehi missakapadehi evaṃ saṃsandane nayadassanaṃ, itaresaṃ pana tikānaṃ saṃkilesabhāgiyādipadehi ceva sesapadehi ca saṃsandane idaṃ nayadassananti “vuttanayānusārena suviññeyya”nti vuttaṃ. Samatikkamananti pahānaṃ. Satipi vāsanābhāgiyasaṃkilesabhāgiyadhammānaṃ lokiyabhāve [M 0.0146] purimehi pana pacchimā pahātabbā tadaṅgavasena, vikkhambhanavasena ca. Evaṃ pajahanasamatthatāya pahānanti vuttaṃ “vāsanābhāgiyaṃ suttaṃ saṃkilesabhāgiyassa samatikkamāya hotī”ti. Saṃkilesadhammānaṃ samatikkamena adhigantabbā vodānadhammā viyāti yojanā. Bhāvanā nāma tividhā jhānabhāvanā, vipassanābhāvanā, maggabhāvanāti. Tāsu maggabhāvanāya gahitāya vipassanābhāvanā gahitā eva hotīti taṃ anāmasitvā itarā dve eva gahitā. Tathāpi “bhāvanābhāgiyassa suttassa paṭinissaggāyā”ti vutte kiṃ sabbena sabbaṃ asekkhassa jhānabhāvanāpi [V 0.0139] paṭinissaṭṭhāti codanaṃ manasi katvā pāḷiyaṃ “asekkhabhāgiyaṃ suttaṃ diṭṭhadhammasukhavihārattha”nti vuttanti dassento “asekkhadhammesu uppannesu maggabhāvanākiccaṃ nāma natthī”ti vatvā “jhānabhāvanāpi diṭṭhadhammasukhavihāratthā evā”ti āha.

He says that 'the part pertaining to seeing' (dassanabhāgiyaṃ) and 'the part pertaining to development' (bhāvanābhāgiyaṃ) are stated by dividing the part leading to penetration into two, as in 'by the four terms beginning with the part pertaining to defilements.' 'The remaining triads' (sesattikānaṃ) means the eight triads beginning with the triad of the seven stations of consciousness. And 'the remaining terms' (sesapadānañca) means the mixed terms such as 'the part pertaining to defilements' and 'the part pertaining to latent tendencies.' The word 'ca' (and) also includes the terms beginning with 'the part pertaining to defilements.' Indeed, for the mundane triad, when referred to by 'the remaining terms,' the method of interpretation is seen in this way by combining them with the mixed terms. But for the other triads, this method of interpretation is seen by combining them with the terms beginning with 'the part pertaining to defilements' and with the remaining terms. Thus, it is said, 'it should be understood clearly in accordance with the stated method.' 'Overcoming' (samatikkamanaṃ) means abandoning. Even though the states pertaining to latent tendencies and the states pertaining to defilements are mundane, the latter should be abandoned by the former by way of substitution and suppression. Thus, because of this capacity to abandon, it is called 'abandoning' when it is said: 'the discourse pertaining to latent tendencies is for overcoming the part pertaining to defilements.' The connection is that the states of purification are to be attained by overcoming the states of defilement. Development is threefold: jhāna development, insight development, and path development. Among these, when path development is taken, insight development is also taken; therefore, without mentioning it, only the other two are taken. Even so, when it is said, 'for the relinquishing of the discourse pertaining to development,' considering the objection, 'Is even the jhāna development of an Arahant entirely relinquished?', he shows that in the Pāli it is said, 'the discourse pertaining to the one beyond training is for the sake of pleasant abiding in this very life,' by stating, 'when the states of one beyond training have arisen, there is no function of path development,' and adding, 'jhāna development is also only for the sake of pleasant abiding in this very life.'

Ekaṃ eva bhavabījaṃ paṭisandhiviññāṇaṃ ekabījaṃ, taṃ assa atthīti ekabījī. Sandhāvitvā samāgantvā, nibbattanavasena upagantvāti attho. Saṃsaritvāti tasseva vevacanaṃ. Kulaṃ kulaṃ gacchatīti kolaṃkolo. Purimapade anunāsikalopaṃ akatvā niddeso.

The rebirth-linking consciousness is the one seed of becoming, a single seed; one who has that is called a 'one-seeder' (ekabījī). 'Having sped and come together' (sandhāvitvā samāgantvā) means having approached by way of arising. 'Having wandered' (saṃsaritvā) is a synonym for that. One who goes from family to family is a 'kolaṅkola'. The designation is made without eliding the nasal consonant in the preceding word.

Tesaṃ sotāpannānaṃ. Etaṃ pabhedanti ekabījiādivibhāgaṃ. Purimabhavasiddhaṃ vivaṭṭūpanissayapuññakammaṃ idha pubbahetu nāma. Yo “katapuññatā”ti vuccati, so paṭhamamagge sādhite caritatthatāya vipakkavipākaṃ viya kammaṃ uparimamaggānaṃ upanissayo na siyāti adhippāyenāha “upari…pe… āpajjatī”ti. Tiṇṇaṃ maggānaṃ niratthakatā āpajjati paṭhamamaggeneva tehi kātabbakiccassa sādhitattā. Paṭhamamagge…pe… āpajjatīti anuppannassa atthakiriyāsambhavato. Evaṃ tiṇṇaṃ vādānaṃ yuttiabhāvaṃ dassetvā catutthavādo evettha yuttoti dassento āha “vipassanā…pe… yujjatī”ti. “Sace hī”tiādinā taṃ yuttiṃ vibhāveti. Vimuttiparipācanīyānaṃ dhammānaṃ paripakkatāya indriyānaṃ tikkhatāya ñāṇassa visadatāya vipassanāya balavabhāvo veditabbo. So hi vomissakanayena saṃsaraṇako idhādhippeto “deve ceva mānuse ca sandhāvitvā”ti vuttattā. Idha kāmabhave ṭhito idhaṭṭhako. Manussadevalokūpapajjanato okārena vokiṇṇo. Ariyasāvakassa taṃtaṃsattanikāyupapatti tassa tassa sodhanasadisaṃ kilesamalādianatthāpanayanatoti [M 0.0147] āha “cha devaloke sodhetvā”ti. “Akaniṭṭhe ṭhatvā”ti etena heṭṭhābrahmalokasodhanaṃ vuttamevāti veditabbaṃ.

Of those stream-enterers. This classification means the division into the one-seeder and so forth. The meritorious kamma accomplished in a previous existence, which is a supporting condition for liberation, is here called the prior cause. With the intention that for the one who is called 'one who has made merit,' when the first path has been achieved, since its purpose is fulfilled, that kamma, like one whose result has ripened, would not be a supporting condition for the higher paths, he says, 'for the higher... it would incur...' Futility for the three paths would be incurred, because the task to be done by them has been accomplished by the first path alone. For the first path... it would incur... because of the possibility of an effective action for what has not yet arisen. Having thus shown the lack of reasoning in the three views, and showing that the fourth view is indeed fitting here, he says, 'Insight... is fitting.' By 'If indeed,' and so on, he clarifies that reasoning. The strength of insight should be understood through the maturity of the states that ripen for liberation, the sharpness of the faculties, and the clarity of knowledge. For he who wanders in a mixed manner is intended here, as it is stated, 'having wandered among devas and humans.' One who stands here in the sensual realm is 'one who stands here.' He is scattered by way of rebirth in the human and deva worlds. For a noble disciple, rebirth in various groups of beings is like a cleansing, a removal of the stain of defilements and other harms; therefore he says, 'having purified the six deva realms.' By 'having stood in Akaniṭṭha,' it should be understood that the purification of the lower Brahma realms is thereby stated.

Saddhaṃ dhuraṃ katvāti saddhaṃ dhuraṃ jeṭṭhakaṃ pubbaṅgamaṃ katvā. Saddhāya anussati paṭipatti, saddhaṃ vā pubbabhāgiyaṃ anussati, saddhāya vā anusaraṇasīloti saddhānusārī. Dhammānusārīti etthāpi eseva nayo. Dhammoti panettha paññā veditabbā. Saddahanto vimuttoti saddhāvimutto. Yadipi sabbathā avimutto, saddhāmattena pana vimuttoti attho. Saddhāya vā adhimuttoti saddhāvimutto. Vuttanayenāti uparimaggavipassanāya balavamandamandatarabhāvena. Diṭṭhiyā pattoti diṭṭhippatto, catusaccadassanasaṅkhātāya diṭṭhiyā nirodhappattoti attho. Diṭṭhantaṃ vā pattoti diṭṭhippatto, dassanasaṅkhātassa sotāpattimaggañāṇassa anantarappavattoti vuttaṃ hoti. Paṭhamaphalato paṭṭhāya hi yāva aggamaggā diṭṭhippattāti. Idanti yathāvuttasotāpannānaṃ saddhāvimuttadiṭṭhippattatāvacanaṃ[V 0.0140]. Aṭṭhannaṃ vimokkhānanti catasso rūpāvacarasamāpattiyo, catasso arūpāvacarasamāpattiyoti aṭṭha vimokkhā, tesaṃ.

Having made faith the foremost (dhuraṃ): having made faith the chief, the leading, the foremost. One whose practice is recollection through faith, or whose preliminary recollection is faith, or whose characteristic is following through faith—thus, a faith-follower (saddhānusārī). As for the Dhamma-follower, the same method applies here. In this context, 'Dhamma' should be understood as wisdom. One who is liberated by believing (saddahanto) is a faith-liberated one (saddhāvimutto). Even if not fully liberated in all respects, the meaning is that one is liberated by faith alone. Or, one who is resolved by faith is a faith-liberated one. By the stated method: due to the varying strength, weakness, or weaker state of insight in the higher paths. One who has attained by vision (diṭṭhiyā patto) is a vision-attainer (diṭṭhippatto), meaning one who has attained cessation through the vision known as the seeing of the Four Noble Truths. Or, one who has reached the end of vision (diṭṭhantaṃ patto) is a vision-attainer; this means it is said to have occurred immediately after the knowledge of the stream-entry path, which is called 'seeing.' For from the first fruition onward, up to the highest path, one is called a vision-attainer. This: the statement concerning the status of stream-enterers as faith-liberated and vision-attainers, as described. Of the eight liberations: the four attainments of the form realm and the four attainments of the formless realm, thus eight liberations; of these.

Phuṭṭhantaṃ sacchikarotīti kāyasakkhī, phuṭṭhānaṃ anto phuṭṭhanto, phuṭṭhānaṃ arūpajjhānānaṃ anantaro kāloti adhippāyo. Accantasaṃyoge cetaṃ upayogavacanaṃ, phuṭṭhānantarakālameva sacchikātabbaṃ sacchikarotīti vuttaṃ hoti, “visamaṃ candimasūriyā parivattantī”tiādīsu (a. ni. 4.70) viya vā bhāvanapuṃsakanti etaṃ daṭṭhabbaṃ. Yo hi arūpajjhānena rūpakāyato, nāmakāyekadesato ca vikkhambhanavimokkhena vimutto, tena nirodhasaṅkhāto vimokkho ālocito pakāsito viya hoti, na kāyena sacchikato, nirodhaṃ pana ārammaṇaṃ katvā ekaccesu āsavesu khepitesu tena so sacchikato hoti. Tasmā so sacchikātabbaṃ nirodhaṃ yathāālocitaṃ nāmakāyena sacchikarotīti “kāyasakkhī”ti vuccati, na tu vimutto ekaccānaṃ eva āsavānaṃ aparikkhīṇattā.

One who realizes the end of what is touched (phuṭṭhantaṃ sacchikaroti) is a body-witness (kāyasakkhī). The end of what is touched (phuṭṭhanto) means the limit of what has been touched; the intention is the time immediately following the formless jhānas that have been touched. And this is a usage in the sense of continuous connection; it is said that one realizes what is to be realized in the time immediately after touching. Or, it should be seen as a neuter term indicating a state, as in 'the sun and moon revolve unevenly' (AN 4.70) and so forth. For one who is liberated from the material body and a portion of the mental body through liberation by suppression by means of the formless jhānas, the liberation called cessation is as if considered and revealed by him, not realized with the material body. However, having made cessation the object and eradicated some taints, it is realized by him. Therefore, because he realizes with the mental body the cessation that is to be realized, as it has been considered, he is called a 'body-witness,' but he is not fully liberated, because some taints are not yet exhausted.

Abhedenāti antarāparinibbāyiādibhedena vinā. “Abhedenā”ti ca idaṃ “saddhāvimuttadiṭṭhippattakāyasakkhino”ti idhāpi ānetvā yojetabbaṃ. Yatheva hi antarāparinibbāyiādibhedānāmasaneneva eko anāgāmī [M 0.0148] hoti, evaṃ yathāvuttabhedaāmasaneneva saddhāvimutto, diṭṭhippatto, kāyasakkhīti tayo anāgāmino honti. Ayañca anāgāmino tādisamavatthābhedaṃ gahetvā gaṇanā katāti veditabbaṃ. “Avihādīsū”tiādi suviññeyyameva.

By 'without distinction' (abhedena) is meant without the differentiation of the intermediate-parinibbāyī and so forth. And this phrase 'without distinction' should also be applied here to 'the faith-liberated, the vision-attainer, and the body-witness.' For just as one becomes a non-returner simply by not mentioning distinctions such as that of the intermediate-parinibbāyī and so forth, so too, by not mentioning the distinctions as stated, there are three non-returners: the faith-liberated, the vision-attainer, and the body-witness. And it should be understood that this enumeration of non-returners is made by taking into account such a distinction of state. The phrase 'in Avihā and so forth' is very easy to understand.

Paññāya eva vimutto, na cetovimuttibhūtena sātisayena samādhināpīti paññāvimutto. Ubhatobhāgavimuttoti ubhohi bhāgehi ubhatobhāgato vimutto. Kilesānaṃ vikkhambhanasamucchinnehi rūpakāyanāmakāyato vimuttoti imamatthaṃ dassento “vikkhambhana…pe… vimutto nāmā”ti āha. Arūpasamāpattiyā rūpakāyato, aggamaggena arūpakāyato vimuttaṃ. Yathāha –

'Liberated by wisdom alone,' not also by the surpassing concentration that constitutes mental liberation—this is the wisdom-liberated. 'Liberated in both ways' (ubhatobhāgavimutto) means liberated from both parts, in both ways. Showing this meaning—liberated from the material body and the mental body through the suppression and eradication of defilements—he said: 'Suppression... liberated by name.' Liberated from the material body by the formless attainments and from the formless body by the supreme path, as it is said:

“Idha, bhikkhave, ekacco puggalo ye te santā vimokkhā atikkamma rūpe āruppā, te kāyena phusitvā viharati, paññāya cassa disvā āsavā parikkhīṇā honti, ayaṃ vuccati, bhikkhave, puggalo ubhatobhāgavimutto”ti (ma. ni. 2.182).

'Here, bhikkhus, a certain person, having touched with his body those peaceful liberations that transcend forms, the formless states, abides in them, and having seen with wisdom, his taints are destroyed. This, bhikkhus, is called a person liberated both ways' (MN 2.182).

Yaṃ [V 0.0141] pana mahānidānasutte “rūpī rūpāni passatī”tiādike (dī. ni. 2.129) nirodhasamāpattiante aṭṭha vimokkhe vatvā –

But in the Mahānidāna Sutta, after mentioning the eight liberations ending with the attainment of cessation, in passages like 'one with form sees forms' (DN 2.129), it is said—

“Yato kho, ānanda, bhikkhu ime aṭṭha vimokkhe anulomampi samāpajjati…pe… ayaṃ vuccatānanda, bhikkhu ubhatobhāgavimutto, imāya ca, ānanda, ubhatobhāgavimuttiyā aññā ubhatobhāgavimutti uttaritarā vā paṇītatarā vā natthī”ti (dī. ni. 2.130) –

'When, Ānanda, a bhikkhu attains these eight liberations in direct order... this, Ānanda, is called a bhikkhu liberated both ways. And, Ānanda, there is no other liberation both ways higher or more sublime than this liberation both ways' (DN 2.130).

Vuttaṃ, taṃ ubhatobhāgavimuttaseṭṭhavasena vuttaṃ. Tattha yasmā āruppasamāpattīsu ekāyapi rūpakāyo vikkhambhito eva nāma hoti, tasmā catunnaṃ āruppasamāpattīnaṃ, nirodhasamāpattiyā ca lābhīnaṃ vasena pañca ubhatobhāgavimuttā veditabbā. Esa nayo kāyasakkhimhipi. Aṭṭhavimokkhekadesepi hi aṭṭhavimokkhasamaññā yathā “loke sattā”ti.

What was stated, that was stated with reference to the foremost of those liberated both ways. Here, since the material body is indeed suppressed even by one of the formless attainments, therefore, by way of those who are attainers of the four formless attainments and the attainment of cessation, five kinds of those liberated both ways are to be understood. This is the same method in the case of the body-witness. For the designation 'eight liberations' applies even to a portion of the eight liberations, just as in the phrase 'beings in the world.'

Terasasu sīsesu palibodhasīsādīni, pavattasīsañca pariyādiyitabbāni, adhimokkhasīsādīni pariyādakāni, gocarasīsaṃ pariyādakaphalaṃ. Tañhi [M 0.0149] visayajjhattaṃ phalaṃ, vimokkho pariyādakassa maggassa, phalassa ca ārammaṇaṃ. Saṅkhārasīsaṃ saṅkhāravivekabhūto nirodhoti pariyādiyitabbānaṃ, pariyādakaphalānañca saha visayasaṃsiddhidassanena samasīsibhāvaṃ dassetuṃ paṭisambhidāyaṃ (paṭi. ma. 1.87) terasa sīsāni vuttāni. Idha pana “yassa puggalassa apubbaṃ acarimaṃ āsavapariyādānañca hoti jīvitapariyādānañcā”ti (pu. pa. 16) puggalapaññattiyaṃ āgatattā tesu kilesapavattasīsānaṃ eva vasena yojanaṃ karonto “kilesasīsa”ntiādimāha. Tattha pavattasīsampi maggo pavattito vuṭṭhahanto cutito uddhaṃ appavattikaraṇena yadipi pariyādiyati, yāva pana cuti, tāva pavattisambhavato “pavattasīsaṃ jīvitindriyaṃ cuticittaṃ pariyādiyatī”ti āha.

Among the thirteen headings, the obstacle-heading and so forth, and the occurrence-heading are to be exhausted; the resolve-heading and so forth are the exhausters; the domain-heading is the fruit of the exhauster. For that is the fruit which is internal to its domain; liberation is the object of the exhausting path and of its fruit. The formations-heading is cessation, which is seclusion from formations. The thirteen headings are stated in the Paṭisambhidāmagga to show the equal status of those things to be exhausted and the fruits of the exhauster, along with the seeing of the accomplishment of their object. Here, however, because it is stated in the Puggalapaññatti, 'For that individual for whom there is the exhaustion of taints and the exhaustion of life, without precedent or successor' (Pug 1.42), he makes the connection only in terms of the headings of defilement and occurrence among them, and thus says 'the defilement-heading' and so forth. Therein, although the occurrence-heading is also exhausted by the path—which, having arisen, emerges and brings about non-occurrence after death—yet because occurrence is possible as long as death has not occurred, he says: 'The occurrence-heading exhausts the life faculty and the death-consciousness.'

Kilesapariyādānena attano anantaraṃ viya nipphādetabbā, paccavekkhaṇavārā ca kilesapariyādānasseva vārāti vattabbataṃ arahanti. “Vimuttasmiṃ vimuttamiti ñāṇaṃ hotī”ti (ma. ni. 1.78; saṃ. ni. 3.12, 14) hi vacanato paccavekkhaṇaparisamāpanena kilesapariyādānaṃ samāpitaṃ nāma hoti. Taṃ pana parisamāpanaṃ yadi cuticittena hoti, teneva jīvitaparisamāpanañca hotīti imāya vāracutisamatāya kilesapariyādānajīvitapariyādānānaṃ apubbācarimatā [V 0.0142] hotīti āha “vārasamatāyā”ti. Bhavaṅgaṃ otaritvā parinibbāyatoti ettha parinibbānacittameva bhaṅgottaraṇabhāvena vuttanti daṭṭhabbaṃ.

They are to be accomplished as if immediately after the exhaustion of defilements itself, and the occasions of reviewing are worthy of being called occasions of the very exhaustion of defilements. For according to the saying, 'For one who is liberated, the knowledge arises: "I am liberated"' (MN 1.78; SN 3.12, 14), the exhaustion of defilements is considered completed with the completion of reviewing. However, if that completion occurs with the death-consciousness, then the completion of life also occurs thereby. Due to this equality of the occasion and death, the exhaustion of defilements and the exhaustion of life are without precedent or successor. Hence, he says, 'due to the equality of occasions.' Here, in the phrase 'having descended into the life-continuum, one attains final Nibbāna,' it should be understood that the consciousness of final Nibbāna itself is spoken of in terms of passing beyond the life-continuum.

Caritanti caritā kāyavacīmanappavatti. Ettha ca yena rāgādhikabhāvena puggalo “rāgacarito”ti lakkhīyati, tayidaṃ lakkhaṇaṃ. Tenāha “rāgajjhāsayo rāgādhikoti attho”ti, tena appahīnabhāvena santāne thāmagatassa rāgassa balabhāvo lakkhīyatīti daṭṭhabbaṃ. Eseva nayo sesesupi.

Conduct (carita) refers to the enacted activity of body, speech, and mind. Herein, that by which a person is characterized as 'of a lustful disposition' due to the predominance of lust—that is the characteristic. Therefore it is said: 'The underlying tendency is lust, meaning the predominance of lust.' It should be understood that the strength of lust, having become firm in the continuum due to its unabandoned state, is thereby characterized. The same method applies to the rest as well.

Sīlavantehīti ādisaddassa lopaṃ katvā niddeso katoti dassento āha “sīlavantādīhī”ti. Ādisaddena dāyakādīnaṃ saṅgaho daṭṭhabbo.

Showing that the designation 'by the virtuous' (sīlavantehi) is made by eliding the word 'etc.' (ādi), it is said: 'by the virtuous, etc.' (sīlavantādīhi). By the word 'etc.' (ādi), the inclusion of donors and others should be understood.

Ārammaṇabhūtā [M 0.0150] ñeyyanti ārammaṇabhūtāva ñeyyaṃ.

That which has become an object is to be known means that precisely what has become an object is the knowable.

Puthujjanabhūmiādīsūti puthujjanasekkhāsekkhabhūmīsu. Tattha puthujjanabhūmivasena saṃvaro, sekkhabhūmivasena pahānabhāvanā, asekkhabhūmivasena sacchikiriyā, puthujjanabhūmisekkhabhūmivasena vā yathārahaṃ saṃvarapahānabhāvanā. Pubbabhāgiyā hi saṃvarapahānabhāvanā puthujjanassa sambhavanti, itarā sekkhassa, asekkhabhūmivasena sacchikiriyā. Nayato dassitanti “yaṃ, bhikkhave, mayā ‘idaṃ na kappatī’ti appaṭikkhittaṃ, taṃ ce kappiyaṃ anulometi, akappiyaṃ paṭibāhati, taṃ vo kappatī”tiādinā (mahāva. 305) nayadassanavasena pakāsitaṃ. Sarāgādisaṃvattananti sarāgādibhāvāya saṃvattanaṃ.

'In the stages of ordinary persons, etc.' means in the stages of ordinary persons, trainees, and perfected ones. Therein, restraint applies to the stage of ordinary persons; abandoning and development to the stage of trainees; realization to the stage of perfected ones. Or, as appropriate, restraint, abandoning, and development apply to the stages of ordinary persons and trainees. For preliminary restraint, abandoning, and development are possible for an ordinary person; the others for a trainee; and realization applies to the stage of perfected ones. 'The method is shown' means it is made clear by way of showing the method, as in the statement: 'Monks, whatever I have not rejected by saying, “This is not suitable,” if it accords with what is suitable and opposes what is unsuitable, that is suitable for you' (Mahāvagga 305). 'Tending to lust, etc.' means tending to a state of lust and so forth.

Aññamaññaṃ saṃsaggatoti “saṃkilesabhāgiyañca vāsanābhāgiyañcā”tiādinā saṃkilesabhāgiyādīnaṃ padānaṃ aññamaññasaṃsaggato. Anekavidhoti dvādasavidho yāva dvānavutādhikacatusahassavidhopi anekappakāro. Lokiyasattādhiṭṭhānādisaṃsaggatoti ādisaddena lokiyaṃ ñāṇaṃ, lokuttaraṃ ñāṇaṃ, lokiyañca lokuttarañca ñāṇaṃ, lokiyaṃ ñeyyaṃ, lokuttaraṃ ñeyyaṃ, lokiyañca lokuttarañca ñeyyaṃ, lokiyaṃ ñāṇañca ñeyyañca, lokuttaraṃ ñāṇañca ñeyyañca, lokiyañca lokuttarañca ñāṇañca ñeyyañcātiādiko sambhavanto paṭṭhānabhedo saṅgahito. Ubhayatthāti saṃkilesabhāgiyādike, lokiyādike ca. Yathārahanti yo yo saṃsaggavasena yojanaṃ arahati, so so dhammo. Sambhavāvirodheneva hi yojanā. Na hi “lokiyaṃ nibbedhabhāgiya”ntiādinā yojanā sambhavati.

'From mutual association' means due to the mutual association of terms such as 'partaking of defilement' and 'partaking of latent tendencies,' etc. 'Manifold' means of many kinds, from twelvefold up to four thousand and ninety-two-fold. 'From association with the determination of worldly beings, etc.'—by the word 'etc.', the following possible distinctions of the Paṭṭhāna are included: worldly knowledge, supramundane knowledge, both worldly and supramundane knowledge; worldly knowable, supramundane knowable, both worldly and supramundane knowable; worldly knowledge and knowable, supramundane knowledge and knowable; and both worldly and supramundane knowledge and knowable, etc. 'In both cases' means in the case of 'partaking of defilement, etc.' and in the case of 'worldly, etc.' 'As appropriate' means whatever phenomenon is appropriate for connection by way of association. For connection is possible only where there is no contradiction in possibility. Indeed, a connection such as 'worldly belonging to penetration' is not possible.

Tīsu [V 0.0143] piṭakesu labbhamānassāti tisso saṅgītiyo āruḷhe tepiṭake buddhavacane upalabbhamānassa vijjamānassa, etena na kevalaṃ saṅgaho eva yathāvuttabhedānaṃ paṭṭhānabhāgānaṃ niddhāraṇāya kāraṇaṃ, atha kho pāḷiyaṃ dassanañcāti vibhāveti. Tenāha “yaṃ dissati tāsu tāsu bhūmīsū”ti. “Teneva hī”tiādinā yathāvuttassa atthassa pāṭhānugamaṃ dasseti.

'Of that which is found in the three Piṭakas' means of that which is found and exists in the Tipiṭaka, the Buddha's word, which was established in the three recitations. By this it is clarified that not only is the compilation the reason for determining the divisions and sections of the Paṭṭhāna as stated, but also the seeing in the Pāli text itself. Therefore it is said: 'What is seen in those various grounds.' By 'Therefore indeed,' etc., it shows the conformity of the text to the meaning as stated.

Sāsanapaṭṭhānavāravaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The explanation of the chapter on the foundations of the Dispensation is concluded.

Nigamanakathāvaṇṇanā

Explanation of the Concluding Discourse

Saddhammāvataraṭṭhāneti [M 0.0151] desantarato āgantvā saddhammassa avataraṇokāsabhūte saddhammassavanadhāraṇaparicayaparipucchāmanasikārabahulānaṃ nivāsaṭṭhānataṃ sandhāyetaṃ vuttaṃ, attano vā santāne pariyattisaddhammassa anuppavesanaṭṭhānatāya evaṃ vuttaṃ. Sesaṃ suviññeyyamevāti.

'In the place of the descent of the True Dhamma'—this is said with reference to a dwelling place for those abundant in hearing, retaining, familiarity with, inquiring about, and reflecting on the True Dhamma, which has become a suitable occasion for the descent of the True Dhamma, having come from another country. Alternatively, it is said thus due to it being a place for the entry of the scriptural True Dhamma into one's own continuum. The rest is indeed easily understandable.

Nettiaṭṭhakathāya līnatthavaṇṇanā niṭṭhitā.

The Explanation of the Hidden Meaning of the Netti Commentary is concluded.

Nettippakaraṇa-ṭīkā niṭṭhitā.

The Subcommentary on the Nettippakaraṇa is concluded.
